  - **Model:** MODNet's photographic portrait model (Zhanghan Ke et al., Apache-2.0) as Xenova's fp32 ONNX export, 26 MB. It runs with the short side at 512 and both sides multiples of 32, values scaled to -1..1, and gives a soft matte. Transparent pixels of the clip count as no one.
  - **Other models considered:** BiRefNet lite (MIT) handles any subject but is 224 MB and several seconds a frame. RMBG-1.4 is non-commercial. ISNet's export is AGPL.
  - **In the compositor:** worked out once per frame from the clip's source picture when an effect needs it (cached by content), and handed over per thread like depth (`PersonScope`).
  - **Remove Background:** keep the people or the background; Edge Shift re-edges by signed distance; Soften feathers.
  - **People mask shape (4):** on every video filter, with expansion and feather, combinable with the qualifiers and invertible. It selects nothing without the model; the Inspector offers the download.
  - **Also:** MCP `montage_add_effect` (refusing without the model), `montage-cli models`, `scripts/fetch-models.sh` and the CI caches.
  - **Tests:** on the Armstrong portrait:
    - the matte's size and values (him 1, the backdrop 0, as the Python reference gives), and the cache;
    - over a red track: the red showing round him and his face kept; keeping the background instead; Edge Shift widening the cut;
    - a People mask darkening only him, and inverted;
    - MCP, and the Inspector's download offer.
- [x] Face Refinement (render/FaceRefine):
  - **Faces:** YuNet on each frame (`cachedFaces`, by content), as fractions with their five landmarks.
  - **Mask:** an ellipse from the box, feathered over its outer fifth. The eyes (11 % of the face's width round each eye landmark) and the mouth (an ellipse between its corners) are left out.
  - **Smooth Skin:** the face minus its blur (2.5 % of its width), taken out where the difference is small (a Gaussian in luma at 0.08), so edges stay. Gated by a soft YCbCr skin box and the mask.
  - **Eyes:** brightened (up to 35 %) and unsharp-masked within a soft circle (7.5 % of the face's width). Skin-coloured pixels get half, so the eyelids do not glow.
  - **Also:** Lighten Face, Show Face Mask, MCP `montage_add_effect` (refusing without the face models).
  - **Tests:** on the JFK portrait:
    - the box against OpenCV's;
    - the mask (cheek in, eye, mouth and backdrop out);
    - cheek texture (mean Laplacian) down by over 30 %;
    - the eyes over 8 % brighter;
    - backdrop and suit pixels identical;
    - the mask view, MCP, and the browser's Refine group.
- [x] Relight (render/Relight), from the depth map:
  - **Normals:** from the depth at the picture's size, smoothed (8 px by default) so texture does not read as shape. Slopes are scaled by Relief per half the short side, so the look does not depend on resolution.
  - **Shading:** against a surface facing the camera, which keeps its light. Turned towards the light, a surface is brightened by `intensity * colour * (n·L - n_flat·L)`; turned away, darkened by `shadows * (n_flat·L - n·L)`.
  - **Reach:** limits it to the nearer part of the depth range.
  - **Also:** Show Surface Directions, and a Depth effect in the browser, MCP and the model prompt.
  - **Tests:**
    - a synthetic dome: normals pointing outwards and flat on the backdrop; light from the left brightening its left side and from the right its right; camera-facing surfaces unchanged; a short reach leaving the far edge unlit;
    - on the Armstrong portrait: the helmet's lit side following the light.
- [x] Generate Voiceover: text to speech (core/G2p, media/TextToSpeech, app/SpeechDialog):
  - **Model:** Kokoro-82M v1.0 (Hexgrad, Apache-2.0) as onnx-community's fp16 export, 163 MB. The fp32 export (326 MB) runs no faster on a CPU, and the int8 one is four times slower. Ten voices, six American and four British, as their style vectors (one per input length).
  - **Phonemes:** Kokoro was trained on misaki's phonemes, so misaki's US and UK dictionaries (gold, then silver) are used. They are read from misaki's own release wheel (Apache-2.0, pinned and checked) with core/Zip.
  - **The C++ port of misaki's lexicon covers:**
    - dictionary growth;
    - lookup with the "None" variant before a pause;
    - the stress rules;
    - spelling out initials;
    - a/an/the/to/am by what follows;
    - -s, -ed and -ing stems with misaki's sound changes;
    - numbers through a num2words port: cardinals, ordinals, years, decimals, versions, money in dollars, pounds and euros, and suffixes;
    - %, &, + and @;
    - abbreviations the dictionary knows ("Dr.");
    - curly quotes.
  - **Without misaki's spaCy tagger:** words with several pronunciations take the usual one ("read" is /rid/). Unknown words are split into known words (voice+over), then sounded out by English spelling patterns, and spelt only if they are short capitals.
  - **Synthesis:** misaki's phonemes go to the vocabulary ids, padded, with the voice's style row for the input's length and the speed. Text is cut into pieces of at most 400 phonemes at sentence, then clause, then word breaks, and joined with 120 ms gaps. The output is 24 kHz mono.
  - **Where to find it:** Sequence › Generate Voiceover (text at the playhead, or a caption track cue by cue, re-spoken up to 1.6x faster to fit a short cue; one undo step; WAVs in the Voiceover folder and bin folder), `montage-cli speak` (and `--phonemes`), MCP `montage_generate_speech` (46 tools), `montage-cli models`, `scripts/fetch-models.sh` and the CI caches.
  - **Tests:**
    - the phonemes for five US sentences (money, percent, thousands, versions, decades, ordinals, years, acronyms, initials, titles, quotes, "an") and a UK one are identical to misaki 0.7.4 with spaCy;
    - num2words, ordinals, years and stress moves;
    - speech length, level and speed;
    - Whisper hears "hello", "welcome" and "Montage" in the speech;
    - MCP from a caption track (each cue fitting before the next) and from text on A2;
    - the dialog, with undo.
- [x] Object Removal (media/Inpaint):
  - **Model:** LaMa (big-lama, Apache-2.0) as Carve's fp32 ONNX port, 208 MB, at a fixed 512 x 512. OpenCV's 92 MB build of the same export was slower here (4.8 s against 3.3 s).
  - **Fill:** the mask's bounding box, doubled (at least 128 px, at most the picture), is cut out and scaled to 512. The hole is grown 2 px at that scale, filled, scaled back, and blended in by the soft mask. Pixels outside the mask are untouched. Results are cached by picture and mask.
  - **The effect:** Object Removal (Refine) fills its own mask, so it works with any mask shape, a tracked object, the People shape and the qualifiers. Grow Mask re-edges it by distance. It does nothing without a mask or the model, and Show Mask shows the area.
  - **Also:** MCP `montage_add_effect`, `montage-cli models`, `scripts/fetch-models.sh` and the CI caches.
  - **Tests:**
    - a red disc on a known striped background: the error inside it against the background drops from 0.45 to 0.009, and nothing outside changes;
    - the flag on Armstrong's shoulder: its saturation from 0.21 to 0.004 (white suit), the rest identical, and nothing without a mask;
    - MCP, and the browser's Refine group.
- [x] Film Look (render/FilmLook), like Resolve's Film Look Creator:
  - **Gate weave and aberration:** one resample. The frame is moved by smooth noise in time (sines at unrelated rates, scaled by the gauge); red and blue are pushed apart radially.
  - **Softness:** a blend towards a blur.
  - **Halation:** highlights above 0.6 (soft-kneed), blurred at about 1.6 % of the width and added in red-orange.
  - **Bloom:** highlights above 0.75 blurred wide (6 %) and added neutral.
  - **Per pixel:** flicker (frame-seeded), lifted blacks and a squared radial vignette.
  - **Grain:** noise on a grid the grain's size (gauge-scaled), new each frame and bilinear between cells. It is 80 % shared across the colours and weighted to the mid-tones.
  - **Sizes and seeding:** all sizes are thousandths of the frame's width; everything random is seeded by the clip frame.
  - **Tests:**
    - halation red-orange beside a white square and none far away;
    - bloom neutral;
    - grain keeping the mean, coarser from 65mm to Super 8, the same for the same frame and new for the next;
    - weave moving a dot by up to 2.5 px between frames (none without it);
    - the vignette only at the corners, the fade lifting black, softness softening an edge, flicker within a few percent, aberration splitting colours at an edge;
    - the effect through `applyVideoEffect`.
- [x] Creator transitions (render/Processing `transitionMix`):
  - **Whip Pan:** a push that is fast in the middle, smeared along the move with a sample every 1.5 px.
  - **Zoom Blur and Spin:** A moves (zooms in, or turns and shrinks a little) towards the cut and B moves back from it, each smeared radially or round the centre by how far the pixel travels; a short dissolve hides the cut.
  - **Glitch:** bands of rows thrown sideways and the colours split, with pieces of B early and of A late. The pattern changes a few times and peaks halfway.
  - **Light Leak:** two warm light blobs sweep across a dissolve.
  - **Luma Wipe:** B through A's darkest parts first, or its brightest.
  - **Clock Wipe:** from twelve o'clock.
  - **Tests:**
    - every one exactly A at the start and B at the end, and neither halfway;
    - Whip Pan with A left and B right and A's stripes smeared away;
    - Zoom Blur with the centre still A and the edges smeared;
    - Spin moving a mark;
    - Glitch shifting rows;
    - Light Leak adding warm light over a plain dissolve;
    - Luma Wipe dark first, and bright first;
    - Clock Wipe's quadrants.
- [x] Audio repair and creative effects (render/AudioFx, audio/AudioRepair):
  - **De-Click** (a source effect): AR(32) prediction errors forwards and backwards, per 2048-sample block, in units of the block's median error.
    - A click's core is where both are over the threshold (16x at sensitivity 0, 8x at 50, 4x at 100); cores closer than the longest click are one click.
    - A click runs from the first sample before its core that the past cannot predict to the last one after it that the future cannot. A burst of crackle predicts itself inside, but not its own start and end.
    - The edges then grow while the error stays over a third of the threshold, since crackle dies away rather than stops, plus a 2–3 sample margin. Clicks longer than Longest Click are left as part of the sound.
    - Each is rebuilt by least-squares AR interpolation (Toeplitz normal equations, Cholesky) with a predictor fitted to 1024 samples either side.
  - **De-Hum:** RBJ peaking cuts at the mains frequency and its harmonics, the same width in hertz at each.
  - **Chorus and Flanger:** one modulated delay line with cubic interpolation and feedback, the right channel's sweep up to a quarter turn later.
  - **Phaser:** first-order all-passes swept exponentially between Low and High, with feedback.
  - **Tremolo / Auto-Pan:** sine, triangle or soft-edged square; auto-pan at constant power.
  - **Saturation:** tanh, a biased tanh (DC blocked) or a hard clip, with first-order antiderivative anti-aliasing. The dry sound is delayed half a sample to line up with it, and the gain keeps a -12 dBFS peak where it was.
  - **Stereo Width:** mid/side. Mono bass uses an LR4 crossover on the side, with the mid through the crossover's all-pass, so the two stay in phase.
  - **Pitch Shift** (a source effect): a phase vocoder (2048-point frames, quarter-frame hop, identity phase locking) stretches by the ratio, then a windowed-sinc resampler (Blackman, 16 zero crossings, low-passed when compressing) brings it back. Frames are centred, so nothing is delayed.
  - **Tests:**
    - De-Hum: four hum components down over 25 dB, and 440 Hz and 1 kHz within 0.2 dB;
    - De-Click: 40 clicks and scratches on a swelling chord all found, their error down 42 dB (scratches' tails included), every other sample identical, clean audio untouched; on the JFK clip, 80 clicks down 65 dB, with 4 false finds on the clean speech changing it by -35 dB;
    - flanger: a still comb cancelling 500 Hz and ringing 1 kHz up to 1.5x with feedback;
    - chorus: channels apart, and no change at zero mix;
    - phaser: notches at 1000 tan 22.5° and tan 67.5° Hz;
    - tremolo and auto-pan: gains at known phases, power kept;
    - saturation: tape odd harmonics only, tube even harmonics, level held at 24 dB of drive, aliasing 26 dB below a plain clip;
    - stereo width: mono, identity, double, and mono bass below 150 Hz;
    - pitch shift: a 200 Hz harmonic tone to within 0.03 Hz at +4 and -5 semitones, level within 1 dB, onsets within 5 ms, an octave up through the mixer;
    - all of them as clip effects in the mixer.
- [x] Tone controls on Color Correct (render/Processing `colorCorrect`):
  - Highlights and Shadows are bumps of luma round 0.75 and 0.25 (0.4 either side, up to 0.25 of a stop's worth); Blacks and Whites reach in from the ends (0.4 wide, up to 0.15).
  - The shift is built per frame as a table over 0..2 kept rising, so no combination turns tones over; brightness is scaled (shifted in the deepest blacks), so hue and saturation hold.
  - Vibrance scales saturation by (1 - saturation)², so muted colours move most.
  - **Tests:** neutral by default; each control moving its own range and leaving the far end within 0.005; a monotonic ramp with all four fighting; an orange in the shadows lightened with its ratios kept; vibrance lifting a muted colour 1.6x and a vivid one under 1.1x.
- [x] Track Matte Key (render/Compositor `renderSequenceFrame`):
  - The effect only marks the clip; compositing does the work. Before drawing, each track's clip at the frame is checked for the key, and the tracks it uses as mattes (and hides) are noted.
  - The fill's layer is multiplied by the matte track's own layer at that frame (its alpha, or its premultiplied luma; Reverse flips it). Nothing on the matte track then means nothing shows (or everything, reversed); naming the clip's own track or one that does not exist turns the key off.
  - **Tests:** red only inside a white square on the track above, which is not seen; reversed; with the square gone; not hidden; luma from a mid-grey square letting half through; an explicit track number, and its own track turning the key off.
- [x] Distort and stylize effects (render/StyleFx), Stop Motion (render/Compositor `clipLayer`):
  - **Distort** effects remap with bilinear sampling and clamped (Motion Tile: wrapped) edges. Twirl turns by angle·(1 − r/R)²; Spherize samples at R·(r/R)^k (k above 1 bulges, below pinches); Ripple moves along the radius by a sine dying away to the radius; Turbulent Displace uses fractal value noise (quintic, 3D so evolution is smooth) in source pixels.
  - **Stylize:** Find Edges is a per-colour Sobel (a full step reads 1); Emboss is grey from luma differences along the light; Halftone sizes each dot's area to its cell's ink, with a 1 px soft edge; VHS uses per-line wobble, a rolling tracking band, head-switching tear, soft luma, colour averaged over the bleed behind it, streaky noise and dropouts, lifted blacks and scanlines.
  - **Tilt-Shift** blends towards a blur outside the band, over as far again; **Camera Shake** uses three octaves of noise per axis and rotation, with zoom from the amplitude and angle.
  - **Stop Motion** steps the frame the clip is drawn at, so its source and effects hold together.
  - **Tests:**
    - Wave Warp: a line becoming a sine (20 rows between a crest and a trough), crossing where it should;
    - Twirl: a mark turning round the middle at its radius;
    - Spherize: bulge flattening a ramp at the middle and pinch steepening it;
    - Ripple: moving and changing frame to frame;
    - Turbulent Displace: moving 1–10 px for 10, the same frame the same, evolution changing it;
    - Motion Tile: half-size tiles repeating;
    - Find Edges: flat white, the step dark;
    - Emboss: flat mid-grey, the step raised;
    - Halftone: 50 % grey to within 6 % ink, mostly pure black and white;
    - Duotone: black and white to the two colours;
    - VHS: changed, the same for the same frame, untouched at no amount;
    - Tilt-Shift: the band's detail kept, the top's cut by 80 %;
    - Camera Shake: moving by about the amplitude, the same frame the same, no edges at 20 px and 3°;
    - Stop Motion: a fade held four frames at a time through the compositor.
- [x] Motion Blur and Deflicker (render/TemporalFx; worked out in render/Compositor where the source frame is read):
  - **Motion Blur:** the motion to the frames before and after comes from `chainedMotion` (the consecutive flows Video Noise Reduction measures and caches). Each pixel averages 2–64 samples spread evenly over the shutter's time along that path, centred on the frame.
  - **Deflicker:** each neighbouring frame's mean brightness per area (16 × 9, or the whole frame) is cached by media, size and frame, so playing on reads one new frame. Each area's gain is the trapezoid-weighted average of its neighbours over its own (half weight at the ends, so flicker that alternates frame to frame cancels exactly and a steady ramp is kept), clamped to 0.5–2 and blended bilinearly between area centres.
  - **Tests** (on exported ProRes footage):
    - a white square moving 16 px a frame: 24 soft pixels along its path at 360° (none without), none across it, and identical with the shutter shut;
    - a gradient flickering ±0.2 stops each frame while brightening 0.02 stops a frame: frame-to-frame jitter down 2,400×, with the brightening kept to 0.1 %.
- [x] GIF, image sequences and social presets (render/Exporter):
  - **Animated GIF:** pass 1 renders up to 32 frames spread over the range into a 5-bit-a-channel histogram, and median cut (widest weighted box first, split at the weighted median) makes the palette. Pass 2 maps every frame through a 32,768-entry nearest-colour table after 8 × 8 Bayer dithering (fixed in place, so it does not crawl), and FFmpeg's GIF encoder writes PAL8 frames. Sequences in HDR or scene-referred spaces are delivered in Rec.709.
  - **Image sequences:** the image2 muxer with a frame number put before the extension (from 0); PNG is RGB or RGBA, TIFF 16-bit RGB. They refuse an audio codec.
  - **Social presets:** H.264 with loudness normalisation to -14 LUFS (TikTok, Reels and Shorts at 256 kbps AAC; YouTube).
  - **Tests:** a GIF of bars with a moving square is 480 × 270, 2 s long, and within 1.7 % mean difference of the rendered frames; a five-frame PNG range writes exactly five numbered files, the first matching the picture; the TIFFs are 16 bits a sample; sound is refused for sequences.
- [x] Multiband compressor (render/AudioFx `MultibandCompressor`):
  - Two LR4 crossovers split low, mid and high; the low band also goes through the upper crossover's all-pass (its low plus high halves), so the three add back to an all-pass, flat in level.
  - Each band has its own peak envelope (shared attack and release), a 6 dB soft knee, ratio and gain.
  - **Tests:** flat within 0.1 dB at 60 Hz, both crossovers, 1 kHz and 9 kHz when idle; a loud 80 Hz squeezed 16 dB while a quiet 6 kHz beside it moves 0.00 dB; +6 dB of mid gain lifting 900 Hz and not 8 kHz; in the mixer.
- [x] Behind People (render/Compositor `renderSequenceFrame`): the effect marks the clip; when its layer is composited, the person matte of the canvas beneath it (MODNet, cached by picture) is refined (edge shift, soften) and the layer multiplied by 1 − matte × amount. The same model checks as Remove Background in the app and MCP. **Tests:** a full-frame red layer over the Armstrong portrait shows red round him and the portrait unchanged on his face and suit; at 50 %, half the red over him.
- [x] Cut to the Beat (render/MusicEdit `cutToBeat`, MCP `montage_cut_to_beat`, Clip menu):
  - The music clip's beats (or downbeats) are mapped to timeline frames as the beat markers are; every Nth is a cut, and the last piece runs on to the music's end if that is at least half a piece.
  - Media are taken in order and cycled; each piece is the middle of a video long enough for it (others are passed over) or a still; picture only (the audio track reference is left empty, so nothing linked is placed).
  - **Tests:** a 20-bar song at 128 BPM with a four-second video, a one-second one and a still: clips from the first bar, a bar each (within 0.06 s), back to back to the music's end, the short video never used and the other two alternating, the video's middle taken, no sound added; every two bars over MCP.
- [x] More camera log spaces (render/ColorSpace): Apple Log (Rec.2020), DJI D-Log / D-Gamut, Fujifilm F-Log2 / F-Gamut, Nikon N-Log / N-Gamut, RED Log3G10 (v3) / REDWideGamutRGB, Blackmagic Film Gen 5 / Wide Gamut (its own white, Bradford-adapted to D65), DaVinci Intermediate / Wide Gamut, and S-Log3 with S-Gamut3. Curves are the makers' published formulas. **Tests:** six code values per curve within 2e-6 of colour-science 0.4.7 and back within 1e-6; each gamut's conversion to Rec.709 within 2e-5 (D-Gamut against the matrix derived from its primaries, as DJI's printed one is rounded); an Apple Log grey reads 0.18 in linear Rec.709.
- [x] Shape Wipe, 3D Cube and 3D Flip (render/Processing `transitionMix`):
  - **Shape Wipe:** each shape is a distance function in units of its size (circle, diamond, square; a five-pointed star and a heart in polar form; a cross as two bars). How far a shape must grow to cover its cell (or the frame) is found by walking the cell's edge, so every shape finishes exactly at the end.
  - **3D Cube and 3D Flip:** the faces' corners are turned about the vertical (or horizontal) axis, pushed back a little mid-way, and projected with a camera two frame-widths away. Each face is drawn through the inverse of its square-to-quad homography; faces turned away are skipped, and a cube's visible faces never overlap.
  - **Tests:** each exactly A at the start and B at the end; a circle of B at the centre halfway with A in the corners; a diamond reaching along the axes before the diagonals; a 4-across grid with B at each cell's centre and A at its corners; the cube's edge in the middle halfway (each direction, and up), with empty corners; the flip showing A then B on a narrowed card.
- [x] Sequence Index (app/SequenceIndexPanel): a sortable table rebuilt on every project change; rows keep their clip or marker through sorting (by item data, not position); the filter matches any column, case-insensitively; activating selects and seeks; names edit in place through `EditorState::edit`. **Tests:** four rows for two clips, a title and a marker; filtering by name, kind and track; the start's timecode; activating selects the clip and moves the playhead; renaming as one undo step, with the index following the undo.
- [x] Make Highlights (render/Highlights, MCP `montage_highlights`, Clip menu):
  - Per half second: loudness over the clip's median (20 dB is full marks), picture change between 64 × 36 frames sampled twice a window (against a little over the clip's 90th percentile, so a single cut is not "lively"), and with a description, CLIP shot matches relative to the best. The sum is smoothed over a second either side.
  - Peaks are taken best first and grown while the score stays over half the peak (2–6 s, or what is left), with a window of air between moments; cut points move off words when a transcript has them; moments then play in the order they happened, in a new sequence sized like the active one.
  - **Tests:** 30 s of exported footage with a square moving from 8 to 12 s and a loud tone from 20 to 23 s: a 7 s edit takes exactly 8–12 s and 20–23 s and nothing from the still, quiet stretches; laid out with picture and sound; over MCP.
- [x] Global Mute (app/PlaybackController): the playback device zeroes what it hands the speakers (its level meters are read before that), and scrubbing skips its snippets; `heard()` reports what the speakers would get. **Tests:** a tone on A1 heard, silent with Global Mute on, the track and clip untouched, heard again when it goes off.
- [x] Remove Retakes (core/TranscriptEdit `retakeRanges`, the Transcript panel, MCP `montage_cut_speech` `retakes`): words are compared plainly (lower case, letters only); a restart is three or more matching words, one of them four letters or more, within thirty words of an earlier attempt that did not finish a sentence or held a filler, "sorry", "again" or "let me". Everything from the earliest such attempt to the start of the kept take goes, so three attempts leave only the last. **Tests:** a filler-broken restart cut to the kept take; three attempts to the third; a sentence repeated for effect, a repeat of little words, and a repeat forty words later all kept; over MCP, "Welcome back to the, uh, welcome back to the channel." leaves only the second take (2 s removed).
- [x] Add B-Roll by What Is Said (render/AutoBroll, MCP `montage_auto_broll`, Clip menu): sentences come from the cut's transcript words (closing punctuation or a pause of 0.8 s); each long enough (1.5 s) is embedded as CLIP text and matched against the candidates' visual indexes (`findShots`), keeping matches of 0.2 or more. The best-matching share (coverage) of sentences get a cutaway as long as the sentence (at most 5 s) centred on the matching moment, placed on V2 with no linked audio. **Tests:** two cutaways made from the portraits and a talking head with two sentences: "an astronaut... in front of the moon" gets the Armstrong shot (0.32) and "the president in a dark suit... at his desk" the Kennedy shot (0.28), each at its sentence's start; placed on V2 with nothing else changed; half coverage keeps only the better match; over MCP.
- [x] Dub into English (app/CaptionsPanel `dub`, MCP `montage_dub`, Captions › More): the caption track is translated to English unless it already is (`translateTexts`, kept as a hidden track), each cue is spoken with Kokoro at its time with the room until the next cue (said up to 1.6× faster to fit), on a new audio track named "Dub (English)", and every other audio clip is ducked (media/AutoDuck `duckClip`) under the dub's clips (`clipSpans`, joined across pauses under a second) by 18 dB. In the app the speech and the ducking are one undo step. **Tests:** German captions over the JFK clip: "Hello world" and "the meeting..." in English at the same timings, two voice clips at 0 and 6 s each ending before the next cue, the original down 18 dB under each and back to 0 dB between them; an English track spoken as it is with no ducking; in the app, the dub on a new track, the original lowered by the chosen 12 dB, and undo taking the speech and ducking together.
- [x] Rolling credits and crawls (render/Compositor `renderTitle`; Title's Roll / Crawl, Start Off Screen, End Off Screen and Ease In and Out; Rolling Credits and News Crawl templates; MCP `montage_add_title` `motion`): the laid-out block (box and bar included) moves from its start to its end position over the clip at a steady speed, with a trapezoidal speed profile when eased. Off screen means just past the frame edge; otherwise it is where the title is laid out if it fits, else its first (or last) line at the title-safe edge. **Tests:** a roll absent on the first and last frames, centred halfway and a quarter of the way through at a quarter of the travel, at equal steps either side, straight up; starting in place; eased slower at first and the same halfway; credits taller than the frame ending with the last line at the safe edge; the crawl template centred halfway along the bottom, leftward, and rightward when asked; still titles unchanged; over MCP.
- [x] Two-up trim view (app/TimelineWidget `trimViewChanged`, app/MonitorPanel `showTrimView`, ViewerWidget `setTwoUp`): each update of a trim, roll, slip or slide gesture names the outgoing and incoming timeline frames (a trim's edge and its neighbour, the roll's cut, a slip's first and last frames, a slide's neighbours) with clip-and-timecode labels; the Program monitor renders both in the background at the size shown, the latest request winning, from the project as just edited (the program's own copy is synced first). Release restores the picture. **Tests:** ripple-trimming red's end shows red's new last frame beside blue's first, labelled, blue's label at the new cut; letting go restores the picture; a slip of blue shows blue on both sides; off in the Playback menu, trimming leaves the picture alone.
- [x] Camera RAW stills (media/CameraRaw, optional LibRaw): files named as RAW (25 extensions) are probed with LibRaw (`adjust_sizes_info_only`, the size upright; make and model into the Camera field) and developed once when opened (camera white balance and matrix, AHD, sRGB primaries with LibRaw's default BT.709 curve, 16 bits) into an RGB48 frame that the decoder's still path scales like any other picture. CI and packaging install LibRaw on all three platforms. **Tests:** the test writes its own uncompressed DNG (an RGGB mosaic from a "camera" that sees linear sRGB): developed at 384 × 216 with the red and blue halves where they were and the grey band neutral within 3 %; scaled on request; Orientation 6 turned upright to 216 × 384 with the left half on top; through the compositor in a sequence; a file that is not RAW refused with a message.
- [x] Focus Repair (render/Deconvolve, effect `focus_repair`): Richardson–Lucy on the Rec.709 brightness with a true Gaussian (separable, edges clamped) of the given sigma, scaled with the preview; Biggs–Andrews acceleration (each step starts from the estimate pushed along the last step by how well the last two agree, at most 0.9); each step's ratio soft-thresholded by the noise level and bounded to 0.5–2; the result applied to RGB as a per-pixel gain (0.25–4), blended by Strength. **Tests:** fine 4 px blocks blurred by sigma 2: error 0.192 → 0.126 in 30 iterations, better than Sharpen at its best (0.141); tint ratios kept within 1 %; flat pictures and zero strength unchanged; the catalogue effect identical to the function; a noise threshold changes a grainy picture less.
- [x] Blemish Remover (render/FaceRefine `removeBlemishes`, effect `blemish_remover`, faces as for Face Refinement): within each face's skin mask, brightness and redness are smoothed to a spot's scale (sigma = largest spot / 3.5) and their Hessians taken; a pixel is a spot core when the smaller scale-normalised curvature is both large (relative to the brightness) and at least 0.4 of the larger, so creases and shadow edges (curved one way) and pores (too faint) are not. Cores must sit on skin (the colour round them) and away from the eyes, the nostrils and the hairline; connected cores no bigger than the largest spot and not long are kept, grown by a blur, and filled by normalised convolution from the pixels outside them. **Tests:** four dark spots painted on the JFK portrait's cheeks, forehead and chin: all found and each healed to within a quarter of its added contrast; eyes, mouth and backdrop untouched; on the clean portrait under 4 % of skin pixels change; zero amount changes nothing; Show Spots marks them; through the compositor on a clip of the spotted picture.
- [x] Swap with Previous / Next Clip (core/EditOps `swapClip`, Clip menu, MCP `montage_swap_clip`): the clip and its neighbour exchange places inside the span they cover (the later one moves to the earlier's start, the earlier to end where the later ended, so a gap between them stays put); every clip linked to each moves by the same amount, after checking each lands on empty track (with all of them lifted out). A dissolve joining the two is re-pointed to the new edit; other transitions on their edges go. **Tests:** two linked clips swapped with picture and sound, a third untouched, the dissolve now from B into A, and back; a gap kept between swapped clips; refusals at the ends, on a locked track and where linked sound would land on another clip (nothing changed); from the menu with the selection kept and one undo step each; over MCP.
- [x] Mastering and archive codecs (render/Exporter presets): ProRes 422 and 422 Proxy, ProRes 4444 XQ with alpha, DNxHR SQ / LB / HQX (10-bit 4:2:2) / 444 (10-bit 4:4:4), GoPro CineForm (yuv422p10, or gbrap12 with alpha), FFV1 (MKV, level 3, intra-only, slice CRCs, FLAC) and uncompressed v210; the Export dialog names each. **Tests:** each exported from bars with the JFK clip's sound and probed back: the codec, profile (Standard, Proxy, XQ, DNXHR SQ/LB/HQX/444), pixel format and audio codec as chosen, and the decoded frame within its codec's tolerance of the rendered one (FFV1 and v210 within 0.006); CineForm RGBA and ProRes 4444 XQ keep a title's transparency.
- [x] Log from Spoken Slate (core/Slate `slateFromTranscript`, `logFromSlates`; media bin menu; MCP `montage_log_media` `from_slate`): the first 20 s of a transcript as lower-case tokens (digits and letters split, so "12B" is "12", "b"); "scene" or "slate" + a number (digits, or words up to "nine hundred and ninety-nine") and an optional letter (a single letter or a phonetic word from the ICAO and older alphabets), "shot" + a letter or number, "take" + a number, or a number and letter before "take". A slate needs a scene or a take. **Tests:** seven phrasings (digits, words, "thirty-five", phonetic letters, "12B", shot-only) parsed; the time it was called; ordinary speech, "let's take a break" and a slate after 20 s not taken; end to end, Kokoro says "Scene twelve, take three. Action!", Whisper transcribes it and the clip is logged scene 12, take 3 while the JFK clip is left alone; over MCP on a saved project.
- [x] Paper Edit (render/PaperEdit `findLine`, `makePaperEdit`; app/TranscriptPanel Source mode; MCP `montage_paper_edit`): lines are (media, in, out, text) from a word's start to a word's end; the panel keeps them in a reorderable list (drag, Delete) from any clips; Assemble widens each by the handle (0.1 s, within the media) and lays them out with the highlight layout (picture and sound, back to back) as a new sequence sized like the active one, then makes it active. Quotes are matched word for word, ignoring case and punctuation, from an optional time on. **Tests:** quotes found (case and commas ignored, the first saying and a later one), a quote not said refused; lines laid out in the given order with their air, exactly; nothing to place gives nothing; in the panel, two selections reordered and assembled into an active sequence, undone in one step; over MCP with a quote and a range.
- [x] Automatic deinterlacing (media/Decoder `deinterlaceFrame`, `fieldDominance`): frames flagged interlaced (the frame's own interlaced and top-field-first flags, so mixed and PAFF streams follow each frame) are copied and deinterlaced in their decoded format before conversion: planar or semi-planar YUV, 8 to 16 bits, every plane (interlaced 4:2:0 chroma lines alternate fields too); the dominant field is kept and a pixel of the other is replaced by the mean of its neighbours only when it lies more than a threshold (8 levels at 8 bits, scaled with depth) outside them. **Tests:** a combed frame (a box that moved between fields over a smooth gradient): zig-zag down over 95 %, the box where the kept field shows it, the gradient untouched; bottom field first keeps the other field; a progressive frame unchanged; an interlaced MPEG-2 file encoded in the test decodes with zig-zag under 0.02 (0.35 without deinterlacing).
- [x] Chapter markers (core/Chapters `chaptersOf`, `youtubeChapters`; Marker `chapter`, saved in projects; render/Exporter `chapters`; Sequence menu; MCP `montage_add_marker` `chapter`, `montage_chapters`): chapter markers inside the export range, in time order, each running to the next, with "Intro" at the start when none is there and unnamed ones numbered; written as AVChapters (MP4 and MOV: QuickTime and Nero chapters; MKV) before the header; YouTube's list as M:SS or H:MM:SS lines, with warnings for fewer than three or one under ten seconds. **Tests:** order, intro, numbering, ends, an over-an-hour time, a range timed from its start, both warnings and the project round trip; MP4, MOV and MKV exports read back with their chapter titles and starts, a range export, and none when turned off; from the menu (a new chapter, a marker made one, no change on a second press, one undo step each), the clipboard list with and without In/Out and its warning, the Sequence Index kind and the Export dialog option; over MCP.
- [x] Quality Check and Broadcast Safe (render/QualityCheck `FlashDetector`, `qualityCheck`, `addQcMarkers`, `broadcastSafe`; app/QualityCheckDialog; effect `broadcast_safe`; MCP `montage_quality_check`): the program is rendered at 160 px and mixed to stereo. Flashing as EA's IRIS and the WCAG definitions count it: each frame's change in linear relative luminance (sRGB decode) over the pixels that moved one way together, when they cover a quarter of the screen, accumulated while it keeps going one way; 0.1 (20 cd/m² at 200 cd/m²) with the darker side under 0.8 is a transition, a flash two of them, and more than six transitions in any second fails, spanning its first to last transition; red as WCAG's saturated red, R/(R+G+B) ≥ 0.8 and (R-G-B)×320 changing by 20. Levels: more than 1 % of the picture outside RGB -5..105 % or luma -1..103 % (SDR sequences, before display conversion). Black: mean luma under 4 % with under 0.2 % of pixels above 10 %. Freeze: runs of frames each within 0.15 % mean luma of the last, black excluded. Silence: 50 ms windows under -60 dBFS RMS; clipping: samples at full scale, joined within 0.25 s; loudness ±1 LU of the target and true peak over the ceiling (BS.1770). Broadcast Safe limits luma (soft knee), then pulls each colour towards its luma just enough to fit, on unpremultiplied values. **Tests:** the flash counter on whole frames (5, 3.75, 3 and 1 flashes a second; a fifth and three tenths of the screen; a 10 cd/m² change; both sides over 160 cd/m²; dark saturated red counted as red only, desaturated red not; a slow fade); Broadcast Safe on over-range red (hue and luma kept), below-black, premultiplied white, strict limits, the knee and highlighting; a sequence of flashing, black, a long still and over-range red found exactly with spans and texts, thresholds, a range, the effect clearing the levels problem, markers replacing the last check's and cancelling; the JFK clip's trailing silence (its pauses not flagged), quiet against -14 LUFS, clipping and true peak at +24 dB; the dialog's checks, table, summary, go-to, markers as one undo step and In to Out; over MCP with markers.
- [x] Track fader automation (core/Automation `AutomationRecorder`, `thinKeys`, `trackVolumeAt`, `trackPanAt`; Track `volumeAuto`, `panAuto`, `automation`, saved in projects; render/Compositor; app/MixerPanel; MCP `montage_automate_track`): Off, Read, Write, Latch and Touch as in Premiere's Audio Track Mixer. The mixer plays a lane in Read, Latch and Touch (Write and Off use the fader), gain ramping linearly between frame values every 64 samples so there is no zipper, pan stepping every 64 samples, on the timeline position the fader acts on after downstream latency. While the program plays, the mixer samples each armed track's fader and pan every frame; the gesture keeps the mix on the fader for lanes being written and on the old lanes elsewhere, and playback stopping makes it one undo step: each pass replaces the lane's keys over it, thinned by Ramer–Douglas–Peucker (0.1 dB, 0.005 pan), with a key either side holding the old level; Touch glides back over a second; a pan left flat adds no lane; Write becomes Touch. **Tests:** lane playback per mode and between frames; thinning to the turns; Write, Latch, Touch and Read passes against what they show and leave behind; the project round trip; in the mix, -30 and -45 dB at the right times, no zipper, Off ignoring it, a pan lane, and an exported WAV; in the app, a Write pass from the strip (the mix on the fader meanwhile, lanes, Touch afterwards, faders following the playhead, one undo step), a Touch pass gliding back, a stopped fader keying the lane, and nothing written when nothing is armed; over MCP.
- [x] Track automation on the timeline (app/TimelineWidget `setShowTrackAutomation`, `trackLanePoint`; Sequence › Show Track Automation, remembered): each audio row draws its track's volume lane across the row on the clip-line volume scale (dashed at the fader's level when there are no points, dimmed when the mode ignores it), with the mode named; clip volume lines step aside on audio rows meanwhile. Dragging the line moves the fader's level or the points either side (offsetLine); a point drags in time and value (Shift: value only); Ctrl/Cmd-click adds a point at the line's level; Alt-click deletes one; each one undo step. **Tests:** the fader's level dragged and undone; two points added at their frames and level; a point dragged down and the mixer's fader following it at the playhead; the line between points moving both; Alt-click deleting; turned off, the line gone.
- [x] De-Reverb (audio/SpeechCleanup `dereverb`, `estimateReverbTime`; effect `dereverb`, a source-audio effect like Noise Reduction): STFT of about 32 ms with 75 % overlap; the late reverberation's power in each bin is the power 50 ms earlier (smoothed with 0.85 recursion) times the room's energy decay over that time, e^(-2·3 ln10/RT60·Td) (Lebart, Boucher and Denbigh); gains are power spectral subtraction with 1.3 over-subtraction, a floor from Max Reduction, a 6 dB-per-frame release against musical noise, and the Amount mix. RT60 when not given: the 500 Hz–4 kHz level in 10 ms steps (50 ms smoothing); from each loud local peak the fall is followed until the sound restarts, and three times the time from 5 to 25 dB down is that fall's RT60 (T20 style); the lower quartile of the falls is the room. **Tests:** JFK at 16 kHz in velvet-noise rooms (direct sound plus reflections from 5 ms decaying to -60 dB at RT60): RT60 found within 15 % for 0.6, 0.8 and 1.5 s rooms (0.63, 0.78, 1.41) and dry speech below 0.35 s; against speech with its first 50 ms of reflections, the late reverberation comes down 2.6 dB in a 0.8 s room (the same with RT60 estimated), 3.2 dB in a 1.5 s room with reverberation louder than the voice, less at 40 % Amount, and dry speech keeps 25 dB; as a clip effect through the source-audio cache.
- [x] Export LUT from Grade (render/LutExport `bakeLut`, `writeCubeLut`, `isColorOnlyEffect`; render/Processing `forgetCubeLut`; Clip menu; MCP `montage_export_lut`): the size³ lattice is laid out as a picture (red across, green in blocks, blue down) and run through the clip's enabled colour-only effects at the frame, unmasked (and Broadcast Safe without its stripes); the result is written red-fastest with TITLE, LUT_3D_SIZE and the domain, six decimals, and the LUT cache entry for that path dropped so clips using the file see it. **Tests:** the identity lattice; a grade of Color Correct (temperature, saturation, gamma, lift), Curves and a hue turn written and read back within 1e-5 with its title and size, and a random picture through the LUT effect within 2 % (mean 0.2 %) of the grade itself; a blur and a masked grade left out and named, a disabled effect ignored; a rewritten file picked up; an empty LUT refused; in the app, a black-and-white grade at the playhead (red comes out grey), the blur named in the status bar, nothing written with no clip; over MCP with a 17-point cube.
- [x] Clip markers (Clip `markers`, `markerFrame`; core/EditOps `addClipMarker`, `removeClipMarkerAt`; ProjectIO; OTIO and FCPXML; Sequence menu; Sequence Index; MCP `montage_add_marker` `clip`): a clip marker's time is in source frames like sourceIn, so it stays on its moment through moves, trims and splits; where it shows is `localForSource` rounded, hidden outside the clip's frames. OTIO writes clip markers on the clip in its source time and sequence markers on the stack, with Montage's label and chapter flag in metadata (OTIO's colour names are coarser); FCPXML writes clip markers inside their clip and chapter markers as `chapter-marker` with a poster offset, and reads `chapter-marker` back as chapter markers. **Tests:** the source moment at a frame, refused off the clip; moved, head-trimmed, trimmed off, at double speed and reversed; a split keeping it on both halves but showing it on one; replaced at the same moment, removed where it shows; the project round trip; OTIO round trip of clip markers (label, duration, comment, chapter) and a sequence chapter marker; FCPXML with `chapter-marker` and the clip marker, read back as a chapter; from the menu (on the selected clip, not the sequence; the Sequence Index row and rename; one undo step each; a message with no clip); over MCP.
- [x] Exposure checks on the monitors (app/ExposureView `exposureView`, `falseColorFor`; ViewerWidget `setExposureView`, `shownImage`; a selector on both monitors): Rec.709 luma of the displayed picture; zebras as 45° stripes 8 px apart, half dark and half light, over ≥ 98 % or 65–75 %; false colour on ARRI's scale (purple < 2.5 %, blue 2.5–4 %, green 38–42 %, pink 52–56 %, yellow 97–99 %, red ≥ 99 %, grey elsewhere); the shown image is cached per frame and mode, the rendered frame untouched. **Tests:** each false-colour band; zebras on the white half only and striped about half dark, the skin band catching 70 % and not white; on the Program monitor, the shown picture is the frame's false colour while the frame itself is unchanged, and back to the frame when off.
- [x] Timeline tabs (app/MainWindow `sequenceTabs_`, `syncSequenceTabs`): a QTabBar above the timeline holds the open sequences in order; any sequence made active is added; tabs follow renames and deleted sequences drop out; clicking switches the active sequence, dragging reorders, closing puts a sequence away and moves to its neighbour when it was active, and the last tab cannot be closed. **Tests:** one tab for the demo, a new sequence opening its own current tab, switching by clicking, a rename shown, closing the active tab moving to the other, the last tab refusing to close, and reopening bringing the tab back.
- [x] Marker lists (core/MarkerList `markersToCsv`, `markersToAvidLocators`, `markersToResolveEdl`, `parseMarkerList`; Sequence menu; MCP `montage_export_markers`, `montage_import_markers`): CSV with Premiere's columns (Marker Name, Description, In, Out, Duration, Marker Type, Color), RFC 4180 quoting, label names as colours; Avid locators (user, timecode, V1, one of Avid's eight colours, name: comment); a CMX 3600 EDL with Resolve's `|C:ResolveColor… |M:… |D:…` notes. Import reads a header row by column name (name/marker name/title, description/comment/notes, in/start/timecode, out, duration, type, colour), tab- or comma-separated, with a byte-order mark tolerated, else Avid locator lines; colours map to labels by name or to the nearest; lists all an hour or more in on a sequence shorter than that are moved back an hour. **Tests:** the CSV with quoting read back exactly; Avid lines written and read; the Resolve EDL event and note; a review tool's notes from a 01:00:00:00 timeline with a BOM and quoted commas; empty and timecode-less lists refused; from the menu (all three formats written, markers cleared and imported back as one undo step, a missing file refused); over MCP.
- [x] Magnify, Channel Blur and Noise (render/VideoFx `magnify`, `channelBlur`, `noise`; Premiere 26.2–26.3): Magnify samples the picture from its centre at 1/magnification (bilinear) inside a circle or square sized as a share of the frame height, with an anti-aliased or feathered edge, a border ring and opacity; Channel Blur runs the Gaussian blur on a copy for each channel with an amount, in both directions or one; Noise adds uniform noise of the given amplitude to straight colour, the same channel value for all three in monochrome, from a hash of pixel, frame and channel so each frame is repeatable. **Tests:** on a ramp, the lens centre unchanged, 12 px out showing 6 px out, outside untouched, the border at the radius, a square lens corner at half opacity exact; channel blur spreading red across a stripe's edge with green untouched and a vertical-only blur leaving a vertical stripe alone; noise absent at 0, mean held and spread 0.4/√12 at 40 %, repeatable per frame and different the next, monochrome moving channels together.
- [x] Smart rendering (render/Exporter `SmartRenderer`, ExportSettings `smartRender`, `exportSequence` `smartRendered`; Export dialog): for ProRes and DNxHR exports at the sequence size with no burn-ins, burned captions, proxies or colour conversion, a frame is copied when it is the only enabled clip on the visible video tracks, outside transitions, footage (not a generator) with no effects, normal blend, speed 1, not reversed or ramped, and an identity transform at that frame; its media must be in the sequence's colour space and match the encoder exactly: codec, size, pixel format, frame rate, progressive, alpha only when exporting alpha, and the flavour (ProRes by FourCC from the chosen profile, DNxHR by profile number). The source packet for the frame (sought, then read sequentially) is written with the output's timestamps; the encoder runs slice-threaded so encoded and copied packets stay in order. **Tests:** a ProRes HQ cut whose first 15 frames are untouched footage and the rest inverted: 15 frames copied, byte-identical to the source's packets, identical when decoded, the rest inverted; nothing copied with it off, for ProRes Proxy, with the clip scaled, or with a timecode burn-in; the Export dialog offering it for ProRes only.
- [x] Project Manager (render/ProjectManager `consolidateProject`, `usedMedia`; app/ProjectManagerDialog; File menu; MCP `montage_consolidate`): the kept sequences are the chosen ones and everything they nest (compound and multicam clips); used media are what their clips reference; the copy drops other sequences and unused media (keeping subclips of kept media and the bin items of kept sequences). Collect copies each used file into folder/Media under a unique name; Consolidate, for videos, finds the span the kept sequences use (clip source ranges in each sequence's own rate, in seconds), widens it by the handles within the media, renders that span through a sequence of the media's size, rate and colour space to ProRes 422 HQ (24-bit PCM sound) or H.264 (AAC), re-probes it, and moves every clip's sourceIn, subclip ranges, start timecode and transcript words by the span's start (words outside it dropped); visual and face indexes are dropped. The project is saved beside its media. **Tests:** a ProRes source, an unused WAV and a still, two sequences, a transcript: collect copies two files, leaves the WAV out, keeps both sequences and renders identically; consolidating the active sequence with half-second handles keeps one sequence, makes A a 2-second `_trim.mov`, moves the clip to source frame 12.5, renders within 2 % and moves the transcript (one word dropped); a missing file is reported and no folder refused; the dialog's options and a collect from the app; over MCP.
- [x] Trim mode (app/MainWindow `selectNearestEdit`, `cycleTrimSide`, `trimSelectedEdit`, `endTrimMode`; TimelineWidget `setTrimEdit`): every clip start and end on the target video track is an edit point and the nearest to the playhead is selected, with the clips either side (either may be a gap); both sides roll (edit::roll), the outgoing side ripple-trims its out point and the incoming side its in point (linked sound follows); cycling skips sides that have no clip; after each trim the playhead goes to the edit, the two-up shows the outgoing side's last frame and the incoming side's first, and the brackets redraw. **Tests:** the message with nothing selected; the edit at 60 found from 55 as a roll, playhead on it, brackets and two-up shown; a roll forward, five frames on the outgoing side pushing Blue along, a frame back on the incoming side lengthening Blue's head, one undo step; Esc ending it; at the track's end only the outgoing side, which cycling keeps.
- [x] Render and Replace for video (render/Exporter `renderClipVideo`; core/EditOps `replaceWithRender` and `restoreUnrendered` now also clear and restore the clip's time remapping and generator; TimelineWidget menu for video clips with effects or speed changes): the clip alone, moved to frame 0 with its transform reset and blend normal, in a copy of the sequence at its media's size (a generator at the sequence's), exported to ProRes 4444 with alpha; the result is imported into a Rendered Video bin and swapped in. **Tests:** a ProRes clip inverted at double speed in a larger sequence: rendered at 128 × 72 and one second long, swapped in with no effects or speed, the program frame within 2 % of before, and restored exactly; in the app, an inverted matte rendered to a .mov in Rendered Video, the frame within 2 %, Restore Unrendered bringing back the matte and its effect.
- [x] Source monitor waveform (app/MonitorPanel `ScrubBar::setWaveform`; EditorState `startAudioDecode` now public): in Source mode the scrub bar grows to 40 px and draws the media's min/max peaks per pixel column behind the In–Out shading, markers and playhead, decoding the audio in the background once if needed and filling in on `mediaReady`; media without sound, stills and sequences show the plain bar. **Tests:** jfk.wav opened in the Source monitor: a waveform at 40 px with green columns drawn across the bar, none on the Program monitor; cleared, the bar back to 18 px.
- [x] Sequence Index Colour column (app/SequenceIndexPanel `Color` column): each clip's and marker's colour label as a swatch and its name, so the filter finds markers and clips by colour. **Tests:** a Rose marker found by searching "rose", its Colour cell reading Rose.
- [x] Workspaces (app/MainWindow `arrangeDocks`, `applyWorkspace`, `saveWorkspace`, `deleteWorkspace`; a workspace bar in the status bar, Window › Workspaces, Alt+Shift+1 to 6, `--workspace`): six built-in layouts (Editing, Colour, Audio, Effects, Captions, Logging) laid out from groups of panels across the top, left and right, panels left out hidden but restorable from Window; saved workspaces are `QMainWindow::saveState` under a name. Splits come before tabs, since Qt turns a split beside a tabbed panel into another tab. **Tests:** Colour shows the scopes left of the Program monitor and the Inspector right of it with the Source monitor and mixer hidden; Audio chosen from the bar puts the mixer in front, left of the Program monitor, the Source monitor behind it; a layout without meters saved, left and restored, Reset bringing back a hidden Source monitor; built-in names and blank names refused; deleting it.
- [x] Hover scrub in the media bin (app/MediaBinModel `setSkim`/`clearSkim`, app/MediaBinWidget `setHoverScrub`): moving the pointer over a video's thumbnail picks the media time across it (a subclip's range for a subclip), decodes that frame through the thumbnail cache (at most about 48 frames per clip, never finer than a frame) and draws it with a red playhead line, keeping the last frame shown until the next arrives; leaving restores the poster. Remembered on or off. **Tests:** a red-then-blue video: a tenth of the way across shows red at 0.1–0.3 s, nine tenths shows blue at 1.7–2 s, leaving clears it, and with hover scrub off moving does nothing.
- [x] Offline media and Link Media (media/Relink `isOffline`, `offlineMedia`, `relinkMedia` with Strict and Replace checks, `relinkFromFolder`; render/Compositor's Media Offline slate; app/LinkMediaDialog; EditorState `isMediaOffline`, a two-second per-file cache for painting; red offline clips and bin tiles; bin Replace Footage; File › Link Media; MCP `montage_relink`). A strict relink needs the same kind, picture size and a length within 0.1 s or 0.5 %; a folder search matches by file name ignoring case, then by name with another extension, nearest the folder first, to a depth; subclips follow their media. **Tests:** a moved card: three items offline with the subclip, the clip rendered as the red slate with white text; a sound, another size, another length and a missing file refused; the folder above finding all three, the subclip and rating kept, the picture back; depth 0 finding nothing; a Windows path's `.mxf` found as the `.mp4`; Replace Footage taking a smaller file and its name but not a sound. In the app: the dialog on open with both files listed, red in the bin's tooltip; a wrong file refused with the reason; locating the take finding the sound beside it and closing the dialog, undone in one step; a folder search; Replace Footage keeping the clip. Over MCP: a consolidated copy's moved media listed and found again.
- [x] Auditions (core/Model `Take`, `Clip::takes`/`take`; core/EditOps `addTakes`, `pickTake`, `cycleTake`, `finalizeAudition`; ProjectIO; Clip › Audition with Ctrl+Alt+Y / Right / Left; a "take n/m" badge on the clip; Project Manager keeping take media whole; MCP `montage_audition`). Each take's in-point is an offset from the clip's own, so trims, splits and slips carry the takes along; picking re-bases the offsets on the new pick and moves linked clips playing the same media with it. **Tests:** a sound refused as a take for a video clip; take 2 played from its in-point in the same place with the effect kept and the linked sound following; after a five-frame head trim, cycling back to take 1 starting five frames later; both halves of a split keeping their takes at the right place; saved and read back; finalized. In the app: added from the bin, Next Take turning the picture from red to blue in one undo step, then finalized. Over MCP: added, picked next, a bad number refused, finalized and listed.
- [x] Duplicate frame markers (core/EditOps `duplicateFrames`; TimelineWidget `setShowDuplicateFrames`, recomputed when the project changes; Sequence › Show Duplicate Frame Markers, remembered). Each video clip's source span is compared with every other clip of the same media; the shared source frames are mapped back to the clip's timeline through its speed and merged; each file gets its own colour. **Tests:** three uses of one file: the two sharing frames 20–40 striped there and the third not; at double speed the stripe halves; another file's repeats in a group of their own; sound ignored. In the app, a take used again striped under its first use, and the toggle.
- [x] Through edits (core/EditOps `throughEdits`, `joinThroughEdit`, `joinThroughEdits`; a white arrowhead into each through edit on the timeline; Clip › Join Through Edits). A through edit needs the same media continuing exactly (within 0.001 frame) at the same constant speed, the same kinds of effects, no dissolve, and neither clip an audition or rendered; joining keeps the outgoing clip's id and settings, adds the incoming clip's markers and moves its out-edge transition, and joins linked clips that run through too. **Tests:** a clip and its sound razored: both cuts found; a frame's gap, a slipped half, another speed or a dissolve breaking it; joined back to one 100-frame clip on each track with the marker kept; two cuts joined at once. In the app, the razor cut marked, joined from the menu, undone.
- [x] Changed media reload and Edit Original (EditorState `reloadChangedMedia` with a QFileSystemWatcher kept on the project's media files and a 400 ms quiet period; MediaPool `forget`; ThumbnailCache `forget`; MediaBinModel `forgetThumbnail`; the bin's Edit Original). A changed file's frames, idle decoders, audio and peaks are dropped, its details re-read (subclips keep their own length), sound decoded again, and the monitors redrawn; a file replaced by a new one is watched again. The render cache already keys frames on the file's size and date. **Tests:** a red PNG in the cut re-saved blue: the change signalled for that item and the frame blue; re-saved larger and green: the new width read and the frame green.
- [x] Quad scopes (app/ScopesWidget `Mode::Quad`, "All Four"): the waveform, parade, vectorscope and histogram analysed from one downsampled frame and drawn in a 2 × 2 grid. **Tests:** a ramp-and-bars frame shows a trace in every quarter; switching back to one scope keeps the signal.
- [x] Track folders (core/Model `Track::folder`, `Sequence::collapsedFolders`; core/EditOps `setTrackFolder`, `folderTracks`, `folderCollapsed`/`setFolderCollapsed`, `renameFolder`; ProjectIO; TimelineWidget `layoutRows` with folder header rows, `toggleFolder`, folder mute/solo/visibility, header context menus). A run of adjacent tracks in one folder gets a 24 px header above it; collapsed, the tracks' rows are left out of the layout (so every row-based drawing and hit test skips them) and their clips drawn in outline on the header's row. **Tests:** tracks put in a folder, refusals for no tracks and a slash, collapsed, renamed (staying collapsed), a taken name refused, saved and read back, emptied and forgotten. In the app: a folder header above A2 and A3; its arrow clicked to collapse them (A4 still shown, the clip on A2 no longer drawn), undone and redone; its M button muting and unmuting both tracks and not A4.
- [x] Keyframe repeat (core/Model `Repeat`, `Param::repeat`, evaluated after the last key as Loop, PingPong or Offset; ProjectIO; Keyframes panel › After Last Keyframe, `setRepeat` on the selected keys' parameters, one undo step). The interpolation moved into a helper so repeating needs no copy of the keys. **Tests:** two keys 0→10 over frames 10–20: held 10 at 25, looped 5 at 25 and 0 at 30, ping-pong 5, 2 on the way back and 2 forwards again, offset 15 at 25 and 32 at 42; held before the first key and with one key; saved and read back. In the app, a rotation set to Loop and Offset from the panel spinning on to 225° at frame 25, undone.
- [x] Clip durations on the timeline (TimelineWidget `setShowClipDurations`; Sequence › Show Clip Durations, remembered): the duration as timecode among the name strip's badges. **Tests:** toggled from the menu, the zoomed-in timeline drawn differently with it on.
- [x] Multi-clip Inspector edits (app/InspectorWidget: `Target::clip`/`effect`, `otherSelected`, `applyToOthers`, `matchingEffect`; a note when several clips are selected). Parameter rows and the colour wheels write the same values to the matching effect of every other selected clip on the same kind of track, at that clip's own playhead-relative time, inside the same edit; fixed attributes match by kind, generators by type, stack effects by type and their place among those of their type. **Tests:** Red and Blue selected: the note says 2, opacity changed once on both, one undo restoring both; one clip selected, no note.
- [x] Background render (render/RenderCache `rangesToRender`; MainWindow `setBackgroundRender`, `startBackgroundRender`, `stopBackgroundRender`; Sequence › Background Render, remembered, off by default). After a quiet spell (4 s) the merged stretches under video clips with effects or generated pictures (titles, shapes) and inside transitions are rendered into the render cache on a worker thread, skipping frames already cached; edits, sequence switches and playback cancel it at the next frame and restart the wait; closing the window cancels it. **Tests:** a blur on Blue makes its whole length a stretch to render; with background render on and a 50 ms quiet spell, at least that many frames are rendered and the job finishes; turned off again.
- [x] Folder VCA faders (core/Model `Sequence::folderGains`; core/EditOps `folderGain`, `setFolderGain`, kept through renames and dropped with the folder; ProjectIO; render/Compositor multiplying each track's gain by its folder's; app/MixerPanel folder strips after the track strips, `folderFader`, rebuilt when the folders change). **Tests:** a steady tone on a track in a folder 6 dB down at -6, the track's own -4 adding to -10, the level kept through a rename, back to -4 out of the folder with the setting gone; saved and read back; in the app, the folder's mixer fader set to -6 dB as one undo step and following the undo, and gone with the project.
- [x] Gap selection and ripple delete (TimelineWidget `selectGapAt`, `selectedGap`, a click on empty track space with the Select tool, highlighted; MainWindow `deleteSelection` closing a selected gap with `edit::closeGap` when no clips are selected). The gap is dropped when the project changes, a clip is selected or a drag starts. **Tests:** Blue moved 15 frames later; a click between Red and Blue selects exactly that gap with nothing else selected; Delete ripples Blue back to Red's end, undone; no gap on a clip; selecting a clip drops it.
- [x] Effect presets (core/EffectPresets `EffectPreset`, `presetToJson`/`presetFromJson`, `applyPreset`; ProjectIO `effectToJsonString`/`effectFromJsonString`; app/EffectPresetStore in the application's Presets folder; the Effects browser's Presets section with delete, import and show-folder; MainWindow `saveEffectsAsPreset`, `applyEffectPreset`, Clip › Save Effects as Preset; a preset dropped on a timeline clip). Applying adds the effects after the clip's own with new ids, keyframes where they were. **Tests:** a blur and keyframed brightness saved and read back, non-presets refused, applied after an existing effect with new ids and the keyframe value kept. In the app: refused with nothing selected; saved from Red and listed under Presets; applied to Blue as one undo step with its radius; deleted.
- [x] Close Up (core/EditOps `closeUp`; MainWindow `closeUp` with face detection on the middle frame; Clip › Close Up). The copy keeps the clip's pictures and effects, unlinked, scaled `zoom` times the clip's own scale, with its position putting the face (u, v) at the middle a little above centre, clamped so the picture still covers the frame; it goes on the track above, or a new track when that is the top or the stretch is taken. **Tests:** a face three quarters across a 1920 × 1080 clip: a track added, the copy over frames 20–60 with the same source, scale 150 and position clamped to −480, −81; outside the clip and audio refused; a second close-up on another new track. In the app, on the Kennedy portrait: on V2 at 150 %, moved towards the face when the model is installed, undone.
- [x] Source edits from Resolve's Cut and Edit pages (core/EditOps `placeOnTop`, `rippleOverwrite`, `nearestEdit`; EditorState `sourceEdit(SourceEdit)`, which Insert and Overwrite now go through; Clip › Place on Top F12, Append at End Shift+F12, Ripple Overwrite Shift+F10, Smart Insert; MCP `montage_place_media` `mode`). Place on Top dry-runs the edit on a copy for its length and kinds, then takes the first video track above the target and the first audio track from the target with nothing over that stretch, adding tracks when none have room. Ripple Overwrite removes the clip and its linked clips, moves what starts at or after the old end by the difference in length on those tracks and the sync-locked ones that can follow (a clip across the old end stays put), and overwrites the range at the old start. Smart Insert inserts at the nearest clip start or end on the target track. **Tests:** core: two linked 60-frame clips, a 20-frame place on top over 30–50 on V2 and A2 with V1 and A1 untouched, a second one on the tracks above; ripple overwrite of the second clip to 20 frames (source 200, still linked, sequence 80 long); an unknown clip refused; nearest edits at 55, 25 and 75 and on an empty track. App: Append at 120 with the playhead after it; two Place on Tops; Ripple Overwrite and its refusal off any clip; Smart Insert at 55 landing at 60. MCP: place_on_top then ripple_overwrite of the clip under it (the clip on top stays), refusals for no clip and an unknown mode.

- [x] Review fixes for card offload and out-of-sync indicators (a read-through of both commits). Offload: every write is checked down to the disk (Qt's flush, fsync, F_FULLFSYNC and an uncached writer on macOS, the closed file's error and size) so a full drive or a late network error is never reported as a good copy; Offload cannot be started twice at once; folders that cannot be read, links and special files are reported (a card with no files is an error); copies are checked against each file's earliest record not marked failed, as ascmhl does, so recorded damage never becomes the reference and hashes Montage cannot compute are never recorded as if checked; C4 IDs keep their case; destinations on the card (also at a Windows drive root), its parent (the copy would be the card) and the same folder twice (case-insensitively where the file system is) are refused before anything is made; a drive root is named by its volume label and another card of the same name goes to "<name> 2"; a copy that fails its check is removed, and a damaged earlier copy that its hash list vouches for is copied again; everything but OS junk is copied (nested ascmhl folders as files, the card's own history even without a new list) while hash lists leave their ignore patterns out; a file changing size mid-copy, unreadable copy histories and cancelling during read-back are handled; names are NFC on macOS. MCP import skips what the project has, half-written parts and the hash lists, groups image sequences, and opens the project before the offload. Sync: Slip into Sync is right for reversed clips; moving every clip at once never overwrites the next clip's head; half-frame offsets (which moving cannot fix) are not flagged; the reference is the group's clip of the same media; large offsets read as whole numbers. Tests: core `syncOffsetsAndRepair` (reversed slip, half frame, cutaway anchor), media `cardOffloadWithMhl` (recorded damage stays a failure, a damaged copy replaced, a same-size stranger left alone, a second card of the same name beside the first, parent and duplicate destinations refused, links reported, an empty card, nested hash lists copied, the card's history without a new list, a C4 history verified), `mcpSyncCheck` (a whole track moved back intact).

App: the menu action asking for a selection.
- [x] Audio-reactive motion (Descript's and CapCut's audiograms, After Effects' Convert Audio to Keyframes, Premiere's audio-driven keys). render/AudioReactive: `trackSoundAt` (the sound an audio track, or every track summed, plays around a frame, through each clip's speed, ramp and reverse), `spectrum` (Hann-windowed radix-2 FFT scaled so a full-scale sine reads 1). Generator **Audio Visualiser** (`audio_viz`): bars, mirrored bars, a waveform line or bars round a circle, from one audio track or all of them, with bar count, colour, size, height position, sensitivity and the window it listens over; bars are log-spaced from 40 Hz to 16 kHz over a 60 dB range. `edit::animateToAudio` bakes keys on any number setting of the clip's Transform, generator or effects: per frame the energy of one band (all, lows 20-250 Hz, mids 250 Hz-4 kHz, highs 4-20 kHz) over a 2048-sample window, scaled so the loudest moment under the clip reaches the "loudest" value and silence the "quiet" one, then thinned to within 1% of the range; refused below -60 dBFS, for unknown settings and tracks. Clip › Animate to Audio… (setting, track, band, quiet and loudest values) on the selected picture clips in one undo step; MCP `montage_animate_to_audio`. **Tests:** media: a 1 kHz sine peaks at its FFT bin near 1; the track sound before a clip silent, 3 s in loud; visualiser bars around 1 kHz three times taller than bass or treble, fewer lit pixels in the quiet part, the waveform and circle styles drawing; a colour clip's scale baked from a quiet-then-loud tone at about 102 then 120 in under 20 keys, the lows band left near 100, a muted track, an unknown setting and a missing track refused; over MCP opacity from 20 to 100 on the mids. App: the menu action, refusal with no sound under the clip, the scale following speech between 100 and 120 and one undo removing it.
- [x] Source audio channel mapping (Premiere's Modify > Audio Channels, Resolve's Clip Attributes > Audio, Final Cut's audio configuration). Model: `Clip::channels` (the source channels a clip plays, counted across every audio stream of the file; empty = the main stream mixed to stereo as before), `MediaItem::audioStreams` (channels per audio stream, from the probe: MXF and camera files often carry a mono stream per channel) and `MediaItem::audioChannelMode` (how new clips take the channels: the mix, a mono clip per channel, or a stereo clip per pair). media/Decoder `decodeAudio(..., channels)`: each audio stream the chosen channels come from decoded with its own channels, then one channel played in the centre, two as left and right, more summed alternately left and right. media/MediaPool `audioKey` names a file's chosen channels so the pool, waveform peaks, cleaned-audio and pitch-kept caches keep them apart; the mixer, waveforms, Animate to Audio, normalise, sync by sound, auto duck, auto mix, speaker switching, voice match and AAF export (a source per channel set, mono when one) all play what the clip chose; EditorState decodes chosen channels in the background. core/AudioChannels: `sourceChannelCount`, `sourceChannelNames` (a field recorder's track names, else Channel n), `channelsLabel`, `edit::setClipChannels` (a picture clip passes them to its sound; refused for channels the file lacks or chosen twice; a clip named for its channel by a split follows), `edit::splitAudioChannels` (a clip per channel or pair on the tracks below with room, new ones when none, linked with the picture and named for their channels); placing media set to mono or pairs splits at once. Clip › Audio Channels (Choose Channels… with the channels named, Split into Mono Clips, Split into Stereo Pairs, Play Stereo Mix), the media bin's Audio Channels menu, a channel badge on timeline clips, saved in projects; MCP `montage_audio_channels`. **Tests:** media: a four-channel recorder WAV with bext track names (Boom, Lav 1) probed as four named channels; channel 2 alone decoded at full level on both sides with nothing of the others, 3+4 as left and right, a fifth channel refused; audio keys round trip; a clip playing the lav mixed as 300 Hz alone; saved and loaded; split into four linked clips on A1-A4 named for their channels and together playing all four tones; a split clip renamed with its new channel; media set to pairs placed as 1+2 and 3+4; an MKV master with three stereo streams probed as six channels across streams, channel 5 the music track alone and 3+6 voice left and music right; over MCP list, set, a missing channel refused, split pairs and a media's mode. App: the menu actions, channel 2 chosen in one undo step and decoded with its peaks in the background, a missing channel refused, Play Stereo Mix and undo, Split into Mono Clips onto four tracks and undo, the media bin's mode placing a clip per channel.
- [x] 360° video (GoPro's Reframe, Insta360 Studio's keyframed reframing, Premiere's VR Projection and VR sequences). `MediaItem::projection` ("equirect") from the file's spherical metadata (MP4 sv3d, Matroska Projection) or the media bin's 360° Footage toggle; `Sequence::spherical` (Sequence Settings › Projection). render/Spherical `reframeEquirect`: a view out of the sphere aimed by yaw, pitch and roll with a field of view, as a flat rectilinear camera (up to 170°), a little planet (looking down, stereographic, up to 330°) or a tunnel (looking up), bilinear with longitude wrapping, rows in parallel. Effect **Reframe 360°** (`reframe_360`, every setting keyframeable): the compositor decodes as much of the sphere as the view's pixels need (360 / fov times the view's width, up to the footage's own) and gives the clip the sequence's shape, so a 2:1 panorama fills a 16:9 or 9:16 frame as a view; works on 360° photos too. 360° footage placed in a flat sequence gets the effect at once; in a 360° sequence it comes in whole, and exports of a 360° sequence carry equirectangular spherical metadata (MP4/MOV sv3d and st3d, Matroska Projection) so players and YouTube show them as 360°. `edit::setReframe360` aims the view, as smooth keys at a frame or as constant values. Look Around: with a Reframe 360° clip selected under the playhead, dragging the Program picture pans and tilts the view (grabbing the scene), one undo step a drag, keyed at the playhead when the view is already keyed. MCP `montage_reframe_360` (aim with keys, mark media 360°, make a sequence 360°). **Tests:** media: on a sphere coded with longitude in red and latitude in green, a 90° view's centre at (0, 0), its right edge 45° right and top 29.4° up; turned 90° and tilted 30° the centre follows; rolled 90° the right edge looks 45° down; a little planet's centre is the ground and its corners the sky, a tunnel's centre the sky; a 360° photo placed in a 16:9 sequence gets Reframe 360° and fills the frame looking ahead, a key-framed move reaches 120° right and 10° up at its second key and about 60° halfway, a key past the end refused; projection saved; a 360° sequence takes the photo whole and its MP4 and MKV probe back as equirectangular, a flat export does not; over MCP marking media, two keys, a key outside the clip and an unknown projection refused, a little planet. App: the media bin toggle and its undo, the effect on placement, the Projection checkbox, Look Around dragging a quarter of the picture turning the view 25° left in one undo step and switching off without a selection.
- [x] Default transitions on every selected edit (Premiere's Apply Default Transitions to Selection, Final Cut's Add Transition on several clips). core/EditOps `addTransitionsToClips`: both ends of every clip given, each edit point once (where two clips meet, one transition across the cut; where a clip meets a gap, a fade), the picture transition on video clips and the crossfade on sound, clips on locked tracks left, a second pass replacing rather than doubling. Clip › Apply Default Transitions to Selection (Shift+D, both kinds); Ctrl+D and Ctrl+Shift+D with several clips of their kind selected do every edit of those clips; MCP `montage_add_transition` takes `clips` (with `audio_type`). **Tests:** core: A|B|C butted, D after a gap and a sound clip, with A, B, D and the sound chosen: five picture transitions (A's head fade, A|B once, B|C, D's head and tail fades) and the sound's two crossfades; a second pass keeps five; a locked track and an empty choice refused. Media: over MCP three wipes on two butted clips, saved. App: Shift+D on Red and Blue gives three transitions in one undo step, the same from Ctrl+D's path, and nothing selected changes nothing.
- [x] Filler words in many languages (Descript's filler word detection, Premiere's filler words with custom words). core/TranscriptEdit `FillerOptions` (the speech's language, discourse fillers, the editor's own words), `isFillerWord(word, language)` with hesitations for English, Spanish, French, German, Italian, Portuguese, Dutch, Swedish, Danish, Norwegian, Polish, Czech, Russian, Turkish, Japanese, Chinese and Korean (with no language known, only sounds that are no word in any of them, so German "er" survives), `fillerWordMask` (discourse fillers such as "like", "you know", "I mean", "genre", "halt", "cioè", "типа" counted only where set off by commas or pauses; the editor's own words and phrases always), `fillerWordRanges` cutting a phrase or a run of fillers as one, `sequenceTranscriptLanguage`. `Project::fillerWords` saved with the project. Transcript panel: Remove Fillers follows the transcripts' language, with a menu to include discourse fillers (remembered) and edit Custom Filler Words; highlights follow the same rules.
