MCP `montage_cut_speech` gains `discourse_fillers` and `filler_words`. **Tests:** core: French "euh", German "ähm", English "er" but not German "er" nor unknown-language "er", Italian, Polish, Russian, Japanese, Chinese and Korean hesitations, Spanish "este" and Russian "ну" not hesitations, a region code accepted; "was, like, huge" and ", you know." and "like" between pauses marked, "I like it" not; a custom "okay so" marked unset-off; a phrase cut as one range to the next word; French "genre" set off; the project's list saved. App: the panel taking the transcript's language, the discourse option remembered, the project's words marking "Ask not" with "um", undone. Media: over MCP a custom filler set, used and kept.
- [x] Lens Flare (Premiere's and Resolve's Lens Flare). render/StyleFx `lensFlare`, effect `lens_flare` (Stylize): light from a keyframeable point that may sit off the frame, as a 50-300 mm zoom (four soft rays, a rainbow ring and six ghosts), a 35 mm prime (six rays), a 105 mm prime (eight rays) or an anamorphic lens (a long blue horizontal streak and oval ghosts); a hot core and halo, ghosts strung along the line from the light through the centre, brightness up to 300 %, a colour and the ghosts' strength; added to the picture, showing on transparent areas too. **Tests:** render: on black, the core above 1.0 at the light, far corners dark, a zoom ghost at 1.55 of the way to past the centre and gone with Ghosts at 0, an anamorphic streak blue far to the side but not above, nothing at no brightness.
- [x] On-screen transform in the Program monitor (Premiere's direct manipulation, Resolve's transform overlay, Final Cut's Transform tool). app/TransformOverlay: the selected picture clip on screen at the playhead shows its box (cropped, turned and scaled as drawn) with corner and edge handles and its anchor. Drag inside to move it (Shift: one axis), a corner to scale it about the anchor, an edge to stretch it along its own axis (Scale Width / Height), just outside a corner to turn it (Shift: 15° steps). Moving snaps the box's centre and edges to the frame's centre lines and edges within 8 screen pixels, with magenta guides (Ctrl/Cmd: no snapping). With nothing chosen, a click on the picture selects the topmost clip there and moves it. Animated settings get a key at the playhead; one undo step a drag; the pointer shows what a press would do. Mask and Corner Pin handles come first, 360° views keep Look Around, adjustment layers have no box. The viewer now takes several overlays, and the overlays' geometry knows Reframe 360°'s shape. **Tests:** app: a full-frame matte's box on the picture's corners with its anchor at the centre; a quarter-width drag moves it 80 px in one undo step; a 3-pixel nudge snaps back to the centre and with Ctrl stays; a corner pulled half as far again from the anchor scales to 150 %; the right edge 1.2 times out stretches to 120 % wide only; a quarter turn from outside a corner turns 90°, with Shift 17° becomes 15°; keyed Position gains a key at the playhead; with nothing selected a click selects the title on top and moves it.
- [x] Transform over MCP and Align in Frame (CapCut's and Descript's canvas alignment, Premiere's Align). render/ClipPlacement: `clipFrameQuad` (a clip's cropped picture as drawn, four corners in sequence pixels), `parseAlign`, `edit::alignClip` (the picture's bounds lined up with the frame's centre, an edge or a corner, a share of the frame's height in from the edges, keyed when Position is animated). Clip › Align in Frame (Centre, Top, Bottom, Left, Right and the four corners) on the selected picture clips at the playhead, inside the action-safe margin, one undo step. MCP `montage_transform`: position (pixels from centre), scale, width and height scale, rotation, opacity, crops and fit, as constant values or smooth keys at a frame, then `align` with `inset`; returns the picture's corners. **Tests:** media: over MCP a half-size matte at (80, 45)-(240, 135); aligned bottom right 9 px in at (151, 81)-(311, 171), rendered red inside those corners and clear outside; cropping half its left side moves its left edge to 231; keys at 0 and 40 reach 100 px; a key outside the clip, nothing to change and an unknown alignment refused. App: a half-size clip aligned top left from the Clip menu lands 9 px in, and undoes.
- [x] Extend Edit (Premiere's and Resolve's E, Final Cut's Extend Edit, Avid's Extend). core/EditOps `extendEdit`: the clip's edge nearest the target (the one on that side when the target is beyond it) moved there, rolled with the clip meeting it or trimmed into a gap or back, linked clips following, refused when the media ends before there, when nothing would be left or on a locked track. Sequence › Extend Edit (E; Shift+X in the Final Cut layout, none in Avid's, where E marks In): in Trim mode the selected edit on its side to the playhead; else each selected clip's nearest edge; else the edit nearest the playhead on the target video track; one undo step. MCP `montage_trim_clip` takes `extend_to`. **Tests:** core: A|B with C after a gap: A's end extended past it rolls the cut (B's in-point follows), B's end trims into the gap, C's head comes back only as far as its media (30 frames, applied -30) and no further, a playhead inside B near its head rolls the cut back, already there and a locked track refused. App: E on Blue rolls its head to the playhead and undoes, rolling back into no media refused, with nothing selected the nearest edit, in Trim mode the selected edit. Media: over MCP the cut rolled to 2 s.
- [x] Numbered image sequences (Premiere's Image Sequence import, Resolve's and Avid's frame sequences; VFX plates and renders back from compositing, animation, time-lapse). media/ImageSequence: `detectImageSequence` (the unbroken run a frame belongs to: same folder, the same name either side of the number, padded to a width or not; a gap ends it), `imageSequenceName` ("shot_[1001-1048].exr"), `isFrameFormat`, `rateFor` (23.976 as 24000/1001), `mediaFileOnDisk`. A sequence's media path is FFmpeg's pattern with its frame rate and first and last numbers after a separator no file name holds, so every decoder, thumbnail and cache that opens media by path plays it like a movie: media/Decoder `openMediaInput` opens it through image2 at its first number and rate, and image2 patterns are no longer taken for stills; probing gives a video of frames / rate seconds. Importing two or more frames of a run in a render format (EXR, DPX, PNG, TIFF, TGA, BMP), or their folder, makes one clip at the sequence's rate (a preference turns it off); a single frame stays a still, and File › Import Image Sequence… makes the run from any frame at a chosen rate (JPEG camera rolls only this way). Interpret Frame Rate… in the media bin (`edit::setImageSequenceRate`: clips keep starting on the same frame and their length, shortened where the run no longer reaches; subclips follow). Offline while the first frame is missing; Relink takes any frame of the run, keeping the rate; collecting a project copies every frame into a folder of its own. Folder imports now report what they brought in. MCP `montage_place_media` takes `image_sequence` and `fps`. **Tests:** media: 48 PNG frames shot_1001-1048 found from frame 1010 as one unpadded run, named shot_[1001-1048].png; a run with a gap stops at it; padded frame_0098-0102 found across the hundred as %04d; probed at 24 fps as a 2 s 64 x 36 video; frame 30 decoding shot_1031; placed at 24 fps, timeline frame 10 showing shot_1011; the path saved and loaded; at 12 fps 4 s long with frame 10 showing shot_1006; collected with all 48 frames copied and the path following; relinked to a frame of the copy keeping 12 fps; over MCP placed whole from one frame at the sequence's 25 fps; offline once its first frame is gone. App: a folder of ten frames imported as one clip at the sequence's rate, one frame as a still, Import Image Sequence at 25 fps and its refusal for a lone file, Interpret Frame Rate to 5 fps and undo, grouping turned off giving ten stills.
- [x] Transcript correction (Premiere's transcript editing, Avid's corrections with the original kept, Descript's Correct; speech-to-text misspells names and jargon, and the errors reach captions, search, script cuts and B-roll matching). core/TranscriptCorrect: `correctWords` replaces words in place (typed words share the old span, split by length, probability 1; a correction cut into is widened to the whole of it) and keeps what was heard on the first word (`TranscriptWord::original`, saved with the transcript); `revertCorrection` / `revertAllCorrections` put back what was heard; `replaceInTranscripts` replaces every whole-word phrase in every transcript of the project (case and punctuation ignored, trailing punctuation kept). The project vocabulary (`Project::vocabulary`, saved) is passed to whisper as its initial prompt ("Glossary: Montage, Kokoro.") so names come out right, and `vocabularySuggestions` finds near misses already transcribed (Levenshtein on letters up to a third of the term; terms under four letters left; several-word terms match as many words), which `applyVocabularySuggestions` corrects keeping punctuation. Transcript panel: a Correct menu (Correct Selection…, Revert Correction, Replace in All Transcripts…, Vocabulary…, Fix Vocabulary Near Misses…), corrected words underlined in green. MCP `montage_edit_transcript` (correct, revert, revert_all, replace, vocabulary, suggest, fix_vocabulary); `montage_transcribe` takes `vocabulary` (the project's by default). **Tests:** core: a correction keeping timing and what was heard, a second correction into it widening to the whole and keeping the first heard words, revert and revert all, replace across two media keeping commas, near misses found ("Montaj." for Montage in both media; exact words and a two-letter term left), applied keeping the full stop and what was heard, then none left; a two-word term ("Kokoro TDS," for Kokoro TTS) matched as two words and fixed keeping its comma; the prompt; the vocabulary saved and loaded. App: the Correct button; Correct Selection on the panel's selected words, revert and undo; Replace in All Transcripts; Fix Vocabulary keeping the comma, a second run finding nothing, and undo. Media: over MCP correct, a refused revert of an uncorrected word, revert, replace, vocabulary set, one suggestion, fix_vocabulary and revert_all.
- [x] Make Shorts (CapCut's long video to shorts, Descript's clip finding, OpusClip; cutting a podcast or talk into vertical clips is the top creator chore and Montage had every piece but the pipeline). render/Shorts: `findShorts` takes sentence-aligned windows of each transcript inside a length range (sentences end at . ? ! and the CJK forms, not at "Mr.", or at a silence over 1.2 s) and scores them: twice `hookScore` of the first sentence (a question, "did you know", "here's", "the secret", "you", a number, "best", "never"... for it; starting on "and", "so", "because", "it" or a filler, or rambling, against it), the mean Highlights liveliness over the window (render/Highlights `highlightCurve`, now shared), twice the share of the topic's words it contains (stemmed, common words left out), less three times its filler density, plus clean starts and ends (a pause or a new speaker), less a dangling question, more than two speakers or mostly silence; the best that do not overlap are taken, padded by a breath that never passes halfway to the next words. `makeShortSequence` makes each a new sequence at 9:16, 1:1, 4:5 or 16:9 keeping the active sequence's shorter side, with the moment's picture and sound, fillers cut and pauses over half a second shortened (Smooth Cuts optional), every picture clip filling the frame and following its subject (Auto Reframe), an audiogram for sound alone, captions from the transcript in a caption look (Creator Pop by default; 22 characters a line and raised clear of the platforms' buttons when tall) and, if asked, the hook line as a title over the first three seconds. Sequence › Make Shorts… (how many, length range, shape, captions, topic, cut fillers, shorten pauses, hook title, follow the speaker) finds the moments into a table to tick, then Create Sequences or Create and Queue (each a render job: H.264 at -14 LUFS with captions burned in), built on a copy with progress and added in one undo step, named "<media> - Short n". MCP `montage_make_shorts` (preview, or made, or rendered to `render_folder`). **Tests:** render: hook scores; twelve sentences with a hook, a second hook and a stretch thick with fillers: three windows inside the range, sentence-aligned, padded inside the gaps, never overlapping, best first, the two hooks first and the fillers never; a topic bringing the garage talk first; nothing fitting, nothing transcribed. Media: a hook found first from real footage (read for liveliness); built at 1080 x 1920 with the footage filling the frame, the "Um," cut and a two-second pause shortened (the short about 2.7 s shorter), Creator Pop captions raised and without the filler, the hook title; sound alone getting an audiogram in a 1080 x 1080 short; an unknown look refused; over MCP a preview changing nothing, an unknown look refused, and a short made and rendered as 180 x 320 H.264 with sound and captions. App: nothing transcribed refused; two shorts from the JFK recording as audiograms with captions, made current, queued at -14 LUFS with captions burned in, undone in one step; the dialog finding two moments, one left out, a square short made without captions or a job.
- [x] Suggest Chapters (Descript's chapters, YouTube's automatic chapters; chapter markers had to be placed by hand). core/ChapterSuggest `suggestChapters`: TextTiling over the cut's transcribed words (as the cut plays them): each break between sentences scored by how much the telling words (common words left out, rough stems) of the three sentences either side share (cosine, smoothed), the depth of each dip below the highs either side, and the deepest breaks above the mean taken while every chapter stays at least `minSeconds` long (30 by default) and there are at most one per minute and a half (three at least); each starts just before its first word, the first at 0:00. Titles are the phrase (a word of four letters or more, or a recurring pair) each chapter says more than the others do (tf × log(1 + chapters / chapters with it)), never two alike. `edit::addSuggestedChapters` writes them as chapter markers, replacing the chapter markers there were (others kept). Sequence › Suggest Chapters… shows them to rename, re-space (shortest chapter) or keep the old chapter markers, in one undo step; Copy Chapters for YouTube then lists them. MCP `montage_suggest_chapters` (suggest only, or apply; returns YouTube's list). core/TextStats now holds the sentence, word-key, common-word and stem helpers Make Shorts and this share. **Tests:** core: three talks (cooking, football, astronomy) of ten sentences split within a sentence of where each begins and titled with a word of its topic ("Pasta", "Coach", "Telescope"), written as markers replacing an old chapter marker and keeping a plain one, YouTube's list valid; a one-minute shortest chapter held; at most two when asked; one subject throughout and nothing transcribed refused. Media: over MCP suggested without writing, then applied as three chapter markers with YouTube's three lines, and too long a shortest chapter refused. App: nothing transcribed refused; three chapter markers from the menu's function and undone; the dialog re-spaced, a chapter renamed and an existing chapter marker kept.
- [x] Dynamic JKL trimming (Avid's dynamic trim, Resolve's dynamic trim mode, Premiere's JKL in Trim mode: trimming by feel as it plays). In Trim mode with an edit selected, L plays its side forward through the edit and J back (again the same way for 2x, 4x, 8x; the other way for 1x that way) and the edit follows live (the timeline, the two-up and the playhead) inside one gesture; K stops and keeps the trim as one undo step, Esc puts it back and stays in Trim mode, and leaving Trim mode puts back one still running. A roll moves the cut, the outgoing side ripples its out point, the incoming side its in point; where a clip or its media ends the trim stops there (edits that stop short are measured, not trusted) and says so. Outside Trim mode J, K and L are the transport's as before. The shuttle runs on a 20 ms timer; tests drive its clock by hand (`setManualTrimShuttle`, `advanceTrimShuttle`). **Tests:** app: the keys refused outside Trim mode; a roll played 5 frames forward moving the cut and the playhead to 65, L again at 2x to 69, J back at 1x to 68, kept by K as one undo step and undone; the outgoing side played back 10 frames rippling the cut to 50; Esc putting a 3-frame trim back while staying in Trim mode with no undo step; the outgoing clip played back past its length stopping at its last frame (59 frames) and put back.
- [x] Layered PSD import (Premiere's merged, individual layers or sequence import; Final Cut's layered graphics; "better PSD support" is item 51 on Premiere's request list; lower thirds, thumbnails and graphics come from designers as PSDs). media/Psd reads Photoshop files with no new library: PSD and PSB headers; RGB, greyscale and CMYK at 8 and 16 bits; the layer records (bounds, blend key, opacity times fill, visibility, clipping, the layer mask, Pascal and Unicode `luni` names, `lsct` group records, adjustment layers noted and left out); channels raw, PackBits, ZIP and ZIP with prediction (through core/Zip's inflater); and the merged picture. A layer is media of its own, `art.psd#layer=3`: a still the canvas's size with the layer where it sits, transparent elsewhere and its mask applied, decoded as 16-bit RGBA with alpha and probed as an image. `importPsd` brings a file in merged (as before), as a still per layer in a bin "<name> Layers", or also as a sequence at the canvas size with a track per layer bottom up named for it, its blend mode mapped onto Montage's (multiply, screen, overlay, soft and hard light, dodge, burn, add, subtract, difference, darken, lighten), its opacity times its groups', hidden layers' clips disabled, groups as track folders, and clipping masks as Track Matte Key on the layer clipped to (the matte left showing). Importing PSDs asks Merged / Individual Layers / Sequence (as Premiere does); the media bin's menu on a merged PSD offers Import Layers as Sequence and as Stills; `importFiles` follows an `import/psd` preference. Offline, relink (to the same layer of the file chosen), collect (the file copied once for all its layers) and changed-file reload (the file watched, every layer reloaded) work on layers. MCP `montage_place_media` takes `psd_mode` (merged, layer with `layer` by name or index, or sequence as a nested clip). **Tests:** a PSD writer in the tests (tests/PsdWriter.h). Media: white, a red layer multiplied at 50 % at (40, 20), a hidden blue layer (ZIP), and a group holding a green logo (ZIP with prediction, its right half masked off) with a magenta stripe clipped to it: names (a Unicode one), bounds, blend keys, opacity, visibility, clipping and group membership read back; single layers decoded at their bounds and transparent off them, the mask applied; the merged picture; probing and a missing layer refused; the sequence's tracks, folder, blend mode, opacity, disabled hidden clip and Track Matte Key, rendered white where nothing is, red at half strength under the multiply, green on the logo, magenta only where the stripe crosses the logo and nowhere else, the hidden layer and masked half not shown; layers-only and merged imports; the file on disk, relinked keeping its layer, collected once; 16-bit PSB with PackBits and 16-bit ZIP with prediction read exactly; over MCP a layer by name, the layers as a nested sequence on V2, and an unknown layer refused. App: the sequence import as one undo step; `importFiles` merged by default, then as layers and as a sequence by preference; a missing file refused; a layer placed in the cut re-read when the PSD is saved again with the badge recoloured.
- [x] Embedded CEA-608 closed captions (Premiere's and Resolve's embedded 608 captions; US broadcast and streaming deliveries ask for line-21 captions inside the video). core/Captions: the SCC writer's pop-on schedule is now `captionsTo608` (byte pairs for caption channel 1 at 29.97 a second) and the SCC reader's decoder `captionsFrom608` (pop-on, paint-on, roll-up), shared with embedded captions; the schedule now sends a caption's erase while the next one loads (into the memory not shown), starting that load two frames earlier and never splitting a control code's two copies, so a caption followed closely by another comes off on time instead of staying until it is replaced (SCC files gain the same accuracy). render/Exporter `cea608`: the caption track's pairs go into each H.264 or HEVC frame as A/53 caption data (the pairs due in the frame's span of the 29.97 Hz line-21 clock, padding between, so 23.976 and 25 fps work), libx264/libx265 told to write them, smart rendering off; other codecs refused with a reason. media/Decoder `readEmbeddedCaptions` reads them back from any H.264, HEVC or MPEG-2 file (decoding the whole video for its A/53 data, with progress and cancel), and `captionsThroughClip` places them where a clip plays its media (trims, speed, reverse). Export dialog: "CEA-608 closed captions in the video" (H.264/HEVC presets); Clip › Import Embedded Captions makes the selected clip's (or the one under the playhead's) captions a caption track in one undo step. MCP `montage_export` takes `cea608`; `montage_captions` takes `import_embedded` (a clip). **Tests:** core: two close captions scheduled one pair a frame with the first's erase sent twice among the second's pairs and never between a control code's copies, decoded back with both shown and cleared within two frames, the same through an SCC file, an overlapping caption replaced rather than erased, no pairs refused. Media: H.264 at 29.97 and 25 fps and HEVC at 23.976 written with embedded captions and read back with both texts (one on two rows) shown and cleared within two frames; ProRes refused; a video without captions refused; over MCP a trimmed clip's captions placed 15 frames earlier from where it starts. App: no clip refused; a clip of a captioned file placed at frame 60 giving a two-caption track where it plays them, undone in one step.
- [x] Remove Mic Bleed (Auphonic's crossgate; "de-bleed" is Premiere's request #35; podcasts and interviews with a mic on each speaker, where every mic also hears the others and the mix sounds roomy and phasey). media/MicBleed `bleedSpans`: each chosen audio track's level 20 ms at a time as the sequence plays its clips (trims, speed, clip gain, channel choice); a mic counts as speaking where it is above its own floor (its quiet tenth plus 6 dB, never under -50 dBFS) and within 10 dB of the loudest mic at that moment (so two people talking at once both stay up), held open a quarter second after; the quiet stretches of 0.4 s or more where it has a clip are its dips. `removeMicBleed` writes them as volume keyframes through the auto-duck writer (`duckClip`), the fades placed inside each stretch: down over 0.15 s after its speaker stops, up over 0.03 s just before they start. Clip › Remove Mic Bleed… (the mics to use, how far to dip; every unmuted audio track with clips by default), one undo step; MCP `montage_remove_bleed`. **Tests:** media: two synthetic mics each hearing the other 18 dB down, with turns, an overlap and silences: each mic open while its speaker talks and while both do, down 24 dB while the other speaks alone and in the silences, open again by the time its speaker starts and still open just after they stop; a deeper dip when asked; one track refused; over MCP both clips keyed. App: nothing on the tracks refused; a host and guest recording dipped in turn from the menu's function and undone.
- [x] Remove Letterbox (Descript's automatic crop; done by hand with crop and scale in Premiere and Resolve): footage with black bars baked in (a 2.39:1 film in a 16:9 file, 4:3 in 16:9, a phone recording framed in landscape). render/Letterbox `detectBars` looks at nine frames across the part a clip uses (320 pixels wide), takes the smallest box of picture any of them shows (frames black all over, a fade, are passed over), and counts bars of 1 % or more, leaving at least a third of the picture; `edit::removeLetterbox` crops them off and scales and centres what is left to fill the frame (from however it was fitted). Clip › Remove Letterbox on the selected video clips, one undo step; MCP `montage_transform` takes `remove_letterbox` (and returns the bars found). **Tests:** media: footage with 30 px bars top and bottom (after half a second of black) found as a sixth of the height each and none at the sides; 40 px pillarbox bars found as an eighth of the width each; the letterboxed clip cropped, scaled to 150 % and rendered with picture reaching the top and bottom rows; nothing to do refused; footage without bars finding none; over MCP the pillarbox cropped. App: nothing selected refused; a letterboxed clip cropped and scaled from the menu's function and undone.
- [x] Watch folders (Premiere's watch folder import, request #49; for cards being offloaded and renders landing): `Project::watchFolders` (saved). EditorState watches each folder and its subfolders and, a moment after anything changes, imports the media files (video, sound, stills, EXR/DPX, PSD, camera RAW; never text or hidden files) that have settled (not written to for a second) into a bin "Watch Folder - <name>", through the usual import (so frames of a run become one image sequence); files already in the project (including frames of its image sequences and Photoshop layers' files) are left, and a file that fails to import is not tried again. Adding a folder brings in what is there. File › Watch Folders… lists them with Add Folder… and Stop Watching; each change is an undo step and so is each import. **Tests:** app: a folder with a picture and a text file: the picture imported once settled into the folder's bin and the text file never; adding the folder twice refused; a picture arriving later in a subfolder imported; the folder kept with the project across save and open with nothing new to import; after Stop Watching a new picture is left alone.
- [x] Title text animations (CapCut's and Descript's animated text, Premiere's per-character text animation, Final Cut's Build In/Out): any title can bring its text on a letter, word or line at a time — Rise, Fade, Pop (overshooting), Drop (bounce), Wave (keeps rippling) or Scramble (shuffling letters that settle) — staggered across a chosen time so the last unit lands then, and optionally leave the same way at the end. Shadow, outline and fill follow each piece; once every piece is in place the title draws exactly as the still title (kerning across the pieces kept). New templates Cascade, Pop Words, Drop In, Wave and Decode; MCP `montage_add_title` takes `text_animation`, `animate_by`, `animation_seconds` and `animate_out`. **Tests:** render: nothing showing at the start of a rise; half way the first letters in and the last well short; every kind by letter, word and line ending identical to the still title; animating out identical at mid-clip and empty at the end; pop, drop and scramble differing part way (scramble still showing letters); the wave moving; word by word the first word ahead of the second; the five templates rendering. Media (MCP): a word-by-word pop with its timing and exit saved; an unknown kind or unit refused.
- [x] HDR light levels and nit scopes (Resolve 21's HDR scope ranges and HDR10 MaxCLL/MaxFALL; Netflix, Apple and YouTube check the HDR10 light levels): `render/LightLevel` measures CTA-861.3's MaxCLL (the brightest channel of any pixel) and MaxFALL (the highest frame-average of the pixels' brightest channels) in nits, for PQ, HLG (through its OOTF on a 1000-nit display) and SDR, with where each happens. Exports to PQ measure what they render and MP4/MOV files carry the measured levels (their light-level box is written with the index at the end); encoders that state them before the first frame (x265's SEI, Matroska's header) use the sequence's analysed levels (`Sequence::hdrMaxCll/hdrMaxFall`, saved), else the mastering peak as before. Sequence › Analyse HDR Light Levels… measures In to Out (or all), saves them as one undo step, warns when MaxCLL is above the mastering peak and offers the brightest frame. The scopes now measure HDR and wide-gamut sequences as delivered (the Program monitor renders them in their own space, keeps a small 16-bit copy for the scopes, then shows the Rec.709 preview), with the waveform, parade and histogram scaled and labelled in nits (0, 10, 100, 203, 1K, 4K, 10K; reference white and the mastering peak marked), a waveform top of 10 000, 4 000, 2 000 or 1 000 nits for PQ, luma weighted by the space's primaries, and the frame's peak and average light read out (red above the mastering peak). `ColorSpace` gains `codeToNits`, `nitsToCode` and `pixelMaxNits`. MCP `montage_measure_hdr`; `montage_render` reports the measured levels. **Tests:** render: PQ, HLG and SDR code values to nits and back; a saturated HLG red dimmer than its neutral; a frame a quarter at 1000 nits and the rest at 100 measuring 1000 and 325 in PQ and in HLG; a sequence of mid grey then a white title on black giving MaxCLL 203 at the title and MaxFALL at the grey; part of a sequence; HDR10 rounding; an empty sequence refused. Media: an HDR10 MP4 carrying the measured 203/203 (not the 1000-nit guess), Matroska stating the mastering-peak guess or the analysed levels; over MCP the levels measured and saved, a part measured without saving, graphics held to a 100-nit peak, a 1000-nit PQ highlight over a 400-nit peak warned about, `montage_render` reporting 203, an SDR sequence refused. App: the scopes reading 1000/325 nits from a PQ frame with the range shown, the waveform cropped to 1000 nits, a warning above a 400-nit peak, HLG without a range, SDR back to code values; the Program monitor giving the scopes a 16-bit PQ frame with graphics white at 0.5807; Analyse HDR Light Levels saving 203/203 and undone; refused for SDR.
- [x] Spell check (Premiere's caption and transcript spell check with Learn and Forget, Resolve 21's in titles and subtitles): `core/SpellCheck` with English (US and UK) from SCOWL's size-60 word lists built in as a Qt resource (`scripts/gen-spell-words.py`; SCOWL's permissive notice kept in each list; possessives dropped where the stem is listed; UK with both -ise and -ize). Words are right in their dictionary case, capitalised or in capitals; possessives by their stem; hyphenated words part by part; words with digits, addresses and (by default) all-capitals acronyms left alone; positions in UTF-16 units for Qt editors. Suggestions are one edit away (weighted: swapped and doubled letters cheapest, commoner words first), then two, plus run-together words split, in the word's own case. The project's vocabulary is always right; `learnWord`/`forgetWord` keep it. UI: red wavy underlines as you type in the Inspector's title text (right-click: suggestions, Add to Dictionary), in caption text in the Captions panel (a ⚠ with the words in its tooltip, the count under the list, and right-click submenus per word with suggestions and Add to Dictionary, each one undo step) and in the Transcript panel; Edit › Spelling turns it off and picks US or UK English for titles (captions use their track's language). Quality Check gains a Spelling check (captions in each track's language, titles in the chosen dictionary). MCP `montage_spell_check` (captions, titles or a text; learn and forget) and `montage_quality_check` `spelling`/`title_language`. **Tests:** core: dictionaries by language tag, loaded once; case rules, possessives, typographic apostrophes; US and UK spellings; suggestions (teh→the, Teh→The, recieve→receive, accomodate→accommodate, paris→Paris, RECIEVE→RECEIVE, alot→"a lot", UK colur→colour); a text with positions, acronyms, numbers, a URL, an address and a hyphenated word; vocabulary terms word by word in any case; learn and forget. Render: QC spelling of two captions and a title (French skipped), with suggestions, the vocabulary allowed, a range, off, and UK titles. Media: MCP over captions and titles, scopes, learn saved to the project, forget, a UK text, a language with no dictionary and a bad scope refused. App: the Captions panel flagging a caption, correcting it from its menu and undoing, Add to Dictionary and undo, turning checking off; a title field underlining and correcting from its menu; Edit › Spelling's UK dictionary accepting "Colour"; Quality Check's Spelling box.
- [x] Colour groups (Resolve's groups with pre- and post-clip grades): `ColorGroup{id, postId, name, pre, post}` in `Sequence::colorGroups` and `Clip::colorGroup`, saved (ids counted on load). `core/ColorGroups`: `gradeChain` (group pre, clip's own, group post), members, and edit ops to make a group (named "Group N" by default; clips leave any other group), join, leave, rename and delete (members keep their own grades). The group's two chains are effect owners for `edit::effectChain`, so the Inspector shows and edits them with the usual effect stack and Add Effect menu under "Group Pre-Clip" and "Group Post-Clip" for any member (rebuilt when the group or its effects change). The compositor runs the whole chain (depth, people and face analysis included when a group effect needs them); render cache keys include the group's effects; smart render skips grouped clips with grades. Clip › Colour Group: New Group from Selection…, Add Selection to Group, Remove Selection from Group, Rename, Delete, Select Group's Clips; each one undo step. MCP `montage_color_group` (list, create, add, remove, rename, delete; groups by id or name) and `montage_add_effect` `group_stage` pre/post. **Tests:** core: making, default names, effect chains as owners, chain order, moving clips between groups, join/leave refusals, rename, project round trip with ids counted, delete keeping clips' grades. Render: two grouped shots rendering exactly as with the chain written onto each clip, order mattering, the post grade warming, the cache key following a group change, ungrouped unchanged. Media: MCP create, pre and post grades, refusals, join by name, the clip's own grade untouched with a chain of two, leave, rename, delete. App: nothing selected refused, a group from the selection, the Inspector's group sections for a member, a post effect reaching another member, join and leave from the window's commands, no sections outside the group, undo step by step.
- [x] Find What's Said by meaning (Premiere 26's media intelligence search, Descript's search by topic): `core/SpokenSearch` cuts each transcript into passages starting at every sentence (the sentence alone when it has four words or more, and a window running on through whole sentences to 30 words; at most 80), scores how many of a query's content words (stemmed) a passage says, and keeps the best hits (overlapping hits of one media item thinned). `media/SpeechSearch` embeds passages and queries with multi-qa-MiniLM-L6-cos-v1 (sentence-transformers, Apache-2.0, trained for questions and the passages that answer them; Xenova's 4-bit ONNX export, 55 MB, a model pack downloaded on first use and by `scripts/fetch-models.sh`, `MONTAGE_SENTENCE_MODEL`) through its own BERT uncased WordPiece tokenizer (accents stripped, punctuation and CJK split, greedy longest pieces), mean-pooled over each text's tokens in length-sorted batches; passage embeddings are kept for the session. Scores are cosine similarity plus a tenth for shared words. The Find Shots panel gains a What's Shown / What's Said switch: said results show the media, its range and the words, open in the Source monitor and make subclips like shots, after a progress dialog the first time. MCP `montage_search_speech` (query, max, media by name or id); `montage-cli models` lists the pack. The all-MiniLM-L6-v2 model was tried first and dropped: its question-to-statement similarities were near zero (0.03 for "where do they talk about money" against "the budget was far too tight"), at 4 bits and in float alike. **Tests:** core: passages (single sentences of four words or more, windows from each sentence start, the cap, times from the words), shared words, best-hit thinning, limit and floor. Media: the tokenizer matching the model's own (Hugging Face tokenizers) on accents, contractions, an em dash and a question; the embedding matching the reference implementation's values and of unit length; five questions finding their passages across two interviews with no words in common; times, one media item alone, a strict floor, an empty query and nothing transcribed refused; MCP best-first hits, a media filter and an unknown media refused. App: What's Said reporting the transcribed recordings, finding a passage by meaning with its words in the list, opening it at its start, and switching back clearing the results.
- [x] Reading text in the picture (OCR): `media/TextReader` runs PP-OCR (PaddlePaddle, Apache-2.0) on ONNX Runtime from a model pack (`ocr`, 21 MB: the PP-OCRv4 mobile detector from RapidOCR's mirror, and the PP-OCRv5 English and Latin-script recognisers with their character lists; `MONTAGE_OCR_MODEL`). Detection scales the picture's long side to at most 960 (sides divisible by 32), normalises blue-green-red as PaddleOCR does, takes 4-connected runs of text probability over 0.3 with a mean over 0.6 and grows each box by area × 1.6 / perimeter (DB's unclip, for horizontal text); each box is read 48 pixels high in -1..1 with CTC's best path (blank first, a space last), pieces at the same height joined into lines, top to bottom; a region limits it (subtitle bands). `core/OnScreenText` turns timed readings into captions (alike readings, by edit distance on letters, are one caption shown as the most frequent reading; line breaks kept; flashes dropped). `readBurnedInSubtitles` samples four times a second at up to 1280 wide and skips reading when the band has not changed; `readSlateFromPicture` reads the first eight seconds twice a second and parses the board with `slateFromText` (core/Slate.h, the spoken-slate parser on written words). Clip › Read Burned-In Subtitles… (bottom, top or whole picture; English or another Latin-script language) makes a caption track through the clip (trims and speed followed), one undo step; the media bin's Log Slate from Picture logs scene, shot and take. MCP `montage_read_text` (frame lines with boxes and confidence, from the sequence or a media item; subtitles; slates). A Python prototype with onnxruntime set the pre- and post-processing before the C++. **Tests:** core: reading similarity, captions from readings (a misread sample joined, gaps, two-row captions, a flash dropped, the last caption's end), slates from written boards. Media: a frame's slate line and subtitle read with their places and confidence, a region alone, French accents with the Latin recogniser; a rendered video's three burned-in subtitles read with their timing, part of it, a missing file refused; a slate card read as scene 7, shot B, take 2 and subtitles not taken for a slate; MCP frame text (sequence and media), subtitles into a caption track, slates logged and missed, a bad region refused. App: Read Burned-In Subtitles with nothing selected refused, two subtitles of a placed clip read into a track and undone, Log Slate from Picture logging only the slate video, undone.
- [x] Export Versions (Premiere's Auto Reframe sequences sent to Media Encoder, CapCut's and Descript's multi-format export; delivering one cut to YouTube, Shorts/Reels/TikTok and feeds meant reframing and queueing each shape by hand). `render/Versions`: `parseVersionShape` reads "16:9", "9x16" or a frame size ("1920x1080" → 16x9); `makeVersionSequences` finds each shot's subject once (`analyzeSequenceReframe`) for every shape that differs from the cut, makes a reframed copy named "<name> <w>x<h>" (an existing one replaced with its sequence media, so making them again does not pile up copies), caps tall versions' caption position at 0.75 to clear the platforms' buttons, and returns the cut itself for its own shape; `versionSettings` names each file after its version in one folder (unsafe characters replaced), renders at the version's size, burns in captions when a visible caption track has any and sets the loudness target. File › Export Versions… (16:9, 9:16, 4:5, 1:1 ticked as wanted; folder; burn in captions; -14 / -16 LUFS or as mixed; H.264, HEVC or ProRes 422 HQ) reframes on a copy with progress, adds the copies in one undo step, queues a render job each and shows the queue. MCP `montage_export_versions` (shapes, folder, preset, captions, loudness) renders them and returns the files with their sizes. **Tests:** media: shape parsing (ratios, frame sizes, nonsense); a 640 x 360 cut with a moving ball and a caption track: 16:9 is the cut, 9:16 is 360 x 640 following the ball with captions raised to 0.75, 1:1 is 360 x 360 with captions where they were, the cut untouched; made again without new sequences or media; an empty sequence refused; settings paths, size, captions and loudness; over MCP two files rendered at 640 x 360 and 360 x 640 into the folder, the version saved in the project, a nonsense shape refused. App: three versions from the demo (the cut itself first, two copies, tall captions raised), three jobs with the right paths, sizes, captions and -14 LUFS; one undo step; made twice without piling up; the dialog queuing a 4:5 version into another folder.
- [x] Review without a service (Frame.io's frame-accurate notes in Premiere, Blackmagic Cloud review in Resolve; Montage could export marker lists but had nothing to send reviewers). `core/ReviewPage`: `reviewPageInfo` (title, a unique id, the copy's file, rate and drop frame, the exported range's offset and length, the markers inside it) and `reviewPageHtml`, one self-contained page (no scripts, styles or fonts from elsewhere; the settings as JSON in the page with `<` escaped so a message cannot close the script; split into literals under MSVC's 16 KB limit). The page plays the copy with frame-accurate timecode (requestVideoFrameCallback's media time where browsers have it, seeks to the middle of a frame), frame and second steps, J/K/L, a scrub strip with the notes and the editor's markers, I/O ranges, notes typed under a remembered name (typing pauses), kept in the browser per review, resolved or deleted, saved as a JSON notes file and merged from other reviewers' files; user text is only ever set as text. `parseReviewNotes` reads a notes file (frames, or timecodes, converted to the sequence's rate; empty notes dropped; sorted), `reviewNotesToMarkers` names each marker after its reviewer (a tick once resolved) in that reviewer's colour, and `parseMarkerList` takes notes files, so Import Markers and MCP `montage_import_markers` read them. `render/ReviewExport`: `reviewPackage` ("<name> - Review.mp4" and ".html": H.264 CRF 21 with AAC, Rec.709, at most 1080 or 720 lines, timecode, watermark text and captions burned in, a range) and `writeReviewPage`. File › Export for Review… (folder, size, timecode, watermark, the editor's markers, In to Out, a message) writes the page and queues the copy; File › Import Review Notes…. MCP `montage_export_review`. Checked in headless Chromium: frame steps, play and pause, scrubbing and marker clicks agree with the video's own time; notes added, kept over a reload, saved and their file read; no horizontal scroll at phone width. **Tests:** core: page info for the whole cut and a range (markers inside it, drop frame at 29.97), the title escaped, nothing from other sites, the settings JSON parsed back with a hostile message intact; a notes file sorted, trimmed, a timecode-only note, an empty one dropped, an unnamed reviewer, resolved ones ticked, colours per reviewer, 50 fps notes on a 25 fps cut, not-notes and no-notes refused, marker import replacing its list. Media: settings (size, names, burn-ins, full size, no timecode); over MCP a 320 x 180, 2 s copy with its page (copy name, length, message, markers), a range of 25 frames, a backwards range refused, a notes file imported as named markers. App: the page written and the copy queued with its burn-ins, notes imported as two markers and undone in one step, the dialog's 720p copy of In to Out without timecode.
- [x] Broadcast MXF delivery (Premiere's, Resolve's and Avid's MXF OP1a exports; broadcasters' ingest takes MXF, which Montage could not write at all). Three presets: XDCAM HD422 (MPEG-2 4:2:2 at a constant 50 Mb/s with a 17.8 Mb VBV buffer, a long GOP of 12 or 15 with two B-frames, 10-bit DC precision, intra VLC and the non-linear quantiser held at qmax 28, which FFmpeg requires for it; 1920 x 1080; eight mono 24-bit 48 kHz tracks), AVC-Intra 100 (x264's avcintra-class 100, 10-bit 4:2:2 intra; four tracks, as the DPP's AS-11 asks) and DNxHR HQ in MXF (any size; a mono track a channel, Avid's layout). `ExportSettings::monoAudioTracks` writes each channel as its own mono track: the mix's channels, then each extra audio stream's (so an M&E or role stems land on tracks 3 and 4 and on), then silence up to the count, each track titled ("Mix L", "Dialogue R", "Silence"); `startTimecode` sets the file's timecode (MXF and MOV), from the export dialog's new Start timecode field or MCP `montage_render` `start_timecode` (with `mono_tracks`). Exports now stop at the end of the cut when asked for a range past it (a request for 50 s of an 8 s cut had rendered 42 s of black). **Tests:** media: XDCAM HD422 with role stems from 10:00:00:00 (mpeg2video 1920 x 1080 4:2:2, eight mono 24-bit tracks, the mix on 1 and 2, dialogue on 3, music on 6, silence on 7 and 8, two seconds on each, the timecode, a range past the end cut to the sequence); AVC-Intra 100 (High 4:2:2 Intra, 10-bit, the mix padded to four tracks); DNxHR at the sequence's 320 x 180; over MCP a start timecode and four tracks, and a bad timecode refused. App: the Start timecode field off for MP4 and on for MXF, and the queued XDCAM job carrying eight tracks, 1920 wide and 10:00:00:00.
- [x] Delete Gaps (Resolve's Edit › Delete Gaps; Montage closed one gap at a time from the timeline's context menu). `edit::deleteGaps` merges what every track covers and closes each stretch empty on all of them, last first, so a picture gap over continuing sound (a J-cut) is left and nothing goes out of sync; clips after it move up with the captions (one reaching into the gap now ends at it), sequence markers (one in the gap lands at its start), In/Out and track automation and track-effect keys (keys inside the gap go); the empty start goes only when asked; a gap is left when a locked track has clips after it. Edit › Delete Gaps, one undo step with how many gaps and how long; MCP `montage_delete_gaps`. **Tests:** core: a J-cut's picture gap kept and the empty stretch closed with captions, markers, automation and In/Out following; the empty start only when asked; nothing left refused; a locked track holding a gap. Media: MCP closing a two-second gap and refusing when none is left. App: the menu command closing a gap, undone, and counting none the second time.
- [x] Motion paths in the Program monitor (After Effects' and Premiere's; the on-screen transform showed only the box at the playhead). `TransformOverlay::motionPath` samples the clip's position a frame at a time (at most 400 points on long clips) and lists the position keyframes (pos_x and pos_y merged); the overlay draws the path dotted with a dot a frame, so bunched dots show slow movement, and a square at each keyframe, filled at the playhead. Dragging a square moves that keyframe (both axes set at its time), one undo step; a keyframe under the anchor leaves the press to Move, which keys the playhead as before. **Tests:** app: no path for a still position; a left-to-right move giving a dot a frame with even steps and two keyframes where the positions are; the last keyframe dragged up 30 pixels moving only it (no key added at the playhead), undone; the existing transform test still keying the playhead with a keyframe under the anchor.
- [x] Room tone (iZotope RX's Ambience Match; a dialogue gap left by a cut drops the room to silence, which every dialogue editor fills by hand). `render/RoomTone`: `learnRoomTone` frames the sound (2048 samples, half overlap), leaves out digital silence and takes every frame within 6 dB of the quietest tenth (all the background between words, none of the speech; taking only the very quietest read the level 3 dB low), then averages their mid and side power spectra (Hann-windowed) and level; `synthesizeRoomTone` overlap-adds random-phase frames of those spectra (hop a quarter frame, Hann synthesis window), mid and side apart so the width is the room's, levels it to the room's and fades 10 ms in and out, repeatable by seed; `clipRoomTone` learns from the part of a clip's media it uses; `gapAt` finds a track's gap and the clips either side; `writeStereoWav` (24-bit). Fill Gap with Room Tone on the timeline's gap menu learns from the clip before the gap (else after), writes "<sequence> Room Tone n.wav" in a Room Tone folder beside the project, imports it into a Room Tone bin and places it in one undo step; MCP `montage_fill_room_tone` (track, a gap or a range, a source clip; reports the room's level). **Tests:** render: a -40 dBFS low-passed room under a 440 Hz voice learned within 2 dB, two seconds made at that level with far more low than high energy and no voice, the same in both ears for a centred room and uncorrelated for a wide one, repeatable by seed, faded, silence and scraps refused, the WAV's size. Media: over MCP a one-second hole between two pieces of a take filled at the take's level as played, no voice in it, a clip or a backwards range refused. App: a gap filled from the clip before (a clip there refused), the WAV in Room Tone, undone, and part of a gap from a chosen clip.
- [x] CineForm exports made the same every time. A full test run caught the mastering-codecs test over its CineForm tolerance once; the CineForm file of the same export differed from run to run (every other codec was identical) while FFmpeg's own command line encoded identically. Valgrind on FFmpeg's CLI found its CineForm encoder reading memory it allocated but never wrote, at 320 x 180 and not at 320 x 184 or 1920 x 1080: below a picture whose height is not a multiple of 8. A fresh process gets zeroed pages, which hid it; Montage reuses its heap, so whatever was there leaked into the picture (mean error 0.013 to 0.023 against 0.0055 when clean). The exporter now refuses CineForm at such heights with a message naming the fix (1080, 720 and 2160 are all fine); the test's sequence is 176 lines and checks the refusal at 180.
- [x] Extend Clip (Premiere's Generative Extend makes the frames a shot is missing with a cloud model; Montage had only Frame Hold, which stops the picture dead). `render/ExtendClip`: `measureEndMotion` runs the stabiliser's camera analysis (`analyzeCameraMotion`: corners, pyramidal Lucas–Kanade, RANSAC similarity) over the clip's last second of media and averages the last half second's motion of the picture's centre, zoom and turn, converted to sequence pixels per sequence frame through `clipFrameToSequence` (so the clip's own scale and fit count); `makeExtension` makes a clip holding the last frame shown (speed 0, as Frame Hold does) with its effects frozen at that frame and, when the picture moved, position, scale and rotation keyed a frame at a time carrying the motion on with its velocity falling as the square of the time left (smooth out of the shot, still at the end), and, for a picture that filled the frame, a growing scale so the drift never shows an edge (and a pull-out never shrinks it); `extendClip` places it in empty space or, with ripple, opens room with `insertGap` on the sync-locked tracks. Clip › Extend Clip › By 1 Second / By 2 Seconds / To the Playhead measures off the UI thread, learns the room tone of the linked sound (render/RoomTone), writes it beside the project and places it under the extension, linked to it, in one undo step. MCP `montage_extend_clip` (seconds or to, ripple, sound). **Tests:** media: a textured pan of 4 px a frame measured at -4 px; a second's extension holding the last frame, its first step the measured motion times (24/25)², about 31 px in all and at rest by the end, scaled past 105 % by then; the cut into it seamless (mean difference under 1 %) and its last frame's edge columns still picture; blocked without ripple, pushing the next clip with it; over MCP 0.6 s of picture and of room tone under the sound, linked, the WAV in Room Tone. App: the menu entries; a shot with sound extended by a second (held picture, room tone, the next clip pushed), undone in one step; refused without room or for a generated clip; extended to the playhead from the menu.
- [x] Screen keyer (Keylight in After Effects, Premiere's Ultra Key, Resolve's 3D and Delta keyers; Montage's Chroma Key measured a colour distance, which loses hair and soft edges). Effect `screen_key` (Keying): the matte is one minus how much the pixel's screen channel leads the other two (Screen Balance weighs the larger against the smaller) against how much the screen's own colour does, times Screen Gain (1.1 by default), then Clip Black (0.05) and Clip White levels, Shrink / Grow (a separable minimum or maximum filter, in pixels at full size) and Soften (three box blurs); despill takes the screen channel down to the others and puts back a share of the brightness that took (Restore Brightness); Edge Desaturate greys the partly transparent edge; View: Composite, Matte, or Status (black below 2 %, white above 98 %, grey between). `estimateScreenColor` finds green or blue (whichever leads over more of the picture, at least 2 %) and takes the median of the cleaner half of those pixels; `keyScreen` adds or updates a clip's Keyer from its picture. Clip › Key Out Green / Blue Screen (one undo step; again re-reads without a second Keyer); MCP `montage_key_screen`. **Tests:** render: a noisy green screen with a skin-toned subject, a half-and-half edge and green spill on the rim: the screen colour read within 0.04, the screen clear, the subject solid, the edge near half, the rim's spill gone with the subject's colour kept, the matte and status views, shrink, grow and soften changing the matte, clip black and gain; a blue screen found and keyed; no screen in a grey picture. Media: over MCP a blue-screen still keyed and rendered with the screen transparent and the subject solid. App: the menu keying a green-screen still with the right colour, run again without a second Keyer, undone; a grey still refused.
- [x] Interpret Footage (Premiere's Interpret Footage, Resolve's Clip Attributes, Avid's Source Settings, Final Cut's Conform Speed and anamorphic override; Montage had only Interpret Colour and an image sequence's rate). `core/Interpretation` holds the choices: the rate to play at with the file's own rate beside it, a pixel aspect ratio, alpha (straight, premultiplied, ignore, invert), field order (progressive, upper, lower) and keep-pitch. Like an image sequence's rate, the interpretation rides on the media path after a separator no file name holds (`clip.mov\x1d` `fps=24,1;file=120,1;par=2`), so everything that opens media by path reads it the same way with no call sites changed: `openMediaInput` strips it; `probeMedia` reports the conformed rate, length and start timecode and the desqueezed width; `VideoDecoder` maps media time to the file's (time scale = new rate / file rate), sizes by the given pixel aspect, forces or skips deinterlacing (`setFieldDominance`) and applies the alpha mode to the decoded frame (`interpretAlpha`); `decodeAudio` reads conformed sound as if recorded at the rate divided by the time scale (varispeed) or time-stretches it with WSOLA (pitch kept); embedded CEA-608 captions follow. The plain file is used where a path names a file: `mediaFileOnDisk`, relative-path relinking in the project file, EDL, OTIO, FCPXML and AAF names, bin tooltips, Reveal; relinking and Project Manager copies keep the interpretation; Smart Render passes over interpreted media. `edit::interpretFootage` (media/Interpret) probes the new reading and updates the media; clips keep starting on the same frame and keep their timeline length (shortened where the media no longer reaches), and subclips, clip markers, transcripts and the visual and face indexes are rescaled; the proxy is dropped; an image sequence's rate goes through `setImageSequenceRate`. Media bin › Interpret Footage… (frame rate from the file or assumed, keep pitch, pixel aspect from the file or a preset or custom value, alpha, field order; several items at once; one undo step); MCP `montage_interpret_media` (frame_rate, pixel_aspect, alpha, field_order, keep_pitch, reset; "file" for the file's own; project reports give the plain path and the interpretation, and media are found by their file however read). **Tests:** core: the path round trip (no "/" or "-" in it), replaced not stacked, the file's own rate and straight alpha leaving no mark, unknown choices ignored, through a project file. Media: a 120 fps ProRes ramp with a 1 kHz tone conformed to 24: five seconds long, timeline frame 10 showing file frame 10 (frame 50 before), the clip's length kept, a clip marker, a subclip and a transcript word moved to the same frames, the sound five times as long at 200 Hz (at 1 kHz with keep pitch), saved and loaded, relinked still conformed, back to the file's rate; pixel aspect 2 filling a 320 x 90 sequence that pillarboxed it before, decoded at 320; alpha straight, premultiplied, ignore and invert on a half-transparent PNG (a still's rate ignored); a progressive-flagged movie of alternate lines left alone, kept as is with progressive, and rebuilt from the bright field (upper) or the dark one (lower); MCP conforming with a pixel aspect, back to the file's rate keeping the aspect, an unknown alpha refused. App: the bin's context menu offering it; the dialog (file rate shown, 25 fps assumed, anamorphic 2x, alpha ignored) conforming a 50 fps clip to two seconds at 128 wide with the clip's length kept; one undo; reopened on conformed footage it starts from that reading and Cancel changes nothing; a bad rate refused.
- [x] Agent link: MCP into the running app (Premiere 26's AI Assistant panel, Avid 2026.8's agentic "Ready to Edit", Descript's Underlord and MCP connector all work on the open project; Montage's 93 MCP tools only edited files on disk). `app/LiveLink` serves MCP over HTTP on 127.0.0.1 (a free port, a random key checked as a bearer token, requests with a foreign Origin refused) and writes the address and key to `mcp-live.json` in the user's configuration folder, readable by the user only (`app/LiveBridge`, `MONTAGE_MCP_LIVE_FILE` overrides). Every existing tool works on the open project with no tool rewritten: a call that leaves out `project` (or names the open file) runs on a copy saved beside the project (so files tools write land there), off the UI thread on its own `McpServer`, one call at a time; if the copy changed, it is loaded and becomes the project in one undo step "Assistant: <tool title>", keeping the editor's playhead and marks and decoding new media's sound for waveforms; if the editor changed the project meanwhile (playhead moves aside), nothing is applied and the agent is told to call again. Answers name the open project, not the copy. `tools/list` marks `project` optional and adds `montage_live_context` (the file, active sequence, playhead, In / Out, selected clips with ids, tracks and times, the last undo step; moves the playhead or selects clips); `montage_undo` on the open project undoes only an agent's step. `montage-cli mcp --live` is a stdio bridge for clients that start programs (Claude Code, Claude Desktop), answering "not running" errors when the link is off. Tools › Let AI Agents Edit This Project (remembered) and Tools › Agent Link… (on/off, the commands for Claude Code and HTTP clients, Copy Command). **Tests:** app: the link started from the window with the connection file written; no key 401, a foreign Origin 403; the tool list with `project` optional and the context tool; a title added over HTTP as one "Assistant:" undo step, the answer naming the open project and the copy removed; project info leaving the history alone; the context tool moving the playhead and selecting the new title, and reporting it; the agent's undo taking its title back but refusing the editor's marker; an edit made while a tool runs winning over the tool's result; the stdio bridge relaying a tool list and a context call from another thread; switched off, the file removed and the bridge answering with an error.
- [x] Assistant panel (Premiere 26's AI Assistant panel, Avid 2026.8's Gemini panel, Descript's Underlord): Window › Assistant, a conversation with a language model that edits the open project through the agent link's tool handling (`LiveLink::handle`, so every MCP tool and `montage_live_context` are available, each change an "Assistant:" undo step). `app/Assistant` (`AssistantSession`) runs the tool loop for Anthropic's Messages API (tools with the last one cache-controlled, the system prompt, `tool_use` blocks run in order and answered as `tool_result` blocks with errors flagged, pictures from `montage_render_frame` passed back as image blocks) and for OpenAI-compatible chat completions (function tools, `tool_calls` with JSON-string arguments, `tool` messages; pictures noted as not shown); tool answers are the tool's text plus its structured content, cut at 24,000 characters; at most 24 model turns a request; Stop aborts the request and trims the unanswered exchange so the conversation stays well formed; New Chat starts over. Settings (service, endpoint, model, key; saved in Montage's settings; `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` / `OPENAI_API_KEY` otherwise; Claude defaults to claude-opus-5-5, the OpenAI-compatible service to a local Ollama). `app/AssistantPanel` shows the conversation, each tool as it starts and finishes (✓ / ✗ with its first line), and failures. **Tests:** app, against a scripted local stand-in for the service: Claude asked for a title calls the title tool (headers with the key and API version, the default model, the system prompt, 90+ tools with the cache mark), the tool's result sent back as a `tool_result` for its id, the title on the timeline as an "Assistant:" undo step and the conversation in the panel; a request left unanswered stopped, with the panel saying so; no key refused without a request; an OpenAI-compatible model (bearer key, `/v1/chat/completions`, system message, function tools) moving the playhead through the context tool, its `tool` message sent back, the answer shown.
- [x] OpenFX video plugins (Resolve, Vegas, Nuke, Fusion, Natron, and in 2026 Kdenlive and Shotcut host them; it opens Neat Video, Sapphire, Continuum, RE:Vision, Dehancer and FilmConvert). The OpenFX 1.5 headers are vendored (`third_party/openfx`, BSD-3-Clause). `render/Ofx` is the host: property sets (typed, multi-dimensional, created on set as plugins expect), the image effect suite (clips, images bottom-up with row bytes, the output image copied back on release, image memory), the parameter suite (define with per-type default properties, values read from the clip effect's keyframed parameters at any OFX time in clip frames, the plugin's own values otherwise, derivatives, integrals, key times and indexes, set values from the plugin), memory, multithreading (threads with their index, recursive mutexes), messages (errors kept for the failure report), progress and timeline suites, and a host property set describing what Montage supports. A binary is opened once (`OfxGetNumberOfPlugins`/`OfxGetPlugin`, `OfxSetHost` when present); each image effect is loaded, described and described in the Filter context (or General); instances are made per clip effect (see the review fixes below), render-unsafe plug-ins are serialised, and each frame is Begin / Render / End sequence render with the render window and scale. Frames are premultiplied RGBA float, or 8-bit for plug-ins that take nothing else. `ofx::Registry` scans the standard folders for `*.ofx.bundle` binaries of this platform, describes new or changed ones in `montage-plugin-probe OFX <binary>` (crashes and hangs blocklisted, results cached by size and time). A clip effect of type "ofx" holds the plug-in's identity and saved description and one parameter per plug-in parameter (`param.<name>`, colours `.r/.g/.b[/.a]`, 2D/3D `.x/.y/.z`, text `str.<name>`), with metadata so the Inspector shows sliders, switches, choices, colours and angles (`pluginParamMeta` gained kind, choices, colour defaults and keyframeability). In the compositor the effect runs where any filter runs (masks apply), and temporal plug-ins are given the clip's own source frames at other times (`ofx::FetchScope`). Effects browser › Video Plugins by the plug-in's grouping; launch scan with the audio plugins; MCP `montage_list_effects` (with parameters) and `montage_add_effect` with `ofx:<id>` and parameters by their own names. **Tests:** a test bundle with two filters (an invert with a keyframeable amount, a mode choice, a tint colour and a text note; a temporal average of the frames either side) and a bundle that crashes while describing itself. Plugins: both binaries found; the scan describing two filters in the probe and blocklisting the crash, then answering from the cache with nothing loaded; descriptions (labels, grouping, parameters, defaults, choices, temporal access); the clip effect's Inspector rows (slider, choice, colour); on a colour matte the picture inverted, half way at a keyframed half amount, tinted, and passed through; saved and loaded; the temporal average reading two neighbour frames through the host and, in a sequence of three stills, the clip's own neighbours; MCP listing it and adding it with parameters and text, an unknown parameter refused; a missing plug-in leaving the picture alone with the reason. App: the Effects browser's Video Plugins › Montage Test › Test Invert, applied to the selected clip as one undo step and shown in the Inspector.
- [x] Camera RAW settings and CinemaDNG (Resolve's Camera Raw panel and CinemaDNG playback, Premiere's Source Settings for RAW, Final Cut's RAW inspector; Montage developed RAW stills only as shot). `core/Interpretation` gained exposure (stops), colour temperature (K, 0 for as shot), tint, highlight mode ("", blend, rebuild) and half size (path keys `rawexp`, `rawtemp`, `rawtint`, `rawhl`, `rawhalf`), so RAW settings travel with the media like the rest of Interpret Footage. `developRaw` takes `RawSettings`: exposure as LibRaw's linear shift where it reaches (−2 to +3 stops, highlights preserved when brightening) and a gain before the output curve beyond it (±5 stops in all), temperature through `whiteBalanceMultipliers` (the light's chromaticity on the Planckian locus by Kim et al.'s fit, taken through the camera's XYZ matrix to the multipliers that make it grey; the matrix from LibRaw's table, else the DNG's ColorMatrix for daylight, else worked back from its camera-to-sRGB matrix), tint alone as a green shift on the camera's own balance, highlight modes 0 / 2 / 5 and half-size decoding (shown at full size). Automatic brightening is now off for all RAW, so a file develops at the camera's exposure and a CinemaDNG run does not flicker. A numbered run of RAW frames is probed as one video (codec "dng", the run's rate and length) and `VideoDecoder` develops each frame as it is shown. `edit::interpretFootage` refuses RAW settings on anything else and exposure beyond ±4; Interpret Footage gains a Camera RAW group (exposure, white balance presets with temperature and tint, highlights, half size) shown for RAW media; MCP `montage_interpret_media` takes `raw_exposure`, `raw_temperature` (or "as_shot"), `raw_tint`, `raw_highlights` and `raw_half` and reports them. **Tests:** media: a grey-card DNG developed neutral; +1 stop brighter and −1 darker, −4 darker than −2; the white balance multipliers for D65 near 1 and for 3000 K boosting blue; tungsten correction turning the card blue, shade warm, daylight neutral, a magenta tint lowering green; half size shown at 64 × 48; Interpret Footage on the item, refused for a PNG and for 9 stops; six numbered DNG frames detected as a run, probed as a quarter-second 24 fps "dng" video, placed as six frames getting brighter frame by frame through the compositor and brighter again with +1 stop; MCP setting exposure, 3200 K and blend and reporting them. The existing RAW stills test still passes with automatic brightening off.
- [x] Review fixes for Interpret Footage, the agent link, the Assistant and OpenFX (a read-through of those four commits found twelve bugs; all fixed, each with a test that fails without it). **OpenFX:** only a plug-in this computer's scan found and did not block is ever loaded: an effect whose plug-in is missing or blocked says so ("is not installed" / "is blocked: Crashed while loading") instead of loading the binary named in the project file, so a crafted project cannot run a library of its choosing and a plug-in that crashes the probe cannot crash the editor through an old project (renders outside the app scan once first, `Registry::ensureScanned`). Instances belong to one clip effect each and render one frame at a time (checked out of a pool, made when none of that effect's is free), so one effect's per-instance state never leaks into another's; free instances past 16 are destroyed (`kOfxActionDestroyInstance`), least recently used first, instead of piling up per thread. Each render tells the instance which parameters changed since its last (`kOfxActionBegin/InstanceChanged/EndInstanceChanged`, user edited). `kOfxImagePropUniqueIdentifier` names a source picture by its size and a hash of its pixels (the same picture, the same name) and each output anew, instead of a reused heap address. Spatial doubles defined in normalised coordinates are kept as fractions of the frame (so they follow its size) and handed to the plug-in in canonical pixels, as the API requires (`ParamDesc::normalised`). A failed load is remembered until the next scan instead of retried every frame. Temporal plug-ins' neighbouring frames are converted into the working space like the frame itself (they were in the media's own space: ghosting on log or HDR footage), and resized to match when a frame comes back another size. **Agent link:** a tool keeps its own server alive, so quitting while it runs no longer frees it under the worker thread; the tool's copy of the project is then removed and nothing applied. Turning the link off drops agents' queued calls and does not apply the one running (the Assistant panel's in-process calls are unaffected). Tools whose `project` is the new file they write (Create a project, Import a timeline) keep it required and never replace the open project. Requests are judged on their headers (origin, path, key, method) before any body is kept, and bodies over 16 MB are refused (413). **Assistant:** new settings stop a request in flight, and switching between Claude and an OpenAI-compatible service starts a new conversation (each keeps it in its own form; the panel says so). **Interpret Footage:** Replace Footage keeps the assumed frame rate but conforms from the new file's own rate (a 30 fps take replacing 120 fps footage conformed to 24 plays at 24, not at the equivalent of 6); subclip items follow the media's new reading (path, size, rate, length, timecode), so their thumbnails decode the right frames. **Tests:** plugins: a third test filter ("Test Probe": a normalised centre written back as pixels; a per-instance count of Amount changes): its centre at 0.5 × 0.25 of a 400 × 200 frame rendered at half size reaching the plug-in as 200, 50, and following an edit; changes counted only for Amount, once per edit, and none on a second effect's own instance; twenty more effects leaving at most 16 instances alive; a project naming another binary refused as not installed and one naming the crashed binary refused as blocked, with nothing loaded; on S-Log3 stills the temporal average equal to the average of the converted frames. App: a huge declared body refused 401 without the key and 413 with it; Create a project and Import a timeline keeping `project` required; the link turned off while an agent's marker tool runs, the tool answered "turned off", its queued call dropped, no marker added and no copy left; a link destroyed mid-tool with nothing applied and no copy left; the OpenAI-compatible service starting with only the system prompt and the new request. Media: the subclip's path, rate and length after conforming; a 30 fps take replacing conformed footage, still at 24 and conformed from 30, 1.25 s long.
- [x] Spectral Repair (Adobe Audition's Spectral Frequency Display with spectral healing, iZotope RX's Spectral Repair; no NLE has it built in: Premiere, Resolve and Final Cut send the clip to an audio editor). `audio/SpectralRepair`: `spectralRepair` works on a clip's whole source audio as a source-audio effect ("spectral_repair", like Noise Reduction: cached, run before the clip's other restoration, the cache key now including effect strings) on regions of time × frequency, each healed or attenuated, per channel or both. Each region is processed by its own short-time Fourier transform (square-root Hann analysis and synthesis windows of about 43 ms, 75 % overlap) over the region and a window's length either side only, written back where the overlap-add is complete, so the rest of the file is copied untouched. Frames whose window touches the region are treated fully and the treatment fades over two hops beyond; frequencies are feathered over two bins. Heal brings each bin down (never up) to the level heard over a quarter of a second just before and just after, interpolated across the region on a log scale (one side alone at the file's edges; 30 dB down if neither); Attenuate scales it by the gain; the blend at the feathered edges is geometric and the original phase is kept. `computeSpectrogram` gives levels in dB of a full-scale sine for drawing; `prominentBands` finds the bands that stand out in a stretch against the second either side (or its median at the file's edges), each grown from its strongest bin while neighbours are 6 dB above their surroundings and within 20 dB of the peak. Regions are kept in the effect's `regions` string (start, end, low, high, mode, gain, channel; invalid entries dropped) in source seconds (`clipSourceSeconds` converts timeline seconds through the clip's timing). `app/SpectralRepairDialog` (Clip › Spectral Repair…, modeless): a spectrogram of the clip's repaired sound over the source it plays (a minute at most, from the playhead), log frequency from 40 Hz with each row the loudest bin it spans, ticks for hertz and seconds, regions outlined (heal cyan, attenuate orange); drag to choose a box, All frequencies, Find Frequencies, Heal, Attenuate (−1 to −96 dB), Remove Last, Clear All; each change one undo step, undo and redo redrawn. MCP `montage_spectral_repair` (clip, regions in timeline seconds with low_hz / high_hz or found automatically and reported, mode, gain_db, channel; `find_only`; `clear`). **Tests:** media: four seconds of a 440 Hz voice with noise, a 3 kHz whistle and a 7 kHz squeak on the right only: the spectrogram's peak at 3 kHz within 30 Hz at its level within 2 dB; the whistle's band found narrow (under 250 Hz) and over 20 dB above its surroundings, nothing found in a quiet stretch; healed, the whistle more than 30 dB down on both channels with the voice in the same moment within 0.3 dB and the file before 0.5 s and after 2.2 s bit-identical, just outside the box within 1e-4; attenuated on the right only by 20 dB (−18 to −22 measured) with the left untouched; the regions string round trip with nonsense dropped; on a trimmed clip, source seconds from timeline seconds, the effect made first among the clip's effects, through the source-audio cache healed and then (a different key) attenuated by 6 dB; MCP finding the band only, healing with the band found and keeping it in source seconds, an attenuation on the left with given frequencies, regions outside the clip or with nothing standing out refused, clear removing the effect. App: the Clip menu action, refused with no clip; the editor's picture bright at the whistle and dark before it; Heal refused with no box; a box across the whistle fitted by Find Frequencies to under 400 Hz around 3 kHz, healed as one undo step "Spectral Heal" with the effect first on the clip, the picture dark there and the voice unchanged; undo and redo redrawn; an all-frequencies attenuation of −12 dB, Remove Last, Clear All removing the effect.
- [x] Review fixes for Camera RAW and Spectral Repair (a read-through of both commits; each fix has a test that fails without it). Numbers written into media paths and effect strings no longer follow the C library's locale (`core/Numbers`: `formatNumber` and `parseNumber` through the classic locale; Spectral Repair's regions keep times to the microsecond), and the app pins `LC_NUMERIC` to "C" after QApplication takes the environment's, so on a German or French system a repair region is no longer silently dropped and a project's RAW settings read the same everywhere. Importing or dropping a CinemaDNG folder (or a watch folder receiving one) now makes one clip at the frames' own rate: `cinemaDng` reads the TimeCodes or FrameRate tag from the first IFD, so numbered DNG photos stay stills. Highlight recovery (Blend, Rebuild) no longer darkens the whole picture: LibRaw then scales by the largest white balance multiplier, and the ratio to the smallest is given back as brightness. Spectral Repair: `prominentBands` measures frame by frame instead of building a spectrogram of the whole stretch, the overlap-add writes each sample back as soon as it is complete (no buffer the length of a long region), the source-audio cache keeps at most three versions per file, twelve in all and 2 GiB of sound, and a background clean-up it drops stops (exports' are never dropped); the editor redraws once per change and closes when another sequence or project opens; regions on reversed clips map to the source as the mixer plays them. MCP: Spectral Repair refuses video-track clips, a positive gain and frequencies outside 0 to 24 kHz, and finds bands in the clip's own channel mix; `raw_temperature` takes only a number or "as_shot". **Tests:** media: under a light that needs camera white balance (neutral 0.5, 1, 0.7), Blend and Rebuild within 8 % of the as-shot brightness (0.29 against 0.43 before); RAW settings and a region with fractional times round trip (under a comma locale where one is installed) and are written with '.'; six CinemaDNG frames recognised with their 24 fps and a DNG photo not; a three-second attenuation 12 dB down with the stretch before it bit-identical; a reversed clip's source times; MCP refusals for a positive gain, 30 kHz and "5600" as a temperature.
