- [x] Digital Cinema Packages (DaVinci Resolve Studio exports DCPs through easyDCP or Kakadu; Premiere, Final Cut and Avid send films to DCP-o-matic). `render/DcpMxf` writes SMPTE track files without asdcplib: OP-Atom, a closed complete header partition padded to 16 KiB and rewritten in place with the final duration, a body partition of frame-wrapped essence (JPEG 2000 codestreams under ST 429-4; 24-bit PCM, one picture frame of samples per KLV, under ST 429-3), a footer with index table segments (VBR picture entries, 5000 to a segment; CBR sound) and a random index pack. The header metadata (preface, identification, content storage, essence container data, material and file packages with timecode and essence tracks, the RGBA descriptor with its JPEG 2000 sub-descriptor filled from the codestream's SIZ/COD/QCD, or the wave descriptor with ST 377-4 multichannel labels for 5.1 or 7.1 DS and the MCA channel assignment), the primer and every label were checked set by set against CineCert's asdcplib, which reads Montage's files and unwraps their essence bit-exactly; the file package's UMID carries the asset id the composition refers to. `render/Dcp`: `exportDcp` mixes the sound first (the sequence's layout routed to 5.1, 7.1 to 7.1 DS, stereo to L and R; a 23.976 sequence mixed so each frame is 2000 samples and played at 24), then renders each frame at 2K in the container (Flat, Scope or full, the frame fitted on black), converts it from the sequence's colour space (HDR tone mapped to P3) to DCI X'Y'Z' (display light through the primaries' XYZ matrix with the white kept, ×48/52.37, ^1/2.6, 12 bits) and hands it to a pool of JPEG 2000 encoders (one per core; libopenjpeg in its DCI 2K profile, CPRL, cinema mode capping frames for 250 Mbit/s; FFmpeg's own encoder if OpenJPEG is missing, reported as not in the profile), writing codestreams in order; it then writes the CPL (ST 429-7 with ST 429-16 composition metadata: full title, territory, version, luminance, main sound configuration and sample rate, stored and active picture areas, the Bv2.1 constraints profile), PKL with SHA-1 hashes, ASSETMAP and VOLINDEX into a folder named by the ISDCF convention (`dcpName`), refuses packages under a second (servers do) or into an existing folder, and removes a half-made folder when stopped. `verifyDcp` checks the asset map and volume index, files and sizes, packing list hashes, each composition's assets and durations, the picture tracks (JPEG 2000, X'Y'Z' 12-bit, DCI size, frame count, peak bit rate) and the sound (24-bit 48 or 96 kHz); `readDcpFrame` decodes a frame's X'Y'Z' codes. File › Export DCP… (title, kind, picture container defaulting by the sequence's shape, frame rate, language, territory, issuer, studio, facility, In to Out, folder) runs it off the UI thread with progress and cancel and reports the check; MCP `montage_export_dcp` and `montage_verify_dcp`. Checked independently with ClairMeta (78 checks: XML schemas, naming, assets, MXF metadata through asdcp-info, picture and sound): Success, with only the information that the name has 10 of the 12 ISDCF parts when no studio and facility are given. **Tests:** render: a second of mid-grey with a 1 kHz tone as a Scope short: 24 frames at 24 fps, 2048 × 858, 5.1, the ISDCF name, in the DCI profile; the check passing; the CPL naming both track files by the ids in their file names, with its rate, duration, aspect, sound configuration, constraints profile and kind, its annotation the name; frame 3 decoded to X'Y'Z' within 12 codes of the grey's values from first principles in the middle and black in the pillarbox; the sound read back as a second with the tone on the left and the centre silent; a damaged sound file caught by its hash; the same package refused a second time, a stopped one leaving no folder, half a second refused; 23.976 to 24, 29.97 to 30, a chosen 25; Flat and Full sizes, Scope chosen for a 2.39:1 sequence; the full twelve-part name with studio and facility. App: the File menu action; a Scope package made in the background from the window and checked, the status saying so; over MCP a trailer made and checked with a studio in its name, the verify tool passing it and then failing it once its volume index is gone, an unknown container refused.
- [x] Redact Faces (news, documentary and reality editors must hide people who have not agreed to be shown; Resolve's and Premiere's answer is a mask tracked by hand per face, YouTube Studio's Blur Faces finds them). `media/FaceTracks`: `trackFaces` decodes the clip's source span (every frame up to 30 per second, long side 1280), finds faces with YuNet at a 0.7 threshold and takes each one's SFace identity every frame; `linkFaceTracks` links them frame to frame greedily by distance (allowing for the time since the track was last seen, up to a second) and size, refusing a link when both identities are known and below 0.2 cosine, preferring the track seen last; a track seen once with a weak score is dropped. `trackedFacesAt` gives each track's box at a media time: interpolated across gaps up to twice the hold, held for the hold beyond either end (and half a frame between analysed frames). `groupFaceTracks` groups tracks by identity (0.42), most seen first, and `matchProjectPeople` names groups after People search's people. Tracks are kept on the effect as locale-independent text (identities quantised to 8 bits); the tracks left showing as a list of ids. `render/FaceRefine` `redactFaces` covers each face's box grown for forehead and hair (Expand) as an ellipse or rectangle, full inside and feathered outside, with a Gaussian blur (up to half the face's size), blocks (24 to 5 across the face) or a solid colour keeping alpha; `outlineFaces` for Show Tracked Faces. Effect `redact_faces` (Privacy) is applied first among the clip's effects (so anything that moves the picture moves the cover with it); within the analysed span the tracks decide, outside it (or with no analysis) the faces detected in the frame are all covered, and the compositor runs the detector only then. `app/RedactFacesDialog` (Clip › Redact Faces…): Find Faces in the background with progress and Stop, each person with a picture from their largest sighting and their time on screen, ticks for who is covered, Cover with (Blur, Pixelate, Solid Colour), one undo step; reopened, it shows the saved analysis and choice. MCP `montage_redact_faces` (analyse once, then `show` / `cover_only` by group number or person's name, style, strength, hold, `reanalyse`). **Tests:** render: blur removes the face's detail and nothing outside; pixelate gives blocks; a solid colour keeps alpha; the ellipse leaves its box's corners and the rectangle covers them; through the effect two tracks with one left showing, beyond the analysed span only the frame's own faces, Show Tracked Faces red and green. Media: synthetic sightings linked into three tracks across a five-frame gap, someone stepping in where another left kept apart, a weak single find dropped; boxes interpolated, held and not held by the hold; the text round trip with malformed text refused; grouping by identity; a clip's media span forwards and reversed; the effect made first. With the models, a generated video of Kennedy drifting right (hidden for four frames) and Armstrong: two people found and on screen throughout, Kennedy covered while hidden; rendered, his face's detail down from 47 to 1 with Armstrong's unchanged; over MCP analysed and pixelated, Armstrong (named by People search) left showing by name, covering only the other by number, the analysis reused, bad groups, names, styles and both lists refused. App: the Clip menu action, the dialog finding Kennedy's face with its picture, covered as one undo step with the effect first, his face's detail down from 40 to 1, left showing pixelated with the frame unchanged, the choice remembered when reopened, closed by a new project.
- [x] Review fixes for DCP export and Redact Faces (a read-through of both commits; each fix has a test). DCP: the encoder pool no longer deadlocks when JPEG 2000 encoding falls behind rendering (one or two cores, or FFmpeg's own encoder): the frames in hand are bounded by writing the earliest one, which is always being encoded or next in line, before pushing another; 50 and 59.94/60 fps sequences play at 25 and 30 from every other frame at their own speed (they had become 48 fps, 25 % slow and lower in pitch); In to Out includes the Out frame; hashing the track files and checking the package report progress and stop when cancelled, and the check runs off the UI thread with the export (it read a feature's files twice on it). Redact Faces: an analysis is used only on the media it was made from (Paste Attributes, a replaced clip or a pasted preset carried another clip's boxes, leaving these faces uncovered) and not after Reframe 360; an analysis that found nobody, or a frame even one past the seconds analysed, also covers the faces found in the frame (with the tracked ones held there); a face seen once, however faintly, is kept (frame holds of 50/60 fps footage analyse one frame); someone stepping into another's place starts their own track unless SFace's 0.36 says it is the same person (it was 0.2); the effect always runs first among the clip's effects; adding it without the face models offers to download them. **Tests:** render: a 60 fps sequence exported with one encoder as a 30 fps DCP of 30 frames from In 0 to Out 59, its tone still 1 kHz; 50, 59.94 and 48 fps rates and steps; the check stopping at 30 %; tracked faces with the frame's faces just past the analysis, another media's or a reframed frame's analysis ignored, an empty analysis leaving it to the frame. Media: the faint single find kept, a 0.3-alike person kept apart.
- [x] IMF masters (Application #2E, SMPTE ST 2067-21:2021; Resolve Studio and Avid export IMF, Premiere and Final Cut need a third-party tool). `render/Jpeg2000`: OpenJPEG 2.5 directly (FFmpeg's wrapper offers only the cinema profiles), optional at build time (`MONTAGE_WITH_OPENJPEG`): `imfRsiz` picks the IMF profile (2K to 2048 x 1556, else 4K or 8K; reversible or lossy), the main level from samples per second over all components and the sub level from the bit rate (raising the main level when the sub level needs it); OpenJPEG sets the profile's coding (32 x 32 code-blocks, CPRL, 128/256 precincts, decompositions) and refuses a picture it cannot code, which the exporter catches from the Rsiz; `imfPictureCoding` gives the SMPTE picture coding label for the Rsiz; encoding to and decoding from memory. `render/DcpMxf` gained the AS-02 layout: OP1a, partition version 1.3, one track per package and no timecode, JPEG 2000 frame-wrapped as progressive frames (P1) with RGBA and JPEG 2000 sub-descriptors carrying the transfer, primaries and coding equations labels, video line map, component range, J2CLayout and (PQ) the mastering display primaries, white point and luminances; PCM clip-wrapped in one KLV (its eight-byte length written at the end) with the WAVE descriptor and ST 2067-2 labels (a soundfield group with title, version, content kind PRM and element kind FCMP; stereo sgST, 5.1 sg51, 7.1 DS sg71); the index table (VBR for pictures, CBR for sound) in its own partition, an empty footer and the body partition pack rewritten with the footer's place. `render/Imf`: `exportImf` mixes the sound at 48 kHz from the first frame's time for exactly the frames' duration (48048 samples for a second at 23.976), renders each frame at the target size fitted on black in the delivery colour (Rec.709, P3-D65 PQ (a new colour space), Rec.2020 PQ, Rec.2020 HLG), quantises to full-range 10 or 12 bits and encodes on a pool of OpenJPEG encoders (the same in-order, never-deadlocking scheme as the DCP's), then writes the CPL (ST 2067-3:2016 with the 2067-2:2020 core constraints: content kind, version, essence descriptor list as RegXML mirroring the MXF descriptors and their instance ids, the Application #2E 2021 identification, one segment with main image and audio sequences whose resources name their descriptors and hashes), the PKL (2067-2:2016) and the asset map; frame rates as Application #2E allows them. `verifyImf` checks the asset map, files, sizes and hashes, each resource's track file, descriptor and duration, the application identification, the pictures' IMF profile and frame count and the sound's sample count; `readImfFrame` decodes a frame. File › Export IMF Master… (title, kind, size, colour, depth, lossless or 800/400/200 Mbit/s, language, issuer, In to Out) runs it off the UI thread with progress and cancel and checks the package; MCP `montage_export_imf` and `montage_verify_imf`. Checked with Netflix's Photon 5.0.1 (IMPAnalyzer) on HD Rec.709 lossless, UHD P3-D65 PQ 12-bit lossless and HD Rec.2020 PQ lossy packages: no errors or warnings; asdcplib's as-02-info reads the files (its bit-rate table lacks the reversible profiles' labels). **Tests:** render: a second of a colour matte at 23.976 with a tone: 24 frames, 320 x 180, 10-bit Rec.709, Rsiz 0x0701 (2K reversible, main level 1), the folder named from the title, the check passing; the CPL's application, rates, durations (24 and 48048), Rsiz, component layout, range, primaries, picture coding label and stereo labels; frame 5 decoded to the matte's exact codes (512, 256, 767); the sound 48048 samples with the tone at 1 kHz; a damaged file caught by its hash; the package refused a second time; a stopped export leaving nothing; an HDR P3-D65 PQ 12-bit lossy 5.1 master at Rsiz 0x0411 with its mastering display (4000 cd/m^2), no coding equations, sg51 labels and the matte's order kept; an unknown colour, 48 fps and an 8K picture refused (UHD offered); the default colours; the profiles and levels for UHD lossless and lossy, 1080p and 16K, and their labels. App: the File menu action; a master made in the background from the window and checked; over MCP an HDR lossy episode made and checked, the check failing once its asset map is gone, an unknown colour refused.
  - Review fixes: frames of 16 MiB or more (lossless UHD and 4K) get the 8-byte KLV length instead of a truncated 4-byte one; at 29.97 and 59.94 the programme is padded with black and silence to a multiple of five frames so the sound fills whole frames; the encoders at once are capped to about 4 GiB of frames in hand (a dozen at UHD) and an out-of-memory in an encoder is reported instead of ending the app; main levels 9 to 11 have their picture coding labels (and a zero label is refused); 120 fps is judged on the size written; the edit rate is written reduced (24000/1001, not 48000/2002); mastering peaks are clamped to 100–10 000 cd/m². **Tests:** a level 9 label; the 120 fps rule; a rate stored as 48000/2002 written as 24000/1001; seven frames at 29.97 padded to ten with 16 016 samples and black, checked; a 17 MiB frame with the 8-byte length, the file walking as KLV to its end.
- [x] AAF import (Media Composer, Pro Tools, Fairlight; Premiere and Resolve import AAF, Final Cut does not). core/AafImport: the compound file read back through the AAF object model (property streams; strong references as storages, vectors and sets through their index streams; weak references by key), mobs gathered by id. Each top-level composition (or, when none says so, each one no other composition uses) becomes a sequence at its first picture or sound slot's edit rate; its slots become tracks with their names. Segments are walked: sequences in order, fillers as gaps, transitions overlapping their neighbours (dissolves or crossfades, trimmed at the cut point), operation groups (Audio Gain as clip gain, the first value of a varying one; a constant speed ratio as clip speed), selectors, essence groups and nested scopes by their first choice, source clips followed through master mobs to the file mob's locator and descriptor (length, rate, a multiple descriptor's sound channels) and the sound channel the slot plays. Files are found where the locator says, beside the AAF, or in a folder below it; missing ones become offline media keeping their names and lengths. Locators and other positioned events (in event or data slots) become markers. Wired into File › Import Timeline and MCP `montage_import_timeline`. tests/tools/make_aaf.py writes an AAF as Media Composer lays one out (pyaaf2, AMA-linked to a movie: filler, a dissolve with its cut point, two sound channels, Audio Gain, a locator). Tests: media `aafImportFromMediaComposer` (positions, source offsets, the dissolve, channels, gain, the marker, media moved beside the AAF found, missing media offline, through MCP, a non-AAF refused) and the round trip of Montage's own AAF export in `aafExportForAudioPost`.
  - Review fixes (with the sidechain's): the macOS build (a narrowing Rational); the compound-file reader checks the sector sizes and every DIFAT sector against the file, never allocates past what a stream's chain holds, and walks each storage's siblings without recursion, each entry once (a self-referencing directory looped, and long sibling runs were cut off at 64); slow motion (a speed ratio below 1) came in at the input's length, now the operation's; dissolves to and from filler become fades in and out, and sound clips' fade lengths become fades; keyframed Audio Gain (Media Composer's and Montage's own) becomes gain keyframes at their places (shifted with a crossfade's trim), an amplitude of 0 the gain floor; nested compositions come in once each as sequences played by nested sequence clips (their other sound slots not doubled); compositions keep the file's order and every composition's warnings are kept; negative lengths and leading transitions cannot place clips before the start. Sidechain: a duplicated sequence's compressors and gates follow their key track to its copy (Duplicate Sequence, Reconform, analysis copies); the Inspector no longer offers the effect's own track; a clip rendered alone (AAF export) keeps the other tracks muted so its key is heard; the key is mixed once per block (consumers fed ahead by plugin latency no longer run its state twice) and fed ahead by its own inserts' latency; the key mixer follows the mixer's non-blocking mode. **Tests:** core: hostile DIFAT and sector size refused, a self-looping directory read once, 300 siblings read whole; media: slow motion at 0.5 out and back, the fade from filler, the nested composition, the round trip's fade out and gain keyframes, a duplicated sequence's key and a clip rendered alone still ducked; app: the Inspector without the clip's own track.
- [x] Picture tracks in AAF export (Premiere's and Resolve's AAF carry video; Media Composer conforms from it). render/AafExport: the video tracks (unless `AafExportOptions::picture` is off) as picture slots after the timecode, V1 up: each clip a source clip into a master mob whose file mob has a CDCI descriptor (size, aspect, frame rate and length from the media, container External) and a network locator naming the original file, linked where it is and never copied; a conformed frame rate (Interpret Footage) is read from the file's own rate and time. Constant speed (and a conform) becomes a Motion Control operation (VideoSpeedControl with its Speed Ratio) around a source clip of the frames used; ramps play at their average and reversed clips forwards, listed. Dissolves between two clips that meet become Video Dissolve transitions at the edit point, each side reaching into the media's handles as far as it has them (shortened or a cut otherwise, listed); other transitions become dissolves and fades to black are left out, listed. Titles, generators, nested sequences and image sequences leave gaps, listed. A picture-only sequence exports without the empty media folder. File › Export AAF, `montage-cli aaf --no-picture`, MCP `montage_export_timeline` `picture` (and video counts in its result). Tests: media `aafExportWithPicture` (two shots with a dissolve and a generated clip on V1, a double-speed shot and a still on V2; read back by Montage's import with the same places, source frames, dissolve, speed and files; footage conformed from 25 to 50 fps as speed 2 from the file's frames; MCP without picture; pyaaf2 reading the picture slots, the dissolve with its cut point, Motion Control and the file descriptors).
  - Review fixes: Motion Control used Mono Audio Pan's AUID (other editors would drop every speed change; Montage's import went by the parameter); two dissolves around a short clip could overlap (now each reaches only as far as the other leaves, in timeline order); a Motion Control's input is rounded up so every frame shown is in it; Photoshop layers are listed as not linked (their path is no file); a file with a start timecode is recorded as a source carrying it (a "tape" mob with a Timecode slot, the file's first frame at its timecode), as Media Composer and Resolve match media; reversed clips start at their source In; stills get handles for dissolves; anamorphic footage has its stored width with the shown aspect; constant Time Remapping is not warned about as a ramp; clips are held within their media's length; File › Export AAF, Sound Only; a picture-only summary no longer names a sound folder, and a failed export leaves no empty folder. **Tests:** the Motion Control AUID, the tape timecode (01:00:00:00 at 25 fps), no timecode on a still; a 10-frame shot between two 16-frame dissolves within the AAF's rule, a 40 % shot fed all 41 frames, an anamorphic file's 8:3 aspect; the sound-only menu item.
- [x] Shared projects (Avid's bin locking, Premiere's Productions, Resolve's collaboration locks). core/ProjectLock: a lock file beside each open project (".<name>.lock": user, host, app, pid, since, heartbeat as JSON), created atomically (open with NewOnly) so of two editors opening at once one wins; Mine (this process), Theirs, Stale (no heartbeat for 180 s by the later of its own clock and the file's time on the shared drive, or its process gone from this machine; a half-written lock is someone's until it is old) or Free; refresh rewrites the heartbeat (QSaveFile) and reports a lock that was taken over; release only removes this process's. A folder that cannot be written opens unlocked. core/Production: a production is a folder with production.json (name); its projects listed recursively (lock files and hidden files left out) with their lock states; `productionOf` finds it from a project inside; `importFromProject` copies sequences with the sequences they nest (deepest first) and their media (a file the project has is reused, subclips keep their source, bins carried), giving everything new ids through `duplicateSequence` (which now also retargets track references such as sidechains). app: EditorState opens Auto (lock, or read-only when held), Edit (refuses when held) or ReadOnly; read-only refuses edits, amends, gestures, undo, imports and saving over the project with a message naming the editor; a timer renews the lock every 30 s, and for a read-only project reloads it when its file changes (keeping the active sequence and playheads) and notices when the lock is let go; `takeEdit` locks and reloads; Save As takes the new file's lock and makes the copy editable; New Project, closing and the destructor release; recovery copies are never locked and recovering takes the original's lock. MainWindow: opening a project someone is editing asks to open it read-only, a stale one offers Take Over; the title says Read-Only and who is editing; File › Edit Project; File › Production (New Production, Open Production, Import from Project with a sequence picker); the Production panel (projects, status, saved time, Open, Open Read-Only, Import Sequences, New Project; refreshed every 5 s, following the open project's production). MCP: writes to a project held by another process are refused naming them; `montage_production` (list with editors, or create) and `montage_import_from_project`. Tests: core `projectLocksAndProductions` (free, mine, again, refresh, release; another machine's lock theirs and left alone; stale by age taken over; a dead process on this machine stale; a half-written lock theirs then stale when old; a production of three projects in folders with mine/theirs/free, found from inside) and `importSequencesFromAnotherProject` (a sequence nesting another, media reused by path, a subclip pointing at it, bins, all ids new and unique, links and the sidechain kept, an unknown sequence nothing); app `sharedProjectLocking` (opened read-only with the title naming the editor, edits/imports/saving refused, Edit refused, their saves followed, taken to edit once let go with their last save, saved, released on New Project; Save As of a read-only project editable and locked while the original stays theirs; a production in the panel with statuses, opened from it to edit, a sequence imported from the project someone else is editing and undone, the menu actions); media `sharedProjectsOverMcp` (a production made and listed with its editor, a sequence imported from the busy project into the free one, unknown names refused, writing to the busy project refused and left unchanged).
- [x] Stereoscopic 3D (Premiere's stereoscopic workflow, Resolve's Stereo 3D, Final Cut's spatial video) and VR180. A stereo file's packing comes from its metadata (MP4/MOV st3d, Matroska StereoMode, H.264 frame packing; `MediaItem::stereo`: "sbs" or "tb", "_half" when each eye is squeezed into half the frame, told apart by the eyes' shape; 360° stereo is never squeezed, and a right-first flag is honoured) or from Interpret Footage (`stereo=` none/sbs/sbs_half/tb/tb_half and `swap=1` in the interpretation; stills are left flat). The media's size is one eye's. media/Decoder reads one eye (`eye=1` in the path for the right; core `eyePath`) by cropping the frame before scaling, so squeezed eyes are stretched back. `Sequence::stereo3d` (Sequence Settings › Stereo): the compositor renders the sequence once per eye (`RenderOptions::eye`), each stereo clip reading its eye; the **Stereo 3D** effect (`stereo_3d`: depth as % of the width, keyframeable, + into the screen, - out of it; Eyes As Shot or Swapped) moves the eyes apart by half the depth each way (not on adjustment layers, nothing in a flat sequence, where stereo footage shows its left eye). render/Stereo `combineStereo`: left, right, half-colour anaglyph (red from the left eye's luma), full or squeezed side by side and top and bottom, difference (twice the gap). `renderProgramFrame` combines the eyes for `RenderOptions::stereoView` (captions drawn on each eye); the render cache keys stereo frames by view. Playback › Stereo 3D View (Left, Right, Anaglyph, Side by Side, Top and Bottom, Difference; remembered). Export: `ExportSettings::stereo` (default full side by side; the size asked for is each eye's, each kept even; difference refused; alpha and smart render off for stereo) writes stereo metadata for packed views; Export dialog's Stereo 3D choice for stereo sequences. `Sequence::vr180` (with 360°): spherical metadata as an equirectangular tile of the front half; VR180 footage (that tile in its metadata) comes in as projection "vr180", and Reframe 360° maps its picture over the 180° in front (`reframeEquirect` span, clamped at the edges, transparent behind; the media bin's 360° toggle counts it as 360°). MCP `montage_stereo` (sequence 3D/VR180, a file's layout and swapped eyes, a clip's depth keyed or constant), `montage_interpret` `stereo`/`swap_eyes`, `stereo_view` on `montage_render` and `montage_render_frame`. **Tests:** core `stereoInterpretation` (path round trip, eye paths, layout override and "none", invalid layouts ignored, project flags and media packing saved and cleared, effect defaults); render `stereoViews` (each view's pixels and sizes, mismatched eyes, names, packing); media `stereoscopic3d` (a flat side-by-side H.264 interpreted as sbs is 160 wide per eye, an invalid layout refused; left eye red and right blue; Left/Right/Anaglyph/SBS/squeezed TB/Difference program views; a flat sequence shows the left eye; Swapped eyes; a title at depth 10 shifted 8 px each way and not at all in a flat sequence; SBS MP4 and MKV, TB, squeezed TB and anaglyph exports probed back with their packing and size, the SBS export's eyes red and blue again, squeezed SBS shown at full size, difference refused; project flags saved; a VR180 export probed as sbs and vr180, a VR180 picture's view 45° right reading three quarters across and nothing behind; over MCP a sequence made 3D with a file's layout, a keyed depth, out-of-range depth and VR180 without 360° refused, an anaglyph frame, a TB render probed back, difference refused, all saved); app `stereoscopic3dUi` (Interpret Footage's stereo choice and swap applied and shown again, the Stereo and VR180 checkboxes, the Stereo 3D View menu driving the Program monitor's anaglyph and side-by-side pictures and remembered, the Export dialog's 3D choice only for stereo sequences).
- [x] Ambisonic (spatial) audio (Premiere's VR audio, Resolve's Fairlight ambisonics, YouTube spatial audio). core/Ambisonics: first-order AmbiX (W, Y, Z, X in ACN order, SN3D): `foaEncode` (a direction and focus), `foaRotation`/`foaRotate` (the turn that brings where a view looks to the front: undoing its yaw, pitch and roll, the conventions Reframe 360° aims by), `foaDecodeStereo` (cardioids 60° either side) and `FoaBinaural` (a cube of eight virtual speakers decoded with max-rE weighting, each heard at each ear through Brown and Duda's spherical head: the interaural delay as a fractional delay line and the head's shadow as a first-order shelf, 2x towards the ear, 0.1x at 150°). `MediaItem::ambisonic` (the order) from the stream's ambisonic layout, or (as reading AAC replaces that with the decoder's 4.0) from the file's SA3D box (media/SpatialAudio `readSpatialAudioBox`), or the media bin's Ambisonic Audio toggle; saved. media/Decoder `decodeAmbisonic` gives the four channels (a higher order's first-order part; conformed files at their speed) under MediaPool's ambisonic key (`AudioBuffer::channels`), decoded in the background for playback. The mixer reads an ambisonic clip's field sample for sample (speed, reverse and ramps), turns it every 64 samples by its Ambisonics effect (`ambisonics`: yaw, pitch, roll keyframeable; Follow the 360° View, on by default, adds the linked picture clip's Reframe 360° angles; Heard As binaural or stereo) and decodes it to stereo through its own binaural decoder (state kept per clip) before the clip's gain, fades and track; in an ambisonic sequence the turned field goes straight to the mix with the clip's gain and fades and the track's fader and automation. Layout "ambix" (core/Surround): four pseudo-speakers W, Y, Z, X (loudness measured on W), `panGains` encodes a direction (angle, height as elevation, distance as focus), so tracks and buses place sound with the surround panner (no speakers drawn, height shown); folded for listening through cardioids, or binaurally (AudioMixer `setAmbisonicBinaural`, Playback › Ambisonic Monitoring, remembered). Export: AAC as 4.0 in ACN order or PCM as an ambisonic layout; MP4/M4A/MOV get Google's SA3D box afterwards (`writeSpatialAudioBox`: inserted at the end of the first audio sample entry, every enclosing box resized, chunk offsets moved when the movie header comes first, nothing done when one is there); other codecs fall back to a stereo mix; ADM masters refuse an ambisonic sequence. MCP `montage_ambisonics` (mark media, turn a clip with keys, follow the view, decode), `ambix` in `montage_set_surround` and new sequences. **Tests:** core `ambisonicsMaths` (encoding left/up/all round; turns by yaw, pitch, roll and yaw-then-pitch; cardioids; a binaural click from the left reaching the left ear 15 to 40 samples sooner and with over twice the energy, the ears equal from the front; the layout's gains, fold and listing; project round trip; effect defaults); media `ambisonicAudio` (a 3 kHz tone placed left in an ambisonic sequence exported as AAC and 24-bit WAV: the M4A carries SA3D and probes as ambisonic, both decode with Y as strong as W and in step, X and Z near silent; codecs without ambisonics give stereo; the field in a stereo sequence heard left binaurally and as stereo, in front turned 90° left, on the right turned round, in front following a linked Reframe 360° view and on the left again not following; in an ambisonic sequence the turned field kept with X as strong as W; listening to the field binaurally and as stereo from the left; SA3D added after FFmpeg's layout and a fast-start remux with the sound decoding identically, added once only, refused for a WAV; over MCP marking, keyed turns, follow and decode, errors, the ambix layout, saved); app `ambisonicUi` (a four-channel WAV marked ambisonic in the bin with undo, the effect listed, the Sequence Settings layout, the monitoring choice driving the Program controller and remembered).
  - Review fixes: a read-through found eleven issues, all fixed with tests: bleeps were skipped on ambisonic clips (now every channel is covered, the tone in W); noise reduction, voice isolation and the other whole-source cleanups now work on a field (W cleaned, and the change it went through, frequency by frequency, made to Y, Z and X, so directions hold; Pitch Shift works on the channels in pairs); Maintain Audio Pitch and pitch-keeping conforms stretch the four channels together (WSOLA cuts every channel at the same places, guided by W); DCP and IMF masters wrote W and Y as left and right (now the field through the cardioids); a failed SA3D write deleted the finished export (now a warning, and with the movie header last the new one is appended and the old one turned into free space, so nothing is copied and a full disk leaves the file as it was); SA3D goes on every four-channel audio track (extra streams too), never on mono tracks, and is read from whichever track has it; in an ambisonic mix the field was read as far ahead as a track's plugin latency and skipped buses (now read at the master's position, through a bus's mute and fader, and the fader automation moves it there); an ambisonic sequence nested in another kept no field (now mixed as its field, and decoded per clip in a stereo or surround parent); a fresh mixer (exports, stems, DCP) folded binaurally (now through the cardioids, as documented; playback and scrubbing follow the monitoring choice); AAC exports of a field turn off mid/side, intensity stereo and noise substitution and get the bitrate for four channels; a stereo track's two channels go into the field 3 dB down at any width (W kept the headroom of a front pair); the clip's turn and the view's are composed as rotations (the clip's in the scene first) rather than added as angles; relinking a field to a file with fewer than four channels makes it ordinary sound, and such media never goes quiet. **Tests:** media `ambisonicReviewFixes` (bleeps in the field and heard as stereo; a bus's fader and mute; nested fields kept and decoded; the cardioid fold of a fresh mixer; kept pitch at double speed and in a conform; denoise keeping W and Y alike; relink and too few channels; rotation composition) and `ambisonicAudio` (SA3D appended after a last movie header and inserted before media data, once only, nothing on stereo tracks, read-only files refused untouched); core `ambisonicsMaths` (the field's −3 dB per channel).
- [x] 3D titles (Final Cut's 3D titles, Resolve's Fusion 3D text): render/Title3D `renderTitle3D`, the "title3d" generator. The text's outline (QPainterPath glyphs, flattened, centred) is extruded `depth` pixels and turned (tilt X, turn Y, roll Z), scaled and placed, then seen through a perspective camera whose lens (`fov`) keeps the face at its set size when unturned. A small z-buffered rasteriser at 2x2 samples per pixel: the front and back faces fill the projected outline by nonzero winding (holes kept), each sample's depth from its ray meeting the face's plane (no triangulation), faces turned away skipped; the sides are two triangles per outline edge, normals pointing out of the letter (checked per contour against the path's winding), smoothed across bends under 35° and sharp at corners, lit per corner. Lambert light from a direction and height with ambient and Blinn shine; face and side colours; opacity; animation in and out (fade, spin, flip up, swing in, zoom from far). MCP `montage_add_title` template `3d` with `depth` and `spin_in`. **Tests:** render `titles3D` (face on: the face colour where the letters are, nothing around; an O's hole open; turned 60° a thin title half as wide and a deep one showing its left side; from behind the back face the same size; flat and edge on next to nothing; lit from the camera fully, from above only the ambient; flipping in from edge on to settled, faded out at the end; the same at a quarter size; the same picture every time); media MCP (the 3d template with depth and spin, its options refused on flat titles and roll on it).
  - Review fixes: a read-through found seven issues, all fixed with tests: nothing was clipped at the near plane, so a face with any point behind the camera vanished whole (a wide lens with a deep title drew nothing; Swing In lost its face at the start); outlines and sides are now clipped against a near plane at a twentieth of the focal length, so what is in front is drawn. The face sat half the depth in front of the frame's plane, so depth magnified it (41% at 1000 px); the extrusion now goes back from the face, which stays at its set size. Every frame allocated a full supersampled float frame (530 MB at 4K): the raster covers only where the title lands, colour is 16-bit, and frames past 4K are not supersampled; an allocation that still fails leaves the title out rather than ending the render. Which way each contour faced was judged from a point beside it, wrong where letters overlap (tight tracking turned a dash inside out); overlaps are merged first (QPainterPath::simplified), outlines turned anticlockwise and holes clockwise by nesting, which also drops the per-contour hit test that grew with the square of the text. Face rows only test the edges that reach their band. MCP refuses wrongly typed depth, size and spin_in, clamps size to 4-1000 and reports a 3D title's text; the timeline, Sequence Index and Move Above Titles treat 3D titles as titles.
- [x] HDR10+ dynamic metadata (Resolve Studio's HDR10+, SMPTE ST 2094-40 application 4; facts and sources in `docs/research/notes-hdr10plus.md`): render/Hdr10Plus. `Hdr10PlusMeter` measures PQ frames in linear light through a 16-bit code table: per scene the brightest R, G and B (maxscl), the mean of each pixel's brightest channel (average_maxrgb) and that channel's 1, 25, 50, 75, 90, 95 and 99.98 % points from a 65 536-bin histogram. Scenes start at the cut's edits (`hdr10PlusCuts`, every video clip's start and end) and where over half the picture moves to other brightnesses at once inside a clip (a 32-bin spread compared frame to frame, at least half a second in). `analyseHdr10Plus` renders a range as exports do (sequence space converted to the PQ output) and keys each scene with a hash of its frames' render-cache keys; `storedHdr10Plus` gives the sequence's scenes for a range only while every scene there still matches, so a regrade or re-cut is measured again. Sequence::hdr10Plus is saved with the project. `hdr10PlusT35` writes profile A (version 1, one window, no targeted display, nine percentiles with the standard's fixed 0 and 0.00255 at 5 and 10 %, fraction_bright_pixels 0 = not calculated, no tone mapping curve): 49 bytes. `addHdr10PlusSei` puts it in an HEVC access unit as an escaped prefix SEI before the first slice (start codes or hvcC lengths); `addHdr10PlusObu` puts it in an AV1 temporal unit as a metadata OBU before the shown (last) frame. ExportSettings::hdr10Plus: PQ exports measure first unless the stored analysis fits (the first 30 % of the progress), the exporter's packet hook adds the scene's message to every HEVC and AV1 frame by its pts (no FFmpeg encoder writes HDR10+ from side data, x265 only from a file with a build option distributions leave off), and `name.hdr10plus.json` is written beside the file in hdr10plus_tool's format (one SceneInfo entry per frame, raw values), which `readHdr10PlusJson` reads back. Sequence › Analyse HDR Light Levels measures the scenes in the same pass for PQ sequences (one undo step, replacing scenes it overlaps); the Export dialog's HDR10+ option is offered for PQ output. MCP `montage_measure_hdr` returns and keeps the scenes; `montage_render` `hdr10plus`. **Tests:** media `hdr10PlusMetadata` (the message read back by FFmpeg's own parser field by field; SEI insertion after a VPS and before an IDR slice with start codes, and before a TRAIL slice with 4-byte lengths, escaped (an all-zero scene), refused without a slice; the OBU before the last of two frames; the meter's per-channel maxima, average and percentiles for half 100-nit grey and half 1000-nit red, a cut within a clip and an edit each starting a scene; a three-shot PQ cut exported to HEVC with 30 HDR10+ SEIs and every decoded frame carrying its scene's maxscl, the JSON beside it with three scenes; the stored analysis used whole and for a part, kept through save and load, refused for the regraded shot's scene only, and re-measured on export; SDR refused; AV1 through SVT-AV1 with 30 metadata OBUs, every frame read back by dav1d); `mcpMeasureHdr` (scenes returned and kept; `montage_render` `hdr10plus` writing the JSON); app `hdrScopesAndLightLevels` (the analysis keeping one scene and undoing it; the Export dialog's option enabled for PQ output only).
  - Review fixes: a read-through found eleven issues, all fixed with tests: a JSON that could not be written beside a finished export failed it, and the failed export's cleanup deleted the video (now a warning; the video stays). One changed scene sent the whole range back to be measured: `hdr10PlusForRange` keeps every stored scene that still matches and measures only the stretches between them (a regraded shot alone). Analysing part of a sequence again dropped the stored scenes it cut into, leaving gaps: the stretch now grows to whole stored scenes (`widenHdr10PlusRange`) and `mergeHdr10PlusScenes` replaces only what it covers, light levels still measured over the stretch asked for. The analysis measured the bare picture while the export delivered burn-ins, burned captions or packed stereo eyes: exports now measure the frames as delivered (one `deliveredFrame` for both) and use stored scenes only without those. The meter runs across rows in parallel with per-band histograms and no libm rounding per pixel. Analysis now runs after the encoder opens, so a missing encoder fails first. The SEI carries its access unit's TemporalId. Hidden tracks and disabled clips no longer cut scenes. An image sequence's JSON is named without its frame number. MCP says where HDR10+ went (`video`, `json` or `none`); the codec-free checks no longer skip with the encoder, the AV1 part says when it cannot run, and an export reusing a stored (marked) scene is tested.
- [x] Control surfaces (Mackie Control, as Premiere, Resolve, Pro Tools, Logic, Cubase and Ardour speak it; protocol facts cross-checked against Ardour's, Reaper's and Tracktion's MCU code and a reverse-engineered MCU Pro capture, since Mackie never published one): control/Midi lists the system's MIDI ports and opens an input and output with no MIDI library: CoreMIDI on macOS, WinMM on Windows, and ALSA's raw MIDI devices (`/dev/snd/midiC*D*`, named from `/proc/asound`) read on a thread on Linux; `MidiParser` splits streams into messages (running status, system exclusive, real-time bytes inside other messages). control/MackieControl decodes fader pitch bend (eight strips and the master), touch notes (0x68-0x70, pressed above 0x40), V-Pot and jog relative steps (a zero count is a step), and button notes, and encodes motor faders, LEDs (on, flash, off), V-Pot rings (single dot for pan), scribble-strip system exclusive (ASCII only), meters (12 at 0 dB, 13 over), meter modes, the ten-digit 3-2-2-3 timecode display (0x40 the rightmost) and the assignment display; the fader law puts 0 dB three quarters up and +12 dB at the top, reading 0x3F70 and above (a ten-bit fader's top) as the top. app/ControlSurface maps the surface onto the mixer's own widgets, so everything the mixer does follows: a touched fader is held as a mouse holds it (Touch and Latch write while held; a fader without touch sensing is held until it rests a second), each received position is echoed (the servo needs it), and a fader under a hand is never moved; motor faders, LEDs, rings, names, levels, transport and automation lights and the timecode are sent as they change, about 30 times a second; meters are resent while they hold. On connecting it sends the device query and meter modes and shows everything; a surface announcing itself later (message 0x01) is shown everything again, addressed by its own device id; disconnecting puts faders down, lights out and displays blank. Edit › Control Surface… picks the MIDI input and output and whether to reconnect at start-up. **Tests:** core `midiAndMackieControl` (parser cases; every decoded and encoded message checked byte for byte; the fader law both ways); app `controlSurfaceMackie` (through a recording stand-in for the connection: the device query and full picture on connecting; a touched fader setting a track's volume as one undo step with its position echoed; the motor following the mixer; a touchless fader held until it rests; the master fader; mute and solo with their LEDs; V-Pot pan, its ring and strip text, push to centre; banking by eight and by one; a Touch pass written from the surface's fader; jog, marker, undo, play and stop with their lights; meters; a surface switching on later; a clean disconnect; the dialog).
  - Review fixes: a read-through found sixteen issues, all fixed with tests: each fader kept its own undo step, so moving two at once made dozens (moves from the surface are now one undo step together), and every queued move was applied as an edit (a fader's moves that waited together are applied once, at the last position); opening a Linux port could block, and a full port blocked the GUI (opened non-blocking, a full port waited on 20 ms at most, then the rest dropped); Windows took no system exclusive in, so a surface announcing itself was never answered (four input buffers, handed back as read), and sent on the GUI thread (now a send thread, with a stuck port reset); macOS made a CoreMIDI client per connection (one, shared); an unplugged surface left its faders held and its connection open (all three back-ends now report it once, and holds are released and the surface closed; a report from a connection already replaced is ignored); banking while a fader was touched dropped the touch (the fader now holds its new track and its motor stays still); the selected track is followed by id; a V-Pot turn and push hold the pan apart from the fader; Play while shuttling plays at normal speed; Cycle no longer toggles a loop Montage does not have; the level line is sent at most ten times a second per strip; the timecode lights dots only after groups that are there; closing Montage clears the surface; the dialog picks the output named like the input. **Tests:** core `midiAndMackieControl` (no dots on a blank display, one after the seconds of "12:34"); app `controlSurfaceMackie` (two faders moving as one undo step; queued moves applied once; a bank change under a touch; play from shuttling; a lost surface released and closed, a stale report ignored).
- [x] Compound and multicam clips in interchange (Final Cut's compound and multicam clips, Premiere's nested sequences; until now FCPXML import reported both and left them out, and every export wrote a nested clip as a file with no path). core/FcpXml: the FCPXML writer puts each nested sequence among the resources as `<media>` (innermost first, each format once): a compound clip's `<sequence>` with its own spine, or a multicam's `<multicam>` with an `<mc-angle>` per video track (carrying its own sound where an audio track holds the same source) and per sound-only source; clips are `ref-clip` (`srcEnable` audio or video when only one half is used) and `mc-clip` with `mc-source` for the angle seen and the angle heard (all when the same; Montage's mix of every source becomes the seen angle's sound). The reader builds each `<media>` once into a nested sequence and its media item (multicam angles in order: picture to video tracks named after the angle, sound to audio tracks; random angle ids and an hour's timecode start handled), places `ref-clip` and `mc-clip` with their halves linked, finds the project's sequence even with compound sequences written before it, and no longer adds a file twice when a nested sequence and the cut both use it. OpenTimelineIO writes a compound clip as a Stack of the nested sequence's tracks of that kind (picture in a video track, sound in an audio track) with Montage's metadata naming the sequence, and reads Stacks back into one nested sequence however many show it; FCP 7 XML writes the nested sequence inside the clip item where first used and by id afterwards (Premiere's form) and reads it back. EDL, OTIO and FCP 7 XML have no multicam clips: `flattenedMulticam` puts the angles' own clips in their place, picture and sound linked, a whole-mix clip taking the seen angle's sound. Checked with OpenTimelineIO 0.18 and its FCP 7 and FCPXML adapters (which read the Stacks, the nested sequence and the compound clip; the FCPXML adapter skips multicam clips). **Tests:** core `interchangeCompoundAndMulticam` (a cut of a multicam clip cut from angle A to B with sound following, then a compound clip of linked b-roll and a title: FCPXML elements written; read into an empty project as the same cut, one multicam used twice with its angles, synced starts and audio tracks, angles seen and heard, source points, links, the compound clip's sequence, each file once; a Final Cut-style file with random angle ids, an hour's timecode start, picture from one angle and sound from another and a connected sound-only compound clip; the flattened cut; OTIO and FCP 7 XML naming the cameras, not the multicam, and reading back with one nested sequence for the compound clip's picture and sound).
  - Review fixes: a read-through found ten issues, all fixed with tests: OTIO import doubled a compound clip's tracks for every extra use (a clip cut in two came back with its sound twice); now the picture comes from the first Stack with picture and the sound from the first with sound. Flattening for EDL, OTIO and FCP 7 XML lost the sound when a track was locked (locks are ignored on the copy, and a failure returns it unflattened) and dropped dissolves at or between multicam clips (put back between the pieces that replace them). FCPXML wrote a retimed multicam clip's mc-source before its timeMap, against the DTD. A multicam clip at another speed, which stays one, was nested whole in OTIO and FCP 7 XML (every angle stacked); it now nests only the angle it shows (`multicamPart`). Reading Final Cut multicams: sound connected inside an angle (a recorder beside the camera) gets a track of its own instead of landing on fixed tracks over other angles; dissolves inside later angles stay on their track; a picture-only angle chosen for sound gives silence, not every source's mix; an unknown angle id is reported and falls back to the first angle. An OTIO Stack without a range (OTIO's default) now shows its whole timeline. FCPXML's sound for a mix not carried with its picture (a J-cut) is the angle its picture shows. **Tests:** core `interchangeReviewFixes` (dissolves kept and a locked track flattened; timeMap before mc-source; OTIO and FCP 7 XML nesting one angle; the J-cut's angle; a compound clip cut in two read back once; a rangeless Stack; a Final Cut file with a connected recorder, a dissolve in the second angle, a GoPro angle chosen for sound and an unknown angle id).
- [x] Sidechain inputs for hosted plugins (Pro Tools', Logic's and Fairlight's key input on plugins): audio/Plugins `Instance::hasSidechain` and `setSidechain` (the key for the next process call, or silence). CLAP: a second input port is the key (mono or stereo, a stereo key to a mono port as its mid, a mono key to both sides), passed as a second audio input buffer. VST3: the first auxiliary input bus is activated as the key and filled each block (zeros when none). LV2: audio inputs with lv2:isSideChain are taken out of the signal inputs and fed the key. `makePluginEffect` marks plugins with a key input (`key_input`); the Inspector then shows the Sidechain track choice (as the Compressor's), and the mixer feeds the chosen track's key signal (before its fader, muted or not, never the effect's own track) on clips and track inserts alike. **Tests:** plugins `pluginSidechain` (a CLAP ducker hearing a stereo key, a mono key on both sides, silence without one, across blocks; the gain without a key input; effects marked or not; in the mixer on a track insert and on a clip, keyed by a muted track, ignoring its own track), `vst3Sidechain` (the auxiliary bus activated and fed, silence after) and the LV2 test (side-chain ports as the key, not signal); app `sidechainInInspector` (the ducker's Sidechain choice shown, the gain's not, choosing a track sets it).
  - Review fixes: a read-through found six issues, all fixed with tests: a VST3 plugin's auxiliary bus was switched on whether or not a key was chosen, so plugins that follow it listened to silence (now on only while a key is chosen, the component briefly deactivated to change it, as the spec requires); Duplicate Sequence, Import from Project and re-conform left a plugin's key on the original's track (now retargeted like the Compressor's); keys were out of step through latency, a keyed plugin on a clip hearing its key late by the clip chain's latency and one after a latent plugin early by it (now each effect hears the key from where its input is: the chain's input less the latency of the plugins before it, and keys are cached by position); CLAP plugins with more ports than main and key were given too few buffers (now every declared port gets its own, silent or scratch); the key's channel pointers went out of scope before the plugin read them; and the Sidechain row also shows for effects saved without the mark once the plugin is known to have a key input. **Tests:** plugins `vst3Sidechain` (the bus off until enabled, on, then off again) and `pluginSidechain` (a duplicate keyed from its own track; known key input; the key's onset ducking at the same sample with the keyed plugin before a 64-sample-latency plugin on a clip and after one on a track, which fails by 64 samples without the fix).
- [x] Pan to Follow (Resolve 19's IntelliTrack panning): render/ClipAnalysis `panFollowSource` (the picture a sound plays with: a linked video clip on screen, else the topmost), `panFollowSubject` (the shot's subject at a frame, from media/Reframe, as the starting point), `trackPanFollow` (a square around a point of the sequence frame mapped into the footage's own frame, tracked with the region tracker both ways from a frame to wherever the sound or the shot ends first, mapped back through the clip's transform: one key per sequence frame) and `applyPanFollow` (a five-frame moving average, then the pan lane in stereo, or the surround x lane in surround and ambisonic layouts with the picture's edges `width` of the way to ±30°; a 360° sequence's picture is the whole circle, x and y lanes from the azimuth so the sound goes behind; the span's keys replaced, thinned to 0.005, keys just outside keep each lane as it was, Off becomes Read). app/PanFollowDialog (Clip › Pan to Follow…): the program frame at the playhead (or the clip's middle) with the point and its square, click or drag to move it, Small/Medium/Large area, the picture's width on the stage; tracked on a copy of the project in the background with progress and Stop, then written as one undo step. MCP `montage_pan_follow` (clip, at, x/y or the subject, size, width; reports the lane and a sampled path). **Tests:** media `panFollowsThePicture` (a textured card crossing a flat frame: the subject found on it, followed both ways from the middle on every frame within 1.5 px with progress reported; the stereo pan crossing from -0.69 to +0.69 within 0.03, thinned, Off made Read, the lane outside the span as it was and a later key kept; half the stage half the pan; 5.1 writing only x, scaled to the front speakers; a 360° sequence writing x and y with the azimuth behind the listener; a linked picture preferred to the topmost; no picture an error; over MCP errors, the subject by default, the pan written and saved); app `panToFollowFromTheClipMenu` (the menu action, the dialog starting on the card at the playhead, a click moving the point, tracking writing the pan as one undo step that undoes and redoes, half the width halving it, a sequence switch closing it).
  - Review fixes: a read-through found ten issues, all fixed with tests: the path was written at the frames tracked even if the clips had moved meanwhile (the dialog notes where the sound and the picture were and asks to track again if either moved, and the frame it starts at and its picture follow edits); a result arriving mid-drag or mid-fader-pass committed the gesture half-way (now it waits for the gesture to end); in surround, a track going to a bus got surround lanes the mixer never uses (now its pan, as the mixer pans it); a Write-mode track was left not hearing the path, and an Off track's dormant lanes came back to life (now both are set to Read with the lanes written replaced and the fader's value kept outside the span); a result could land in a project opened meanwhile, or after closing (now dropped); flat surround wrote only x, so the angle was only right at the front edge (now x and y, the direction at the distance the track was heard at); the picture followed is what is seen at the point clicked (a still or nested sequence there is refused rather than footage hidden beneath being tracked); read-only projects are refused up front; the tracker's own errors (unreadable media) are reported; MCP refuses non-numeric points and fractional frames; the time followed counts frames, not keys. **Tests:** media `panFollowsThePicture` (Read kept, Off and Write replaced with the fader's pan outside, a later path held before by a key, surround direction and distance at y 1 and 0.4, a bused track panned in stereo, a still over part of the picture refused there and the video beside it followed, MCP argument errors); app `panToFollowFromTheClipMenu` (a clip moved during tracking leaves the lanes untouched and asks again, a result during a gesture waits for it, a project switch writes nothing).
  - Review fixes: unsaved changes are never thrown away when the project goes read-only (taken over while this machine slept, or recovered while someone else holds it): it is not reloaded over them, Edit Project asks before discarding them, and closing offers Save As; renewing the lock tells a lock that was taken over (readable, naming someone else) from a drive briefly away (unreadable: kept) or a lock file gone missing (written again); a stale lock is taken over by moving it aside under a name of the process's own, so of two editors taking it over at once only one wins (a fresh lock moved by mistake is put back); each running Montage writes a random token so its own lock is known even after the machine's name changes; heartbeats from the future are not believed; the same file reached by another path (a link, a mapped drive) keeps its lock; In and Out marks on a read-only project do not count as changes; watch folders wait while read-only; the live agent link and MCP undo refuse a project someone else is editing; the Production panel no longer rescans the production after every edit; Import from Project carries audition takes, gives colour groups new ids (Duplicate Sequence too) and leaves faces to be named again. **Tests:** core: the future heartbeat, refresh held/unknown/lost and the missing lock rewritten, the token after a host rename, takes, colour groups and faces on import; app: a network blip keeping the lock, a takeover with unsaved changes neither reloaded over nor discarded unless asked, marks not counted; MCP undo refused.

## Next: from the October 2026 gap research

Ranked by what the market leaders have that Montage lacks, weighed against the work:

- [x] Out-of-sync indicators with Move/Slip into Sync (above).
- [x] Checksum-verified card offload with ASC MHL (above).
- [x] Sidechain keying for the Compressor and Noise Gate (above), and plugins' key inputs (CLAP, VST3, LV2; Audio Units still to come).
- [x] AAF import (Media Composer and Pro Tools sequences in) and picture tracks in AAF export (above).
- [x] Shared projects: project locking, read-only opening, productions and import between projects (above). Per-bin locking inside one project is not planned: projects are small and a production splits work by project, as Premiere's Productions do.
- [x] Stereoscopic 3D: side-by-side and top-and-bottom footage, stereo sequences, depth, anaglyph and packed views, stereo exports, VR180 (above).
- [x] Ambisonic (spatial) audio (above).
- [x] Pan to Follow: object-following audio panning (above).
- [x] Spatial video: MV-HEVC (Apple spatial video) in, Phase 6 #15, and out, Phase 8 #15. (Higher-order ambisonics: Phase 6 #14.)
- [x] Extruded 3D titles (above).
- [x] HDR10+ dynamic metadata on HDR10 exports (above). Dolby Vision is not planned: its metadata and RPU tools are licensed.
- [x] Control surfaces in Mackie Control mode (above); HUI followed in Phase 4 (#13a). EuCon and Tangent colour panels are still to come.
- Runners-up: AI motion deblur.

# Phase 4

The third gap analysis (`docs/research/phase4-roadmap.md`, October 2026) ranks what is still missing against Premiere 26.5, Resolve 21.1, Final Cut Pro 12 and Media Composer 2025.12. Worked through in order:

- [x] 1. ProRes RAW with RAW controls, and current camera logs (Apple Log 2, D-Log2, GoPro Log, L-Log) (M).
  - [x] The logs (facts in `docs/research/notes-camera-logs-prores-raw.md`): Apple Log 2 is Apple Log's curve over Apple Gamut (Apple Wide Gamut, xy from ACES' `CSC.Apple.AppleLog2_to_ACES`, Bradford to AP0 matching OCIO's matrix); GoPro GP-Log (base 400, Rec.709) and GP-Log2 (base 600, Rec.2020) by GoPro's own formula, with sensor linear 0.0517 as 18 % grey (GP-Log2's white paper; assumed for GP-Log); Leica L-Log over Rec.2020, with the manual's two cut points. DJI's D-Log M and D-Log2 have no published formula and are not guessed. **Tests:** render `cameraLogSpaces` (code values at six exposures against colour-science and GoPro's formula, round trips, the Apple Gamut matrix against ACES', GoPro and Leica grey reading as grey).
  - [x] ProRes RAW (FFmpeg 9's decoder: linear Bayer with white balance, camera matrix and gain as frame side data). media/ProResRaw develops each frame the way FFmpeg's own notes give the pipeline: the sensor's range (black 0x100, white from the header) to 0–1, the as-shot white balance on the mosaic, sites past the sensor's white clipped (white, not magenta, highlights), a bilinear demosaic (or each 2x2 cell one pixel), the camera-to-XYZ D65 matrix, the gain, then XYZ to AP1 (Bradford D65 to ACES white) encoded as 16-bit ACEScct, so the scene's range up to 222 times grey's white fits and grading starts from scene light. The decoder opens ProRes RAW in software with FFmpeg's cropping off and cuts the recommended crop after (on whole 2x2 cells, as FFmpeg's aligned crop would shift the colour pattern); the media's size is the cropped one (4096x2160 from a 4112x2176 Atomos mosaic) and its colour space is ACEScct. Shown at half size or less it develops at half size (2x2 cells straight from the mosaic). A 4K frame takes about 80 ms at full size and 9 ms at half on four cores (ACEScct from a 30,720-entry table on the float's exponent and top mantissa bits, within 1.5 of 65535, instead of a log per channel). Interpret Footage's RAW controls apply: exposure, white balance as the light's temperature (Bradford from the as-shot light, or D65 when the file does not say) and tint, half size; highlight recovery is LibRaw's and does not apply. Builds with an older FFmpeg leave ProRes RAW as it was (no colour parameters to develop with). Checked against FFmpeg master's decoder on the first frame of FFmpeg's ProRes RAW sample (ticket 7887): natural skin and neutral greys. **Tests:** media `proResRawDevelop` (grey stays grey through the matrices at full and half size, a colour through Rec. 709 to AP1, as-shot white balance, clipped sites white, exposure and gain, 200x headroom, black, temperature and tint directions, the crop on whole cells, RAW controls offered), `proResRawDecode` (probe size and ACEScct, a developed frame neutral at full and half size, exposure through Interpret Footage; run in the macOS packaging job, which fetches the sample's first frame).
  - Review fixes: a read-through found seven issues, all fixed with tests: FFmpeg 9.0's ProRes RAW decoder claims frame threading without finishing its setup, so each seek waited for eight serial decodes and tiles never ran in parallel (now slice-threaded), and probing decoded a frame (now the first frame's header is read: 6 ms instead of 174); a chosen temperature or tint was applied after the clip, so blown highlights took its cast (the balance is now multipliers on the mosaic, scaled so the smallest is 1, before the clip, as LibRaw does for stills); the frame's one-pixel border took the wrong colour (the demosaic now reads the real sites past the crop and mirrors only at the mosaic's edge) and odd crops were off by a pixel (cut exactly at full size); LibRaw's highlight modes were accepted for ProRes RAW and silently cleared a proxy, and a ProRes RAW item first in a selection reset the others' modes (now left out for ProRes RAW, each item keeps its own); projects saved by a build that read ProRes RAW differently are probed again on opening; a failed sample fetch in CI could be cached for good. Bright saturated colours still clip where the first channel saturates after balance, as LibRaw's Clip mode does.
- [x] 2. HDR viewer on HDR and EDR displays (M). render/HdrView turns an HDR sequence's frames into the light they ask for: linear Rec. 709 primaries relative to SDR white (BT.2408: PQ's 203 nits and HLG's 75 % on its 1000-nit reference display are 1.0), negative outside Rec. 709 (extended-range surfaces show wider gamuts), with a hue-keeping roll-off of the brightest channel above 75 % of the display's headroom only when the master can be brighter than the display; the playback worker renders it as half floats beside the SDR picture while the viewer can show it (none for SDR sequences; a rendered preview has only its SDR picture, so HDR renders live). app/HdrSurface is a native window over the viewer drawn with Qt's RHI into an extended-linear swap chain (Metal on macOS EDR, Direct3D 11 scRGB on Windows with HDR on), from Qt 6.7 (its HDR swap chain information; the private RHI module is found by name from 6.9). It draws the picture with the roll-off in the shader at the display's live headroom (macOS: `maxColorComponentValue`, 1.0 at SDR white; Windows: peak nits over the user's SDR white level, scaled by SDR white / 80 for scRGB), and over it the viewer's own drawing (guides, handles, labels, compare, the exposure check, the background) painted into a texture at SDR white, uploaded only when it changes; it passes every mouse, wheel and drag-and-drop event on to the viewer and never takes keyboard focus, so shortcuts keep working. Shaders are baked by `scripts/bake-hdr-shaders.sh` (qsb, Qt 6.5's format: SPIR-V, GLSL ES 100/120/150, HLSL 5.0, MSL 1.2) into an include, so building needs no shader tools. Playback › HDR Viewer (on by default) and an HDR badge on the monitor; on an SDR display, Linux or an older Qt the monitors tone map to SDR as before and say why once. **Tests:** render `hdrViewForHdrDisplays` (PQ and HLG reference white, highlights within and beyond the headroom, hue kept, wide gamut negative, SDR, half-float frames and scRGB scale), app `hdrViewerOnHdrSequences` (the controller's HDR light for a PQ sequence and none for SDR, the fallback, and with a stand-in HDR display on Qt's Null RHI: surface shown with the badge, the picture's area clear in the overlay, guides reaching it, input reaching the viewer, two-up and SDR taking it away).
  - Review fixes: a read-through found five issues, fixed with tests where a test can reach them. The display was asked before the surface was placed in the viewer, so a Mac asked about its main screen and Windows about the display at desktop (0,0): the surface is now set on the viewer's screen and position first, Windows asks DXGI about the viewer's own monitor (`MonitorFromWindow`, `IDXGIOutput6`, the SDR white level from the display configuration) for HDR, headroom and SDR white, and an SDR display under an HDR main one is refused instead of clipping (Qt makes the swap chain for the main display, so HDR on a secondary needs the main one in HDR too); a display that said no is asked again when the window moves to another screen. The headroom was read only when frames were drawn, so a paused frame kept the first frame's 1.0 roll-off (macOS reports it once a frame is up) or a stale one after a brightness change: it is read again straight after the first frame and every second while shown, and a display that stops showing HDR hands back to the SDR picture. A lost GPU device (driver reset) left the surface frozen over the viewer, retrying every frame: it now hands back to SDR with the reason. Turned off and on, the viewer showed the last HDR picture (another frame by then); hidden and shown, it kept a stale one: both refreshed. Playback: the half-float cache held two thirds more bytes than meant (a third as many frames now), and trimming could drop the frame just rendered ahead, rendering it again each tick (frames passed go first, and no more are rendered ahead than fit); the viewer's drawing was repainted and compared 30 times a second, paused too (now sent when the viewer repaints, checked four times a second, and not on each new SDR frame it covers); the picture was copied on the GUI thread before upload (uploaded in place); the surface took keyboard focus on a click. Rendered previews (Render In to Out) hold SDR pictures, so HDR viewing still renders frames live. **Tests:** app `hdrViewerOnHdrSequences` (off and on: no stale picture, the next frame brings it back; the stand-in display going away: back to SDR within the second, with the reason). A second read-through of those fixes found four more, fixed: overlays that move with playback (ADR streamers and punches, handles on animated clips) fell to four updates a second under HDR (they are now sent with each new picture, when they changed); Windows asked about the monitor most of the main window was on rather than the viewer's (now the surface's own window); a device lost while remaking the swap chain or at the end of a frame left the surface frozen (now handed back to SDR too); an SDR white level that could not be read (the displays changing between two calls) fell to 80 nits (retried, then Qt's reading).
- [x] 3. Stacked timelines and editing from a sequence (S–M). core/EditOps copies part of a sequence and places it in another: `copySequenceRange` cuts a copy of the source at In and Out (`razorAll`, the pieces' ids above every id in it) and keeps the clips between, timed from In, with the transitions wholly inside; `copySequenceClips` takes chosen clips with their links; `pasteRange` puts either on the tracks from a chosen video and audio track up (adding tracks where needed, the lowest of each kind on the chosen one), overwriting or inserting (rippling the tracks it lands on), with new clip, link group, effect and transition ids; `placeSequenceClips` does both for a sequence at the same frame rate, refusing one that contains the target (`sequenceUses`, nested at any depth) or an empty range. Clip › Insert Sequences as Clips (Premiere's "insert and overwrite sequences as nests or individual clips", saved): Insert, Overwrite, Append and Smart Insert from a sequence in the Source monitor, and sequences dropped from the bin, bring its own clips between the marks instead of one nested clip; a sequence at another rate still goes in nested, with a message. The bin offers Open in Source Monitor and Open in Stacked Timeline for sequences. The Stacked Timeline panel (Premiere's stacked timelines, Resolve's stacked timeline tabs) shows another sequence read-only under a pick list (every sequence but the open one): click clips to select them (Shift/Ctrl adds), click the ruler for its own playhead, I and O mark a range and X clears it (taken from the main shortcuts while it has focus), Ctrl+wheel zooms; drag the selection, or the marked range from an empty part of the tracks, onto the timeline, where it shows as ghosts and snaps by its earliest clip (Ctrl inserts); Swap edits the stacked sequence and stacks the open one. **Tests:** core `editingFromASequence` (a range cut at both ends keeps the clips' source offsets, links and a transition inside it and drops one crossing the edge; inserting ripples; landing on V2/A2 adds tracks; chosen clips keep their spacing; self, a nesting cycle, another rate and an empty range refused; a source whose clip ids collide with fresh ones copied correctly). App `editingFromAnotherSequence` (a source edit from a reel nested with the option off and as its two cut pieces with it on, the reel untouched; the bin opening it stacked; two clips dragged at frame 100 keeping their spacing and selected; a range marked with I and O on the stacked timeline inserted with Ctrl into a 200-frame clip; Swap; a cut that nests the reel refused going back into it; the open sequence never shown in the stack).
  - Review fixes: a read-through found six issues, all fixed: after a source edit as clips the playhead went to the end of the first piece, inside the material (now after all of it, so the next Insert follows on); a J- or L-cut cut at In lost its link (the copy is now cut track by track, each piece keeping its clip's group); a range starting with a gap snapped by its first clip, so an insert split the clip ending at the snap point (a range now snaps by its start); an Out on the end frame made the range a frame long, leaving a frame gap on rippled tracks (clamped to the end); the stacked panel kept its zoom and scroll for another sequence (reset); the bin's sequence actions held a pointer into the media list across the menu (they hold the id). **Tests:** core (a J-cut kept linked, an Out past the end clamped), app (the playhead after an as-clips edit).
- [x] 4. Aux sends and full mixer automation (M). Model: `Send` (bus, level, pre/post fader, mute, a level lane) on audio tracks; a mute lane on tracks (a switch: each key holds to the next, muted at 0.5 or more); volume, pan and mute lanes and an automation mode on buses. core/Automation reads them (`laneMuted`, `trackMutedAt`, `sendLevelAt`, `busVolumeAt`, `busPanAt`, `busMutedAt`); core/EditOps `setSend` (one per bus), `removeSend`, `removeBus` (its routed tracks back to the master, its sends dropped); saved in the project. The mixer (render/Compositor): a track's mute lane gates it (and its sends) over 64 samples; pre-fader sends take it after the inserts and mute, post-fader sends what leaves the fader and pan (in surround, before the speaker panner), each at its own level with its lane gliding as the fader's; a bus reading its lanes rides its mute and fader every 64 samples and steps its pan. Sends to a bus with another plugin delay than the track's output are not realigned (fine for reverbs and delays); exports, stems (a track's stem carries its sends' share of each return) and nested sequences hear it all. Audio Mixer: send rows on each track strip (bus name, level slider, right-click Pre-Fader / Mute Send / Remove Send) and "+ Send" (an existing bus or a new effects return); bus strips gain an automation mode and a pan; in Write, Latch or Touch a pass also records each track's mute and send levels and each bus's fader, pan and mute (mutes are switches: Touch writes them from the first press to the stop, as Latch), one undo step with the fader lanes, Write handing over to Touch; stopped, mute buttons, send sliders and bus controls follow their lanes and moving a bus fader or send slider on a lane keys it at the playhead. MCP `montage_automate_track` sets mute points, sends (by bus name, making a return that is not there yet) with their levels, pre/post, mute and level points, and with `bus` a bus's mode, volume, pan and mute. **Tests:** media `auxSendsAndMixerAutomation` (post-fader send at -6 dB adding to the mix and following the fader; pre-fader at its own level with the fader down and ignoring pan; a muted send, a muted or turned-down bus; a send level lane; Off ignoring lanes; a track mute lane silencing it and its send between frames 15 and 30; bus volume, mute and pan lanes and Off; saved and loaded; an export; removing the bus); MCP (sends making a return by name, a mute lane held, a bus's Touch mode and lanes, an unknown bus refused, a send removed); app `mixerSendsAndBusAutomation` (New Effects Return and its row; a level drag as one undo step; no second send to a bus; a Touch pass writing a latched mute from frame 20 and a held send level, one undo step; the mute button following its lane; a bus Write pass handing over to Touch; a bus fader keying its lane when stopped; deleting the bus removing the send row).
  - Review fixes (d4fdfce): (1) Duplicate Sequence, and so re-conform, Auto Reframe copies and production imports, remapped track outputs to the copy's buses but not sends, which went nowhere (or, from another project, to the wrong bus): sends now follow, any to a bus not copied dropped. (2) Close Gaps and re-conform moved the volume, pan and surround lanes but not the mute lane, send levels or bus lanes; they now move all of them (Close Gaps also bus and master effect keys). (3) A track could send to the bus it already feeds, doubling it there: `setSend` refuses, "+ Send" leaves that bus out, routing a track to a bus drops its send there, and the mixer skips such a send. (4) An edit during a pass ended it with the lanes being written wiped: fixed by 4b's handling of interrupted passes. (5) An ADM object's sends went into the object's own channel, and twice when it had an LFE send: the object channel is dry and its sends reach the bed's returns once. (7) The mute button over a muted stretch of a mute lane did nothing (and elsewhere muted the whole track): on a track or bus reading its lane it now switches the lane at the playhead to its next point, the stretch before it as it was. (8) MCP mute points muted from the start of the timeline (a lane's first key reaches back): a switch's points get a key just before the first holding what was there. (9) A Write pass raised a send or bus set below -60 dB (the slider's floor) to -60: the level set stays unless the control is raised.
