Not changed: (6) sends to a bus whose plugins have another latency than the track's output are not realigned (documented). **Tests:** core `sendsAndLanesFollowEdits` (no send to the own output; Close Gaps moving mute, send, bus lanes, bus and master effect keys; a copy's send on its own bus), `reconformToNewCut` (send level, mute and bus fader lanes remapped); media `immersiveMixAndAdmMaster` (an object's send heard in the bed once, with and without an LFE send, the object dry), MCP (mute and choice points keep what was before them); app `mixerSendsAndBusAutomation` (the mute button switching its lane after and before the first point, undone; no send to the output bus; routing to a sent-to bus dropping the send; a -90 dB send through a Write pass).
- [x] 4b. Insert parameter automation (the rest of #4). On a track or bus in Write, Latch or Touch, an insert parameter moved during a pass (an Inspector row, or a plugin's own editor) goes to the pass instead of into the project (`EditorState::captureParameter`, taken by `MixerPanel::captureInsert`): a lane per parameter made at its first move, heard at once, recorded with the fader's recorder and written into the parameter's keys at the stop with the rest of the pass (one undo step). Only what is moved is written: Touch while held (a slider being dragged, a plugin knob the plugin reports grabbed, else half a second after the last change), Latch and Write from the first move; Bool and Choice parameters latch and are written as holding keys. The master has no automation mode, so its inserts are keyed as before. Edits during a pass: the pass does not change the project while it records; what it plays goes to playback as a live mix laid over the sequence (`MixerPanel::liveMix` to `PlaybackController::setLiveMix`, `MixerPanel::heard()`), so other edits, gestures, saves and undo work as ever, and at the stop one "Write Automation" edit lays only the lanes written over what those lanes hold then (`AutomationRecorder::rebase`), on the sequence recorded (another made active writes it there first; another project drops it); controls the pass writes keep their values through refreshes; lanes whose track or send moved are left alone. Before, an edit during a pass committed its live state, which wiped the lanes being written (review finding 4 on d4fdfce). MCP `montage_automate_track` `inserts` (effect id, parameter, points, clear; range-clamped, choices held; a plugin's `param.<id>`), listed afterwards. **Tests:** app `insertAutomationAndInterruptedPasses` (nothing taken outside a pass or on a track in Read; an EQ mid held at +6 dB over frames 10-19 in Touch, heard at once and gliding back; a choice latched from frame 30 through an edit at frame 40; one undo step after the edit's; undo mid-pass undoing the last pass while recording goes on; a value typed into the Inspector's EQ row recorded); media MCP (an EQ's mid points, clamped; a choice held; unknown parameter and effect refused).
  - Review fixes (bd8f267): the pass held an EditorState gesture to play its live values, and stepping aside for edits (4b's first form) still left: (1) controls being written snapped back to their values before the pass on any edit, and that was recorded; (2) the stop overwrote edits made meanwhile to the lanes of tracks it recorded, written or not; (3) a sequence switch right after an edit (New or Duplicate Sequence) never reached the pass, which was lost; (4) each frame after an edit began a gesture, so slider drags elsewhere stopped merging into one undo step; (5) a pass could reopen its gesture under another component's drag, which then committed it, live values (wiped lanes) included; (7) the stop wrote by index without checking the track; (8) a second stop while waiting moved the end. All fixed by the live mix: the pass no longer uses a gesture, and writes only lanes it wrote, by id, over the project as it is at the stop. (6) A plugin's stepped parameters (VST3's run 0 to 1) were rounded to 0 or 1: switches hold their keys, rounded only where the values are whole (on/off, choices, mutes). (9) MCP `inserts` took any `param.<id>` on a plugin and any value: the parameter must be one the plugin recorded, values are kept in its range, stepped ones hold. (10) The plugin editor's settings save is no longer paused during passes. **Tests:** app `insertAutomationAndInterruptedPasses` (values heard while the project is untouched; a latched fader keeping its value through an edit's refresh; a pan point added during the pass kept; a sequence switch mid-pass writing on the sequence recorded; a stepped parameter's keys holding at 0.333), `mixerAutomation`, `mixerSendsAndBusAutomation`, `surroundPositionAutomation` (heard values); media MCP (a stepped plugin parameter held at its own values and clamped; an unknown plugin parameter refused).
  - Review fixes (e97aedc): (1) a mute pressed during a pass was released by the next refresh of the strips (soloing, a save...), and the release recorded: switches keep showing what the pass plays. (2) Insert rows in the Inspector and plugin editors showed the project's value, not what the pass plays, so a checkbox could not be turned back off and steps did not add up: they ask the pass (`EditorState::liveParameter`). (3) The plugin editor's settings save cut other components' drags short again: it waits for them. (4) MCP took a continuous plugin parameter with a wide range (20 Hz to 20 kHz) for a stepped one: the plugin's own stepped flag decides. (5, 6) A lane or level changed during the pass (a fader level set elsewhere, undo) was ignored until the stop and then overwritten either side of what was written: the recorders follow the project (`AutomationRecorder::rebase(lane, still)`), showing and gliding back to what it holds, and the overlay plays width, LFE and object only when changed during the pass. (7) Stopping wrote the pass with an edit even while another gesture (a drag) was held, cutting it short: a pass that wrote nothing makes no edit, and one that did waits for the drag to end. (8) Controls were put back by index after a track moved: only on the same track and send. **Tests:** app `insertAutomationAndInterruptedPasses` (a mute kept through an edit's refresh and written; a fader level set under the pass shown and glided back to; the Inspector row showing the played value; a stop during a held drag written after it; a silent pass leaving a gesture alone); media MCP (a continuous plugin parameter ramps).
  - Review fixes (030d25b): (1) following the project, a recorder for an insert parameter the effect did not store yet read the lane first, which adds the parameter at 0, and then took that 0 as its level: it glided back to 0, not the parameter's default. The level is read first. (2) A second stop, or a playback started and stopped again, while a written pass waited for a drag moved where it ended, and the pass's values went on playing: the first stop holds, the project plays from it, and the later playback records nothing. (3) A sequence switch while it waited wrote the pass at its last frame played: it is written as of the stop. (4) The pass's edit refreshed the strips while the pass was still set, so they showed its values rather than the project's: it is cleared first and written from what it held. **Tests:** app `insertAutomationAndInterruptedPasses` (a Latch pass played and stopped again while it waits, written to the first stop only; an insert parameter the effect does not store yet gliding back to its default).
  - Review fixes (9f67bfb): a plugin parameter the plugin had not stored yet, moved during a pass by less than its thinning tolerance (or first moved in the pass's last frame), was stored as 0 at the stop (its minimum): writing the pass added it at 0 and wrote no keys over it. It is added at its default. **Tests:** app `insertAutomationAndInterruptedPasses` (a parameter moved within tolerance left at its default).
- [x] 5. Voice-cloned speech (M).
  - [x] Engine, Generate Voiceover, CLI and MCP. media/VoiceClone runs Chatterbox Multilingual (Resemble AI, MIT; the ONNX export pinned by revision and SHA-256, 1.6 GB, downloaded on first use) on ONNX Runtime: the reference (mono, windowed-sinc resampled to 24 kHz, quiet edges off, the loudest 10 s) goes through the speech encoder into speaker conditioning and prompt tokens; the text, normalised as the model was trained (punctuation, capital, full stop) and tagged with its language, is tokenized by a C++ BPE reader of the model's tokenizer.json (added tokens longest first, spaces as [SPACE]; Korean as jamo, Chinese as Cangjie codes), giving the same ids as Hugging Face's tokenizer; the 4-bit language model reads it with a KV cache into speech tokens (greedy, repetition penalty 1.2, stopped at STOP_SPEECH), and the flow-matching decoder turns prompt and generated tokens into 24 kHz sound. Long text is spoken a piece at a time (split after sentences, then clauses, then words). Sessions are kept for the run. The decoder's graph (about 24,000 nodes, its flow steps unrolled) took 73 s to load: the time was ONNX Runtime's sequential memory planner, so it runs in parallel execution (two branches, the other cores within nodes), which loads in 6 s and runs about as fast; a first clone takes about 19 s on four cores, later ones about 8 s for a sentence. Speech is trimmed and fitted by WSOLA (`cloneSpeechToFit`: sped up to its speed, then up to 1.6 times to fit its room, or made to fill a room exactly within 0.7 to 1.4 times). Voice samples come from what playback decoded (MediaPool), as the clip plays it (its channels and source range). Every file is written with its provenance in the WAV's INFO comment ("Generated speech (French) in a voice cloned from ... with Chatterbox Multilingual in Montage, <date>; the speaker's consent was confirmed"), and its media gets that note as its comment and the keyword "AI voice". (The original Chatterbox adds an inaudible PerTh watermark; that watermarker is not part of the ONNX model.) Generate Voiceover gains "Clone the voice in the selected clip" and "Clone a voice from a recording…", with a language (23) and a consent checkbox that Generate waits for; `montage-cli clone-speech "text" --voice sample.wav -o out.wav --consent [--language] [--from/--to] [--speed] [--exaggeration]`; MCP `montage_clone_speech` (text at a time, a caption track cue by cue, or text in place of what is said from replace.start to replace.end, fitted to that time and put over it, in the voice heard there unless voice_clip or voice_media is given; consent: true required). Checked by ear through Whisper: "The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog, and then it runs away." in JFK's voice comes back word for word. **Tests:** media `voiceCloning` (normalising, splitting, languages, the reference, trimming, WSOLA keeping 220 Hz, the note in a probed WAV; with the model: token ids matching Hugging Face's for English, French, German, punctuation and Korean, Chinese as Cangjie, speech in the sample voice whose length follows the text, French, refusals, cancelling, fitting and filling a room; MCP refusing without consent and an unknown language, replacing two seconds of JFK in his voice between the two parts kept, a German line from a file on A2, the note and keyword on the media), app `clonedVoiceover` (a clip's voice and the voice heard at a frame, the dialog's clone choices waiting for a selected clip and consent; with the model: generated at the playhead in the Voiceover bin with its note in the file and the bin, one undo step).
  - [x] Regenerate misspoken words from the transcript, and dub in the speaker's own voice. The transcript panel's Correct menu (Sequence view) has "Regenerate Selected Words in This Voice…" (Descript's Overdub, Resolve 21's Speech Generator): the selected words are said again as typed, in the voice of the clip heard where they start (`clipVoice`), in a chosen language (the transcript's by default), after the editor confirms the speaker's consent; the speech fills the words' time exactly (`cloneSpeechToFit` with `exact`) and goes over them on that clip's track (`ClonedVoice::replace`: the range lifted, then the new clip placed), and the new media gets a transcript of the typed words spread over its length by their letters, so the transcript still reads; one undo step (`TranscriptPanel::regenerateSelection`). Dub (Captions › More, `CaptionsPanel::dub` with a `ClonedVoice`) offers "The speaker's own voice (cloned)", sampled under the first caption, into any of the 23 languages the track can be translated into (Opus-MT routes, through English where needed), on "Dub (<language>)"; MCP `montage_dub` takes voice: "clone" with `to`, consent: true and optionally voice_clip or voice_media. **Tests:** app `regenerateWordsAndDubInTheSpeakersVoice` ("my fellow" replaced by "our dear" in JFK's voice between the parts kept, within the words' frames, the new words in the panel, one undo; an English caption track dubbed in the speaker's voice onto "Dub (English)", ducking the original); media `dubIntoEnglish` (refused without consent; the English track dubbed into German in the speaker's voice: a hidden German caption track, "Dub (German)" with a clip per cue, each noting its cloned voice).
  - Review fixes (ab97fe4): (1) a build without ONNX Runtime no longer compiled (the sample and stretch helpers used headers included only with it): included always. (2) Text was tokenized as written, but Chatterbox's tokenizer lower-cases and NFKD-normalises every language first, and the model was trained on that: now the same (ids checked again against the tokenizer with that preparation; still word for word through Whisper). Japanese kanji are not in the vocabulary (upstream converts them with pykakasi, which is GPL): Japanese with kanji is refused with a message asking for kana, voiced kana are read as kana plus mark, and Dub does not offer Japanese (translations come in kanji). (3) Long Chinese pieces were cut off (the token cap allowed English speeds): pieces of syllabic scripts are shorter (90 characters) with more room each (10 tokens a character for Chinese, 8 Korean, 6 Japanese). (4) Replacing words with a line too long for their time, even 1.6 times as fast, ran over what followed: it is refused with how long it takes. (5) Asking for twice the speed of a line short of room slowed it to 1.6 times: the speed is only ever raised. (6) Undoing a cloned voiceover left its media in the bin unlabelled (the note was written with the placement, after the import): it is written with the import. Probing now reads a file's comment tag into the media's comment, so a generated WAV says what it is when imported anywhere. (7) WAVs were written with std::ofstream, which cannot open non-ASCII UTF-8 paths on Windows (also for Kokoro voiceovers): written through QFile. **Tests:** media `voiceCloning` (new token ids, Japanese kana and voiced kana, kanji refused, a too-long replacement refused, twice the speed kept when short of room, a WAV with a non-ASCII name probed with its comment); app `clonedVoiceover` (the bin's copy still labelled after undo).
  - Review fixes (c90db06): (1) Regenerate took its voice and track from the first audio track with any clip under the words, so with music on A1 above the dialogue it cloned the music and cut it, and with a host and a guest on two tracks it cloned whoever was on the upper one: it now takes them from the heard clip (unmuted, soloed if any are) whose own transcript holds most of the selected words; Dub's and MCP's fallback voice likewise prefers a heard clip with a transcript. (2) Only that one track was cleared, so a linked copy of the same channels on another track, or the next clip's speech when the selection ran on, kept saying the old words under the new ones: every track carrying the selected words is cleared in the same undo step. (3) An exact fit could still run up to 3 % past the words (the refusal allowed for rounding generously): the margin is a rounding error now, and the new clip is never longer than the words' time (app and MCP). **Tests:** app `regenerateWordsAndDubInTheSpeakersVoice` (music on A1 above the speech on A2 and a copy on A3: the voice and track are the speech's, the music untouched, the copy cleared too, one undo).
- [x] 6a. Stem separation (L). media/StemSeparation runs Spleeter (Deezer Research; the official v1.4.0 2-, 4- and 5-stem release, MIT, trained on Deezer's catalogue). No converted weights are hosted anywhere: `scripts/gen-spleeter-graph.py` transcribes Spleeter's `unet.py` (a U-Net per stem: six 5x5 stride-2 convolutions with batch norm, six transposed ones with skips from each convolution before its normalisation, a 4x4 dilated sigmoid; ELU in the 4- and 5-stem models, LeakyReLU/ReLU in the 2-stem; a softmax across stems in the 5-stem) and `_build_masks` (ratio masks, exponent 2) into an ONNX graph without weights, each weight external data naming its offset and length inside the checkpoint's `model.data-00000-of-00001` (read from `model.index`, TensorFlow's LevelDB table of BundleEntryProtos, parsed by the script); the three graphs (30-75 KB) are compiled in like RIFE's. On first use the release's tar.gz (checked by SHA-256) is gunzipped and untarred by new core readers (`gunzip`, RFC 1952 with CRC and length; `tarEntries`, ustar with GNU long names and header checksums) and the data file written beside the graph. `StemSplitter` streams: the input after a frame of silence, 4096-point periodic-Hann frames every 1024 samples (TensorFlow's stft with pad_end), 512-frame segments of the first 1024 bins' magnitudes through the network, each stem's masked spectrum (zero above, as Spleeter's "zeros" extension) inverted with av_tx and overlap-added with the 2/3 window compensation, handed out as soon as no later frame can change it; `separateStemsToFiles` reads a stretch of a file (its clip channels or conformed sound made whole first, else a block at a time through the new `decodeAudioStereoBlocks`) into 32-bit float stereo WAVs at 44.1 kHz, removing them on failure or cancel. Checked against Spleeter's own TensorFlow code (its `unet.py` run on the same checkpoint, its STFT, masks and inverse): the masks to 0.00002 for all three models, the separated sound to 0.0001 over 14 s across a segment boundary; 4 stems run at about seven times real time on four cores, model loading included. Clip › Separate Stems… (2, 4 or 5 stems, mute the original; `separateStems`): each selected audio clip's source plus two seconds either side (`stemSource`) separated in the background with progress and cancel, the WAVs in a Stems folder beside the project and the bin's Stems folder (each noting what it was separated from, keyword "Stem"), then one undo step placing a copy of the clip per stem (timing, speed, volume, pan; not its effects, channels or markers) on the free track named for the stem or a new one, linked with the clip, the clip disabled (`placeStems`). `montage-cli stems <file> [--stems] [--from/--to] [-o]`, MCP `montage_separate_stems` (clips, stems, mute_original, handles), `montage-cli models` lists the packs, `scripts/fetch-models.sh` fetches the 4-stem model (the others with `--stems`) and CI caches it. **Tests:** core `gzipAndTar` (a gzipped GNU tar with a folder and a 124-character name; a flipped byte, not gzip, a bad header checksum and an empty archive refused); media `stemSeparation` (names and packs; stereo blocks equal to a whole decode, chosen channels too; with the model: a synthetic 13 s mix matching TensorFlow's level of each stem and channel overall and across the boundary, and its samples there, to 0.0002; a stretch the right length; cancelled and wrong counts leaving nothing; JFK over drums, bass and a pad giving vocals 0.95+ correlated with the speech and under 0.1 with the pad, the rest 0.9+ with the pad, drums and bass with the rhythm; a clip's stems on four new tracks named for them, linked, in sync to the frame, the clip disabled, adding back up to its sound at 0.99+; a second time onto four more tracks; MCP writing them into Stems beside the project with their notes, leaving the clip on, refusing three stems), app `separateStemsFromTheMenu` (the action and dialog; nothing selected refused; with the model: four stem clips on named tracks from the Stems folder, linked, half a second of handle, the clip muted, one undo taking it back).
  - Review fixes (06cf2c2): (1) stems went on new tracks at 0 dB straight to the master, so a clip on a track 15 dB down through a bus came back 15 dB louder and unprocessed: new stem tracks take the clip's track's level, pan, inserts (with new ids), sends, output, surround position and automation, and a stem track is reused only when it mixes the same. (2) The graph unpacked on first use was kept for good once stamped, so a new version's graph would never be used (in CI too, from the cache): it is compared with the compiled one on every load and rewritten when it differs (RIFE's too), and the masks' size is checked. (3) The app made two undo steps (an import, then the placing): the stems are brought in and placed in one; a read-only project is refused before separating, and files are removed if the stems cannot be read or placed. (4) MCP separated the clips one by one, so a clip that could not be separated after one that could left that one's WAVs behind: every clip is checked first, and files written are removed on any failure. (5) Each clip's sound was decoded from the start of its file: the stereo block decoder seeks to a second before the stretch (`decodeAudioStereoBlocks` from, `decodeAudioStream` seekTo), dropping what comes before by the first frame's time. (Clips with their own channel mapping still decode their file whole, as decodeAudio does for playback.) (6) WAVs past 4 GB (about 3 h 20 min) had wrapped sizes: a JUNK chunk keeps room for RF64's ds64, which such a file becomes. (7) A stretch ending before it starts gave empty files and success: refused. (8) Two clips whose names differed only in case wrote the same files on Windows and macOS: names are kept apart regardless of case. (9) The media test's Stems path check never matched Windows separators. Also: the note names the file without Interpret Footage's settings, and the menu item no longer shares a mnemonic. **Tests:** media `stemSeparation` (a stale graph on disk replaced on load; blocks from 4 s equal to a whole decode from there; an inverted stretch refused leaving nothing; the stem tracks at the clip's track's -15 dB, pan, bus, send and EQ; a clip on a differently mixed track getting tracks of its own; MCP refusing a title among the clips before writing anything), app `separateStemsFromTheMenu` (one undo step bringing in and placing, one undo taking the stems out of the bin).

Duplicating a sequence or importing it from another project carries its fields over to the new clip and effect ids, and media values to the media brought along. `fillGraphicsFromTable` reads CSV (BOM, quotes, tab or comma; columns by field id, else label, any case): the first row sets the graphic, each further row a copy of it straight after, on its track where free, else on the track above; every value is checked before anything changes. The library (`graphicsTemplatesDirectory`, $MONTAGE_TEMPLATES_DIR to move it) holds each template as a project of its own, `<name>/<name>.montage` with the files it plays copied beside it (`saveGraphicsTemplate`, `listGraphicsTemplates`, `insertGraphicsTemplate`: brought in with new ids and placed on a new top track at its own length, in the bin's Graphics folder). (7) A template at another frame rate came in at the wrong length: a nested sequence's length is converted to the sequence's rate (`sourceLimit`, for compound and multicam clips too). (8) Table fills: a blank row is skipped, a blank cell keeps the graphic's value (text is emptied), cells are trimmed, a quote after a space opens quoting. (9) Cancelling the colour picker gave the clip its own copy of the template's colour and an undo step: the picker previews as a gesture, one step on OK, nothing on Cancel. Audio search by description (S–M). media/SoundSearch runs CLAP (LAION's clap-htsat-unfused, Apache-2.0; Xenova's ONNX export pinned by revision and SHA-256: the float audio model, the fastest of the exports on a CPU and exact, and the half-float text model, because the int8 exports' integer matrix kernels are the ones that crashed ONNX Runtime on Windows; 370 MB). `clapFeatures` ports transformers' ClapFeatureExtractor for this model (Slaney mel filters and norm, 64 bands 50 Hz-14 kHz, periodic Hann, 1024-point FFT every 480 samples, centred with reflection, power in dB; a short recording repeated to fill ten seconds): it matches transformers' features to 0.00001. `ClapTokenizer` is RoBERTa's byte-level BPE (GPT-2's pre-tokenizer), matching transformers' ids. `indexSound` embeds 10-second windows every 5 s (2 s under 30 s, plus a last window to the end) into a VisualIndex kept on the media (`MediaItem::sounds`, saved with the project, carried by Interpret Footage's retiming and cleared on relink and consolidate like the visual index); the sound is read a block at a time (`decodeAudioBlocks`, a drain added to the audio decoder) so an hour-long file is not held whole. `findSounds` finds moments as `findShots` does. The Find panel gets What's Heard (index, search, open, subclip), `montage-cli sounds`, MCP `montage_find_sounds`, and `scripts/fetch-models.sh` and CI fetch the model. Checked against transformers with ONNX Runtime: JFK's speech scores 0.35 for "a man giving a speech" and under 0.01 for applause, drums or a dog. **Tests:** media `soundSearch` (blocks equal to a whole decode and stopping early; features of silence and of a repeated 1 kHz tone; with the model: RoBERTa ids, JFK as one window and a 39 s synthetic song as seven, "a man giving a speech" finding JFK and "music with piano chords" the song, the index saved and loaded, MCP indexing on first search and saving), app `findWhatIsHeard` (the third mode's status, indexing on search without an undo step, the result opening in the Source monitor).

(2) Find Similar with the panel left on What's Heard indexed sound (offering the 370 MB download) instead of the videos it searches: it switches the panel to What's Shown first. (3) Windows CI fetched the model but never pointed the tests at it: it does. ALE and ASC CDL interchange (S). core/Cdl: the ASC CDL v1.2 maths (slope, offset, clamp, power; saturation about Rec. 709 luma, clamped), `(s s s)(o o o)(p p p)` text both ways, .cc/.ccc/.cdl XML read (any nesting, CDL 1.0's SATNode, namespace prefixes, one value for three) and written (urn:ASC:CDL:v1.01, six decimals), a clip's CDL as its new "cdl" effect (placed first after input transforms) and a media item's as `asc_sop`/`asc_sat` metadata. The effect grades in the working space or in a chosen space it was made in (a camera log, ACEScct), converted there and back exactly (linear light through the space's primaries and curve, no display rendering, so only the CDL changes the picture), and is baked by Export LUT from Grade. core/Ale: Avid Log Exchange read (Heading, Column and Data; any line ending, trailing tabs, short rows; FPS 23.976/29.97/59.94 exact) and written; `applyAle` matches rows to media by source file, by name with or without extension, or by tape and start timecode, fills in scene, shot, take, tape (or camroll), camera, description and comment, stores the CDL and grades every clip of that media with it; `aleFromMedia` writes name, tracks (VA1A2), start, end (the frame after the last), duration, tape, source file, the log fields and the CDL (the media's, else its first graded clip's). EDLs write `*ASC_SOP` and `*ASC_SAT` after a graded event and read them back onto the clip. File › Import ALE and Export ALE (the selected media, else all); Clip › Import CDL (onto the selected video clips; a collection matched by id to each clip's name, media name, file or tape) and Export CDL (.cc for one, .ccc or .cdl for several); MCP `montage_ale` (import, export) and `montage_cdl` (get, set, import, export). **Tests:** core `ascCdlAndAle` (the maths with clamping and no NaN below zero; SOP text; .cc, .ccc and .cdl read and written, malformed files refused; an ALE with CRLF and trailing tabs matched by file and by tape and timecode, a row reported unmatched, log fields, the CDL on the media and both clips of it; exported and read back into a fresh copy; EDL CDLs both ways; set replacing, placed after a transform); render `ascCdlEffect` (the effect's values, half-transparent pixels, ACEScct round trip exact, an offset in ACEScct against one in Rec.709, off); media `ascCdlAndAleOverMcp`; app `aleAndCdlFromTheMenus` (import, undo, a .cc onto the selected clip, a .ccc and an ALE out).
  - Review fixes: a read-through found twelve issues, all fixed with tests: ALE export wrote timecodes at the sequence's rate (a 23.976 clip at 01:00:00:00 in a 24 fps sequence came out as 01:00:03:14); each row now uses its media's own rate and the heading the media's most common one. Drop-frame was assumed at 29.97 and 59.94 both ways: probing now notes a drop-frame start timecode (';'), exports write drop-frame only then, and imports read ':' as non-drop. A CDL set to the media's own log graded after the input transform's display rendering, so an on-set grade could not be reproduced; it now grades the media's values before that transform, and new CDLs go first in the chain. The colour-space round trip is baked into a cached 65^3 LUT (it cost about 0.3 s a 4K frame). File names are taken without Interpret Footage's settings and from Windows paths. Names lost their last dot-part when matching ("Sc12.2" matched "Sc12.1"); only media file extensions are dropped now, and a collection is searched for an exact id first. Clips cut from subclips get their parent's CDL, and subclips its log. CDLs are read at the clip's own start, not its place in the sequence (keyframed CDLs). The FPS is written without the locale's decimal comma. A malformed timecode in an ALE no longer ends the app. MCP refuses a CDL onto an audio clip. Corrections a file leaves out (unreadable numbers, ColorCorrectionRef) are reported even when others are read. **Tests:** core `cdlAleReviewFixes`; media `cdlInTheMediasOwnLog` (a LogC3 grey graded in LogC3 before the rendering, against the same CDL after it) and the MCP audio-clip refusal; the CDL's new place in `ascCdlAndAle`.
- [x] 10. Editing growing files (M). EditorState follows files still being written: every write the media watcher sees is noted (`noteMediaFileWritten`), and a file written for over 2 s without a 1.5 s pause counts as growing (`isMediaGrowing`); while it grows its media's length and streams are re-read every `growingRefreshMs` (15 s by default; 5, 30, 60 s or only when it stops, remembered) with the decoders reopened to see the new end, no undo step and no reload, and the quiet-file reload (which would never come, or come between a recorder's bursts) leaves it alone; 3 s without a write and it has stopped, and is reloaded in full (thumbnails, waveform). A save in a few quick writes is not growing. A clock (`setClock`) and a tick (`checkGrowingMedia`, every second while files are written) keep it testable. The bin shows a red GROWING badge and says so in the tooltip, and its Growing Files menu refreshes them now or sets how often. MCP `montage_refresh_media` re-reads the media (named or all) whose length or streams changed on disk and lists them with their old and new lengths. Clips already in sequences keep their length. **Tests:** media `refreshMediaOverMcp` (a WAV whose header gives no length, as recorders write it: nothing changed, then 1.5 s more recorded and refreshed by name to 2.5 s and saved; an unknown name refused), app `growingMedia` (by the test's clock: written every 0.8 s, growing after two seconds with its length read then; read again five seconds later while still growing, with no undo step; the tooltip; reloaded in full and no longer growing 3.5 s after the last write; two quick writes not growing).
  - Review fixes (0150914): (1) every refresh dropped the file's decoded sound and waveform (MediaPool::forget), and nothing asked for them again, so a growing file's clips played silent for the whole recording: a refresh keeps the sound (`forget(path, false)` drops pictures and idle decoders only) and `MediaPool::refreshAudio` reads every cached rate and channel choice again on a worker, replacing each when read (a decode already going is left to finish; with none cached the file is decoded); "Only When They Stop" no longer reads the file once at detection. (2) The live link refused any tool that spanned a refresh (the project had changed outside an edit): what files say (`copyProbedFacts`/`sameProbedFacts`: length, picture size and rate, streams, codecs, stereo packing; a subclip's range is its own) is set aside when comparing, files read again in Montage keep what it read, and files the tool read again itself (montage_refresh_media) are reloaded so what plays matches (`mergeToolResult`). (3) A length read outside an edit was lost on undo (snapshots held the old one), never marked the project changed and was dropped by a gesture's reset: `applyProbedFacts` sets it in the project, every undo and redo state (`History::forEachState`) and a gesture's origin, and marks the project changed (reloads of changed files too). (4) Stale entries outlived a project switch, removal or relink and re-watched the file in the next project every few seconds: entries for files no media uses are dropped, and a reload adds a file to the watcher only if media use it. (5) A save in writes spread over a second counted as growing (it measured from the first write to now): growing means written for over two seconds (first to last write). (6) Probing ran on the window's thread every refresh: lengths are read on a worker (a file still being read is skipped; a read begun before a reload is not applied, nor one from another project). (7) A failing test left the clock pointing at a dead local: `setClock({})` restores the real clock, done by a guard. (8) Refresh Now was disabled unless a file was seen growing, though a file written on a network share by another computer is never seen: with none growing it reads every media file's length again (Refresh All Media Lengths Now); the docs say so, and no longer claim states or a stop action that do not exist. **Tests:** app `growingMedia` (by the test's clock, growing only from the fourth write; its length read on a worker; its sound still playable after a refresh and then holding the new part; no undo step but the project changed; read again five seconds later; undo and redo after the read keeping the new length; a save over a second not growing; nothing read while growing in "only when they stop"; another project open dropping it), `agentLinkKeepsWhatFilesSay` (a length read meanwhile kept and the tool's edit applied with the editor's playhead; the tool's own re-read kept and its file named; an edit in Montage, a subclip's range included, still refusing the result).

In-app scripting (M). automation/ScriptHost runs Lua 5.4 (lua.org's release, MIT, fetched at configure time with its SHA-256 and compiled as C++ so its errors unwind as exceptions; `MONTAGE_WITH_LUA`): a script calls any MCP tool as `montage.<name>{...}` (an `__index` on the `montage` table, refused for names that are not tools) or `montage.call(name, args)`; tables go to JSON (a table keyed 1..n is an array), results come back as tables (the structured result with its text as `message`, else `{text = ...}`), and a tool's error is a Lua error carrying the tool's message and the script's line. `montage.tools()`, `montage.args`, `montage.project`, `montage.json`, and `print` to the host's output; a count hook asks the host whether to stop and lets it keep its window alive; nested results and tables are stack-checked (and self-holding tables refused). `toolScriptOptions` runs a script's calls on an MCP server, giving the tools that take a project the one named; `montage-cli script <file.lua> [--project p] [args]` uses it (Ctrl+C stops). App: Tools › Scripts lists the scripts folder (`scriptsDirectory`, $MONTAGE_SCRIPTS_DIR) and runs one, Run Script…, Open Scripts Folder, and the Script Console dock (editor, output, Run with Ctrl+Return, Stop, Open, Save); its calls go through the agent link (`LiveLink::handleScript`) to the open project, each change labelled "Script: <name>" and merged into one undo step for the run (`EditorState::setUndoGroup`, which merges edits of one key however far apart until the editor edits or undoes), `montage.undo{}` takes back only the run's own step, and the window processes events every 30 ms while a script runs; closing the window stops a running script first. **Tests:** media `luaScripts` (a project made and two titles added by name and by `montage.call`, read back as tables; arguments, JSON, integers; a tool's error with its line; an unknown tool; a Lua error; a parse error; the tools listed; a table holding itself refused; a script that never ends stopped; clean under valgrind), app `scriptsInTheApp` (two titles from one run as one undo step "Script: titles.lua" and its printed playhead; `montage.undo{}` refusing the editor's change and taking back its own; a failing run keeping what it did as one step with the error's line shown; Stop during an endless script with the window responsive; the scripts folder listed and a script run from it with arguments, and opened in the console).
  - Review fixes (411e5f6): (1) Stop was an ordinary Lua error, so a script that caught errors (pcall, xpcall, coroutine.resume) swallowed it and went on calling tools, and debug.sethook could take the hook away: a stopped script is ended with a value only the host holds, which those functions pass on (a prelude wraps them), the hook then fires at every instruction, the stop is checked before each tool as well as after, and debug.sethook is gone. (2) Stop could not end the wait for a tool already running, so the window would not close until it finished: Stop leaves the wait (the answer's state is shared, so a late answer is harmless), `LiveLink::cancelScript` answers the run's waiting calls as not run and keeps a running one from being applied, and closing the window stops the script and closes when it has; in the CLI a second Ctrl+C ends at once (exit 130, also for a stopped script). (3) montage.undo{} could take back an earlier run of a script with the same name: only a step this run made (`EditorState::lastMergeKey`). (4) A project opened while a script ran would have been edited by its later calls: calls are refused once the project changes. (5) JSON: tables with gaps became objects (now arrays with null; montage.null for null, and null array elements come back as it rather than holes); NaN, infinity, integers past 2^53, functions and keys that are neither strings nor numbers are refused instead of changed. (6) The CLI took -p after the script as its own (options end at the script; `--` too); a "#!" line and a byte order mark are skipped as Lua's loader does. (7) C modules could not load (package.cpath empty, loadlib and the C searchers gone); io.read and io.lines() without a file are errors in the app, which has no input to wait for; MSVC builds Lua without warnings, and AUTOMOC is off for it. Left: non-ASCII arguments on Windows (as every CLI command), os.execute blocking the app while it runs. **Tests:** media `luaScripts` (stopped inside pcall, xpcall and coroutine loops with no tool after the stop, and inside a pcall'd endless loop; the hook and C loaders gone; gaps and montage.null as null; NaN, infinity, 2^60, a boolean key and a function refused; a BOM and "#!" line skipped with line numbers kept; stdin refused; clean under valgrind), app `scriptsInTheApp` (Stop with pcall around tools; a waiting call answered and a running one not applied on cancel; montage.undo refusing an earlier run of the same script; a project opened mid-run stopping its calls with the reason shown).

ScriptHost passes the description to the host (`ScriptOptions::panel`), which answers with the panel's values and the button pressed, returned to the script as a table, or with nothing, which ends the script there successfully (a second end value beside Stop that pcall, xpcall and coroutines pass on, raised again by the hook if caught); `montage.set_panel{...}` goes to `ScriptOptions::setPanel`. - [ ] 12. Motion Deblur (M): NAFNet-GoPro-width32 (MIT; GoPro/REDS CC BY), exported to ONNX here and hosted pinned (`docs/research/phase5-roadmap.md`).
- [x] 13a. HUI control surfaces (S–M). control/Hui speaks HUI, the protocol Pro Tools drives the Mackie HUI with and the mode most surfaces offer besides Mackie Control (X-Touch, iCON, FaderPort, Avid and SSL in HUI mode), from theageman's HUI reference: a `hui::Decoder` turns the surface's two-message switches (zone, then port) and faders (high, then low seven bits) into the same `mcu::Event`s, in Mackie Control's terms (`noteOf`/`portOf` map zones and ports to Mackie notes both ways; RTZ, End and the automation keys' Off are extra events), with V-Pot, scroll and jog deltas, the ping reply and system reset; encoders for the ping, faders, lights (zone select then port), rings, meters, the four-character strip and Select-Assign displays, the 2x40 main display's ten-character zones (its own characters for backslash and tilde) and the eight-digit timecode (rightmost first, points after hours, minutes and seconds). ControlSurface takes a protocol (`SurfaceProtocol`, Edit › Control Surface…'s Protocol, remembered; switched while connected, the same ports are taken over in the new one): everything it sends goes through per-protocol helpers, and messages are decoded in order before a fader's queued moves are coalesced (so HUI's paired messages coalesce too). A HUI is pinged every second (without it its faders stop), shown everything again when it answers after a silence or sends a system reset, shows names on the strips and names and levels (the pan while it turns) on the main display, gets both meter sides (sources disagree on which bit is the left) sent falling too and let fall when levels stop, and has no master fader. Both protocols gain Cycle/Loop (the program monitor's loop, lit while on), and the HUI RTZ, End, keypad Enter (marker) and automation Off. The surface's selection and bank start afresh in another project (track ids start again there). **Tests:** core `huiProtocol` (switches, touches, faders, V-Pots, jog and scroll decoded; a port before any zone ignored; the button and light map; every encoder byte for byte, timecode with drop-frame, short and long text), app `controlSurfaceHui` (pinged first and shown everything, nothing of Mackie Control's; touch, move and echo as one undo step; a queued fader's four messages applied once; mute, solo, V-Pot pan on the main display, V-Select; banking; select, Touch and Off with their lights; RTZ, End, jog, Loop, marker, Undo, Play and Stop; meters both sides falling to nothing; pings every second, a ping answer not resending, a system reset resending; disconnecting clean; switched to Mackie Control while connected; the dialog's Protocol remembered).
  - Review fixes (dbd7390): (1) a HUI that went off line during a GUI stall of 2-3 s (it gives up after about two seconds without a ping) was not shown everything again when it answered, because "back" meant three seconds without an answer: an answer after more than 1.8 s counts, a reset counts as an answer, and several resets on power-up resend once. (2) The surface's bank and selection were reset whenever the project was read again, including a read-only project following its editor's saves and File › Edit Project: they start afresh only for another project (another file, or an unsaved one). (3) Loop toggled the monitor's button, while its light showed playback's loop, which ADR's takes set without the button: Loop now follows playback and sets the button to match. (4) Switching protocol while connected counted as a new connection, so an unplug reported just before was ignored: the same connection is started again in the new protocol. (5) The HUI timecode went out whole on every frame: from the rightmost digit up to the last that changed (a frame's change is one or two digits). Left: both meter sides get the louder channel, as the reference and a logger disagree on which bit is the left. **Tests:** core `huiProtocol` (timecode digits and a partial message), app `controlSurfaceHui` (an answer a second after the last not resending, one after 1.9 s resending, two resets and an answer resending once; the same project opened again keeping bank and selection, a new one clearing them; Loop following playback's loop with the button out of step; one timecode digit for a frame; an unplug just before a protocol switch closing the surface; nothing of Mackie Control's at all: no notes but the ping, no Mackie system exclusive).
- [x] 13b. OSC control for Stream Deck, Loupedeck and X-keys through Bitfocus Companion (S). control/Osc reads and writes OSC 1.0: messages (int32, int64, float32, double, strings, blobs, true/false/nil/impulse, time tags and the rest read flat), messages without type tags, nested bundles (eight deep; time tags not waited for), refusing malformed packets whole; values read as numbers, text and switches. app/OscControl listens on a UDP port (localhost unless network messages are allowed; Edit › Control Surface… › OSC, remembered and started with Montage) for: the transport (play, stop, play/pause, shuttle, step, go to a timecode or seconds, start, end, previous and next edit, In, Out, a named or numbered marker, loop on/off, undo, redo, save, record), any menu command by its Keyboard Shortcuts id written as an address (`/montage/command/sequence/add_marker`, or the id itself; a switch's value sets it), any agent tool on the open project through the agent link as one undo step "OSC: <tool>" (its JSON arguments as a string), and the mixer through its own controls (track volume, mute and solo set or toggled, pan, master volume; one undo step a move, so automation passes record them). A 0 or false (a button's release, as TouchOSC and Companion send) does nothing for one-shot commands. Feedback goes to the sender's address at the feedback port: playing, timecode and loop as they change, tools' answers as /montage/reply/<tool>, and anything not understood or refused as /montage/error. The dialog lists every address. The surface's transport commands and OSC's share one table. **Tests:** core `oscMessages` (an empty message's bytes; seven argument types both ways; number, text and switch readings; no type tags; nested bundles in order; six malformed packets and a bundle nested ten deep refused), app `oscControl` (over a real UDP socket: go to a timecode, a release ignored, play and stop, feedback of timecode and playing, an unknown address answered with an error; seconds, steps, In, start and end, loop; a named marker, the Add Marker command by its address, a release ignored, undo; track volume as one undo step, mute set and toggled, solo, master; an unknown track and a word for a volume refused; a tool's marker as one undo step "OSC: add_marker" with its answer back, bad JSON and a tool's error refused; a malformed packet answered; the dialog's status and its address list).
  - Review fixes (4800c00): (1) a feedback port equal to the port listened on made Montage answer its own feedback with errors for ever, inside one read loop, freezing the window: its own feedback addresses (/montage/state/, /reply/, /error) are never answered, its own datagrams are not read, nothing is sent to itself, the dialog refuses the two ports being the same, and a read takes 256 datagrams at most before the event loop runs. (2) A float 0.0 (TouchOSC's release) was taken as a press, so a play/pause button toggled twice, undo went back two steps and shuttle jumped to double speed: any zero number or false is a release. (3) Agent tools over OSC ran with neither the agent link's opt-in nor its key, so any local program (or, with network messages allowed, any computer on the network) could run tools that write files or edit other projects: they are off until Allow agent tools is ticked, then refused a "project" argument (the open project only), and the tooltips say what network messages allow. (4) Fader and pan moves were not recorded in Touch and Latch passes (which write only while a control is held): a move is held for a second, as the control surface's are. (5) /montage/tool/undo could never succeed: refused, pointing to /montage/undo. (6) Tools' answers went to whoever sent last, unparseable packets made their sender the feedback peer, and a new receiver waited for a change before getting any state: answers go to the asker, a malformed packet is answered without becoming the peer, and the whole state is sent again when the sender or feedback port changes. (7) A port that could not be opened at start-up failed silently and stayed enabled: said in the status bar; in the dialog it unticks and is saved off; the OSC group sits above the dialog's buttons. (8) The test checked errors against datagrams left from earlier steps: it drains the socket first and checks each error's text. **Tests:** app `oscControl` (TouchOSC's 1.0 then 0.0 toggling once; feedback addresses not answered; a new feedback port getting the whole state; a feedback port pointed at the listening port handled a bounded number of times; a fader held then let go; tools off until allowed, a "project" argument and /tool/undo refused; the tool's and the malformed packet's own errors).
- [x] 13c. Blackmagic Speed Editor and Editor Keyboard over HID (S–M): done as Phase 6 #8.

- [x] 14. Freeform storyboard bin (M). core/Storyboard: each media item's spot on its bin's board (`MediaItem::board`, x and y in thumbnail cells, saved with the project only once placed); `storyboardOrder` reads rows top to bottom (an item within half a cell of a row's first joins it) and left to right, with media not yet placed after, in their order; `boardLayout` gives the placed their spots and the rest the next rows clear of everything placed; `arrangeStoryboard` puts media in a grid; `assembleStoryboard` places media one after another from a time on given tracks, inserted or overwriting (a subclip as its range, picture and sound linked). App: the bin's Board view (StoryboardView: a free-movement icon view over the bin's model, sharing its selection): thumbnails dragged anywhere and saved as one undo step "Arrange Storyboard"; every layout puts them back at their spots (so rebuilds keep them); the board scales with the thumbnail size (Ctrl+wheel, Thumbnail Size, remembered); drags to the timeline go in storyboard order (the bin model's drag order, set while the board shows); right-click Assemble Selected/All at Playhead (overwrite or insert, on the targeted tracks), Arrange in Grid (rows as wide as the view); rating keys and file drops work there too. MCP `montage_storyboard` (board, move, arrange, assemble; media by name, file or id; keep_order). **Tests:** core `storyboard` (order as given with nothing placed; rows with a slightly lower item, and the unplaced last; the layout below an item reaching past its row; saved and loaded, unplaced not saved; a grid two wide; assembling with a subclip's range, linked sound, then inserting and pushing what followed; nothing to assemble refused), media `storyboardOverMcp` (the layout for unplaced stills, moves by name and id changing the order, an unknown name refused, a one-column arrangement saved, assembling from 2 s in board order, named media in the order given on V2, a sound track as video refused), app `storyboardBin` (the button; moving three thumbnails saved as one undo step in cells, nothing moved then no step; kept through a rebuild and scaled with the thumbnails; a drag's media in board order; assembling everything at the playhead on the targeted track and only the selection; Arrange in Grid).
  - Review fixes (8c365db): (1) dragging thumbnails did not work interactively: the model offered only Copy drops, so a plain drag was refused, and a Ctrl-drag moved every selected column (each placing the row's thumbnail from the board's corner); the board now starts its own drags (its media in storyboard order, marked as its own) and moves the selected thumbnails by the distance from where the pointer was pressed, one undo step for those it moved (media not yet placed are pinned where they show, so nothing else shifts). (2) The board did not follow changes that kept the same media shown (Arrange in Grid, undo, an agent's moves), and a later drag saved the stale places: every rebuild puts the thumbnails back at their spots. (3) Assemble could put a sequence into itself (a new sequence's item sits in the bin): the sequence, and any it plays inside, is left out, in the core so MCP too. (4) Thumbnail Size only spread the 128-pixel thumbnails apart (or cropped them): the board's delegate draws them at its size. (5) A drag or Arrange in Grid on a search or smart bin rewrote spots of media from other bins: neither is possible there. (6) Moving media to another bin, or making a subclip, carried the old spot along: it starts afresh. (7) Media not yet placed followed the order they were clicked: the board's row order. (8) MCP: an unknown bin is an error, and the board lists the sequence's own item as the app does. (9) Assemble kept to the tracks there are, names clips from subclips after them, carries on past media that cannot be placed (failing only when none can), and counts the clips it made. Left: insert mode opens a gap per item, as a drop of several does. **Tests:** core `storyboard` (subclip names; the sequence itself and a medium without picture or sound skipped, the rest placed; nothing placeable a failure naming it; spots reset by a bin move and a subclip), media `storyboardOverMcp` (an unknown bin refused), app `storyboardBin` (a real press, drag enter and drop moving one thumbnail by the distance dragged as one undo step, the others left; undo moving it back; the not yet placed pinned when another is saved; Arrange in Grid shown at once; a search making the board unarrangeable; a new sequence's own item not assembled into it).
- [x] 15. Spatial video (MV-HEVC) in and out (M): decoding where FFmpeg is 7.1 or later; encoding through x265's own multiview encoder, built with Montage (Phase 8 #15).

# Phase 5

The fourth gap analysis (`docs/research/phase5-roadmap.md`, October 2026) ranks what is still missing against Premiere 26.5, Resolve 21.1, Final Cut Pro 12.4 and Media Composer 2026.8, after Phase 4's remaining items:

- [x] 1. Proxy overhaul: ProRes Proxy, DNxHR LB and 10-bit HEVC (HDR) proxies at chosen sizes, with sound; camera proxies attached; proxies for AI analysis (S–M). media/Proxy: `createProxy` with `ProxyOptions` (format: intra-frame H.264 with AAC, ProRes 422 Proxy with PCM, DNxHR LB with PCM at 16-pixel sizes no smaller than 256 x 128, or 10-bit HEVC through x265 or the hardware encoder with AAC; at most a width, half or a quarter; sound or none), rendered through a sequence in the file's own colour space for ProRes and HEVC (so an HDR10 or HLG file's proxy stays HDR, tagged) and Rec.709 for the others (tone mapped). `pictureSource`/`soundSource` decide what a clip reads: its proxy when proxies are on, or when the file is offline and the proxy is there (sound only from a proxy that has it; an offline file's proxy sound plays whole, without the clip's channel picks), with the files' presence looked at every two seconds at most and proxies probed once. `analysisSource`: visual search, auto-tags, scene detection and slate OCR read a proxy at least 640 wide when one is there (faces keep to the file, their boxes being in its pixels). `findCameraProxies`: video files under a folder whose names begin with the original's (case aside), the shortest first, of the same length within a second. App: Create Proxy Media… asks format, size, sound and where (Montage's cache, or Proxy/<name>_Proxy beside the original, falling back to the cache on a read-only card; remembered), writes each under another name and then puts it in place (a proxy being played is never half written) and attaches them in one undo step; Attach Proxies From Folder… (one undo step); opening a project whose offline files all have their proxies says so in the status bar instead of opening Link Media. CLI `proxy --format --width half|quarter|N --sound`. **Tests:** media `proxyFormatsAndOfflineEditing` (each format's codec, size and sound; DNxHR kept at its least size; an audio file refused; an HDR10 file's ProRes and HEVC proxies HDR10, its H.264 and DNxHR not; with the original moved away the picture and sound from a ProRes proxy, the file's sound with a silent proxy, a frame rendered blue from the proxy and its tone mixed at level; analysis using a 640-wide proxy and not a 256-wide one; a camera proxy found below the folder by name, case aside, past a same-named file of another length), app `proxyMediaFromTheBin` (the options dialog filled, remembered and cancelled; a ProRes half-size proxy with sound made beside the original and attached as one undo step with nothing half written left; attached again from the folder; the project opened with the original gone and no Link Media; opened without the proxy asking again).
  - Review fixes (bbbf60f): (1) exports, the CLI and MCP renders used an offline file's proxy too, so a master could silently carry upscaled proxy pictures and sound: only viewing falls back (`RenderOptions::offlineProxies`, `AudioMixer::setOfflineProxies`, set by playback, the monitors, scrubbing and the render cache); deliveries show Media Offline. (2) A proxy's picture was converted from the original's colour space, so a tone-mapped proxy of HLG, PQ or log footage was transformed twice: `pictureColorSpace` converts from the proxy's own tagged space (Rec.709 when untagged), in the frame and for OpenFX neighbours. (3) A clip playing some of a file's channels (or split to mono clips) played the proxy's whole mix: only clips that play everything take the proxy's sound. (4) Playback never decoded a proxy's sound that was not decoded at load (proxies attached later, or a drive ejected mid-session), so it stayed silent: the real-time mixer asks `MediaPool::requestAudio` to decode it in the background. (5) Beside-the-original names came from the media path with its interpretation, and A001.mov and A001.mp4 shared one proxy: names come from the file on disk, a clash gets a few letters of hash, and a file imported twice is made once. (6) Camera proxies matched any name beginning with the clip's ("Clip10" for "Clip1") and other originals of the project: the name must go on after a separator, the project's own files are skipped and the shape must match. (7) The render cache keyed frames on the old rule: it hashes the file the picture is read from. (8) Replacing a proxy deleted the old one before the new one was in place: decoders let go first and the new file replaces the old in one rename, with its failure reported. (9) A stereo file offline showed its proxy in the left eye and Media Offline in the right: both eyes show the proxy. (10) Proxies on with the proxy file gone showed nothing: the file. (11) A proxy without sound for a file with sound no longer skips Link Media; ProRes and DNxHR proxies to .mp4 are refused with the reason. Left: a dead network share is still looked at (every two seconds, from the audio thread too) while its files are offline. **Tests:** media `proxyFormatsAndOfflineEditing` (ProRes to .mp4 refused; a missing proxy with proxies on giving the file; each HDR proxy's picture space; delivery showing the red slate and silence, viewing the proxy; the real-time mixer hearing the proxy shortly after asking; a channel-picked clip silent; "pxsrc_moved0" and the project's own "pxsrc_moved_b" not taken as proxies), app `proxyMediaFromTheBin` (a sound-less proxy asking for the file; A.mp4 and A.mov getting two proxies in one batch).
- [x] 2. Drawn review annotations with replies, status and version comparison (S–M). The review page (core/ReviewPage) gets a drawing layer over the picture (a canvas fitted to the frame inside the player, its strokes in 0..1 of the frame: pen, arrow, box and ellipse, five colours, undo and clear; drawing pauses playback and belongs to the frame it was drawn on, refused on another frame until its note is added or it is cleared), drawings shown while paused on a note's frame or range (and the editor's own drawn markers); notes as a drawing alone; replies inline; a status of Open, In progress or Done (`done` kept for older readers); each note's version. Notes files (`montageReview: 2`) carry status, replies, drawing, version and update time; loading one merges by note id (new replies added, the later status taken). Versions: `reviewVersions` reads a folder's pages ("<name> - Review.html" is 1, "… v<n>.html" n); `reviewPackage` takes the next number (or `ReviewExportOptions::version`) and, with `compare`, the latest earlier version whose page and copy are both there (`readReviewPage` reads a page's own settings back), which the page plays side by side or under a wipe, kept on the same frame of the cut (by time, so another rate or start works) and showing where the earlier version has no picture. Frame stepping no longer loses frames when keys come faster than seeks. Core: `ReviewStroke`, `parseReviewDrawing`/`reviewDrawingJson` (unknown tools, colours and malformed points left out; widths and points clamped; 200 strokes of 4000 points at most), `ReviewNote` status, replies, version, id and drawing; markers named with a tick or half circle and the later version, replies in the comment, drawing and note id kept (`Marker::drawing`, `Marker::reviewId`, saved with the project); `addImportedMarkers` brings markers from the same notes up to date where the editor moved them and never lets a note replace a marker (the next free frame). App: ReviewOverlay paints the drawings of markers at the playhead over the Program monitor (Playback › Show Review Drawings, remembered); Export for Review's Version (the next by default) and Compare; Import Review Notes says how many it brought up to date. MCP `montage_export_review` `version`/`compare` (returns `version` and `compares_with`), `montage_import_markers` returns `added`/`updated`, and the project summary counts a marker's drawing strokes. **Tests:** core `reviewPages` (strokes kept, defaulted, clamped and capped; notes with status, replies, versions, ids and a drawing alone; their markers' names, comments, drawings and ids; a note at an editor's marker and two notes on one frame all kept; imported again: updated where moved; markers without an id replacing as before; the page's version, previous and drawings read back; saved and loaded with the project), media `reviewPackages` (versions 1 and 2 found, the next is 3 comparing with 2's range, the same number replacing without comparing, version 0 refused; a drawn note with replies and a status imported over MCP to the next free frame, then brought up to date), app `reviewDrawingsAndVersions` (a drawn line painted over the Program monitor on its frame and not with the option off or elsewhere, a marker's range; the dialog's next version and Compare, a v2 copy queued and its page; notes imported twice adding one marker; its drawing at its frame). The page was also driven in Chromium: drawing, a drawing-only note, reply, status, side-by-side and wipe comparison, saving and merging a changed file.
  - Review fixes (2fe063a): (1) the page's `display` rules beat the `hidden` attribute, so comparing always covered the old version with "Not in version 1 here", the drawing toolbar and every reply box were always shown and empty reply blocks drew a border: `[hidden]` always hides. (2) Importing notes again replaced the marker whole, losing replies another copy had and letting whichever file came last set the status and colour: replies from both are kept, the status set later (`Marker::reviewStatusAt`, from the page's `statusUpdated`) wins, and the colour stays. (3) On the page a reply in an older copy undid a status change made since: the status has its own time. (4) A note moved to a free frame took its drawing off the frame it was drawn on (over another shot at a cut), and could be pushed past the end: it goes to the nearest free frame inside the cut and keeps its drawing on its frame (`Marker::drawnAt`, used by the Program monitor and the page). (5) A sequence name with [ or ] was read as a wildcard (and the case was ignored), so versions were missed and v1 overwritten: pages are matched by name exactly. (6) Markers imported before notes kept their ids were added again on a new import: one at the same frame, by the same reviewer, saying the same, is taken as that note. (7) A second finger or another mouse button while drawing mixed strokes: one pointer, the main button. (8) Drawings showed over the old version in a wipe: kept to the new version's side. (9) Notes loaded from a file without versions were saved as the current version: as not said. (10) Comparing during playback could seek the old version constantly on a slow computer: put right only after a quarter of a second's drift, at most every 0.75 s. (11) A sequence titled "@@TITLE@@" froze the export (the placeholders were looked for again in what was put in): filled in one pass. Left: an earlier page of the same name from another project in the same folder counts as the version before. **Tests:** core `reviewPages` (the nearest free frames and drawn frames; colour kept; replies from two copies and the later status; an earlier marker without an id taken as the note; the cut's last frame; `drawnAt` on the page; placeholders in a title), media `reviewPackages` (the drawn frame beside a marker; [ ] and case in names), app `reviewDrawingsAndVersions` (a drawing shown on its drawn frame, not the marker's). The page was driven in Chromium again: the toolbar, reply box, empty replies and the old version hidden until wanted; an editor's drawing on its drawn frame; an older copy's reply not undoing a status; a second pointer and the right button ignored.
- [x] 3. Colour-chart calibration (S–M). render/ColorChart: the ColorChecker Classic's published sRGB values; `sampleChart` measures the 24 patches' middle halves through a homography from the patch area's outline (any four corners, so a chart at an angle is read too); `detectChart` finds one with no outline given: a 360-wide copy and summed-area tables, every rectangle of four shapes in four turns (upright, upside down, on either side) scored by first the grey row (white down to black, and different enough not to be a plain wall) and then how well any 3 x 4 colour matrix maps the patches to the chart (least squares), then refined edge by edge; `fitChart` fits a 3 x 3 matrix and offset in linear light (a 2.4 gamma), held gently towards no change, with the patches' mean CIE76 delta E before and after and the worst after. The Chart Match effect (Color, its nine matrix values, offsets and an amount) applies it through gamma tables, in parallel; Generate LUT includes it. App: Clip › Match Colour Chart… (ChartMatchDialog: the clip's frame as decoded, before its effects, with the outline found or dragged by its corners and the patch grid drawn through it; each patch as shot, as matched and as the chart has it; delta E live as the outline moves; Find Chart; every clip of the media) puts the Chart Match first in the clip's effects in one undo step, replacing one there. MCP `montage_match_chart` (found or from `corners`, `same_media`, `measure_only`; corners and delta E back). **Tests:** render `colorChartCalibration` (a chart under a warm cast found to within a few pixels with a tiny residual; its patches as shot; the fit taking delta E from over 5 to under 1; the effect grading the picture to the chart's values, none of it at amount 0; found upside down and on its side with the patches in the chart's own order; no chart on a gradient), media `matchChartOverMcp` (measured only, nothing changed; both clips from given corners; the same effect replaced again; bad corners refused), app `matchColourChart` (found as the dialog opens; the outline moved off it and Find Chart putting it back; applied first in the clip's effects as one undo step, the other clip untouched; the played picture within delta E 4 of the chart; every clip of the media; Cancel changing nothing).
  - Review fixes (829c84d): (1) the chart was measured as Rec.709 but the effect works on the picture in the sequence's working space after the colour group's pre-clip grade and an on-set CDL, so in an HDR or wide-gamut sequence (and behind a group grade) the fit was applied to the wrong values and the delta E shown was not what played: `chartFrame` gives the picture as the effect receives it, `sampleChart` takes its space and converts to Rec.709 values (no tone mapping; HLG through the same OOTF as the compositor), and the effect records the space (`space`) and works in linear Rec.709 through it. (2) The per-pixel work was single-threaded (an OpenMP pragma the build ignores): rows in parallel. (3) Premultiplied alpha was ignored, so empty pixels took the offset and edges the wrong colour: straight colour, empty pixels skipped. (4) Finding the chart took up to seconds on tall frames, before the dialog appeared: the search copy is capped on its longest side, searched coarse to fine (about nine times fewer places), and runs in the background with Apply off until it ends. (5) The gamma table crushed shadows (0.01 became 0.002) and clipped colour outside the gamut: exact through the fourth root of the light, negatives mirrored, so no change is no change. (6) Matrix values were limited to ±4 at three decimals: ±16 at four, offsets at five (the Inspector shows them). (7) Grade versions counted the Chart Match as part of a grade and lost it on switching: it stays under every version. (8) Apply worked with no chart found: it waits for a chart found or an outline placed, with every patch inside the picture. (9) MCP: corners off the frame, in a line or crossed are refused, `clip` takes a name, and an audio clip is refused. (10) A pixel that is not a number could read past the table: black. (11) The corners move with the keys (1–4 or [ ] pick one, the arrows move it, Shift ten pixels). **Tests:** render `colorChartCalibration` (turned the other way and in a tall frame; outlines in a line or off the picture incomplete; no change keeping shadows, highlights and negatives; empty and half-covered pixels; NaN; the chart read back and graded in PQ, HLG, P3 and Rec.2020; not a grade effect), media `matchChartOverMcp` (corners in a line or off the frame refused; by name; in a PQ sequence behind a group's pre-clip grade, what plays as close to the chart as promised), app `matchColourChart` (Apply off while searching; the corners moved by keys; a picture without a chart not applied).

Multi-object masks with edge refinement (S–M). core/ObjectMask: a mask holds further objects (`more`), each with its own clicks, segmented frames, name and `subtract`; `maskLogits` joins the added objects (the largest logit) and cuts the subtracted ones out, on each frame any added object was segmented; saved with the project (`more`, `name`, `subtract`). render/ClipAnalysis: `withObjectPoint`, `withObjectPrompts`, `segmentClipObjectFrame` and `trackClipObject` take the object (`which`), `withNewObject` (to add or cut out; the first when the mask has none), `withoutObject` (the next object that adds taking the first's place), `withObjectSubtracted`, `currentObject` (a chosen index clamped to the objects there are). Edges, for every mask shape: `consolidateMatte` (a closing by distance transforms: holes and gaps narrower than twice the setting filled, the outline kept) and `refineMatteEdges` (a colour-line matte: each pixel near the edge placed between the sure object's and the sure background's colours nearby, mixed in by the amount), the mask's Consolidate, Refine Edges and Refine Radius. App: the Inspector's Objects row (the list, + add, − cut out, Remove, Cut Out), the Program monitor's clicks going to the chosen object and its tint showing the whole mask. MCP `montage_object_mask` (`object` an index, `new` or `new_cut`; `points` at `at`, segmented at once; `track` forward, backward or both; `subtract`, `name`, `remove`; each object's clicked and segmented frames back). **Tests:** core `projectRoundTrip` (a named, cut-out further object saved and loaded), render `objectMaskMatte` (added objects joined, a cut-out one taken away, a frame with only a cut-out object no mask; through an effect), render `maskEdgeRefinement` (a blocky mask snapped to a disc's edge, half of it in between, a flat picture leaving it be; a hole and a slit closed with the outline kept; a loose ellipse refined onto the disc through an effect), media `objectMasksSeveralObjects` (two balls clicked as two objects, each tracked on its own with the real model, both in the matte, one cut out, the first removed; over MCP a cut-out object clicked and tracked both ways, added back, removed, bad input refused), app `objectMaskFromViewer` (the object list's add, cut out, choose, undo and remove). Review fixes (4add76b): (1) Consolidate set every pixel more than a pixel inside the shape to 1, flattening the inner half of a feathered edge, and gave what it filled a 1 px edge: it now changes the mask only near what the closing adds, which takes the closed shape's signed distance (outside it, from a transform of its own, so no haze spreads round) through the mask's own feather; (2) Consolidate's loops and Refine Edges' sampling pass run across threads, Refine's colour planes are at least halved in size, and Consolidate returns early when the closing adds nothing (the usual case); (3) removing the first object promoted the next even when it was a cut-out, turning it into the whole mask: the next object that adds takes its place, and cut-outs left alone stay cut out under an empty first object (removing that removes the mask); (4) Refine Edges' band stopped at its radius, leaving a hard step across a wide feather: a soft edge running on from the band (partway and still changing; not a flat partway interior, nor a stray pixel elsewhere) is now unknown too, the sure colours are looked for beyond it, and gradients are left to themselves (neither Consolidate nor Refine applies); (5) the object being picked was an effect setting (`mask.object`): choosing one made an undo step, marked the project changed, was refused in a read-only project, threw away cached frames and could be keyframed, and a segmentation finishing after a choice was amended into the wrong step; it is now the editor's choice (`EditorState::maskObject`, no undo step), and an object's name is left out of frame keys; (6) Consolidate counted beyond the frame as inside, joining shapes to the frame's edge: it works on a copy with a ring of background; (7) − and MCP `new_cut` on a mask with no object made an object that adds: they are refused (− and + are off) until the first object is picked; (8) the viewer tinted only the whole mask, so a cut-out object showed nowhere: with several objects the chosen one is tinted too (amber when it adds, red when cut out); (9) Refine Edges clamped colours to 0–1 (HDR and super-whites lost), counted transparent pixels as black and cut off abruptly where object and background colours meet: colours are kept as they are, transparent pixels are neither sampled nor changed, and the mix eases off as the colours draw together; (10) MCP tracking without `at` started from the clip's start (failing for an object clicked later, and losing the backward pass): it starts from the first frame the object was clicked on; `remove` with other settings is refused. Tests: render `maskEdgeRefinement` (a 20 px feathered disc's ramp kept while a slit in it fills; two feathered discs joined with no haze round them; nothing joined to the frame's edge; a 30 px feathered edge refined onto the disc; a stray faint pixel changing nothing; a mask at 0.96 inside refined; colours above white and transparent pixels), render `renderCacheKeysAndFrames` (renaming objects keeps the key, a cut-out changes it), media `objectMasksSeveralObjects` (a cut-out kept under an empty first; the next adding object promoted; no cut-out on a mask with no object; nothing saved for the choice; tracking from the clicked frame without `at`; remove with a name refused), app `objectMaskFromViewer` (+ and − off until there is an object; choosing makes no undo step).
