GPU and NPU inference: CoreML, WinML/DirectML, CUDA, OpenVINO (M). media/Inference: `InferenceMode` Auto (ONNX Runtime 1.23+'s device policy PREFER_GPU), Efficient (PREFER_NPU) or Cpu, set by the app and overridden by `MONTAGE_INFERENCE`; `openModel` (used by all 17 model loaders) opens each session with the policy on a copy of the loader's options and, when the device chosen refuses the model, again with the loader's own options on the processor, counting the fallbacks with the last reason (`inferenceFallbacks`, `lastInferenceFallback`); `inferenceDevices` lists the runtime's devices (provider, vendor, CPU/GPU/NPU; the providers built in on older runtimes); `registerInferenceLibraries` registers the execution-provider plugin libraries in a folder with the process's one environment (so WinML's, CUDA's, OpenVINO's or QNN's plugin packages work without a rebuild); `inferenceCheck` runs a 65-byte Identity model written on the spot. App: Tools › AI Models Run On (Automatic, Power Saving, Processor Only; remembered as `ai/inference`; Devices… with the fallbacks), plugin libraries registered at start from `execution-providers` beside the program and in the data folder. CLI `devices`. The test suites keep models on the processor so results do not depend on the machine's GPU. **Tests:** media `inferenceDevices` (a CPU among the devices; the test model running in each mode; names parsed; the environment variable winning over the setting; an empty folder registering nothing), app `aiRunsOn` (the menu's three choices exclusive and remembered, Devices… there).
  - Review fixes (c59f7fa): (1) only opening a model fell back to the processor, so a device that took a model and then failed running it (CoreML is chosen by default on Macs) left that model failing until a restart, and changing the choice did nothing to models already open: the loaders hold an `OrtModel`, which runs the model again on the processor when the device fails mid-run (and keeps it there), opens it again on its next run after the choice changes, and serialises runs on a GPU or NPU (not every device takes two at once); speech enhancement, translation and voice cloning, run a frame or a token at a time, stay on the processor (`ModelRuns::OnProcessor`). (2) A missing or broken model file counted as a device fallback: only a model that then opened on the processor counts. (3) A library linked under two names was registered twice: links are skipped and a file or name already taken is not registered again; why a library was refused is kept (`lastInferenceLibraryError`) and shown in Devices… and `montage-cli devices`. (4) The command line ignored the provider folders and the saved choice: `initInference` does both for the app and the CLI, and `devices` says when the test model went to the processor. (5) With MONTAGE_INFERENCE set the menu still offered choices it ignored: they are fixed with the reason, and the value is read in any case. (6) The device features need ONNX Runtime 1.22, not 1.23; an older runtime lists only the processor (where its models run). (7) The test model is opened from memory, not a temporary file. **Tests:** media `inferenceDevices` (skipped without ONNX Runtime; the variable restored however it ends; any case; a mode changed after a model opened; a text file named as a library and a link to it not registered, and why), app `aiRunsOn` (fixed while the variable is set).
- [x] 6. Voice conversion for ADR (S–M). media/VoiceClone: `convertVoice` (speech to speech, as Resolve's Voice Convert): Chatterbox's speech encoder turns the speech into its speech tokens (the words, timing and delivery without the voice, 25 a second) and the voice sample into its speaker embedding, features and prompt tokens; its flow-matching decoder and vocoder say the tokens again in that voice, exactly as long as the speech (a piece ends up to 40 ms short and is padded with silence), so it stays in sync. The encoder hears ten seconds at most, so the speech is converted a piece at a time, `voiceConversionCuts` cutting each piece of at most 9.5 s at the quietest 20 ms in its last three seconds; silent pieces stay silent. `voiceSoundFrom` reads a file's sound as `voiceSampleFrom` does but whole. core/Adr: `voiceConversionRange` (what a clip plays and a second's handle either side, as far as its media goes) and `addConvertedTake` (the converted file as a take of the clip, picked, at the same moment). Takes keep their own channel picks (`Take::channels`, saved with the project), so a production clip playing channel 4 plays the converted mono file's sound and gets channel 4 back when its original is picked again; linked clips follow a picked take only when they can play it as they are (a sound-only take no longer swaps the picture for a WAV, and a clip on a channel the take does not have stays). App: Clip › Convert Voice… (app/VoiceConvert: the selected sound, or the sound linked to the selected picture; into the voice of another clip, those with a transcript first, or a recording; the speaker's consent checkbox) and the ADR panel's Convert Voice… (the chosen take, offering the guide clip under the cue first); the file goes in Voiceover beside the project with its provenance in its INFO comment and the bin (keyword AI voice), and becomes a picked take in one undo step after its import. CLI `convert-voice --voice --consent [--from --to --voice-from --voice-to]`. MCP `montage_convert_voice` (`clip`, `voice_clip` or `voice_media`, `handles`, `consent` required). **Tests:** core `convertedVoiceTakes` (the range with handles and clamped to the media; the take in place at 1 s of the file; the clip's channel kept with its take, the picture and a clip on channel 2 not following; saved and read back; back to the original with its channel), media `voiceConversion` (pieces cut at the quiet gaps, none longer than 9.5 s; a short voice sample and silence refused; with the model: JFK's 11 s in a synthetic voice, the same length, Whisper hearing his words, one speaker with the voice sample and two with JFK; over MCP refused without consent, a voice, or with the clip as its own voice; a trimmed clip's 5 s with handles as its picked take at the right place, the file's note and keyword), app `convertVoice` (the clip a selection converts; the dialog's voices, the consent and a recording; converted as a picked take beside the project; one undo giving the original back), app `adrPanelRecordsTakes` (Convert Voice… off until a cue has a take). Done on the processor: 11 s of speech converts in about 35 s on four cores.
  - Review fixes (081a00b): (1) every take added from the bin, MCP or the ADR panel now played its file's whole mix, and picking one lost a clip's channel pick (the lav of a poly WAV giving way to boom and lavs together), and takes in older projects did the same: an added take plays the clip's channels where its file has them, and a take saved before takes had channels plays the clip's (`channels` is now always written). (2) A picture left on its take under a sound-only take stayed there when another take was picked (the original's sound under another take's picture): a linked clip on any take's media follows a take it can play, from where it is. (3) A clip faster than real time had the end of its last frame missing from the converted sound: the range takes the frame's whole span. (4) A loud last piece under 0.1 s came out silent: only silent pieces stay silent. (5) A clip name with a slash or colon made no file: such characters are replaced (`convertedVoiceStem`). (6) A file left behind when its import failed is deleted, and MCP's when the take could not be added; a take that cannot be added says the sound is in the bin; MCP files the media in Voiceover as the app does. (7) The clip is looked up again after the model's download dialog. **Tests:** core `convertedVoiceTakes` (another four-channel take keeping channel 4; the picture catching up from B; a project without take channels; a 2x clip's range; a name with a slash and quotes), media `voiceConversion` (the refusals' reasons; the bin).
- [x] 7. C2PA Content Credentials (S–M). Through c2pa-rs (the Content Authenticity Initiative's library, MIT or Apache-2.0): `scripts/build-c2pa.sh` builds its C API crate (c2pa-c-ffi 0.91.0 from crates.io, checksum pinned, Rust's own cryptography, no HTTP) as a static library in a prefix CMake finds (`C2PA_ROOT`, `MONTAGE_WITH_C2PA`, `MONTAGE_REQUIRE_C2PA`); CI builds and caches it on Linux, macOS and Windows (MSYS2's Rust). core/Provenance: `sequenceProvenance` (what the enabled clips in a range, and sequences nested in them, play and which AI models made or changed it: Object Removal, AI Frames, generated, cloned or converted speech as Generated; Super Scale, Face Refinement, Blemish Remover, Relight, Enhance Speech, Voice Isolation as Enhanced; masks, mattes, depth and face finding as Assisted), `iptcSourceType`. media/ContentCredentials: `signContentCredentials` signs a file in place (into a copy beside it that then takes its place, keeping its permissions): a c2pa.created action by Montage (IPTC composite, or compositeWithTrainedAlgorithmicMedia when a model generated any of it), a c2pa.edited action per AI use with its IPTC source type, a CAWG training-and-mining assertion saying it may not train AI unless allowed, and the credentials its source files carried (a camera's, a stock library's) as componentOf ingredients with c2pa.placed; `readContentCredentials`/`credentialsLabel` (state Trusted, Valid or Invalid with the problems, signer, time, the app or device, actions, whether AI made it); `checkCredentialSigner` signs a moment of silence in memory, so a refused certificate is known before a long export; MP4, MOV, M4A, WAV, MP3, JPEG, PNG, TIFF, WebP, HEIF, AVIF, AVI, PDF and SVG (not MKV, MXF or WebM). render/Exporter: `ExportSettings::credentialCertificate/credentialKey/credentialAllowAiTraining` sign the export with the range's provenance; a file that cannot hold them is refused before rendering and a failed signing fails the export, leaving no unsigned file. App: the Export dialog's Credentials row (Sign with Content Credentials, the signing certificate and key chosen once and checked, Allow AI models to train on it; off for formats that cannot hold them or a build without the library); imported files' credentials are read in the background into the bin's Content Credentials column and tooltip (not an undo step; kept through undo and redo). CLI `credentials <file> [--json]`, `sign <file> --cert --key [--algorithm] [--project] [--allow-ai-training]`. MCP `montage_render` `credentials` {certificate, key, allow_ai_training} (returns the AI uses recorded), `montage_read_credentials` (`full` adds the manifest store). Throwaway test certificates in tests/data/c2pa (a test authority and a signer, e-mail protection use). Signatures are not time-stamped (no HTTP in the build), and a signer is Valid rather than Trusted until it is on a trust list. **Tests:** core `provenanceForCredentials` (a plain cut's file and no AI; generated, enhanced and assisted uses each once; a range, a turned-off effect and clip; AI frames; generated, converted and cloned speech through a nested sequence; the bin field), media `contentCredentials` (the manifest's actions, source types, training assertion and title; formats; the label; the test signer accepted, an authority's own certificate and a missing key refused; a file without credentials; a signed WAV read back Valid with its signer, title and generator, still playing, permissions kept; MKV refused; a changed file Invalid; over MCP a refused certificate failing before rendering, MKV refused, a signed export saying AI made it and carrying the camera file's credentials as an ingredient), app `contentCredentialsInTheApp` (the dialog's controls on for WAV and off for MKV; signing without a certificate refused with nothing written; a signed export; imported, the bin showing who signed it, no undo step of its own, kept through undo and redo).
  - Review fixes (575b7d5): (1) c2pa.h declares its functions imported from a DLL on Windows, so linking the static library there would fail: `C2PA_DYNAMIC_LOADING` is defined for it, and rustc's native libraries keep their order and repeats (the Windows GNU runtime needs them). (2) A PNG or TIFF export is numbered frames, which passed the extension check and failed after the whole render: `exportCarriesCredentials` refuses image sequences and GIFs up front (dialog, MCP, exporter). (3) Any reader error that did not say "not found" read as broken credentials, so a file c2pa-rs cannot follow showed "(invalid)" in the bin and, as a source, failed the export and deleted it: errors are sorted by kind (no manifest: none; manifest, signature, verification or decoding errors: invalid; anything else: unreadable, nothing said), and a source whose credentials cannot be taken in is left out rather than failing the export. (4) Provenance missed colour groups' grades (`gradeChain`) and stems split by Spleeter (Enhanced), and counted a clip starting at the out point (exclusive). (5) Signing a file that already had credentials (montage-cli sign on a camera original) replaced them: they are kept as its parent ingredient, with a c2pa.opened action. (6) Only the dialog and MCP checked the signer, so a render-queue job with a moved or refused certificate rendered fully and then failed: the exporter checks it first. (7) The C2PA cache key now includes the Rust version (MSYS2 updates its toolchain). (8) With signing ticked and a format that cannot hold credentials the export went out unsigned silently: the checkbox says so; MCP refuses a `credentials` that is not an object. (9) Two reads of one file's credentials could finish out of order: only the latest is applied. Tests: core `provenanceForCredentials` (the out point, a colour group's Relight, a stem), media `contentCredentials` (signed again keeping the camera's credentials as parent; MKV and a refused signer leaving the file byte for byte and nothing beside it; a file c2pa-rs cannot follow not read as invalid credentials; frames refused, one MP4 allowed; MCP `credentials` not an object), app `contentCredentialsInTheApp` (ticked for MKV says unsigned; settings cleared after).
- [x] 8. Lens-profile correction (M). core/LensProfile reads lensfun's database format (versions 1 and 2: cameras, lenses, mounts and their adapters, calibrations per sensor; `real-focal` and version 1's `<real-focal-length>`; focal lengths and widest aperture guessed from the name as lensfun does) with Montage's own parser rather than linking lensfun (LGPL-3.0); the name guessing and the Hermite interpolation are ported from lensfun's code and credited as such (THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md): `findCamera` by the words of a recording's Camera Model, `lensesFor` (the camera's mount, then those it takes by adapter; not lenses measured on a smaller sensor; duplicate names by the nearest sensor), `fixedLens` (a camera whose mount the database does not list for interchangeable lenses), interpolation as lensfun's (the calibration for the picture's crop factor; a Hermite spline over focal length on the terms times the focal length; vignetting by inverse distance weighting over focal, 4/aperture and 0.1/distance), and `Correction`: lensfun's normalisation (half the calibration sensor's shorter side; pixel spans between the outer pixels' centres), its coordinate chain (scale, a fisheye's projection to rectilinear with the measured or nominal focal length, then poly3/poly5/ptlens distortion, then poly3 or linear TCA for red and blue), the pa vignetting model (r = 1 at the calibration sensor's corner), and a radial autoscale (binary search, at 129 angles, for the smallest scale whose frame edge comes from inside the recorded frame, or the largest showing all of it, within the radii where the correction still rises). A recording's crop factor is the camera's with the recording's frame as large as fits on a sensor of the calibration's shape (a 16:9 mode takes the full width), times a Recording Crop. Checked against lensfun 0.3.4 on every lens of the database whose name is unique (1813 cases of distortion and geometry, TCA and vignetting at 1920 x 1080 between calibrated focal lengths): positions within 0.26 px (lensfun works in single precision) and vignetting within 0.1 %, except one fisheye zoom's vignetting (Canon EF 8-15mm, 8.6 % at 12.5 mm f/4.8; Montage follows lensfun's current interpolation code, the difference from 0.3.4 not traced further). The effect `lens_correction` (Distort; render/VideoFx `lensCorrection`) holds the lens's profile as XML (`lensXml`, so a project renders the same without the database) with its camera and lens names; focal length, aperture, distance and scale keyframe; distortion, chromatic aberration, vignetting and fisheye-to-rectilinear switch off one by one; Framing fills the frame, shows the whole picture or neither. Vignetting is undone in linear light through the working space's transfer (render/TransferTables, moved out of Processing.cpp), then the frame is resampled once (red and blue from their own places, coverage from green's, the recorded frame's edge pixels reaching its edge). media/LensDatabase: the database as a model pack (lensfun's 56 files at commit 43f9e00 from raw.githubusercontent.com, 5.1 MB, each SHA-256 checked; `scripts/fetch-models.sh` too), else `MONTAGE_LENS_DB`, else lensfun's own installed or updated copy; `suggestLens` (the camera from the media's Camera Model, its fixed lens), `lensChoiceOf`, `applyLensCorrection` (first in each clip's effects, replacing one there and keeping its other settings). App: Clip › Correct Lens… and the Inspector's Choose Lens… (app/LensCorrectionDialog: searchable camera and lens lists, focal length within the lens's range, aperture, what the profile corrects, every clip of the media); adding the effect from the Inspector opens it and puts it first; Reset keeps the lens. MCP `montage_correct_lens` (camera and lens by name words, focal, aperture, `params`, `same_media`, `list` with `query`). Tests: core lensProfiles (parsing, finding, interpolation, lensfun's numbers for a zoom with TCA, a stereographic fisheye, the GoPro HERO11's 8:7 sensor at 16:9 and vignetting, the fill and whole-picture scales, the XML round trip), render lensCorrectionEffect, media correctLensOverMcp, app correctLensInTheApp; test data tests/data/lensfun/test-lenses.xml. Not done: DNG opcode lists (WarpRectilinear, FixVignetteRadial) for camera raw stills, and the lens centre offsets lensfun's format allows (one lens uses them).
  - Review fixes (88bcf3a): the autoscale searched past where a barrel polynomial turns back (poly3 k1 < 0, ptlens a < 0), so 316 of 1501 rectilinear profiles filled at 1/16 scale; it now stays within the rising part (test: a poly3 barrel lens). `findCamera` took a maker's name in another language for a model name, so an unknown model of a known maker found one of its cameras; model names must now have a word that is not the maker's. Aperture "Not known" was applied as the widest (LensChoice's -1 now means the lens's widest, 0 no vignetting correction). The dialog follows typed camera and lens names (by text, not the list's index; Apply waits for a lens named) and finds the clip again after the database download. Numbers in lens names are read without the C library's locale. MCP: a named fixed-lens camera brings its lens; string, range and whole-number checks. The database is looked for again while none was found. Dropping Lens Correction on a clip opens Correct Lens… (first in its effects). Show Whole Picture on a fisheye stays at the smallest scale (its picture reaches 90 degrees off the axis).
  - Review fixes (163a076): the Effects panel's Lens Correction opens Correct Lens… too; cameras sharing a name (one per sensor shape or mount) keep the one picked; a camera whose model is named as its maker ("AEE DV") is found again; MCP `focal` and `aperture` must be numbers.
- [x] 9. SDI/HDMI/NDI reference output (M). render/ReferenceOutput: the broadcast modes (720p to 4K DCI, 23.98 to 60, 1080i; a DeckLink device lists its own; `matchMode` at the sequence's rate: its size, else the smallest that holds it), the signal a sequence goes out as (`signalFor`: BT.709, BT.2020, PQ or HLG, P3-D65 PQ as BT.2020 PQ mastered on P3; sRGB and P3 SDR as BT.709; or the viewer's Rec. 709 picture; HDR needs 10 bits), `fitInto` (fit, fill or 1:1, bilinear), and a `Scheduler`: a packing thread converts each submitted frame (sequence space to the signal's, fitted, limited-range 4:2:2 Y'CbCr kept at 16 bits) into a ring of up to 40 frames, the six nearest the playhead first, and a sending thread sends the frame at the playhead (plus the picture offset, ahead in the playing direction), repeating the last and counting a late frame when the one due is not ready while playing; embedded sound follows the mode's cadence (1601/1602 at 29.97) from a cursor that runs on and resyncs only past 1.5 frames of drift, at speed 1 only. render/VideoPacking: v210 (FFmpeg's layout and tail), UYVY and P216 from the 16-bit 4:2:2. **NDI** (render/NdiOutput; the NDI SDK's headers in third_party/ndi, MIT): the runtime loaded when first used (NDI_RUNTIME_DIR_V6/V5, then the system's), a source "Montage" sending P216 or UYVY asynchronously, double-buffered, clocked by the runtime, with `<ndi_color_info>` metadata, 100 ns timecode and planar float sound; receivers counted. **DeckLink** (render/DeckLinkOutput; Desktop Video SDK 12.0 headers in third_party/decklink, under Blackmagic's permissive header licence): libDeckLinkAPI (Linux) or the DeckLinkAPI framework (macOS) loaded when first used; devices with their modes, 10-bit and HDR support and channels; scheduled playback (5 frames, 3 preroll) in v210 or UYVY, RP188 timecode, 48 kHz 32-bit sound scheduled with its frame, and HDR metadata on each frame (EOTF, colorimetry, primaries and white of the mastering display, its luminance, MaxCLL and MaxFALL). PlaybackController: a sink on the render worker gets each frame rendered (at the mode's resolution while playing, at least the whole picture parked; not through the render cache), the playhead and speed, and its own mix of the sequence's sound. App: Playback › Video Output › Reference Output… (app/ReferenceOutputPanel: interface, device, mode or the sequence's, bit depth, colour, HDR metadata, framing, embedded sound, picture and sound offsets, status with frames sent, late and NDI receivers; kept between sessions; opened again when the sequence's format changes). MCP `montage_reference_output` on the agent link (an app tool LiveLink answers itself: list, status, start, stop). Tests: render videoPacking (v210, UYVY and P216 against hand-packed words, colour bars' code values), referenceOutput (modes, signals, fitting, the scheduler on a manual virtual device: parked, playing, offsets, cadence, late frames), ndiOutput (a stand-in NDI runtime, tests/plugins/fake_ndi.c: the frames, metadata, timecode and sound it was sent), deckLinkWithoutDriver; app referenceOutputFollowsProgram (the panel on the virtual device, MONTAGE_VIRTUAL_OUTPUT: frames follow seeks; the MCP tool). Not done: DeckLink on Windows (its COM API; NDI works there), AJA cards, capture (input), a stand-in DeckLink driver to test against (no card here: the DeckLink path is built and unchecked), interlaced modes fed field-rendered frames (a whole frame goes out in both fields), SD modes.

- [x] 10. Node-based grade graph (L). core/GradeGraph: a clip's grade as `GradeGraph{nodes, links}` (`Clip::grade`, empty: the effect stack as before); `GradeNode` corrector, parallel, layer or key_mixer (label, switch, layer blend modes, place, `shared`); `GradeLink{from, to, input, key}` (0: the clip's picture, the output). A corrector's effects are the clip's own marked with its key (`Effect::node`, so they survive splits, pastes and copies whose ids change) or its shared node's (`Sequence::sharedNodes`, edited once through `effectChain` for every clip using it); its settings a hidden `grade_node` effect (mix, key input gain, invert and mode: multiply, add, subtract or replace; and the mask.* window, qualifier and depth). `validGrade` (unique keys, known kinds, each input fed once, pictures to picture inputs and keys to key inputs, no loop, the output fed), `gradeOrder`, `serialGrade`; edits `convertToNodes` (each colour effect a node, in series), `addGradeNode` (serial, parallel and layer joining the mixer the anchor already feeds, outside: the anchor's key inverted), `addKeyMixer`, `removeGradeNode` (what it fed takes what fed it; a mixer left with one input goes too), `connect`/`disconnectGradeNodes` (refused when the graph would not hold together), `setGradeNode`, `moveEffectToNode`, `flattenGrade` (the nodes' effects in the order they ran, shared ones copied in), `share`/`use`/`unshareGradeNode`, `rename`/`removeSharedNode` (an unused one), `setGradeNodeParams`. Saved in projects; grade versions each keep their graph; duplicated sequences get their own shared nodes; colour groups' pre and post grades run around the graph. render/GradeGraph: `applyGradeGraph` runs where the clip's first node effect sits: a plain serial chain in place, otherwise each node after its inputs, a corrector's matte (its window, qualifier and depth, joined with its key input, scaled by its mix) limiting its effects, a parallel mixer weighting its branches by their keys (else averaging them), a layer mixer blending each input over the ones before with its mode where its key is, a key mixer adding keys. Clip LUT export (`bakeClipLut`) bakes the graph and names nodes limited by a window or depth; the render cache hashes shared nodes' effects. Copy and paste: copied clips carry the shared nodes they show into other sequences; Paste Attributes' Colour nodes replace the clip's graph and its effects (Effects pastes those outside a graph); Remove Attributes likewise. App: the Nodes panel (Colour workspace, Window menu): the graph drawn from the picture to the output with picture (green) and key (blue) ports and links, toolbar and context menu (Use Nodes, Serial, Parallel, Layer, Outside, Key Mixer, Remove, Share, Use Shared, Unshare, Flatten), Alt+S/P/L/O, Ctrl+D and Delete taken from the timeline while the canvas has focus, click to pick (`EditorState::selectGradeNode`), drag to move (one undo step), drag a port to link, right-click a link to unlink, double-click to rename. The Inspector shows the picked node (its Window, Key and Mix settings, its effects or its shared node's, Add Video Effect into it) or each node in turn and the effects outside the nodes; a layer mixer's blend modes; Move Up/Down within a node. MCP `montage_grade_nodes` (list, convert, add, key_mixer, remove, connect, disconnect, set with settings, move_effect, share, use_shared, unshare, rename_shared, remove_shared, flatten); `montage_add_effect` with `node` or `shared`; Lua through `montage.grade_nodes`. **Tests:** core: converting, serial, parallel and layer nodes and their mixers, refused links, removals reconnecting, outside nodes, moving effects, renaming, flattening; saved and loaded, split, sequence copies, versions, unsharing; paste of the grade and of effects outside it, shared nodes carried into another sequence and dropped when missing, settings, shared node renames and removals, blend modes. Render: a serial graph matching the stack, parallel branches by their windows and unkeyed averages, a layer mixer's normal and multiply, an outside node, a key input, a switched-off node, a shared node's change seen on two clips, the LUT bake. App: Use Nodes picking the first node with the Inspector showing it alone, picking by clicking, a drag in one undo step, Alt+P, a key linked by dragging, a loop refused, Ctrl+D, rename, share and use on another clip, Delete, Flatten. MCP: convert, add parallel, an effect into a node, its window and mix, share and use, list, refusals, saved.

The child is started with `-u -X utf8 -c` and reads its boot code as the first line of standard input; the boot takes copies of standard input and output for the protocol (one JSON object a line), points standard output at standard error and standard input at nothing (so programs it runs, and reads, cannot break the protocol), says it is ready, and gets the script, its name, arguments, project, the tools' names and whether it is quiet. A failure is "<name>:<line>: <Type>: <message>" from the script's last frame (or the syntax error's line), its own traceback frames printed first; `sys.exit(0)` succeeds, another code fails; a process that dies says its exit code and last line of standard error. The interpreter search also asks the Python found for `sys.executable` (a launcher's or shim's own), tries Homebrew's and python.org's before macOS's `/usr/bin/python3`, and the `py` launcher on Windows, five seconds each. **Tests:** media four threads making 160 calls, a two-line error, a program given `sys.stdout`, "\r" and flush, a module beside the script with a `traceback.py` in the working directory, quiet refusing a SQLite file and a spawned process and allowing `os.putenv` and SQLite in memory; app the Lua example saved as `.py` unchanged.

Remote render nodes driven by `montage-cli` (M). render/RenderFarm: a farm is a shared folder. `submitFarmJob` writes jobs/<id>/ (the id sorts by the sender's time: yyyyMMdd-HHmmsszzz-random) aside and renames it in whole: job.json (name, sequence, stems, the export's settings through `exportSettingsToJson`, which covers every field, ids as text, and its inverse, which keeps defaults for what is missing and refuses wrong types; who sent it, when) and project.json (`projectToJson` with no project path, so every path is absolute). A node claims the oldest waiting job by creating jobs/<id>/claim with `QFile::NewOnly` (one creation succeeds, on local disks, NFS and SMB) holding its own token, then renders on a thread while it writes status.json (state, progress, node, started, a heartbeat count) and nodes/<name>.json every half second, checking for a cancel file, for being stopped, and that its claim is still its own; it ends the job done (encoder kept), failed (a half-written file removed, nothing it did not write) or cancelled, or, stopped, gives it back (claim and status removed). A watcher notices a node that stops answering by its heartbeat count not changing for a minute of the watcher's own time (`FarmHeartbeats`; no clocks compared), and `reclaimFarmJob` takes the job back by renaming the claim aside (one rename succeeds; only a rendering job). `mapFarmPath` (folders not name prefixes, `\` and `/` alike, `to`'s separators) is applied to every string in the project's JSON and the output, watermark and signing files. `cancelFarmJob` (a waiting job at once), `retryFarmJob`, `removeFarmJob` (renamed away first; not while rendering); `renderExport` is the render both the queue and nodes run. `montage-cli farm <folder> submit` takes `render`'s options (`prepareRender`, split from `render`) and `--name`, `jobs|nodes [--json]`, `cancel|retry|remove <id>`; `montage-cli node <folder> [--name] [--map FROM=TO]... [--once] [--poll S]` logs each job and gives its job back on Ctrl+C. The Render Queue panel: Farm Folder… (kept in the settings), Send to Farm on a queued job, the farm's jobs (node, output, status: waiting for a node, rendering n%, cancelling, not answering, done with the encoder, failed with why, cancelled) and running nodes, read every 1.5 s, with Cancel, Retry and Remove. (2) two watchers could judge the same job silent and the second take back the first's fresh claim: a claim is taken back only from the token judged silent (`reclaimFarmJob`'s `claim`, `FarmJob::claim`; (4) Ctrl+C's give-back removed whatever claim was there: only its own; (5) the panel's lists rebuilt themselves from inside their own selection signals, without end: signals are blocked during a rebuild (the queue's list too); (6) Windows paths mapped only in the same letter case, and a map from "/" lost a separator: drive, share and backslash paths match case-insensitively, the root keeps its separator; (7) the panel read the share on the window's thread every 1.5 s, hidden or not: its timer reads on a worker, only while the panel shows; (8) crashed nodes counted as running for ever: a node whose heartbeat has not moved for a minute is not counted; (9) settings reading let a non-object `burnIn`, a non-list `extraAudio`, wrongly typed list values and ids that are not numbers through: refused; (10) the job id now has milliseconds (two jobs in one second listed in order), and `rendered` is documented as counting jobs ended. **Tests:** media a take-back refused for another claim; a node whose claim is replaced stops, removes its own folder and leaves the other's file as written; no `.montage-farm-*` folder left; mapped Windows and share paths in either case, POSIX case kept, maps from the root and to a folder with a trailing separator; the five refused settings; app the farm's status read by the panel's timer on a worker while it shows.

- [x] 14. 3D camera tracking (L). media/CameraSolve: `trackFeatures` follows Shi–Tomasi corners with pyramidal Lucas–Kanade (up to 400 at 960 px wide), topping them up as they leave. `solveCamera` picks keyframes (every 3 % of the width of median motion, or when half the features are gone). It starts from the keyframe pair with the most parallax. The essential matrix comes from an eight-point RANSAC on normalised points, made essential, and decomposed with a cheirality check. Pairs that one homography explains (a camera that only turns, a flat scene) are passed over. Each further keyframe's camera is fitted to the points it sees by pose-only Levenberg–Marquardt with Huber weights, starting from its neighbour's. New points are triangulated (linear, from every registered view) when their rays are at least 0.75° apart and fit within 3 px. A full bundle adjustment runs every five keyframes. The final one also frees the focal length: Levenberg–Marquardt with the Schur complement, 6 parameters per camera plus the focal length, 3×3 point blocks, and the first camera fixed. It is run from fields of view of 30°, 45°, 60° and 80° (or the lens's, when known). The most keyframes registered wins, then the smallest error. Every frame's camera is then fitted to the points. Points that only fit the keyframes are dropped (something moving on its own) and the frames solved again. `orientScene` stands the scene on the plane most points lie on (RANSAC, level enough for a camera tilted at most 60°, refined by least squares within three robust deviations): y up, the first camera 1.6 high and looking along -z. Also `sceneRectangle`, `groundPoint`, the solve as text, and `writeNukeChan` (ZXY rotations, vertical field of view) and `writePointsPly`. render/ClipAnalysis: `solveClipCamera` over the media the clip shows; `clipCameraSolve`, `cameraSolveFrame`, `sceneRectangleAt`; `sceneCornerKeys` projects a scene rectangle into each frame, through both clips' transforms, as Corner Pin keys; `placeSceneTitle` adds a title above the footage on the first track with room. The 3D Camera Tracker effect (Transform) holds the solve and changes nothing in the picture. Its Inspector section has the solve's lens, error and points, with Solve, Place Title (Place At X/Y, width as a share of the distance, Stand Upright, Turn) and Export (.chan and .ply). The Program monitor shows the points tracked in the frame on screen, coloured by their error. MCP `montage_camera_track` (solve with an optional field of view, info, place, export); `montage_add_effect` points there for it. Tests: synthetic tracks with 0.33 px noise and wrong tracks (focal within 5 %, error under 1 px, the camera path within 2 % and 0.2°, wrong tracks left out, a known focal kept); the ground, rectangles, text and exports; a camera that only turns is refused; a ray-cast textured scene rendered as PNGs (focal within 5 %, error under 1 px); over MCP a placed title stays on the ground spot through the shot (within 3 px); the Inspector, viewer points and placement in the app.
  - Review fixes: (1) a shot with fewer than 20 long tracks alive for a stretch made every frame a keyframe, and widening the keyframe step never brought the count under the cap, so the solve looped for ever and Cancel could not stop it: widening is now tried 12 times, then the keyframes are thinned evenly to the cap, and Cancel is checked; (2) one keyframe that did not fit (a blurred one) ended registration in that direction: up to three in a row are now passed over; (3) `montage_add_effect` with `group_stage`, `node` or `shared` still added a bare 3D Camera Tracker; (4) MCP solve added a second tracker when the clip had an unsolved one; (5) a given field of view outside about 3° to 147° failed the focal length's sanity range: a known lens is no longer checked against it; (6) a damaged solve string could ask for a huge allocation before reading anything; (7) a scene with no ground was exported y down (upside down in Nuke and Blender): it is turned y up, the first camera looking along -z; the README notes Nuke's vertical aperture.
  - Not done: zooms (one focal length per shot); lens distortion (correct it first with Lens Correction); masking out moving things before solving (they are dropped as outliers); planar scenes (a homography solve); a 3D view of the scene; placing anything but flat titles (3D models, shadows); a solve on the render farm.
- [x] 15. OGraf graphics (M). render/OGraf: the EBU's open HTML graphics format (as Resolve 21 plays it). `readOGraf` reads a manifest (*.ograf.json): name, main module, real-time and non-real-time support, step count, custom actions, and the data's schema, whose top-level properties become fields (string, multi-line string, a string's choices, number, integer, boolean, or JSON for anything else); `ografDefaultData` gives the schema's defaults. A graphic renders in a headless Chrome, Chromium or Edge already installed (`ografBrowser`: MONTAGE_CHROMIUM, the usual install places, or the PATH), driven over the Chrome DevTools Protocol by Montage's own small WebSocket client (Qt Network; no browser library linked). The browser starts with a profile of its own and port 0 (the port read from DevToolsActivePort, or from what it prints, as a snap's private /tmp hides the file), its output to a file, with its sandbox unless running as root (and again without it if it stops at once for want of one). The page is offline: requests are intercepted, and only `http://ograf.localhost/` (the files really inside the graphic's folder) and Montage's harness page are served; everything else fails at once, and what escapes interception (WebSockets, WebRTC) goes to a proxy that is not there. The harness imports the module, defines its custom element, loads it with the data (`renderType` "realtime" or "non-realtime") and surfaces its errors. A graphic that can go to any time gets `setActionsSchedule` with the clip's actions and `goToTime` for each frame. Any other plays on a clock of its own, injected before the page's scripts: `Date.now`, `performance.now`, timers and animation frames run on virtual time, which the renderer steps a frame at a time, setting every CSS transition, CSS animation and Web Animation (shadow roots included) to the frame's time; actions are called at their times. Each frame is a screenshot with a transparent background (PNG, straight alpha). The same graphic, data, size, rate, length and actions render the same pixels. Frames are cached (MONTAGE_OGRAF_CACHE, else the cache folder) under a key of all of those plus the graphic folder's files and times, the browser, and the renderer's version; a render writes to a part folder renamed when done; one render runs per key while others wait for it (with its progress; Cancel stops waiting); one that failed is tried again after two minutes, not each frame; the cache is kept to MONTAGE_OGRAF_CACHE_MB (2 GB), the oldest renders going first. An action that fails fails the render with its error. The "ograf" generator (OGraf Graphic, Titles; hidden from the Effects panel): its graphic, data and actions as strings, Play Out Before the End (default 1 s) and Next Step Every (for graphics with steps). It plays in at its start, steps on every so often, and plays out before its end, unless its actions say otherwise. `ografFrame` renders at the sequence's size whatever the clip's size on screen or the preview's, and scales. App: Clip › Add OGraf Graphic… (at the playhead, on the first free track above the target, its data the defaults); the Inspector shows the graphic's name and description (or why it cannot be drawn), a row per field writing the data (one undo step a field; text when typing pauses, numbers when finished, as each value renders again), and Render (its frames now, with progress and Cancel). MCP `montage_ograf` (info: fields and defaults; add with data over the defaults, actions, out and advance; set, another graphic's defaults beneath the data; render, which says why a graphic fails). Qt Network is now required. Tests: media `ografGraphics` (tests/data/ograf: a real-time lower third in a shadow root, with a CSS transition, animation frames and a timer, checked frame by frame against their times; the same pixels twice; a seekable graphic told each time and its schedule; a graphic that throws, a missing module and a missing browser say why; Cancel; on the timeline, cached, at half size, its own actions, changed data; over MCP), app `ografGraphicInInspector` (adding, the fields and their edits and undo, Render, the Program monitor). The tests skip the rendering without a browser.
  - Review fixes: (1) a clip scaled on the timeline (or a preview at a fractional scale) rendered the graphic at its own size, often a pixel off the sequence's or another shape, each a new render (an animated scale rendered it again every frame): it always renders at the sequence's size now; (2) data holding "%2" and the like was spliced into the page's script by successive QString::arg calls, breaking or changing it: one call or plain joins now; (3) the browser's output went to a pipe nobody read (a chatty browser would stop) and a browser that died was only noticed after 30 s, so the sandbox retry never ran: its output goes to a file and its exit is noticed at once; (4) snap Chromium hides DevToolsActivePort: the port is also read from what it prints; (5) WebSockets and WebRTC escape request interception: a dead proxy catches them, and links out of the graphic's folder are not followed; (6) a failed render was never tried again in a session and the Inspector did not say why: retried after two minutes, the reason shown; (7) Render waiting on the preview's render showed no progress and could not be cancelled, and Cancel was only seen between frames: both fixed; (8) every keystroke rendered the graphic again and the cache grew for ever: text is written when typing pauses, numbers when finished, and the cache is pruned; (9) the cache key left out the browser and the renderer's version. Also: timestamps with 15 digits, an action that fails fails the render, a graphic with no steps (stepCount 0) gets none, the choice list shows no value when the data has none, MCP rejects actions whose params are not objects, and the add command looks up the sequence after its dialogs.
  - Not done: OGraf's real-time rendering for live output (the reference output plays the rendered frames); the `updateAction` sent when data is keyframed (data is one value per clip); custom actions from the Inspector (MCP's `actions` only); a graphic's own `goToTime` with audio; graphics that fetch from the network (refused, by design: a render is offline); a browser bundled with Montage; rendering only the frame shown while data is edited (the whole clip renders for each value); export says nothing of a graphic that cannot be drawn (it is transparent; the Inspector and MCP say why).

# Phase 6

The fifth gap analysis (`docs/research/phase6-roadmap.md`, October 2026) ranks what is still missing against Premiere 26.5, Resolve 21.1, Final Cut Pro 12.4 and Media Composer 2026.8. Items that cannot be built or tested in the cloud build (the GPU compositor needs Qt 6.6's RHI, Blackmagic RAW and Tangent need vendor runtimes, capture needs a card) wait for last.

- [ ] 1. GPU compositor and grade: decode upload, transform, blend modes, the colour effects, LUTs, colour-space and OCIO transforms and the grade graph on the GPU, real time at 4K (L) **First, second and third parts: done.** render/GpuCompositor draws the Program monitor's frames through Qt's RHI (Metal on macOS, Direct3D 11 on Windows, OpenGL elsewhere; built where Qt is 6.6 or later, `MONTAGE_WITH_GPU`, stubs otherwise) on one thread of its own that owns the device; render threads hand it a `gpu::Frame` (layers bottom first, each a decoded `Frame16` or a picture made on the CPU, its colour steps and its placement) and wait. Decoded frames go up as 16-bit RGBA (an RG16 texture of twice the width) and are unpacked, premultiplied and converted to the working space there through the same 65³ LUT `convertColor` uses (`conversionLut`, now shared); Color Correct (`colorCorrectSetup`, factored out of the CPU effect, its tone curve as a table), Curves (1025-entry tables), Hue Curves and .cube LUTs (1D and 3D, Apply In spaces baked as the CPU bakes them) run per pixel with the CPU's arithmetic, unpremultiplied and premultiplied again as it does; the transform with crop, opacity, flips and edge coverage is a manual bilinear (`texelFetch` only, no hardware filtering, so every backend samples alike); the 13 blend modes, cross dissolves (as `renderSequenceFrame` times them), flattening and the display conversion follow, then the frame is read back as float or straight to 8-bit as `toRgba8` rounds it. Shaders in src/render/shaders are baked by `scripts/bake-gpu-shaders.sh` (qsb: GLSL 330 and ES 300, HLSL 5.0, MSL 1.2) into GpuShaders.inc, so building needs no shader tools. render/Compositor: `gpuProgramFrame` builds the frame (`clipLayer` split into `clipSource`, which can leave the decoded frame for the GPU when nothing runs at the source stage, and `finishClipLayer`); a clip with a grade graph, a mask, or an effect with no GPU form is made on the CPU (`clipLayer`) and blended on the GPU, as are transitions other than a cross dissolve; a frame with an adjustment layer, a track matte, Behind People, stereo 3D, a trim pass or a picture larger than the GPU takes renders on the CPU whole (`gpu::stats` counts which, `MONTAGE_GPU_LOG=1` prints why). Captions are drawn on the CPU after readback. `RenderOptions::gpu` (off by default: exports, thumbnails and the render cache stay on the CPU); `renderProgramFrameRgba8`. App: Playback › GPU Rendering (on by default where there is a GPU, `playback/gpuRendering`; the device is set up only when it is wanted), the Program monitor's worker asks for 8-bit frames from the GPU for Rec.709 sequences and float ones otherwise (scopes, the HDR viewer and reference output as before). `MONTAGE_GPU=off|opengl|metal|d3d11|d3d12|software`; `montage-cli bench --gpu`. Tests (test_render `gpuCompositorMatchesCpu`, `gpuFallback`; skipped without a GPU): 16-bit media with varying alpha, a tinted LUT, a scaled, rotated, cropped, faded layer with Color Correct, Curves and Hue Curves in every blend mode, a generator, a dissolve, half size, flipped, an empty frame, a look applied in ACEScct on log footage, and PQ shown as Rec.709, each against the CPU renderer (largest difference 2e-5 on Mesa's llvmpipe), 8-bit within one step.
  - Keyers on the GPU: Chroma Key, Luma Key and Screen Key (without shrinking, growing or softening its matte, which look at the pixels around) are gpucolor.frag's modes 18 to 20, changing the alpha as the CPU's do, through the effect's mask; render `gpuCompositorMatchesCpu` checks each (Chroma Key's matte and result, a masked inverted Luma Key, Screen Key's result, matte and status views) against the CPU, and a softened Screen Key falling back to the CPU for its clip.
  - Screen Key's matte shrunk, grown and softened on the GPU too: its matte drawn first (mode 21), then a running minimum or maximum along rows and columns (22) and three box blurs each way (23), each window held inside the picture as `minMaxFilter` and `boxBlurPlane` hold theirs, and the key reading that matte (`ColorOp::params[10]`, `[11]`). `gpuCompositorMatchesCpu` checks shrinking, growing, softening and both together (result and matte views, each also changing the CPU's picture) and a softened key through a mask, all on the GPU; a box blur off by one pixel's weight fails it.
  - Review fixes: the tests now also compare the status view on each changed matte, the radii at half size (halved before rounding), and the key on a partly transparent picture after its grade. A wide blur reads 2r + 1 pixels a pass where the CPU keeps a running sum (soften is at most 20 px, so at most 21 reads a pass at full size).
  - Not done: OCIO transforms (OCIO's own GPU shaders differ from its CPU processor in the last digits, so they would break the GPU-matches-CPU rule), depth, people and object masks, Refine Edges, Show Mask on an effect, HDR Palette, and the other effects on the GPU (each such clip is a CPU picture uploaded at the canvas's size); YUV decoded frames converted there (swscale still makes RGBA64); decoding straight into GPU memory (hardware-decoded frames come back to the CPU first); exports and Render In to Out on the GPU; speed measured on a real GPU (the cloud build has only llvmpipe, a software rasteriser slower than the CPU renderer, so "real time at 4K" is unverified); Vulkan.
  - Review fixes: the texture pool let textures go mid-frame while passes recorded earlier still read them (it now trims only between frames: idle for eight frames, then least recently used past 768 MB), and the render-pass textures the pipelines were made against are kept out of it; a look applied on log footage's camera picture (an AMF's LUT in ACEScct) ran twice (it is now left out of the GPU's steps); an empty frame read an unwritten texture (now transparent, as the CPU's); a render target that cannot be made fails the frame instead of drawing nothing; a picture too large for the GPU is made on the CPU for that layer alone rather than failing the whole frame after the work; the bench times 8-bit frames either way; the shader bake script runs on macOS.
  - **Second part: done.** Masks, more effects and serial grade graphs on the GPU. A colour effect's mask (ellipse, rectangle, Bézier path and gradient with their feather and expansion, as `drawMaskShape` factored out of `effectMatte` draws them; the HSL qualifier with its softness; invert and opacity) is drawn on the CPU at the canvas's size and sent as an R32F texture, the qualifier worked out per pixel in the shader on the effect's input as `effectMatte` does, and the effect's result mixed with its input through it (`gpuMask`). New shader steps with the CPU effects' arithmetic: CDL (slope, offset, power, saturation; Apply In spaces baked as `applyCdlEffect` bakes them), Color Space Transform (the shared `conversionLut`), Invert, Black & White (with tint), Hue/Saturation (with vibrance and lightness) and Levels. A grade graph whose nodes are in series (`serialGrade`) runs on the GPU node by node: each node's window and qualifier from its settings and its Mix fade the node's effects against its input (`Mark` keeps the input, `Mix` blends it back), as `applyGradeGraph` does; colour groups' pre- and post-clip nodes and effects that run at the source stage stay where the CPU puts them (`gpuChainOps` mirrors `finishClipLayer`). Anything else (a parallel or layer mixer, a key input, a depth mask, Show Mask, Refine Edges on a shape) keeps the clip on the CPU as before. Tests: `gpuCompositorMatchesCpu` gains masks (an ellipse with a qualifier, an inverted 70 % gradient, a qualifier alone), the new effects with a masked Invert, serial nodes with a window at 60 % and a node faded to 50 % (all on the GPU), and a parallel mixer (on the CPU); `gpuFallback` uses Refine Edges for its CPU clip.
  - Review fixes (second part): a grade node with Show Mask on and no window of its own (its matte only its Mix) drew its effects on the GPU instead of its matte as grey (now left to the CPU); the GPU's steps read keyframes at the clip's time before Stop Motion steps it (harmless while Stop Motion keeps a clip on the CPU, now the stepped time as the CPU uses).
  - **Third part: done.** Every grade graph on the GPU, as `applyGradeGraph` takes it (`gpuGraphOps`): each node's picture and key kept in numbered slots (`ColorOp::Store`, `Load`, and `Pass` steps drawn from slots into a slot, each slot let go after the last step that reads it). gpucolor.frag gains the graph's steps: a node's key (its window and qualifier on what comes in, joined with the key it is given, inverted, by its gain, added, subtracted, replaced or multiplied, then by its mix), a node's picture mixed with what came in through its key, a key shown as grey (Show Mask on a node), keys added (key mixers and a mixer's own key), a parallel mixer's weighted and plain sums and its average, and a layer mixer's branches blended in their modes where their keys are. Chains with no keys passed and no key shown keep the Mark/Mix path. Keys are RGBA32F textures (a 4K key takes as much memory as a picture). Tests: `gpuCompositorMatchesCpu` gains a node showing its key, a parallel mixer with a windowed branch, a layer mixer in Screen, a key mixer feeding an inverted, scaled, added key, and an outside node, all on the GPU.
  - Review fixes (third part): Show Mask on a node in an otherwise plain chain (no window, no mix) was ignored by the CPU, which ran the chain's effects in place, while the GPU showed the key; the CPU now shows it too (`applyGradeGraph`'s plain chain leaves out a node showing its key).
  - GPU Rendering stays on by default (as the established editors have it: it draws only the Program monitor, exports stay on the CPU, what the GPU can't draw falls back), with a startup guard: turning it on marks the settings until the GPU has drawn a frame or Montage closes normally, so a launch a graphics driver crashed while starting the GPU leaves it off the next time, saying why in the status bar and the menu item's tooltip. **Tests:** app `gpuStartupGuard` (the mark set and cleared, or never left when the GPU can't start).
- [ ] 2. Blackmagic RAW (.braw) decoding with RAW settings: ISO, white balance, tint, exposure, highlight recovery, gamut and gamma, sidecars, and half and quarter decode for playback (M) **Open-source stand-in, Magic Lantern Video: done.** Blackmagic RAW has no open decoder (only Blackmagic's SDK), so RAW video from an open format came first: media/Mlv reads Magic Lantern's MLV 2.0 (the layout of Magic Lantern's mlv.h; FFmpeg's MLV demuxer agrees on the bit packing): the blocks are indexed once (RAWI's size, bit depth, black and white levels, CFA pattern and ColorMatrix1; VIDF frames by number, after their frame space; WAVI and AUDF; IDNT camera, LENS lens, focal length and aperture, EXPO ISO and shutter, WBAL white balance, RTCI date), across a recording's chunks (.MLV, .M00, .M01..., either case); frames are uncompressed (10, 12 or 14 bits, most significant bit first in little-endian words) or lossless LJ92 (`decodeLosslessJpeg`: SOF3, Huffman tables with a 9-bit fast lookup, predictors 1 to 7, one to four interleaved components, point transform, restart intervals at row ends), a missing frame showing the one before. Colour: the camera's white as shot (Canon's custom white balance gains, else its kelvin, else daylight) through ColorMatrix1, the matrix to XYZ D65 with Bradford adaptation from the light, its colour temperature by McCamy for choosing another; developed by ProRes RAW's code (`developProResRaw`: levels, white balance with clipping kept white, bilinear demosaic or 2x2 cells at half size, into ACEScct AP1), from the first red site for other CFA patterns. Decoder: `probeMedia` gives the picture's size, rate (23976/1000 as 24000/1001), length, codec "mlv", ACEScct, sound facts, camera, lens, ISO and date; `VideoDecoder` develops each frame when shown (half size when shown at half or less, or asked for), keeping the last; its sound (16 or 24-bit PCM) is read here, from its blocks. Interpret Footage's RAW settings apply (exposure, white balance by temperature, tint, half size; highlights clip at the sensor's white, as for ProRes RAW). Tests: media `magicLanternVideo` writes MLV files as the camera does from a scene known in camera RGB (a mid grey under daylight and a red): facts as shot; the mosaic exactly; the grey developed neutral at 18 % (ACEScct 0.4135) and the red red; frames differing; half size; a tungsten balance chosen turning the daylight grey blue; the tone heard; lossless with predictors 1, 4, 6 and 7 (6 across three chunks) giving the same mosaic; 12-bit levels and a missing frame; a GRBG pattern; H.264-class and non-MLV files refused.
  - Review fixes: (1) an over-full Huffman table wrote past the fast lookup (on the stack): codes are checked before they are placed; (2) 23.976 recordings showed frame 1 twice and every later frame one late (the decoder kept 23976/1000 while the sequence asks for 24000/1001 times): both use one rate; (3) damaged sizes made huge allocations: frame sizes over 16384, lossless frames not the picture's size and frame numbers far past the frames there are are refused; (4) the sound went through FFmpeg's demuxer, which reads every picture to reach it, returns silence after a seek, sees no sound when the header's count is still 0 (a recording cut short) and misses lower-case chunks: the PCM blocks are read here; (5) a preset white balance (daylight, cloudy...) took the temperature last set: presets use their own light; (6) each frame allocated its mosaic and picture afresh and unpacked on one thread: buffers are kept, rows unpacked in parallel, developed straight into the decoder's frame, and playback scales without high quality; (7) every open rescanned the whole recording and held every chunk open: the index is shared while the file is unchanged and one chunk is open at a time; (8) the 1x3 anamorphic modes came in squeezed: RAWC's binning and skipping give the pixel aspect, and half size is chosen on the sensor's samples, not the stretched picture; (9) frames numbered from other than 0 had nothing at the start: frames count from the first recorded; (10) a pattern that is not Bayer is taken as RGGB, and pictures are whole cells (even sizes for 4:2:0 exports); (11) short restart and scan segments were read past; (13) chunks of another recording (another GUID) are left out, .M99 is read, and a choice of white balance keeps the as-shot tint. A second review: the shared index also follows the chunks' sizes and times (a card still copying), a frame scaled for playback is scaled again when asked for high quality, lossless frames a little larger than the picture (encoder padding) are read, and the capture dialog notes writes by the file's path (not an id another project may reuse).
  - Not done: Blackmagic RAW itself (no open decoder; its SDK is not open source); dual ISO and focus pixel removal; MLV App's LZMA and delta compression; checked against camera files (none in the cloud build; the bit packing was checked against FFmpeg's demuxer; 10 and 12-bit lossless levels are taken from RAWI as written, unchecked against real recordings).

Tangent colour panels (Elements, Ripple, Wave 2, Arc) (M). control/Tangent: Tangent Hub's TUBE protocol (TIPC revision 4 on, over TCP to 127.0.0.1:64246), written from Hammerspoon's `hs.tangent` and CommandPost's Tangent manager (both MIT; Tangent's own SDK was not used): length-prefixed big-endian messages, the Hub's InitiateComms, ParameterChange/Reset/ValueRequest, MenuChange/Reset/StringRequest, ActionOn/Off, ModeChange and Transport read (unmanaged-panel messages skipped), the application's ApplicationDefinition, ParameterValue, MenuString, AllChange, ModeValue and DisplayText written (and the Hub's side too, for tests), and controls.xml (`controlsXml`: modes, nested groups of parameters with their ranges and steps, menus, actions and trackball bindings; ids as `%#010x`) with an empty Element Tk map so the Hub's defaults apply. app/TangentControl: on by default (`tangent/enabled`; Edit › Control Surface…), it looks for the Hub every 5 s, writes its controls into Montage's data folder (`tangent/`) for Tangent Mapper and names itself. Primaries: every Color Correct control (ids hashed from their names, so maps stay valid as controls are added), Lift, Gamma and Gain bound for the trackballs (red, green, blue, then the master ring); a turn changes the graded clip's Color Correct at the playhead (the Inspector's clip and its picked node, else the top picture clip at the playhead; a shared node's Color Correct in the shared node; the node's Color Correct added if it has none; its keyframe if keyframed, at a time held inside the clip), held to its range, and is answered with the new value; a run of turns on one node (a trackball's three colours interleaved) is one undo step; Reset puts it back. Nodes: a menu stepping through the clip's correctors (the Nodes panel follows). Transport: the jog wheel steps frames, the shuttle ring's turns add up to play at 1 to 32 times either way (back in the middle it stops). Commands: every menu command grouped by menu, plus Play / Stop and next and previous node. The displays show the clip and node; when the clip, node or values change elsewhere (undo, the Inspector, the playhead) the Hub is told to ask for every value again. Tests: app `tangentPanels` against a stand-in Hub (introduction and controls.xml, value requests, turns merged into one undo step and undone, the range held, reset, the node menu moving the Nodes panel and turns going to that node's Color Correct, the displays, jog and a menu command).
  - Review fixes: (1) controls.xml put its groups straight under the root; they are now inside `<Controls>`, as the Hub reads them; (2) a trackball's interleaved red, green and blue turns made an undo step each: the run is merged by clip and node; (3) turning the connection off aborted a socket the disconnect handler had already let go (a null `deleteLater`); the socket is let go once, its signals cut first; (4) a shared node's turns went into a hidden Color Correct on the clip: they change the shared node's own; (5) the shuttle value was taken as a speed, but the Hub sends turns to add: they are added up; (6) a keyframe could be written past the clip's end; (7) the 200 ms watch looked up the graded clip 27 times a tick and told the Hub to ask for every value at every tick of playback over a keyframed grade: once a tick, and once playback stops; (8) more than 64 panels in InitiateComms left the rest to be read as commands; the shuttle starts from the middle again once playback was stopped some other way; while playing, the Hub is told of changed values once playback stops; the panels' nine-letter names are "Lift R" rather than "Lift (Red"; "connected" is said once, when the Hub has greeted Montage. Tests: controls.xml's `<Controls>` and short names, interleaved turns undone in one step, a shared node graded in place, the shuttle's turns adding up.
  - Not done: checked against a real Hub and panels (none in the cloud build); the Hub's own default maps for Montage (Tangent Mapper maps them; Tangent's map file format is not documented in the open sources); unmanaged panel mode; HDR Palette, Curves and Colour Warper controls (only Color Correct's); modes beyond one.

HDR and SDR trim passes: one grade mastered in HDR, with per-target trims (SDR Rec.709 100 nits, HDR10 at 1000, 600 or 300 nits, HLG) by clip or scene, each previewed and rendered as its own deliverable (M). render/TrimPasses: a sequence's trim targets (`Sequence::trimTargets`: id, name, a display space and its peak; presets sdr, hdr10-1000, hdr10-600, hdr10-300, hlg, or a space and peak of one's own, ids kept unique). `applyTrimPass` maps the master to a target (PQ above the target's peak rolled off by BT.2390's EETF on the brightest channel, hue kept; then `convertColor` into its space, HDR into SDR tone mapped as before) and trims the frame by its shot's trim on the target's code values: lift, gamma, gain, saturation (about the target primaries' luma), a highlight roll-off (a tanh shoulder from a knee) and mid-tone detail (luma less a wide blur, weighted to the mid-tones). A shot is the top enabled picture clip on a shown track (titles, shapes, OGraf graphics and adjustment layers are not), and its trim takes in the whole frame, as Dolby Vision's per-shot trims do. Trims are `Clip::trims` (a "trim" effect per target id, keyframable on clip frames: split clips keep their keys on the same frames, `setTrim` sets a keyframed control at the playhead), saved with the clip; Paste and Remove Attributes gained "Trim passes" (`AttrTrims`). `RenderOptions::trimTarget` shows a target (the program frame in its space, displaySpace converting from there; the frame cache keyed by it); the Program monitor's playback, scopes, HDR viewer and reference output follow it (the reference output's signal is the target's space and peak). `ExportSettings::trimTarget` delivers a target in its space with its peak as HDR10 metadata (the master's measured light levels and HDR10+ analysis not reused; HDR10+ measured on the pass); not GIF or EXR; render farm jobs carry it. The Trims panel (Colour workspace, beside the Inspector): the target shown (Master or one of them), + presets and − to remove, the shot at the playhead's six controls (one undo step per run of changes), Reset, Copy to Selected, and Queue Passes (each target into a folder through the render queue: HDR as 10-bit HEVC with HDR10+ on PQ, SDR as H.264). MCP: `montage_trim` (list, add_target, remove_target, set, reset, copy) and `montage_render`'s `trim_target`. Tests: render trimPasses, media trimPassesOverMcp, app trimPassesPanel.
  - Not done: Dolby Vision metadata (proprietary: trims stay Montage's own, not an RPU); trims analysed automatically from the master (a starting trim per shot from its light levels, as Dolby's analysis does); scene-wide trims across several shots other than Copy; the Program monitor showing master and a target side by side; Export Versions combining reframed shapes with trim passes; trims in nested sequences (the outer shot's applies to the whole picture); a target per output for the reference output and the viewer at once.
  - Review fixes: an HLG master on a PQ target under 1000 nits is rolled off in PQ after conversion, not clipped; Paste Attributes from a clip with no trims leaves the clips' own; the panel's boxes take typed values once finished and are not rewritten while focused, and moving the playhead no longer rebuilds the target list; a track matte's hidden source is not taken as the shot (`hiddenMatteTracks`, shared with the compositor); captions are drawn after the trim in the viewer as in exports; Queue Passes names files by id when two targets share a name; switching targets refreshes the render bar; trims can be keyframed (the panel's Keyframe button, `setTrim`'s `keyframe`, MCP `keyframe`); a plugin test that took any 0..2 box found the Trims panel's and now looks in the Inspector.
- [x] 6. Dialogue-gated loudness: measure the "Dialog LKFS" Netflix asks for (−27 ±2 LKFS, −2 dBTP, the programme figure when dialogue is under 15 %), as a meter, a Quality Check target and an export normalisation (S). media/Loudness: each 100 ms step of a `LoudnessMeter` is speech or not (`setSpeech` for what comes next, `markSpeech` after the fact); `speechResult` is BS.1770's two gates over only the 400 ms blocks that are speech throughout, `speechShare` the share of the sound (steps above −70 LUFS) that is speech, `momentaryAt` a step's 400 ms loudness. render/DialogueLoudness: `measureDialogueLoudness` mixes the range (stereo, or the sequence's own channels with BS.1770's surround weights) and finds the speech from, in order (`SpeechGate` auto, or one asked for): the Dialogue-role clips heard (their own mix, a step counting where it is within 20 LU of the dialogue's loudness, so the pauses between lines are not speech), the transcribed words heard (`sequenceTranscriptWords`, padded 0.1 s, pauses under 0.5 s bridged; tracks left out and muted roles not heard), or by listening (AutoMix's `classifyAudio` on the mix, each second judged with the seconds either side). It reports the dialogue's and the programme's LKFS, the true peak, the speech share and spans, and which counts (`value`: the programme's under 15 % speech); always labelled an estimate. The live meter marks speech from the timeline (Dialogue clips, else transcribed words) and shows D (dialogue LKFS) and SP (speech share) under the readout, with a Netflix target (−27, ±2 LU, the dialogue's figure while speech is 15 % or more; true peaks red over −2 dBTP for Netflix and ATSC). Quality Check's Netflix entry measures the dialogue within 2 LU (`QcSettings::dialogueGated`, `loudnessTolerance`); Export › Loudness has "-27 LKFS dialogue, -2 dBTP (Netflix)" (`ExportSettings::dialogueGated`: the mix and normalised extra streams brought to the target by their dialogue; render farm jobs carry it); `montage-cli render --dialogue-gated`. MCP: `montage_dialogue_loudness` (gate, surround; returns both figures, the share, the speech spans and the gate used), `montage_quality_check`'s `loudness_dialogue` and `loudness_tolerance`, `montage_render`'s `loudness_dialogue`. Tests: media loudnessOverSpeech and dialogueGatedLoudness (JFK as Dialogue then a far louder tone: each gate measures the speech, QC and the Netflix export land on it, over MCP too), app loudnessReadout.
  - Not done: Dolby's Dialogue Intelligence itself (licensed only as object code inside professional meters; Montage's gate is its own, so the figure is an estimate and is labelled so); the listening gate in the live meter (it marks speech from the timeline only); the dialogue-gated loudness range (LRA) and short-term readings Netflix's reports also carry.
  - Review fixes: picture clips' transcripts no longer mark speech when the dialogue's sound is muted or left out (an M&E is measured by its programme); Auto picks its gate from what is in the range measured (a stretch with no Dialogue clip or word is listened to); the live meter works out its speech marks outside the locks the audio thread takes; the description says why the programme figure counts (too little speech, or none in whole 400 ms blocks); a fractional QC tolerance prints as given; the live reading says it counts whole Dialogue clips.

CEA-708 captions: native DTVCC services next to the 608 already embedded, 708 read back, MCC sidecars and 708 in broadcast MXF (S–M). core/Cea708: `captionCcData` gives each video frame its A/53 cc_data: the 608 pairs as before (on the 29.97 Hz line-21 clock) and, with 708, caption service 1 as pop-on windows: each caption is loaded into a hidden window up to a second ahead (eight windows in turn; DefineWindow anchored where the caption sits, top, middle or bottom and left, centre or right, sized to its rows and columns, up to 4 rows of 42; ClearWindows, SetWindowAttributes justified to match, SetPenAttributes, SetPenColor from the track's text, box and outline colours, SetPenLocation, the text in G0, G1 Latin-1 and G2 through EXT1 for curly quotes, ellipsis and the like), shown at its start (Hide the other window, Display this one) and hidden at its end. Service blocks go into DTVCC packets (cc_type 3 then 2, sequence numbers, at most 128 bytes), within A/53's 600 entries a second (`ccCountForRate`: 20 a frame at 29.97, 24 at 25, 10 at 59.94), padded with invalid entries. `captionsFrom708` reads service 1 back: packets reassembled, the C0, C1, G0, G1 and EXT1 codes interpreted on eight windows, and what the windows shown say after each packet becomes a caption placed by its window's anchor. Exports: `ExportSettings::cea708` (on by default with `cea608`; render farm jobs carry it), the export dialog's closed-caption box says both, MCP `montage_render` `cea708`. `readEmbeddedCaptions` takes a `CaptionStandard` (Auto: 708 when the video has it, else 608). MacCaption MCC V1.0: `captionsToMcc` writes a SMPTE 334 caption distribution packet (ANC DID 61h/SDID 01h, cdp_frame_rate, sequence counter, ccdata section, footer and checksum) for every frame with both 608 and 708, with MCC's letter compression, at the sequence's rate when MCC has it (24, 25, 30, 30DF, 50, 60, 60DF) else 29.97 drop frame; `parseMcc` reads one (708 first, else 608); `.mcc` in the Captions panel's import and export, `montage-cli captions` and MCP `montage_captions`. Tests: core cea708Service (three captions with placement and non-ASCII characters through cc_data and MCC, a 608-only MCC, 25 fps), media embeddedClosedCaptions (608 and 708 inside H.264 at 29.97 and 25 and HEVC at 23.976, each read on its own; 608 alone).

  - Review fixes: Auto import reads roll-up and paint-on from the 608 when a file has it (708's decoder now scrolls a window past its rows and keeps text growing on screen as one caption, and says when text was painted); rows over 42 characters or more than four are wrapped again rather than cut; eight windows in turn and packets sized to go out whole each frame, displays and hides ahead of loads, so a caption after a short one is shown on time (one at frame 0 a frame late at most); MCC timecodes are read at the sequence's own rate when the file names its nominal one (23.976 as "24" no longer drifts); 59.94 drop frame writes MCC V2.0; field 2's nulls beside each 608 pair; the same words twice back to back stay two captions.
- [x] 8. Blackmagic Speed Editor and Editor Keyboard over USB and Bluetooth: jog, shuttle and scroll, source and timeline keys, trim and multicam keys, LEDs (S–M). control/SpeedEditor has the protocol from blackmagic-misc (Apache-2.0) and CommandPost's hs.blackmagic (MIT): the challenge answered on feature report 6 (`authResponse`, `authenticate`, giving the seconds until it must be done again), keys held on report 4 turned into presses and releases (`Decoder`, both devices' key codes, names, labels and bracketed second functions), the wheel on report 3 (its mode and value) and the battery on report 7; key lights (report 2), the wheel's lights (output report 4) and its mode (output report 3). control/Hid opens HID devices with no library: hidraw on Linux (USB and Bluetooth, read from sysfs uevents), IOKit on macOS, SetupAPI and the HID API on Windows; a reader thread each, losing the device reported once. app/SpeedEditorControl looks every three seconds while on (Edit › Control Surface…, on by default; each interface tried, the one that answers the challenge kept), authenticates again half a minute before the device's time runs out, and works the editor as Resolve does: JOG a frame a step (360 units), SCRL a quarter second, SHTL eight speeds each way from a quarter to 32x by how far it is turned (new `PlaybackController::setSpeed`; leaving shuttle stops); SOURCE and TIMELINE pick the monitor the wheel and STOP/PLAY drive; TRIM IN, TRIM OUT and ROLL select the nearest edit's side for the wheel to trim (Trim mode), ROLL twice slides the clip at the playhead, SLIP SRC and SLIP DEST slip the clip after or before the edit (one undo step a slip), AUDIO LEVEL and TRANS DUR held set the level of the sound at the playhead (or the selected sound) half a dB a step and the edit's transition length; CUT, DIS and SMTH CUT make the nearest edit on the target video track a cut, a dissolve (a second) or a smooth cut (a sixth of one), keeping its length; CAM 1–9 switch the multicam angle (cut while playing or with LIVE O/WR latched); the rest are menu commands (source edits, In, Out, Ripple Delete, Add Edit, Full Screen Program, Match Frame, Snapping, and new Clip › Edit Video Only / Edit Audio Only and Sequence › Clear In / Clear Out). Keys pressed twice within 0.4 s do their bracketed function (Clear In/Out, Undo, Add Marker, New Title; TRANS waits so a title does not bring a transition). Lights follow the editor four times a second: the angle shown, the edit's transition, LIVE O/WR and checkable commands' state (SNAP, VIDEO ONLY, AUDIO ONLY); the wheel's mode light. Any key can do any menu command or built-in function (Keys…, kept in settings). Video Only and Audio Only reach the core: `placeMedia`, `placeOnTop`, `rippleOverwrite` and `placeSequenceClips` take `useVideo`/`useAudio` (media without the kind asked for is refused saying so). Gamut tools: a CIE 1931/1976 chromaticity scope with Rec.709, P3 and Rec.2020 triangles, an out-of-gamut overlay, and ACES Reference Gamut Compression as an effect (S). render/Gamut has the maths: `codeToXy` (a pixel's code values in any space to its D65-relative chromaticity), `xyToUv`, `gamutChromaticities` (a gamut's primaries and white), `spectralLocus` (the CIE 1931 2° observer, 380–700 nm every 5 nm), `GamutCheck` (a colour needing a negative amount of one of the target's primaries, with a small tolerance) and `acesGamutCompress` / `AcesGamutCompressor` (ACES 1.3's RGC: each channel's distance from the achromatic axis compressed above its threshold with the published limits, thresholds and power, forward and inverse, on linear AP1, or from any working space and back, display transfers' negative values kept). The scopes get a sixth mode, CIE Chromaticity (1931 xy or 1976 u′v′): the frame as delivered (HDR included) binned by chromaticity and drawn in the colours it plots over the spectral locus with wavelengths, a tenth grid, D65, and the Rec.709, P3 and Rec.2020 triangles (and the signal's own gamut, dashed, when it is another), with the share of lit pixels outside Rec.709 and P3. The monitors' exposure checks get Out of Gamut: Rec.709 and Out of Gamut: P3, magenta stripes over the pixels whose delivered colour is outside, from the frame the scopes get (`ViewerWidget::setSignal`, used only while it is the shown frame's). ACES Gamut Compress is a colour effect (Direction: Forward or Inverse): on a clip of media it works on the media's colours as they come, in AP1 from the media's space, before the input transform clips them to the working gamut (as the source-space CDL does); on adjustment layers and in nodes in the working space; LUT export holds it (in the working space). **Tests:** render `gamutTools` (D65, Rec.709 red, Rec.2020 green and P3 blue where the standards put them; black has none; u′v′ of D65; the locus at 520 and 700 nm; outside and inside checks both ways, a converted colour rounded to 16 bits still inside; the RGC leaving an inside colour, landing each limit on the edge, bringing a far colour in and the inverse undoing it; against OpenColorIO's "ACES 1.3 Reference Gamut Compression" on eight ACES2065-1 colours within the spec's 0.002; the effect in an ACEScct working space, inverse, an inside colour untouched, LUT export; an ACES2065-1 picture outside AP1 rendered in a Rec.709 sequence comes out compressed, not clipped), app `gamutScopeAndChecks` (a half Rec.2020-green, half grey frame: half outside Rec.709 and P3, plotted at the green and D65 in xy and u′v′; Rec.709 frames all inside; the overlay striping just the green, nothing against Rec.2020, and nothing with another frame's signal) and `exposureChecks` (the two new checks offered; a Rec.709 sequence never marked).
