- [x] 10. Film density and texture: ColorSlice-style hue vectors (six plus skin) with subtractive saturation and density; Mid-tone Detail and Texture Pop (S–M). render/ColorSlice: **Colour Slice** (Color category) works in ACEScct's log encoding of the light: for each of red, yellow, green, cyan, blue and magenta (raised-cosine weights that share every hue between neighbouring vectors) and skin (its own centre, 25° by default, and width, taking its share first) a hue shift (the same brightest channel and spread at the new hue), saturation and density, plus global density, saturation, Depth (scales every density), Subtractive Saturation (on: the brightest channel stays and the others move in stops, so richer colours get darker as with film's dyes; off: round the brightness, which stays), Protect Highlights (the change fading out from half to full white) and Mix. Density darkens a colour by up to a stop times its saturation (four stops between its brightest and darkest channels counts as full) after the saturation change, keeping its hue. It is colour-only, so exported LUTs hold it. **Texture Pop / Mid-tone Detail** (Color category) changes brightness only, as one gain on all three channels, so colours keep their balance and do not fringe: Mid-tone Detail (−100 to 100 %) adds or removes the brightness's difference from a self-guided filter of it (radius 24 px times Detail Scale, so edges of a couple of stops or more keep most of themselves and get no halo), weighted to the mid-tones; Texture Pop (0 to 200 %) adds its difference from a 1.5 px blur (three boxes) above the Noise Floor. Sizes are sequence pixels scaled to the preview. Both take masks, grade versions and nodes as any video filter. **Tests:** render `colorSliceAndTexture` (defaults leave colours alone; red density darkens a rich red by about a stop and a pale one by less, the same hue, grey and blue untouched; Depth 50 % halves it; subtractive saturation keeps red's channel and darkens, additive keeps the brightness; a red hue shift towards yellow leaves blue alone; skin density darkens skin and not green; Protect Highlights and Mix; colour-only for LUT export, the texture effect not; a flat picture unchanged; fine texture grown, faint texture held by the noise floor and grown without it; channel ratios kept; negative mid-tone detail softening a wide pattern alike at full and half preview size; a four-stop edge with little halo).
  - Review fixes: subtractive saturation could push channels below no light in ACEScct's straight toe (now held at zero unless already below); hue vectors and saturation are measured in Rec.709's primaries whatever the working space, so a grade lands alike in Rec.2020 or a camera gamut; vectors left alone are skipped and colours with nothing to change are passed over; the box means' vertical pass walks rows in order, the detail buffers are fewer and every pass runs in parallel; Texture Pop's blur is a true Gaussian of any size, so Detail Scale and the preview's scale change it (a whole-pixel box did not below 1.3); a matte's transparent surround no longer counts as black next to its edge (alpha-weighted means); the mid-tone weight centred on mid grey as its comment says; the Inspector shows Colour Slice one vector at a time (Global, the six hues, Skin with its centre and width). Tests added: heavy saturation never negative, the same density on Rec.709 red in a Rec.2020 working space, Texture Pop alike at half preview size, an opaque square on transparency unchanged at its edge, app `colorSliceInInspector`.
- [x] 11. Motion Deblur (M). media/MotionDeblur: NAFNet's GoPro model (Liangyu Chen et al., MIT; GoPro dataset, Seungjun Nah et al., CC BY 4.0) as OpenCV's model zoo publishes it in ONNX (MIT, int8 weights, 92 MB), downloaded the first time (`deblurModel`, pinned by revision and SHA-256; $MONTAGE_DEBLUR_MODEL, $MONTAGE_DEBLUR_MODEL_URL; scripts/fetch-models.sh and CI fetch it) instead of hosting an fp32 export of our own: its output on the zoo's example matches the zoo's published result. `motionDeblur` runs it on the un-premultiplied colour (the code values as they are, as it was trained) with alpha kept, in overlapping tiles (768 px by default, 64 px blended where they meet, the last tile flush with the edge), pictures smaller than the model's 384 px mirrored out to it; Strength mixes over the original, and values beyond 0..1 keep what the model never saw. The **Motion Deblur** effect (Blur & Sharpen) runs on the clip's source frame as decoded (so previews pay for their own size), after noise reduction and before Motion Blur, the last two results cached as Super Scale's are, and does nothing until the model is here (adding it in the app offers the download; MCP's montage_add_effect says to fetch it). It takes seconds a frame on a processor, so long stretches want Render In to Out or Render and Replace. Provenance lists it (Enhanced, NAFNet); `montage-cli models` shows it. **Tests:** media `motionDeblurring` (lettering, a ring and bars smeared 13 px in light: over 1 dB nearer the original; half strength half way; two overlapping tiles within 0.01 of one pass; a 100 × 80 picture with half alpha, alpha kept; as a clip effect on a still, over 1 dB nearer; provenance).
  - Review fixes: playback, scrubbing, thumbnails and multicam ran the model on every frame (seconds to a minute each) with no way out: `RenderOptions::slowEffects` (set by rendering ahead: Render In to Out and background render) and deliveries (`highQuality`) run it; other views take a result already made for the frame, else go without and say so (`RenderOptions::issues`, `RenderIssues`), and `RenderOptions::cancel` stops it between tiles. Frames left without it (skipped, failed, cancelled) are no longer kept in the render cache, which also keys frames with Motion Deblur or Super Scale on whether their model is downloaded; an export fails with the reason (the model missing or failing) instead of going out without it. Only `Ort::Exception` was caught, so running out of memory could end the program: any exception now fails the run (Super Scale too), and a model that will not open is not tried again every frame. Runs take turns (each uses every core; two would double a gigabyte of working memory, which the session now hands back after each run), and two views wanting the same frame wait for one run. Nested sequences take it too (it did nothing there). Strength mixes a cached full-strength result, so changing or animating it does not run the model again; the cache keys on every pixel (8192 samples could mistake one frame of clean CG for another) and holds a few hundred megabytes. Tiles are evenly sized (an HD frame is about 1.1 times its pixels of work, was 1.7). Nearly transparent pixels are left as they were and fed to the model as their nearest visible colour, not black. Not on proxies, as Super Scale. Tests added: three tiles against one pass compared across the joins, a 300 × 120 picture deblurred through mirroring with a transparent corner kept and a value above 1 kept, the quick view going without and saying so, the frame rendered ahead then shown by the quick view, half strength mixed from the cached result.

ACES Metadata Files (AMF) with CLF looks (S–M). render/Clf reads Common LUT Format 3 (SMPTE ST 2136-1): Matrix (3×3 or 3×4), Range (clamping or not, one end or both), LUT1D (one or three channels), LUT3D (trilinear or tetrahedral, blue fastest), ASC_CDL (Fwd, Rev, NoClamp), Log (all eight styles, camera log with its linear segment) and Exponent (basic, mirror, pass-through, monCurve), each in the bit depths it says (integer depths scaled to 0..1 as they run); half-domain 1D LUTs are refused with the reason; files are kept while unchanged on disk. The LUT effect takes `.clf` beside `.cube` and gains Apply In (a colour space: the working space's code values to it and back by linear light, baked with the CDL effect's code into a 65³ LUT). render/Amf reads and writes AMF 2.0: amfInfo, clipId (name, file, numbered sequence with any index character, uuid), the input transform (an IDT/CSC id, a file, or an inverse output transform), looks (a CDL with its working space as an id or file, written as SOPNode/SatNode and read as those or ACES 1's ASC_SOP/ASC_SAT, or by ColorCorrectionRef from a .cc/.ccc beside it; a look file; a look id) and the output transform (one id, or ACES 1's RRT and ODT). ACES 1 and 2 ids map to Montage's spaces (`acesInputSpace`, inexact for LogC3 at other exposure indexes and Venice's gamuts; `acesOutputSpace` with the peak from the name) and back to ACES 2 ids (`acesInputTransformId`, `acesOutputTransformId` choosing the nearest peak ACES has); `clfToAces` writes a space ACES has no id for as a CLF (its curve as a 4096-point 1D LUT, then its primaries to AP0) with an id Montage reads back as that space. `applyAmf`: the clips whose name, media name, file (frame numbers aside) or tape match its clip, or the media's `amf_uuid` (or the chosen clips): their media's Interpret Colour from the input transform (ACES2065-1 when it was applied), its looks not yet applied as effects first in the chain (marked, so the next AMF replaces them: cdl in its space, lut in ACES2065-1, ACES Gamut Compress forward or inverse), the sequence's colour space and peak from the output transform when asked; notes say what it could not carry. `clipAmf`/`writeClipAmf`: the media's (or the pull's frames') space, the enabled CDL, LUT looks applied in ACES2065-1 (copied beside the AMF) and gamut compression, and the sequence's output transform; other colour effects are named as not carried. VFX pulls write `<shot>.amf` (their frames as a numbered sequence; `VfxPullOptions::amf`, a checkbox in the dialog). ALE `AMF_NAME`/`AMF_UUID` columns both ways; an ACEScg colour space. App: Clip › Import ACES Metadata (AMF)… (asks whether to take the output transform's space) and Export ACES Metadata (AMF)… (a file for one clip, a folder for more); MCP `montage_amf` (read, import, export) and `montage_vfx_pull` `amf`. **Tests:** render `clfMatchesOcio` (three CLF files in tests/data/clf, written by its script: every operator, integer depths, both interpolations, out-of-range values, within 1e-4 of OpenColorIO's results; the LUT effect in the working space and in ACEScct at half strength; files refused with their reasons), `acesMetadataFiles` (ids both ways for every space; an ACES 1 and an ACES 2 AMF in tests/data/amf read; applied to the clip its name matches, again replacing its own effects but not others, onto a chosen clip with the sequence's space; written back with the look file copied, an inverse output transform for video, a CLF for the working space matching OpenColorIO; a LUT in another space named as not carried; valid against the Academy's schema when `MONTAGE_AMF_SCHEMA` names it), media `vfxPullsWithHandles` (each shot's AMF: its frames, linear Rec.709 by CLF, a CDL carried; `montage_amf` export, read and import), core `aleAmfColumns`, app `amfFromTheMenus`.
  - Review fixes: looks ran after the input transform's display rendering, on clipped display values, so an ACES look never saw scene light: on camera footage the compositor now runs the CDLs and LUTs first in a clip's chain whose Apply In is a scene-referred space, and the gamut compression, in their order on the picture as it comes, each in its own space by scene light (`sourceLookSpace`, `applySourceLook`), before the input transform; the CDL in the media's own space keeps its place. - [ ] 13. Capture from SDI, HDMI and NDI: ingest a feed or a deck to ProRes, DNxHR or XDCAM with timecode and up to 16 channels, editable while it records; crash record and timed stops (M) **Network feeds and FFmpeg's capture devices: done.** media/Capture: `Recorder` records, on a thread of its own, any feed FFmpeg opens (SRT, RIST, UDP or RTP transport streams, RTMP, HLS... by URL) or a capture device through libavdevice (V4L2, ALSA and PulseAudio on Linux, AVFoundation on macOS, DirectShow on Windows; built where libavdevice is, `MONTAGE_WITH_AVDEVICE`), with a sound device of its own if asked, into Apple ProRes 422 (Proxy, LT, 422, HQ; prores_ks, 10-bit) or Avid DNxHR (LB, SQ, HQ, HQX) in QuickTime or MXF, its sound 24-bit PCM at 48 kHz (up to 16 channels; the first 16 of more, one to one). The file is begun at the first picture (its size, rate and colour tags from it); its timecode is the one given, else the feed's SMPTE 12-M timecode, else the time of day, written as a tmcd track. Each input is read on a thread of its own with a deadline (a feed that never starts or stops sending is given up, `openTimeout`); pictures are placed by their time at the feed's rate, so late or missing frames are repeated (up to a second at a time) and early ones dropped (counted), and sound by its time against the pictures'. QuickTime is written in fragments half a second long (`empty_moov`), so the file opens while it grows and a crash keeps all but the last half second; `onFirstFragment` says when it can be imported. Timed stops (`seconds`, pictures and sound ending together), Stop, and the feed ending or failing (`onFinished` with the reason) all finish the file. app/CaptureDialog (File › Capture…, non-modal, recording carries on while hidden): feed kind (network or each device kind this build has), address or device, sound device, codec, QuickTime or MXF, folder, clip name (numbered on, never overwriting), start timecode (checked), length; the status line shows time, size, rate, sound channels, timecode, frames dropped or repeated and errors. A QuickTime recording is imported into the Captures bin about a second in and followed as growing media (EditorState: reread as it grows, in full once it stops); MXF, or a recording shorter than its first fragment, is imported when it ends. Tests: media `captureFromFeeds` (lavfi's test pattern and tone in real time into ProRes HQ at 10:00:00:00 for 3 s: the partial file opened mid-recording, 75 frames, the timecode, the sound, the pattern moving to the last frame; DNxHR SQ in MXF with the time of day; a feed that never comes given up with nothing written; MPEG-2 transport streams sent by the ffmpeg command over UDP and SRT, 2 s each), app `captureWhileEditing` (a bad timecode refused, recording into the Captures bin, cut in while it records, growing then reread at its whole 6 s, the status and the next clip name, a feed that does not open).
  - Review fixes: (1) AVFoundation (and any device read without blocking) answers "nothing yet" between frames, which ended the recording at once: such reads are tried again; (2) devices do not look at FFmpeg's interrupt, so a feed with no signal held Stop (and quitting) for ever: devices are read without blocking and given up after the open timeout with nothing; (3) the macOS app bundle lacked the camera and microphone usage strings macOS needs before it lets an app open them; (4) everything ran on one core: decoders and encoders use them all, the queue is held to 512 MB by dropping pictures to the next keyframe (never sound or a reader's end), and pictures are no longer copied each frame; (5) the reader's demuxer could add streams while the recording read them: what the recording needs is copied out first; (6) sound early by more than a frame was placed from the start, and lost sound never caught up: sound is placed by its time throughout (silence for gaps, dropped where early, a tenth of a second's jitter left be; a sound device by its own clock from where it started); (7) a jump in the feed's clock dropped every later picture or left a hole: a jump of more than two seconds (one back) is taken as going on from there; (8) a set length could be overshot, and sound could end early or late: repeats stop at the length, sound queued at the stop is kept up to the picture's end, and silence fills to it; (9) the feed's sound or picture format changing mid-recording read the wrong planes: swr is made again for the new sound, sws for the new picture (scaled to the file's size); (10) a sound device stopping marked a good recording failed: it is a warning, the recording kept; (11) MXF refused rates outside its list after creating the file: rates within half a percent of a broadcast rate are taken as it, MXF refuses others first, and a file begun but not usable is taken away; the timecode is checked against the rate before anything is written; (12) the dialog's destructor changed the recorder's callbacks while it might call them; (13) a late end notice from a recording could land on the next: notices carry the recording's number, and the dialog's own flag says whether it records. Also: RGB and full-range feeds converted with their own matrices and the file tagged to match (BT.709 at 720 lines and up); interlaced feeds encoded and tagged as such; a feed joined mid-GOP starts at its first whole picture; a sound stream that cannot be converted leaves the picture recording; drop-frame timecode kept; FFmpeg input options in the dialog; tests use free ports, a longer sender and real-time sources.
  - CI fix: macOS and Windows tell of another program's writes late, so the clip being recorded was not seen growing there; the dialog says itself that its file is being written (each frame recorded), on every system.
  - Not done: SDI and HDMI cards (Blackmagic DeckLink, AJA) and NDI need their makers' SDKs and runtimes (not open source); deck control (RS-422 / VTR) and batch capture from a log; XDCAM HD422 (MPEG-2 4:2:2 in MXF); several sound devices at once; checked against real cameras and capture cards (none in the cloud build).
- [x] 14. Higher-order ambisonics (to third order, 16 channels) with binaural monitoring through SOFA HRTFs (M). core/Ambisonics: `ambisonicEncode` (the real spherical harmonics to third order, ACN, SN3D, order l scaled by focus^l; `foaEncode` is its first order), `ambisonicRotation`/`ambisonicRotate` (each order's channels mixed among themselves, the mix fitted by least squares over 64 turned directions against a pseudo-inverse made once, so it is exact whatever the convention's signs), `allradDecoder` (Zotter and Frank's AllRAD: 2000 virtual speakers on a Fibonacci grid, VBAP over the speakers' convex hull with imaginary speakers overhead (its share to its neighbours) and underneath (dropped), max-rE weighting, LFE rows zero; then each speaker equalised to the same diffuse-field power, which keeps first-order sound from collecting in sparse overhead speakers, and the whole scaled to keep a sound's power), `Hrirs`, `binauralDirections` (Lebedev's 50-point grid, exact for third order) and `sphericalHeadHrirs` (Brown and Duda's head rendered as taps), and `AmbisonicBinaural` (a sampling decode with max-rE at Lebedev's weights folded into one filter pair per channel, convolved directly with no added latency, scaled so a diffuse field keeps its power: measured heads and the model, and every order, within 3 dB). core/Surround: layouts `ambix2` (9) and `ambix3` (16), `ambisonicOrder`, `ambisonicLayoutFor`, `layoutDecoder` (AllRAD per speaker layout and order, cached); panning encodes to the layout's order; the stereo fold reads the first order of any. media/Sofa: `readSofa` (libmysofa, optional at build: a SOFA file or a zip holding one, its 48 kHz file when several; resampled, loudness-normalised, each of the 50 directions the nearest measured, delays as leading zeros, at most 512 taps at 48 kHz), `sadieKu100Pack` (SADIE II's KU100 SOFA zip from Zenodo, sha256-pinned, Apache-2.0), and the process-wide choice `setBinauralHrtf` ("" the model, "ku100", or a file) with `binauralHrirs` (cached per rate; read in the background during playback, the model meanwhile, then renderers rebuilt by generation). media/Decoder `decodeAmbisonic` returns the highest order the channels hold (4, 9 or 16); `MediaItem::ambisonic` from the stream layout or SA3D is capped by the channels. The mixer: an ambisonic clip at its own order (media marked lower plays its first channels) is turned at that order every 64 samples; in an ambisonic sequence it goes into the field at the sequence's order (lower orders as they are, higher ones cut); in a speaker surround sequence it is now decoded to the speakers with AllRAD and kept like a field (past the stereo inserts, through the track's fader and automation and a bus's), rather than folded to stereo and panned; in stereo it is heard binaurally at its order through the chosen head (renderers per clip and for monitoring, rebuilt when the order, rate or head changes) or through the cardioids. Export: PCM carries the field at its order with an ambisonic layout and SA3D of that order; AAC takes the first order (it has no 9- or 16-channel layouts); AAC field bitrate scales with the channels. App: Play › Ambisonic Monitoring adds Spherical Head Model / Measured Head (SADIE II KU100, downloaded after asking) / Personal HRTF (SOFA File)…, remembered and checked as chosen (`setBinauralHrtfChoice`); the bin's Ambisonic Audio marks media to the highest order its channels hold; Sequence Settings offers second and third order. MCP `montage_ambisonics` `order` and `hrtf` (head, ku100, a path; reports the sequence's order, the head and whether SOFA is supported), `ambix2`/`ambix3` in layouts. Fixture tests/data/sofa (a made-up 26-direction head, written by make_fixture.py with netCDF4).

Tests: media `higherOrderAmbisonics` (the pool's keys and channels per order, a nine-channel decode, noise reduction on a 16-channel field keeping its shape, in the mix and with Pitch Shift on a second-order field, an export's mixer keeping its head across a change of HRTF).
- [x] 15. Spatial video (MV-HEVC) in, and out where the hardware allows (M). **In: done.** **Out: done in Phase 8 #15** (x265's own multiview encoder, built with Montage; the "Not done" below is how it stood then). media/Decoder reads MV-HEVC as a stereo layout of its own ("mv", each eye a whole view): found from the file's eyes box (vexu, both eyes) or, without one, from the HEVC parameter sets holding two views (`multiviewStream` opens a decoder on them; nothing is decoded); each eye's view is chosen by the stream's 3D reference displays message (`view_pos_available`), else the base view is the left; the decoder asks FFmpeg for that view alone (`view_ids`; the right eye's in software, as hardware decoders give the base view only). Interpret Footage's Swap Eyes and Not Stereo work on it (not stereo: the first view, flat). It needs FFmpeg 7.1 or later (`multiviewDecoding`); older builds see the first view, as before. **Not done:** MV-HEVC out. FFmpeg 8 encodes it only through `hevc_nvenc` on NVIDIA GPUs reporting MV-HEVC (frames fed as alternating views with view ids), and Apple's own VideoToolbox/AVFoundation path is outside FFmpeg; neither can be checked here, so exports stay side-by-side 3D (which Vision Pro and YouTube also take). **Tests:** media `spatialVideo` (tests/data/mvhevc/spatial.mov, made by `make_fixture.sh` with x265 4.1 built for multiview: probed as "mv" at 128 × 72, the left eye's red square and the right eye's blue one each in its own place in a stereoscopic sequence, swapped, and flat as its first view; skipped, after checking the file reads flat, without FFmpeg 7.1). **FFmpeg 8.0 colour fix:** 8.0's swscale (fixed in 8.0.1) turned blues green converting 4:2:0 at its own height to 16-bit RGBA with bilinear filtering (an unsigned shift in `yuv2rgba64_1`); the decoder tries a small blue picture once (`bilinearRgba64Sound`) and uses bicubic where it fails. The spatial video test caught it. Review fixes: projects saved before this (stereo check version 2) find their MV-HEVC files when opened, also those without an eyes box; "mv" forced on a file of one view reads flat in the probe as in the decoder; the base view's id is read rather than taken to be 0; an eye's view that cannot be chosen fails the open instead of showing the other eye; a stream that places its eyes (3D reference displays) is not reversed again by its eyes box; without an eyes box a second view of id 0 (an alpha layer's) is not taken for an eye; the eye's view is chosen before the decoder first opens (no hardware decoder opened and dropped); the fixture has no B-frames (its timestamps were out of order); a second fixture with an eyes box (views reversed) covers that path; Interpret Footage, MCP and the error message list "mv".

# Phase 7

The sixth gap analysis (`docs/research/phase7-roadmap.md`, October 2026) ranks what is still missing against Premiere 26.5, Resolve 21.1, Final Cut Pro 12.4 and Media Composer 2026.8, with Baselight, Pro Tools, Descript and CapCut for what editors now expect. Testable items first; monitor calibration (needs a probe) and anything needing hardware wait for last.

- [x] 1. Script view with lined coverage: the screenplay (Final Draft, Fountain or text) as a panel. Each take is a vertical line down the lines it covers. Click a line to cue every take at that line. Tests: core `screenplays`, `projectScript`; render `linedScript` (layout, hit tests, painted page, PDF page count); media `mcpBuildsACutFromAScript`; app `linedScriptPanel`.

Dialogue match: make ADR or a pickup sound like the production take, matching room reverb, a tonal match that follows the reference over time, and level, with room tone under it; plus a convolution reverb (S–M) audio/RoomResponse: a radix-2 FFT; `Convolver` (zero latency: the first 512 taps directly, the rest uniformly partitioned overlap-save, due a block after their input) and offline `convolve`; `roomImpulse` (each octave's noise, 125 Hz–8 kHz, decaying at its own RT60 after a pre-delay, unit energy, a seed per side) and `loadImpulseResponse` (any sound file, trailing silence trimmed); blind room measurement from speech: `bandReverbTime`/`estimateRoom` (De-Reverb's falls, now `reverbTimeFromLevels`, per octave) and `lateReverbDb` (in the quieter moments, the sound against what a room of that decay would hold there, noise floor taken out). New Convolution Reverb effect (Room Level, Direct Level, Pre-Delay, decay per octave, or an impulse-response file). render/DialogueMatch: `planDialogueMatch` estimates the reference's room, finds the Room Level by bisection that leaves the target as much late reverberation as the reference, fits Match Voice's EQ to the roomed sound (or every half second over 2 s windows against the same moment of the reference, smoothed, as keys) and sets the clip volume from loudness (Auto Mix's LUFS); `applyDialogueMatch` puts the EQ first and the room after it, tagged, replacing an earlier match or Match Voice EQ; `matchDialogue` for clips of a sequence (the reference as played, its clip volume counted). Clip › Match Dialogue to Reference… (after Set Voice Reference; room, tone, over time, level, and room tone under each clip on the next free track below, a new one if none); MCP `montage_match_dialogue`. Tests: media `roomResponse` (live = offline convolution within 1e-3 (4e-5 measured), reset and a shared kernel; impulse-response files trimmed, normalised, both sides kept; made rooms' octave RT60 within 10 %; speech in rooms of 0.8 and 1.4 s: broadband RT within 15 %, octaves 500 Hz–4 kHz within 30 %; late reverberation rises with the room's share), `dialogueMatch` (JFK through a 0.8 s room on a thin boom against dry, louder ADR: late reverberation matched within 0.3 dB, spectrum 7.8 → 0.9 dB apart, loudness within 0.1 LU; over MCP; the mixer plays exactly the planned sound; a reference whose tone changes halfway is followed; short references; a file's response through the mixer, also after a seek), app `matchVoiceToAReference` (one undo step).

Use it to build the M&E for foreign versions, to rebalance, or to take music out of a scene (M) With Bandit v2 (Watcharasupat, Wu and Lerch, 2024; code Apache-2.0, the English model's weights CC BY-SA 4.0, downloaded from Zenodo record 12701995 on first use, 447 MB). scripts/gen-bandit-graph.py builds the network from the authors' code as an ONNX graph (64 musical bands, 8 time and band GRU pairs, a complex mask per stem; PyTorch's gate order turned into ONNX's) whose weights are read in place from their checkpoint; it matches their PyTorch model to 4.5e-6. media/BanditSeparation: 48 kHz, 8 s chunks every 6 s crossfaded over 2 s (the authors' every 1 s is six times the work), the last chunk ending where the input does (trailing silence makes the network give nothing), input shorter than a chunk looped, a mono recording (both channels equal) separated once; torchaudio's centred, normalised STFT and its inverse. Separate Stems offers "Dialogue, music and effects (a film's mix)" (stems 3 in StemSplitter, `separateStemsToFiles`, MCP `montage_separate_stems`, `montage-cli stems --stems 3`); its clips get the Dialogue, Music and Effects roles. New audio-stream choice for masters: the mix, then the dialogue and an M&E (`StemsDialogueAndME`; a stream's role may join several with "+"; MCP `audio_streams: "dme"`; Export's Audio streams). On a synthetic mix (JFK, a chord, noise bursts) the stems are 27.6, 20.3 and 15.1 dB SDR against 9.6, -10.7 and -16.7 dB in the mix; the C++ separation matches a Python emulation on the authors' model to 5e-7. CI fetches the model (`scripts/fetch-models.sh --dme`). Timecode from audio (LTC): read SMPTE or EBU LTC from any channel, set it as the file's timecode with the original kept (audio-only files too), sync by it, silence the LTC channel; generate LTC (S) audio/Ltc: the 80-bit word (ST 12-1: BCD digits, drop-frame and colour flags, eight user-bit groups, the polarity bit that keeps the zeros even, at bit 27 or at bit 59 at 25 fps), drop-frame numbering both ways, `encodeLtc` (any rate and sample rate, each sample the square wave's average over 31 µs so edges rise in about 25 µs), and `LtcDecoder` (biphase mark at any sample rate, the slow average taken off, hysteresis at a fifth of the recent peak, transitions placed between samples, bits of 0.33 to 0.66 ms, the bit length followed as the speed drifts, words found by their sync word). media/LtcTimecode: `findLtc` takes the longest run of words that follow on from each other (a lost word allowed), the rate's whole frames from the word length and the highest frame label, 23.976 or 29.97 from the measured rate, and fits frame number against time for the timecode at the first sample and the timecode's speed against the sound's clock; `readLtc` reads the first 30 s of every channel of every audio stream (new Decoder `decodeSourceChannels`) and takes the channel with the longest run. New MediaItem fields `ltcChannel`, `ltcTimecode` and `fileTimecode` (saved; carried through subclips, Interpret Footage, consolidation; cleared on Replace Footage); `useLtcTimecode` makes the LTC's start the media's (and its subclips') timecode, keeps the file's own and the LTC's rate as `timecode_rate`, and takes the LTC channel out of what the file's clips on audio tracks play (a clip playing only it is disabled); `useFileTimecode` goes back. New clips leave the LTC channel out (`newClipChannels`: the first stream's channels less it; makeClip and placeMedia). Merge Clips, Sync Dailies and multicam sync by timecode follow from `timecode`. Sound files' Timecode column shows their timecode at their recorder's or LTC's rate. Generating: `writeLtcWav`, `writeSequenceLtc` (the sequence's timecode, drop frame at 29.97 and 59.94, a word every two frames at 48 to 60 fps), `placeLtcTrack` (a new muted audio track named LTC, the clip's role Timecode). Tests: media `ltcTimecode` (each rate at 44.1, 48 and 96 kHz, 26 dB down in noise, upside down: the start within 5 µs, user bits; drop-frame labels over a day; across midnight; a 0.1 % slow clock measured; dropouts; noise and tones refused; a camera's AAC sound with LTC on its right channel and a recorder's WAV with it on its left: their channels, starts within 0.5 ms, the clips' channels, nothing of the LTC in what the clip plays, matched two seconds apart by timecode, subclips, saved and loaded, back to the file's; multi-stream defaults; sequence LTC at 29.97 DF and 50 fps; over MCP), app `timecodeFromLtcInTheBin` (one undo step, Add LTC Track). Gyro stabilisation from camera motion data (GoPro, Sony, DJI, Insta360, Canon, Blackmagic RAW, phone loggers), with rolling-shutter correction from the same data and horizon lock (M) (partly: GoPro GPMF, CAMM and `.gcsv` logs; the others are under Not done) media/MotionData (no new dependency; parsers of Montage's own): GoPro's GPMF (KLV: DEVC, STRM, SCAL, GYRO and ACCL, a payload's samples spread over its packet's time), CAMM (gyro, acceleration and exposure samples, the rolling-shutter skew taken as the readout) and `.gcsv` logs (scales, readout), read through FFmpeg's demuxer (`gpmd` and `camm` tracks) or beside the clip; accelerometer readings sent apart from the gyro's interpolated onto its times. `syncMotionData` lines them up with the point tracker's per-frame movement: the gyro's integral over each frame (on a 1 ms grid) correlated with the movement across, down and the roll, over ±2 s (2 ms, then 0.1 ms), choosing which sensor channel and sign is each camera axis (proper rotations only), the score a weighted mean of the correlations (0.4 needed); `refineGyroLens` then measures the focal length from points followed between the twelve pairs of frames that turned most, predicting each through the turn the data gives (each row at its own time when the readout is known); the readout itself barely shows between neighbouring frames, so it comes from the data or the Rolling Shutter setting. `gyroPath`: the orientation integrated from the readings (quaternions), sampled twice a frame at the picture's times, with the way up from the accelerometer smoothed over about two seconds in the world; kept in the effect as text (each step's turn in microradians, rounding carried on). `gyroCorrections`: the smoothed orientation (three running means of the orientation, together Gaussian-like and Smoothness wide; 0 holds the first frame), Horizon Lock turning it about the view axis until up is straight up (less when looking up or down), and the camera's spin for rolling shutter; render/Processing warps each output pixel through the turn, then by the turn of the source row it lands on (two passes, a table of turns per row); Zoom to Fill finds the zoom that keeps all eight edge points covered in every frame. Stabilize: Method › Camera Gyro (offered once data is found and lined up), Horizon Lock, Gyro Offset (ms); Analyze reads and lines up the data when there is some and switches to Camera Gyro, saying why when it is not used. MCP `montage_add_effect` stabilize does the same (`camera_gyro` in its answer). Tests: media `gyroStabilization` (a synthetic camera shaking in three axes, rolled 0.1 rad, its sensor on its side, 60 % readout, with a `.gcsv` log 37 ms behind: the log read, the lag within 3 ms, the axes and signs, the lens within 2 % once refined, the path's text round trip; Camera Gyro holding a levelled view within 0.03 of a still level camera's picture where the shaky one is 0.25 off, without Horizon Lock the rolled view, a near-zero readout worse, Zoom to Fill leaving no edge; MCP adding it with the data; the same readings in a CAMM track and in GPMF payloads (with SCAL) lined up alike; a clip without data, and noise, refused), `goproGyro` (GoPro's HERO5 sample: 400 readings a second, gravity at 9.8, lined up with a score over 0.85, stabilised to under half the shake; HERO8's in-camera stabilised picture refused; skipped without `MONTAGE_GOPRO_SAMPLES`, which CI downloads), app `stabilizeAndTrackFromInspector` (Camera Gyro not offered for footage without data). Not done: Sony (rtmd), DJI, Canon and Blackmagic RAW motion data, Insta360's own trailer, lens distortion in the warp. Review fixes: the plan caches handed out a plan they could free on the next frame (cleared before adding, not after); a reading that is not a number dropped, and the readings integrated over each window on their own; GPMF's image orientation (IORI) marks footage steadied in the camera, refused with that reason; lenses wider than about 127° (focal under 0.25 widths) refused, which keeps 360° cameras' data out, and 360° media (a projection set) refused up front; Cancel reaches the line-up and the lens refinement; a `.gcsv` readout running another way than top to bottom not used; smoothing three running means rather than a weighted mean per frame (linear in the clip's length, not quadratic); the Inspector reads only the path's header; MCP reports the data as used only when Camera Gyro is the method; the HERO5 test checks the way up from the accelerometer; CI keeps whichever sample clips downloaded; no other products named in the text.
- [x] 7. Restoration: automatic dirt and dust removal, a dust-busting brush, vertical scratch removal, a tracked patch replacer, and clone or heal paint for rigs, logos and boom shadows (M) render/Restoration (no model, no new dependency). `detectDirt`: a pixel is dirt when it is lighter or darker than both neighbouring frames, followed along the motion (`chainedMotion`, as Video Denoise), by more than a threshold (5 σ of the measured noise, at least 0.03, scaled by Sensitivity) while those two agree; pieces grown by hysteresis at half the threshold and kept only when no larger than Largest Speck and the motion around them was followed well (the ring around a piece must match across frames, which keeps misaligned texture out). The motion at each pixel is the mean of four points outside the defect (a speck pulls the motion measured on it). `fillFromNeighbours` puts in the neighbours' mean along that motion. Dust Spot Removal: spots marked by hand (`DustSpot`, frame, centre, radius as text); `dustMatte` takes the pixels in the spot that differ from the neighbours (the whole circle when there are none, filled from its surround). `detectScratches`: a thin-line response (the pixel against both sides at Width + 1), per band of an eighth of the frame, a column kept when its mean stands out from the band's 90th percentile and from noise and the sign agrees down more than 60 % of the rows, runs no wider than Width; `fillAcross` fills each row from either side. `seamlessPaste`: Poisson blending (the source's gradients, the surroundings' level), solved coarse to fine by SOR; `fillFromSurround`; `shiftedImage`. Paint strokes (`PaintStroke`, as text: clone or heal, frame, whole clip, tracked, source offset, radius, softness, points) drawn as soft capsules (`strokeMatte`); a tracked stroke moves and scales with the effect's mask box from the frame it was drawn on (`paintStrokesAt`). New effects (Repair): Dirt Removal and Dust Spot Removal run at the source stage with the frames either side (Compositor `clipSource`, before Video Denoise and the grade); Scratch Removal, Patch Fill (needs a mask; Seamless or Copy; the source is an offset from where the mask is, so it moves with a tracked mask, and the offset is keyframable) and Paint (Clone and Heal). app/PaintOverlay on the Program monitor: armed from the Inspector (Paint on the Monitor, Mark Spots on the Monitor; Clear This Frame, Clear All), Alt-click sets the source, a drag is one stroke, a click marks a spot and Ctrl-click takes it away, Esc stops (each one undo step). MCP `montage_add_effect` takes `spots` and `strokes` as text. Tests: render `restorationKernels` (six specks on texture found and filled within 0.03 with nothing else changed and a larger blot kept; dark and light scratches found, a bar and an edge left, filled within 0.03, polarity; a patch blended seamlessly within 0.01 where a copy is 0.1 off; heal within 0.015, clone exact, a stroke on its frame only, a tracked stroke following its box), media `restorationOnFootage` (lossless panning footage with four specks and a smudge on one frame: the specks taken out within 0.06 and nothing else changed, the smudge left; clean frames untouched; Show Detected; Dust Spot Removal removing the smudge and nothing else), app `restorationPaintInProgramMonitor` (arming, Alt-click and a drag making one stroke with the right offset, undo and redo, the offset kept, Clear This Frame, Esc, spots marked and removed on their frame).

Synthetic focus: click to focus, keyed rack focus or focus that follows a tracked face or object, aperture shapes (blade count and rotation, anamorphic oval, cat's-eye toward the edges), highlight bloom, focus breathing, and depth haze (M) (partly: a tracked object is followed only as a keyed point) render/LensBlur: `lensBlur` blurs each pixel by its own signed circle of confusion from the depth map (`signedCoc`: sharp within the range, growing over the falloff to the full radius; behind the focus negative), the picture cut into layers by blur (two neighbouring layers share a pixel by how near it is to each), far to near, each layer blurred with the aperture's taps at a scale that keeps its radius small and laid over the ones behind, which are first filled in under it (a push-pull pyramid, only in the layer's box), so a near edge blurs over the background with no dark or sharp halo and the background never bleeds over a sharp subject; gathered in linear light (TransferTables), highlights over a threshold lifted by an invertible curve first so they open into bright discs; `apertureWeight` (round, or 3 to 12 blades turned, squeezed tall as anamorphic lenses, cut toward the frame's edges by a second disc for a cat's eye), `depthAt` (the median round a point), `breathe` (scale about the centre, edges held). Lens Blur (Depth) uses it: Follow Focus Depth, Focus Point (point_x/point_y; keyed points rack focus between the depths at the keys either side, eased) or Nearest Face (the largest face's depth; the focus depth when there is none); f-stop scales the radius (f/2.8 the radius as set); blades, blade rotation, anamorphic squeeze, cat's eye, highlights, threshold and breathing (the picture grows a little as the focus comes near). Multi-layer EXR in and out: choose a layer (AOV) per clip, pick Cryptomatte mattes by clicking objects, write the clip's mattes and layers into VFX pulls; HTJ2K compression in EXR and in IMF (S–M) OpenEXR 3.5 (with Imath, and its bundled OpenJPH, libdeflate and zstd; BSD/MIT) is fetched and built static (`MONTAGE_WITH_OPENEXR`). media/Exr: `readExrInfo` (the display window, compression, layers by the name before the last dot, the main picture "" first; a bare channel other than R, G, B, A or Y its own layer, Z the commonest; multi-part files' layers named after their part; deep parts skipped), `readExrLayer` (a layer as RGBA floats over the display window; one channel shows grey, X/Y/Z as R/G/B), `readExrChannels` (every channel as stored, half or float, for copying), Cryptomatte 1.2 (`<name>NN.R/G/B/A` rank pairs named by the header's `cryptomatte/<key>/name`, the manifest in the header or a sidecar beside the file (never above it); ids the names' MurmurHash3 as floats (`cryptomatteId`, 8 hex digits), `CryptomatteFrame::idAt` and `matte` (coverage summed over the ids), a small cache keyed by file and modification time), and `writeExr` (layers of 1 to 4 channels, half or float, any of OpenEXR's 13 compressions, string attributes). The decoder reads an EXR (or sequence) through OpenEXR when a layer is asked for (the interpretation's `layer=`) or when FFmpeg can't (HTJ2K, zstd); `probeMedia` too. A clip's `layer` (saved) is passed to the decoder by the compositor, proxies aside. Masks gain a Cryptomatte shape (7): `mask.crypto_layer` (which Cryptomatte, "" the first) and `mask.crypto_ids` (hex ids); the compositor tells the matte which EXR the frame came from (CryptomatteScope), so it follows a sequence frame by frame; GPU masks fall back to the CPU for it. App: the Inspector's Layer row for a multi-layer EXR clip (one undo step), and for a Cryptomatte mask its Cryptomatte, the objects picked by name and Clear; in the Program monitor a click on an object adds it (Ctrl/Cmd-click removes it, one undo step each), the picked objects tinted and the name of the one under the pointer shown. Export: an OpenEXR compression (ExportSettings `exrCompression`, export dialog, render farm, MCP `exr_compression`) writes the frames through OpenEXR (`exrLayers` adds more layers to each). VFX pulls (dialog, MCP): OpenEXR compression, the source render's other layers and Cryptomattes copied as they are (their manifests inline; the plate shows the clip's layer, the main picture then as "main"), and the clip's masks as one-channel `matte_<effect>` layers drawn as the compositor draws them on each pulled frame; the pull list gains a Layers column. IMF: `htj2k` (dialog: Lossless, High-Throughput) codes the pictures through OpenJPH (lossless 5-3, 32 x 32 blocks, 128/256 precincts, CPRL, a tile-part per component with TLM; Rsiz 0x4000 and a CAP marker), with HTJ2KPictureCodingSchemeGeneric, the J2KExtendedCapabilities (Pcap, Ccap) in the MXF sub-descriptor and the CPL, and the ST 2067-21:2023 application id; verifyImf accepts an HTJ2K codestream naming Part 15. MCP: `montage_probe_media` lists an EXR's layers and Cryptomattes (objects by name and id), `montage_place_media` takes an EXR `layer`, `montage_add_effect` documents shape 7. **Tests:** media `multiLayerOpenExr` (a synthetic HTJ2K render with diffuse, Z and a Cryptomatte: its layers and objects, the decoder reading the main picture and a layer, a missing layer refused, Invert limited to the ball on the timeline, saved and loaded, a zstd pull with main, Z, the Cryptomatte with its manifest and the Invert's matte, an HTJ2K export, an unknown compression refused, MCP probe and placing a layer) and `cryptomatteSamples` (Cryptomatte's sample images, MONTAGE_CRYPTOMATTE_SAMPLES, fetched in CI: 32,000 bunny objects' ids against their names, the centre leaf, several Cryptomattes beside layers, a sidecar manifest, UTF-8 names); render `exportsImfMaster` (an HTJ2K master verified, its CPL and MXF labels, decoded by OpenJPEG to the same codes, lossy HTJ2K refused); app `cryptomattePicking` (the Layer row and its undo, the mask's controls, clicks adding the ball and floor, the render inverting both, Ctrl-click and its undo, Clear).

Monitor calibration: a calibration 3D LUT per display and per output (GUI viewer, full-screen, SDI or NDI reference, HDR viewer), and a network patch generator so calibration software can measure a display through Montage (S) render/MonitorCalibration (no new dependency): `loadCalibrationLut` reads a .cube (3D or 1D), an Autodesk .3dl (Lustre or Flame: the input lattice line, blue fastest, 10/12/16-bit from its "Mesh" header or its values; `parse3dl`) or a CLF/CTF baked to 65^3, kept until the file changes; `calibratedImage` runs an 8-bit picture through it on all cores (each level's lattice place worked out once; the same trilinear interpolation as `Lut3D::apply`); `calibrateImage` a frame as shown over black; `calibrateHdrPicture` the HDR viewer's linear-light picture through a LUT made for PQ BT.2020 (to PQ and back through tables). app/DisplayCalibration keeps a LUT per screen (by its name) for the viewers and full-screen output, another per screen for the HDR viewer, and one per reference output device, in the settings, with Bypass all. Applied last, where each display is drawn: the Source and Program viewers (picture, compare reference, two-up halves; `ViewerWidget::drawnImage`), the full-screen output (`CleanFeedWindow::drawnFrame`), the HDR viewer's pictures as they are rendered (`PlaybackController::setHdrCalibration`, following the viewer's screen), and the reference output's signal after its conversion and framing (`Scheduler::setCalibration`, so black borders are corrected too; frames packed again when it changes; status says "calibrated"). The pictures themselves (scopes, caches, renders, exports, exported LUTs, the remote review stream) never see it. The patch generator (app/PatchGenerator) speaks the network pattern protocol calibration software drives grading applications and PGenerator with: Montage connects to the software's port (20002), each message a 4-byte big-endian length and an XML `<calibration>` with a colour, a background, a bit depth and the patch's place (or a list of rectangles); `PatchStream` frames and parses them. Patches fill a chosen screen (a frameless full-screen window, Esc stops) or go out on the reference output as code values, unconverted (`Scheduler::setPatch`), around the display's calibration unless asked to go through it (to verify one). It waits for the software and connects again when it goes. Playback › Video Output › Monitor Calibration… holds it all. Tests: .3dl order, headers and errors; files read and read again when rewritten; 8-bit pictures within a level of `Lut3D::apply` at every level; HDR identity round trip; messages framed in pieces; patches as pictures; the output calibrated, patched and back; in the app, the viewer and full-screen drawn through the screen's LUT and bypassed, the dialog, and a stand-in calibration program driving patches to a screen and to the reference output.

- [x] 12. Sky replacement: find the sky, key it with refined edges, put in a still or moving sky that follows the camera, and match the foreground's colour to it (S–M):
  - render/SkyReplace and the `sky_replace` effect (Keying › Sky Replacement), applied when tracks are composited like Track Matte Key. The sky is found on a smaller copy: bright, smooth pixels grown from the top of the frame, with a guard against creeping down a soft horizon into hazy water. Sky seen through leaves joins it when it has the sky's colour at that height nearby. Holes with nothing dark in them (clouds) are filled, while a bird stays. Scraps are dropped. With the depth model installed (Use Depth, on by default), near things are kept out, so a pale wall reaching the top stays. Edges are then put on the picture's own with the colour-line matte refine, and Edge Shift and Soften follow. Lowest Sky and Sensitivity tune it, a mask limits where the sky may be, and View shows the matte.
  - The new sky is the picture on another track (0 = the one above, hidden unless Hide is off; a still or a moving sky) or a gradient from its top colour to its horizon colour at the sky's lowest row. Sky Exposure and Sky Defocus adjust it. Follow Camera moves it with the camera's analysed motion (measured when the effect is added to footage, in the Inspector and MCP; Analyze redoes it), or with the clip's 3D camera solve turned only (a sky at infinity: K R R0ᵀ K⁻¹), by Follow Amount.
  - Match Foreground moves the foreground's colour part of the way the new sky differs from the old (per-channel gains). Light Wrap lays the sky's blurred light over the foreground's edges. The GPU compositor falls back to the CPU for it. Provenance lists Depth Anything V2 when Use Depth is on. The Inspector offers to get the depth model when it is missing.
  - Tests: a rendered scene with a known sky (hills, a tree with thin branches closing a pocket of sky, a soft cloud, a bird) checks a sure-region error under 1% and IoU above 0.93, the pocket, cloud, bird, trunk and branches, Lowest Sky, a picture with no sky, and depth keeping a pale building out. Also tested: the composite (untouched ground without match or wrap, warmer ground when matched, wrap at the horizon only), the gradient and its horizon, the warp, following analysed motion and a 3D solve (f·tan θ), and the sequence path (V2 hidden, sky red, ground warmed, gradient, matte view). An app test adds it from the Inspector on footage and checks the motion is measured. Also checked by eye on six GoPro sample frames (overcast, blue sky over a white building, a hazy sea, fisheye, indoors) with and without depth.
  - Review fixes:
    - A dissolve into or out of the shot showed its old sky. Transitions now finish each clip's layer the same way, and a sky track's own transitions mix the skies.
    - A moved or smaller picture was searched and moved in canvas terms. It now works within the clip's opaque bounds on the canvas, for the search, the camera motion, the mask and the sky.
    - With the effect in a grade node, the sky track's hiding and the effect disagreed. Both now use the effects compositing finds.
    - A long pan ran off the sky picture and smeared its edge. The sky is now anchored at the clip's middle frame and mirrored past its edges.
    - With nothing on the sky track, the matte and depth were still worked out every frame.
    - Analyze on it read camera gyro data and named its undo step after Stabilize.
    - A disabled 3D Camera Tracker could supply the solve, and a solve with no focal length gave a broken move.
    - Provenance claimed depth when the model was not installed.
    - The depth button wiped Use Depth's keys.
