    - A planar or rotation-only solve from distant features when the 3D solve fails on a pure pan (the analysed 2D motion covers pans).
    - A sky library.
    - Sky reflections in water.
- [x] 13. Face reshaping and eye contact: slim or widen the face, jaw, nose, eyes and mouth; turn a presenter's gaze to the lens when they read from a script (M)
  - media/FaceMesh fits MediaPipe's Face Mesh V2 to each face YuNet finds: 468 points over the face and 5 on each iris. The model is Apache-2.0, converted to ONNX with Google's weights unchanged, 4.9 MB, and downloaded with the face models on first use. It runs in two rounds, as MediaPipe tracks: the face's box upright by its eyes, then the points' own box. It also gives the head's yaw, pitch and roll. Meshes are cached by the picture's content.
  - Face Shape (Refine) has ten sliders from -100 to 100: Face Width, Jaw, Chin Length, Forehead Height, Nose Width, Nose Length, Eye Size, Eye Distance, Mouth Width and Lip Height. Each moves its group of mesh points in the face's own directions. The picture follows by an affine moving-least-squares warp, worked out on a fine grid. Still points near moving ones are left out of the warp, so the skin between them gives way instead of pinching. A ring well outside the face holds still. Show Face Mesh draws where the points go.
  - Look at Lens (Refine) turns each open eye's iris to where it would be looking at the lens for the head's pose. That is about the eye's middle (a twentieth of its width toward the outer corner, as measured on portraits looking at the lens), moved against the head's turn by the eyeball's radius. Up and down are kept within a band that leaves the iris showing between the lids (at least a third of its radius either way), since the lids are not moved, so it corrects side to side fully and up and down only a little. The iris is drawn again at its new place: what was under a lid is drawn from its tone at that distance from its middle. Where it was is filled from what is round it (a membrane over the hole, so the white beside it and the lids' shadow above blend in). The lids' edges are kept. Amount sets how far. Past Largest Gaze Corrected (20° by default) it fades out over 10°, so looking well away is left alone. Shut or blinking eyes are left alone. Show Irises Found marks each iris and where it goes.
  - Both are in MCP (montage_add_effect `face_shape`, `look_at_lens`) and in Provenance (MediaPipe Face Mesh V2). The Inspector asks to download the models when they are missing.
  - Tests:
    - render `faceShapeWarpAndIris` (no model): the warp carries the picture under a moved point and holds the ring and beyond; nothing moved changes nothing; a drawn eye's iris moves to the middle at its size, the white filled in behind it, the skin outside the lids untouched, and half the amount moves it half way; head pose from a turned and tilted mesh.
    - media `faceShapeAndLookAtLens` (with the models): the mesh on the JFK colour portrait sits on the face. Wider face and larger eyes are checked on a picture of its own coordinates: the moving points land within 20% of their moves (5% measured). Far from the face nothing moves. On a closer copy of the portrait looking up (`jfk-looking-up-close.jpg`, public domain), each iris's highlight moves with it to where the lens puts the iris. On Armstrong's portrait, which looks at the lens with the head near frontal, each iris is already within a tenth of the eye's width of where the lens puts it, and the portrait looking up reads as tilted up.
  - Review fixes:
    - Each face and each eye copied the whole frame (about 130 MB at 4K). The warp and the iris now copy only the part they read.
    - The fill where the iris was took over a thousand passes on a large iris. It is now solved on a coarser copy first, so it settles in a few passes.
    - The warp's grid was as fine as the pixels for faces 100 to 200 pixels across. It is now about 200 nodes across at any size.
    - Up and down correction could be nothing at all on narrow eyes. It now always allows a third of the iris's radius.
    - A mesh without points was read past its end.
    - The tests checked the gaze target only against itself. They now check it on a portrait looking at the lens and check the head's tilt. The warp test now checks inside the circle by its ring, and an iris half under a lid is moved.
    - MCP's message for missing face models, the CLI's model list, Provenance (YuNet too) and the README now include both effects.
  - Not done:
    - Moving the lids with the gaze. Up and down are limited because of this, so someone reading a script well below the lens is only partly corrected.
    - A learned gaze model. Look at Lens redraws the iris and does not re-render the eye.
    - Steadying the mesh over time. Each frame is fitted on its own, so a strong reshape can shimmer slightly on moving footage.
- [x] 14. Parameter expressions and links: drive any keyframable parameter with a short formula (time, frame, wiggle, loops, another clip's parameter, an audio level), and bake it to keys (S)
  - `Param::expr` holds a Lua 5.4 formula (or statements ending in `return`); `Param::at` runs it on the keyed value (`keyedAt`), so every render path, the GPU compositor, the mixer and the Keyframes panel see it. It can use `value`, `time`, `frame`, `fps`, `duration`, `wiggle(freq, amount [, octaves, falloff, seed])`, `noise`, `random([lo, hi])`, `loopOut(cycle|pingpong|offset|continue)`, `param([clip,] name or effect.name)` (another setting at the same moment on the timeline), `audio([track [, low|mid|high]])` (a track's level), `linear`, `ease`, `clamp` and Lua's maths by name.
  - render/Expressions: one sandboxed state per thread (no files, programs, clocks, loading, error catching, metatables, string matching or repeating; the libraries read-only; 64 MB and two million instructions an evaluation). Noise and random numbers come from `Param::exprSeed` (set when the expression is), so a frame is the same on any thread and after saving. Time counts from `Param::exprFrom`, which moves with the keys, so a split clip carries on where it was. Results are kept within the setting's range (angles may turn on), switches and choices whole. A failing expression gives the keyed value; its error is kept per setting for the Inspector. Expressions reading each other in a loop stop at 16 deep with an error.
  - `ExpressionScope` says which project, sequence and clip expressions belong to on a thread; the compositor, mixer and image row threads (and OpenFX plug-in threads) set or carry it, the app's window falls back to its open project. It caches where each setting is for its life.
  - Inspector: an "=" button (and Expression… on the box's menu) on a clip's own Transform, generator, sound and effect settings (not speed) opens an editor that checks the formula as it is typed and shows its value at the playhead; Bake to Keyframes and Remove. The button is checked while there is one and red while it fails; the box shows the result and is not editable then. The Keyframes panel draws it as a curve ("=" after the name). Projects save it. MCP `montage_expression` (set, bake, get). Bake samples every frame and thins to keys within a thousandth of the values' span (held keys for switches).
  - Frames using expressions are not put in the render cache, and a clip whose Transform has one is not copied through by smart rendering.
  - Tests:
    - media `parameterExpressions`: time, frame and the keyed value; statements with their own variables; wiggle within its amount, smooth, the same on another thread and after saving, different per setting, and carried on across a cut; random steady per frame; the four loopOut kinds; another clip's setting by name and id; the tone's level (about 25 % at -3 dB) and its band; eighteen ways out of the sandbox or into a hang refused with the keyed value and an error; the range clamp and angles past a turn; a loop between two clips stopped; a fade rendered; saved and loaded; baked to two or three keys; MCP set, error, bake and get.
    - app `inspectorExpressions`: the editor refusing a broken formula and applying one in one undo step; the box disabled; the Keyframes row; the failing button; bake and remove.
  - Review fixes:
    - `pcall` could catch the time limit and `string.rep`/`gsub` ran long in C: no error catching, matching or repeating now, and `table.move` is gone.
    - Expressions could change the shared libraries or metatables for every later one: they are read-only now and metatables out of reach; math's names no longer overwrite `type`.
    - Memory running out while setting up a call would abort: it is now set up protected, and a panic restarts the thread's state.
    - Every evaluation searched the sequence for its setting and took a lock: the scope caches it and errors are only written when they change.
    - Errors were kept per formula text, so two settings with the same formula shared one; they are kept per setting.
    - Seeds came from the clip and effect ids, so a split, pasted or duplicated clip changed its wiggle; and trimming a clip's head restarted its time. Both now travel with the setting.
    - The render cache and smart rendering could reuse frames an expression changes.
    - Keyframe buttons, Add Key, nudging and pasting wrote the expression's result into the keys; they use the keyed value.
    - Speed accepted an expression (a clip's length cannot follow one); the "=" button showed on trims, grade nodes and groups, where expressions do not run.
    - Results outside a setting's range, unrounded choices, NaN in whole-number arguments and fractional clip ids in `param` are handled.
  - Not done:
    - Expressions in AAF, OpenTimelineIO or OpenFX interchange: bake first.
    - The Keyframes panel does not cache an expression's curve, and baking a long clip holds the window briefly.
- [x] 15. Lip-synced dubbing: re-render the mouth to match a dub or regenerated words (M–L) With MuseTalk 1.5 (Tencent Music Entertainment, code and weights MIT), whose UNet draws the lower half of a 256 x 256 face crop in one pass over sd-vae-ft-mse's latents (Stability AI, MIT), guided by whisper-tiny's encoding of the sound (OpenAI, Apache-2.0). The three files (3.9 GB) download from the authors at pinned revisions (`lipSyncModel`, MONTAGE_LIPSYNC_MODEL); scripts/gen-lipsync-graphs.py builds four ONNX graphs from the authors' code (the UNet with its timestep-0 embedding and positional encoding of the sound, the VAE's encoder and decoder, and whisper's log-mel front end and encoder giving its input and each layer's output) whose weights are read in place from the PyTorch zip and the safetensors; the graphs are compiled into Montage and written beside the weights. They match the PyTorch models to 1.5e-6 (UNet), 3.5e-5 and 1.4e-5 (VAE) and 2.4e-3 on a scale of 244 (whisper). media/LipSync: `lipSyncWindow` cuts what a frame hears as MuseTalk's audio processor does (ten encoding frames of five layers from two video frames' worth before it); `lipSyncBox` crops from the face mesh (across the jaw, from as far above the nose's bridge as the chin is below it to a little under the chin); `lipSyncMask` covers the face below the nose, the chin's outline pushed down for an opening jaw, feathered as MuseTalk blends; `lipSyncFace` runs encoder, UNet and decoder and blends back (Lanczos resampling both ways). The "lip_sync" effect (Lip Sync, Refine: Amount, Sound From an audio track, every track when none) runs on the source picture before the grade, on the largest face (YuNet and Face Mesh V2), with whisper's encoding cached per 30 s of sound from the clip's start and results cached by picture, nearby sound and amount; like Motion Deblur it runs in exports and renders ahead (about 4 s a frame on four cores), and other views show the frame without it and say so. The render cache key follows the driving track's clips under the clip. Dub gains "Lip-sync the speaker's picture to the dub" (with a consent box), which gives the picture clips playing under the dub Lip Sync to its track (`edit::lipSyncToTrack`, part of the dub's undo step); MCP `montage_dub` `lip_sync` and `montage_add_effect` "lip_sync" need `consent: true`. Provenance lists it as Generated. `scripts/fetch-models.sh --lipsync`. **Not done:** only the largest face is redrawn; frames are drawn one at a time (no temporal smoothing, so fast speech can flicker slightly); log footage is seen as it is stored; CI does not download the 3.9 GB model, so it runs the model-free part of the test only. **Tests:** media `lipSyncDubbing` (the window's indexing at 25 and 30 fps and past the end; the box and mask of a synthetic face; `lipSyncToTrack` skipping generators and clips not under the sound, and repointing; MCP refusing without consent; with the model: on JFK's portrait, a frame hearing silence keeps the lips 0.050 of the face apart and frames through his speech open them to 0.079, nothing outside the crop changes, Amount 0 changes nothing; as a clip effect with jfk.wav under it, an interactive view goes without and says so, rendered ahead it is there and the view then has it; provenance). Review fixes: the sound whisper hears was a 30 s read of the one clip at the stretch's middle (and past its trim), so a dub, a clip a line, was mostly unheard; `trackSoundFrom` (render/AudioReactive) now mixes each clip over its own span from its trim, a named track heard even muted (what the picture says is not what is monitored); results are cached by the picture, the whole stretches of sound the frame's window draws on, the frame and the amount, and the render cache key by the clips in those stretches and which tracks are heard (not every clip under the clip), so a dub edit re-renders only the frames that hear it; a Sound From track since deleted leaves the picture as it is; a face too small to draw counts as no face instead of failing the export; a cancel no longer notes an empty reason; adding Lip Sync in the app asks for the person's consent each time (the Dub's own box covers it there); notices for the MuseTalk port in media/LipSync.cpp, transformers, librosa's mel filter bank (ISC) and the modification; the MuseTalk licence text completed. Tests: `lipSyncSegments` at a stretch's edge, `trackSoundFrom` with two trimmed clips and a gap, muted and named, `lipSyncTrack` for a deleted track.

# Phase 8

The seventh gap analysis (`docs/research/phase8-roadmap.md`, October 2026) ranks what is still missing against Premiere 26.5, Resolve 21.1, Final Cut Pro 12.4 and Media Composer 2026.8, with Baselight, Nuke, Pro Tools, Nuendo, Descript and CapCut, and open tools for delivery (DCP-o-matic, IMF Tool). Testable items first; the last two (OP-Atom hand-off, MV-HEVC out) cannot be checked in their target apps here.

- [x] 1. Conform check against the offline reference: lay the editor's reference movie against an imported timeline. Flag every shot that differs: a wrong take, a slip of some frames, a missing shot, or a different reposition. Solve each shot's reposition (scale, rotation, position, keyed when it moves) from the reference (S–M)
  - Done: `render/ConformCheck` (`conformCheck`, `applyConformFix`), Sequence › Conform Check… (`app/ConformCheckDialog`), MCP `montage_conform_check`. Each shot (the longest stretch where a clip from media is the picture on top) is compared at a quarter, half and three quarters in: grey, levels normalised (so a grade is not a difference), with a mask of what stays the same across the reference's shots (burnt-in timecode, a watermark, bars), closed so the changing digits are covered. In order: as it is; reframed (features of the reference followed into the shot by pyramidal Lucas–Kanade from the clip's own Transform, a similarity fitted, twice; when that does not hold, a coarse search over sizes and positions at about 64 pixels across first), keyed every 12 frames (at most 25 keys) and kept as one key when it stays within what the measure wanders by; slipped, placed as it is, searched two seconds either side (preferred to a reframe that only moves the picture, the same thing in a panning shot); slipped and reframed; another take (the scene's other media, or all of it, scanned at 128 pixels across, the best two searched frame by frame); missing (a gap where the reference is neither flat nor only its overlays). Fixes: the slip, the take with its linked sound, the Transform (static or keyed; Fit kept, anchor and width/height scale reset). Tests: test_media `conformCheck` (two synthetic takes panning over value noise; reference with burnt-in timecode: reframe within half a pixel and a tenth of a degree, a keyed push, a 3-frame slip under a heavy CDL grade, the other take, a missing shot; applied over MCP, everything then matches), test_app `conformCheckDialog`. Not done: playing the reference in step in the Source monitor with a wipe or difference view; finding a take by a picture index over the whole project.
  - Review fixes: another take's own Fit (a take of another size was drawn at the first one's, and its scale keyed wrong); thumbnails drawn at a uniform scale (Stretch); the compositor's flips, crop and animation presets in the placement, and the keys found with them taken out (Fit, flips, width and height scales kept); a slip given at the clip's head speed as edit::slip takes it; Reframed without keys and a slip that does not fit no longer count as fixed; fixes refused on locked tracks and tried on a copy first (a take swap that would run past its media, or out of sync, changes nothing; an audition picks its take); candidates only movies of their own (not nested, multicam or merged sequences, subclips or stills; a still is not slipped either); the coarse search keeps its four best peaks, refined at about 160 pixels across, each followed (fine texture can look most alike a little way off); corners found with the mask flattened (a burnt-in timecode's digits left the picture few); one small scan of the takes shared by all shots; cancel checked in every search; the dialog drops its results when the project changes (an edit, an undo) and adds markers once; MCP keeps "No media" when the reference is neither in the project nor a file. Tests: take B at twice the size, the slipped shot cropped, the push flipped; the dialog's results dropped by an undo.
- [x] 2. Dialogue alignment: fit an ADR take, a pickup or a converted voice to the guide track's timing, keeping its pitch (as VocAlign does), or line up several microphones of one take with the boom by shifting alone (S–M)
  - Done: `audio/DialogueAlign` (`alignSpeech`, `findDialogueShift`, `applyDialogueWarp`, `edit::alignDialogue`), the ADR panel's Align to Guide (the cue's line against the guide clip), MCP `montage_align_dialogue` (`mode` warp or shift, `precision`, `from`/`to`, `remove`). Frames 5 ms apart (10 ms past 20 s): the first six cepstra of 40 mel bands, each over its mean and spread across the speech, and the level against the speech's own, weighted six times (the rhythm a different voice keeps), with deltas; gated to speech by level. DTW in a band of a quarter of the line (half a second to three) plus the handles, steps between 1:2 and 2:1 in speech, either side free to wait in a pause, start and end free within the handles; the path smoothed within each stretch of speech or pause, anchored at the ends, the guide's speech edges and onsets and a grid (200, 80 or 30 ms: Loose, Normal, Tight), slopes clamped. A `dialogue_align` source-audio effect holds the warp (result seconds to the file's, offsets held beyond it) and the take's media id, so on an audition only that take is warped; it is played by WSOLA, or, for a fixed delay, by a windowed sinc to a fraction of a sample. Shifting: FFT cross-correlation at about 8 kHz, then at the full rate over the loudest two seconds, its sign the polarity, a parabola through the peak. Tests: test_media `dialogueAlignment` (jfk.wav re-timed by a known piecewise curve of ±20 %: every onset within 10 ms in the warp and as heard; a second microphone 3.2 ms late and inverted, shifted to within a sample and put the right way up; over MCP), test_app `adrPanelRecordsTakes` (the button). Tried on lines said phrase by phrase by different voices of the speech model at different speeds and pauses: about 4 ms mean error for the same voice, 10 ms across voices. Also fixed: slow speech from the speech model came out as NaN throughout (its half-precision model overflows), now said at another pace and stretched. Not done: Regenerate Words aligning its result by itself.
  - Review fixes: a line over two minutes is refused for stretching (shifting has no limit) and running out of memory fails the alignment instead of the app; an alignment names its take by media, channels and the part of the file it played, so two takes from one file, or the same file's other channels, are told apart; consolidate moves the warp with the trimmed media and a production import remaps (or drops) it; a guide that is itself aligned is heard as it plays; a warp string edited by hand cannot ask for hours of output or crash the render (points beyond an hour's offset are dropped, the output capped a minute past the file, failures play the sound as it was); a lengthened take stops where its file does; removing refuses a locked track and says when there is nothing to remove; cancel reaches the stretch and the speech-synthesis retries; expressions get five seconds of wall clock (one was tight on a busy machine); the face-mesh cache key covers the whole turned crop (2.2 times the box) and skips non-finite boxes; Align to Guide runs in the background with a cancellable progress dialog. Tests: alignment applies by media, channels and span.
- [x] 3. DCP for festivals and distribution: SMPTE subtitles and closed captions from a caption track, encrypted packages with KDMs for named cinema servers (and DKDMs to keep), 4K, and stereoscopic 3D (M)
  - Done: `render/DcpSecurity` (OpenSSL 3, optional: `MONTAGE_WITH_OPENSSL`, required in CI and packages by `MONTAGE_REQUIRE_OPENSSL`): a certificate chain per user made once (root, intermediate, leaf; RSA 2048, SHA-256, the ST 430-2 extensions, roles and dnQualifiers; in the data folder's dcp-certificates, MONTAGE_DCP_CERTIFICATES), enveloped XML signatures over Canonical XML (`render/XmlCanonical`: C14N 1.0 and Exclusive C14N, with and without comments, by element or Id) on the CPL and PKL (`signXml`, `verifyXmlSignature`, which also checks the chain), and ST 430-1 KDMs (`makeKdm`, `readKdm`): the 138-byte key blocks under RSA-OAEP to the recipient, the public and private parts signed by reference. An encrypted package's keys are kept only as a DKDM to Montage's own leaf (data folder's dkdm, MONTAGE_DCP_DKDMS); `makeKdms` (File › Make KDMs…, MCP `montage_make_kdm`) makes one KDM per server certificate (a chain file's leaf) for a window inside the DKDM's and the certificate's. `render/DcpMxf`: ST 429-6 encryption (AES-128-CBC triplets with the plaintext offset, the check value and the HMAC-SHA1 MIC under the FIPS 186-2 derived key, the cryptographic framework and context in the header), ST 429-10 stereoscopic picture track files (left and right per edit unit), and ST 429-5 timed text track files (the ST 428-7 XML and its font as generic stream partitions), all read back by `TrackFileReader`. `core/Captions`: `captionsToDcst` and `parseDcst` (ST 428-7 2007/2010/2014 and Interop DCSubtitle), italic captions throughout (SRT, WebVTT, TTML, ASS, DCST; Captions panel Italic). `exportDcp`: 4K (Flat 3996 x 2160, Scope 4096 x 1716, Full 4096 x 2160; six decompositions, POC and six tile parts), 3D from a stereoscopic sequence (each eye at half the bit budget; "-3D" in the name; FrameRate 48), a caption track as MainSubtitle or closed captions (Language, MainSubtitleLanguageList, "-CCAP"), or burned in (lower-case language in the name), with Liberation Sans (OFL) embedded; encryption with picture, sound and subtitle keys; codestreams now with TLM, Bv2.1's guard bits and MCT. `verifyDcp` adds signatures, key ids against the track files, every codestream against the Bv2.1 rules (`checkDciCodestream`, as libdcp checks), both eyes, and subtitles (own id, font size, language, 3 lines of 32 for closed captions, 79 characters), with Bv2.1's advice (52 characters, 15 frames, 2 frames apart, not in the first 4 seconds) separate. Export DCP… gains Resolution, Stereoscopic 3D, Subtitles and Shown as (on screen, closed captions, burned in) and Encrypt; MCP `montage_export_dcp` `resolution`, `stereo`, `subtitles`, `subtitles_as`, `subtitle_language`, `encrypt`. Checked with ClairMeta (encrypted 3D with French subtitles opened through a KDM, closed captions, 4K: Success) and asdcplib (asdcp-info, asdcp-unwrap decrypting with the key and catching a changed frame by its HMAC). **Not done:** Interop packages, KDMs with trusted device lists from a cinema's FLM, and 4K 3D (servers do not take it).
  - **Tests:** render `dcpSecurity` (chain, signatures checked against OpenSSL and an independent C14N, KDMs opened), `dcpTrackFiles` (stereo, timed text and encrypted track files, through asdcplib when MONTAGE_TEST_ASDCP is set), `exportsEncryptedStereoDcpWithSubtitles` (each eye decrypted with the key from the DKDM, the box shifted between them; the subtitles decrypted and read back with italics and place; a KDM for a test cinema opened with its key and not Montage's; a window past the DKDM's refused; ClairMeta when MONTAGE_TEST_CLAIRMETA is set; a changed CPL failing its signature; closed captions and their advice; burn-in naming), `exportsDigitalCinemaPackage4k`; core `italicAndCinemaCaptions`; app: the dialog's new options, and over MCP an encrypted package with subtitles and a KDM made from its DKDM.
  - Review fixes: the track file reader checks every length from the file against what is left of it (a damaged or hostile MXF, as Check DCP and MCP read any folder, could read past its buffers or ask for exabytes), the index entries' size and count, and an encrypted element's offsets; an encrypted element without its integrity pack is refused. Canonical XML kept characters past U+FFFF whole (they became "??", so a title with an emoji failed its signature at a server). The signing chain is never replaced: with a file missing it is reported (the DKDMs are encrypted to its leaf), made once under a lock, the key file owner-only from creation, each file written whole. The command-line tool now shares the app's data folder, so DKDMs made over its MCP open in the app. KDMs are read only from their signed parts, which the signature must cover exactly, with each key block's window matching the public one; a DKDM must be signed by Montage's own leaf (anyone can encrypt to it); compositions and packing lists must be signed as a whole; signer chains must be issued by certificate authorities. The DKDM, KDMs and signed files are written whole (a full disk no longer leaves a truncated DKDM behind a reported success); a failed random source stops the export instead of encrypting with a zero key; a signing certificate ending within 30 days is refused for encryption (its DKDM would expire with it); KDM file names are sanitised and two servers of one name kept apart; the window is checked against the server certificate's start; MCP refuses an unknown `subtitles_as`; the dialog starts with no subtitles. Tests: damaged and cut-short track files (clean under valgrind), an emoji title signed and checked, the chain left alone when a file is missing, a KDM window planted outside the signed parts ignored, a DKDM signed by another chain refused.
- [x] 4. Recover interrupted recordings: rebuild a camera or recorder file whose recording stopped without writing its index (a flat battery or a pulled card) from a good clip from the same camera; also MXF without its footer, and WAV or BWF files with wrong sizes (S–M)
  - Done: `media/Recover` (`checkRecording`, `repairRecording`, `repairedPath`, `sidecarTimecode`), written here (untrunc is GPL-2.0 only and none of it is used). MP4 and MOV: the reference clip's first picture and sound tracks give their sample descriptions (avcC or hvcC with the NAL length size and picture parameter set ids, ProRes, AAC's AudioSpecificConfig from esds, PCM's bytes per frame from stsz or the sample entry, ipcm included), sample duration, edit list, chunk sizes, the NAL types the camera uses, each AAC frame's first element, and the order of chunks (picture, sound, other tracks, gaps between them). The damaged file's media data is walked by a depth-first search with backtracking (a dead end is remembered by position): a picture first (H.264 and HEVC NAL units by their length prefix, header rules and slice header: first slice, slice type, a known PPS; an access unit ends at the next picture, so a slice cut off by the end of the file leaves it out; ProRes by `icpf` and its sizes), then by what the reference has after the last chunk: an AAC frame (the least length a fresh decoder makes a frame of, by bisection), a PCM chunk (the reference's sizes, then a scan in frames for where the next picture starts, or the rest of the file), another track's chunk or a gap of a size the reference has, skipped. The file ends where only something cut off is left (a picture whose length runs past the end, a partial frame or chunk, zeros); when nothing fits, a second pass skips damaged bytes to the next two pictures. The display order of H.264 and HEVC pictures comes from decoding them (entropy only) and goes into ctts with an edit list; sound keeps the reference's edit (AAC's priming). The new file is the boxes before the media data, the media data as far as it is whole (a 64-bit mdat past 4 GiB) and a new moov: mvhd, tkhd and mdhd with the true durations, stts, ctts, stss, stsc (runs merged), stsz and stco or co64; tref and timecode tracks are dropped. MXF: the KLVs walked (a run-in allowed), content packages counted by their first element's key and kept while whole, the header's Duration and ContainerDuration set from the edit rates of their tracks and descriptors, the header partition closed and complete, every partition's footer offset set, and a footer partition (aligned to the KAG) with VBR index segments (4096 entries each; random access, sequence header, MPEG-2 temporal offsets from temporal_reference, key frame offsets; CBR when the file's own index is) for what the file's own segments do not cover, and the random index pack. WAV, BWF and RF64: the data's size from the file's length (to whole frames) unless it is right and whole chunks follow, the RIFF size from the total; past 4 GiB written as RF64 with ds64 (in the JUNK placeholder when there is one); an RF64 that fits is written as RIFF with its ds64 turned into JUNK; chunks before the data (bext, iXML) kept byte for byte. Start timecode from a Sony `<clip>M01.XML` LtcChangeTable or a StartTimecode element in a P2 or XF clip XML. app: `RepairMediaDialog` (Media bin › Repair Media…: the problem in a sentence, a good clip guessed from the newest that opens beside it, Save As, import with the keyword Recovered, a comment and the sidecar's timecode; in the background with progress and cancel), importing a file that does not open and looks interrupted offers Repair…, and Offload Card names such copies (`OffloadResult::damaged`) with a Repair… button. `app/WorkProgress.h` shares the progress runner. MCP `montage_repair_media` (with `project` import), `montage_offload` lists `damaged`; `montage-cli repair`. A three-minute 720p H.264 and AAC recording cut at 90 % rebuilt in under 4 seconds. Not done: other camera codecs (XAVC's own metadata tracks are skipped as other tracks; DNxHD, AVC-Intra and H.264 in MXF long GOP get no temporal offsets), clip-wrapped MXF, a timecode track in the rebuilt MP4 (the timecode goes on the media item).
  - Tests: media `recoverInterruptedRecordings` (FFmpeg-made H.264 with AAC in MOV, HEVC with PCM in MP4 and ProRes with 24-bit PCM in MOV, B-frames, cut at 70 % with the moov gone: every picture one of the original's and in place but the last three, the sound sample for sample the original's; a reference is required; a Sony sidecar's timecode; a reference from another camera finds nothing; over MCP into a project bin with the keyword and timecode; an MPEG-2 and PCM MXF cut at 70 %: duration in the header, pictures and sound the original's, a seek to 2 s landing by the new index; a WAV and an RF64 with zero and unset sizes: sizes right, bext and iXML read back, samples exact), app `repairMediaDialog` (Offload Card names the interrupted take and offers Repair…, the bin's Repair Media rebuilds and imports it labelled, a file that opens has nothing to repair). Clean under valgrind.
  - Review fixes: the reference's tables are bounds-checked (sample counts against the sizes there are, chunk offsets inside the file, the counts of stco, co64 and stsc, mdhd and a version 1 mvhd by their size; a trak with a 64-bit size; PCM frame sizes kept sane), so a damaged reference fails or rebuilds instead of reading past its tables. The output may not be the damaged file or the reference (canonical paths, by case too on Windows and macOS); an existing output is replaced only with `montage-cli repair --overwrite`, MCP `overwrite` (a relative `output` is beside the clip) or the save dialog's own question. A reference with pictures that finds none fails (its PCM sound alone could match another camera's), and the PCM rest of the file is only taken after other samples. QuickTime's older PCM tables (stsz 1, each sample a frame) are read and written by the frame. Other picture and sound tracks the reference has are named as not rebuilt; the new moov's times are when the recording started (the damaged file's last change, less its length), and the reference's udta is left out. An mdat size is trusted only when a box follows it. MXF: a system item starts its content package (stream offsets were short by it), every package but the last is whole when the next starts and the last when it has as many elements as the first, the file's own index segments count only from the first package on, without a gap, inside what is kept, MPEG-2 flags and key frame offsets follow closed and open GOPs as FFmpeg's muxer writes them, the KAG is capped and a partition pack is patched only when it is whole; whether an MXF has its footer is read from its random index pack or header (no walk through the file). WAV: a ds64 too short for its sizes becomes JUNK and a new one goes in, a whole file with a few stray bytes at its end is not taken as interrupted, and an AVI is not taken for a WAV. Cancel is checked inside the long scans, the AAC trial cache is bounded, decoders keep their messages to themselves without changing FFmpeg's log level for the app, and the reference clip is guessed from file structure, not by opening each with FFmpeg on the interface's thread. Tests: a reference with a 64-bit trak rebuilds the same bytes; 60 references with damaged tables do no harm; the repair refuses to write over the damaged file or the reference (also by another spelling of its path); a ProRes reference with the older PCM tables rebuilds the same pictures and sound; an HEVC and PCM reference fails on a ProRes and PCM recording; the MXF is written as a recorder writes it (nothing gone back to, so its header is open and incomplete) and its new index entries (stream offsets, flags, temporal and key frame offsets) match those in the whole file's footer; the dialog will not write over a taken name.

- [x] 6. Pulldown and frame-rate conversion for delivery: remove 2:3 pulldown (including cadence broken by old edits), add it on export (23.976p to 29.97i), speed a programme up or down for PAL (24 to 25, pitch kept or not), and convert standards with motion compensation (25 ↔ 29.97, 50 ↔ 59.94) (S–M)
  - Done: `media/Pulldown` (`detectPulldown`, `pulldownFrames`, `weaveFields`, `pulldownRate`), `core/Interpretation` `pulldown` (stretches "start:position:order", `parsePulldown`, `pulldownValue`), `media/Decoder` (a pulldown-removed file plays at four fifths of its rate, each frame woven from its two fields' file frames, decoded in order and cached; a field alone has its lines made from the lines either side), Interpret Footage's Pulldown (None, find the cadence, starts on A to D; Removed kept when opened again), MCP `montage_interpret_media` `remove_pulldown`. The cadence is found by a Viterbi over twelve states (five places in the cadence in either field order, and video with none) from each frame's combing against the clean frames round it, its first field woven with the frame before's second, and fields repeated from the frame before (as a share of the motion round it); a break costs a fixed amount and the frame where it falls is judged on itself alone, so a cut made after the pulldown starts a new stretch on the right frame. `render/RateConversion` (`planRateConversion`, `deliveredFrames`, `deliveredPicture`, `deliveredSequence`, `editBetween`, `shotLook`, `looksLikeCut`) and the exporter: the programme rendered and mixed as it is, written at the delivered rate (`ExportSettings::rateChange`, `rate`, `cadence`, `rateKeepPitch`): pulldown into fields (2:3 or 2:2:2:4, the second field woven in after the conversion to the encoder's format, coded interlaced top field first) or as repeated frames at 59.94 or 60; a speed change (`audio/TimeStretch` `SpeedStream`: streaming varispeed through a 512-phase windowed sinc, or streaming WSOLA keeping the pitch), half to twice the speed; a standards conversion that keeps the length, each frame RIFE's or the optical flow's in-between of its two nearest, or the nearest at a cut (an edit with no transition across it, or two pictures far more different than the frames round them, compared by soft-binned histograms and a small luma picture). Captions (embedded, 608 and sidecar ones written with the export), markers and chapters go on the delivered frames; HDR10+ metadata is measured on the delivered frames; broadcast MXF and the DPP take a speed change to 25. FFV1 can now be coded interlaced. Export dialog: Frame rate (the deliveries the sequence's rate has) and Keep pitch; render farm jobs carry the settings; MCP `montage_render` `rate_change`, `frame_rate`, `pulldown_cadence`, `keep_pitch`. Tests: test_media `pulldownAndFrameRates` (FFmpeg's `telecine` on a moving test pattern in two parts, the second cut two frames into its cadence: the break found on its frame and every whole frame given back bit for bit; weaving; a 23.976 export as 29.97i FFV1, through FFmpeg's `fieldmatch,decimate`, equal to the 23.976 export frame for frame; 2:2:2:4 at 59.94; over MCP), `speedChangeAndStandardsConversion` (24 to 25: 4 % shorter, a 1 kHz tone at 1 kHz with its pitch kept and at 1041.7 Hz without; 25 to 29.97 of a red shot cut to blue, in the sequence and inside one file, with no blended frame; a ramp's in-betweens), test_app `frameRateDelivery`. **Not done:** removing other cadences (2:3:3:2, 2:2:2:4, PAL's 2:2 with a field shifted), 25p to 50i and other field-rate conversions, burn-in timecode at the delivered rate (it shows the programme's frames), File › Export Captions at another rate, and loudness measured after a speed change (the programme's is used; a pitch-kept change keeps it). At a break in a removed cadence the frame left with one field is made from it alone.
  - Review fixes: a removed cadence's frames are found by time, each stretch keeping time with the file from where it starts (they were counted four in five from the start, so each break put the picture a frame further behind the sound); the frame map is sized from the video stream's own length; a file with its pulldown removed still counts as 29.97 (or 30) for Interpret Footage, so it can be changed again or put back (it was refused, and the dialog's Pulldown greyed out); Find the Cadence applies what it found (closing the progress dialog had counted as cancelling), items it found none in, or that are not 29.97 or 30 in a mixed selection, keep their pulldown and still take the other settings; "starts on A to D" with the field order as the file says reads the file's field order; HDR10+ scenes are cut where the delivered frames' cuts are; a caption or marker ending at the range's end ends with the delivered programme; a speed change hears nothing after the range's end; the in-between caches are keyed by the export and the picture size; UK DPP takes a standards conversion to 25 as well as a speed change; the export dialog starts with the frame rate as the sequence's every time. Tests: frames after the break at the time they were shown, the last frame held, pulldown kept while the alpha changes and then put back, the map by time, and Find the Cadence in the dialog.
- [x] 7. Steady depth and focus that follows: depth that does not shimmer on video, for Lens Blur (Depth), Depth Fog, Depth Map, Sky Replacement and Relight; focus that follows a tracked point, object or face; a sky matte held steady through a shot (M)
  - Done: `media/VideoDepth` (Video Depth Anything Small, Apache-2.0; Base and Large are CC BY-NC and not used): `scripts/gen-vda-graph.py` transcribes the authors' network (DINOv2 ViT-S/14, the DPT head, the four temporal modules) into three ONNX graphs without weights (`src/media/vda-small-{encoder,temporal,decoder}.onnx`, compiled in), each weight named by its bytes in the authors' checkpoint, which is downloaded on first use (116 MB, `scripts/fetch-models.sh` `video-depth`, `MONTAGE_VIDEO_DEPTH_MODEL`) and unpacked once; the position embedding is resized in the graph as the original resizes it. Matches the authors' PyTorch to about 2e-6. `estimateVideoDepth` runs it as the authors do: 32-frame windows overlapping by ten (the first, every twelfth and the last eight frames of the window before), each fitted to the last by scale and shift on its first two frames and blended into it over eight, stretched by the whole run's 1st and 99th percentiles. `analyzeVideoDepth` keeps a clip's depth on disk (beside the render cache, `$MONTAGE_DEPTH_CACHE`, compressed, invalidated when the file changes); `frameDepth` gives the depth effects (Lens Blur (Depth), Depth Fog, Depth Map, Relight, depth masks, Sky Replacement's depth, VFX pulls) the analysis where it reaches and the still model elsewhere; stills keep V2. `render/FollowFocus` (`followFocus`, `pullFocus`, `analyzeClipDepth`, `clipDepthAnalysed`): Lens Blur (Depth)'s Focus Depth keyed to follow a point (tracked both ways from where it is picked), the effect's object mask or the nearest face: the median steady depth inside the target each frame, held where it is lost, eased by a Gaussian centred on each change (a lag, as first planned, left a subject moving towards the camera soft all through its move), simplified to the keys that keep it within a quarter of a percent. Sky Replacement's Steady Through the Shot (on by default): each frame's coarse sky decided cell by cell by a vote of up to four frames either side, carried along the optical flow and counted where the pictures agree, then refined to the frame's edges and averaged with the neighbours' refined edges where they agree. Inspector: Steady Depth (status, Analyse Depth) on depth effects and Sky Replacement with Use Depth on video clips; Follow Focus: Point, Object, Face under Lens Blur (Depth). MCP `montage_steady_depth`, `montage_follow_focus`. Provenance names Video Depth Anything Small where a clip's depth effects use an analysis. Tests: test_media `steadyVideoDepth` (a dolly past labelled planes: frame-to-frame depth change 0.0028 steady against 0.0129 frame by frame, at least 3× lower, the planes' order kept; the analysis's range, nothing beyond it, invalidated when the file changes), `followFocusOnABall` (a ball coming towards the camera over a textured wall: it keeps all its detail on frames across the shot while the wall keeps 2 to 4 %; focus left where it started goes soft; easing centred and holding; over MCP), `steadySkyMatte` (a slow pan over hills and trees with grain, from the drawn frames so it is the same on every system: the matte changes by 0.62 px a frame beyond the pan held steady, 1.06 px frame by frame, measured by area so a sliver that comes and goes counts; limit 0.75 px and 0.7 of the frame-by-frame figure). **Not done:** depth for reframed, 360° and stereo views and nested sequences (they keep the still model's), analysis at more than 30 frames a second (in-between frames are mixed from the two either side), and GPU inference (about 2 s a frame at 518 on four CPU cores, so analysing is a step of its own).
  - Review fixes: footage read with Interpret Footage settings is analysed (the file on disk was looked for under its interpreted path, so it never was); a second analysis of a file covers what the first did too, so cutting one take into clips and analysing one no longer sends the others back to the still model (one stretch of depth is shared, so Follow Focus keys made before it can move a little); a clip of high-frame-rate footage, analysed every few frames, counts as analysed to its last frame; a frame's depth is the frame shown at that time (24p in a 30p sequence was mixed with the next frame's, so depth edges led moving subjects); rendered frames kept on disk and Sky Replacement's carried looks are keyed by the analysis, so Analyse Depth shows at once, and a sky's neighbours are kept by their clip's own layer (with expressions in the project too) and apart from the frame itself; an analysis written by another process is seen; at most 2 million samples for the percentiles and only the ten carried frames' features are held (memory grew with the clip), with running out of memory reported; depth kept at 12 bits (2.2 times smaller) and the folder kept under 20 GB (`MONTAGE_DEPTH_CACHE_GB`); analyses write their own part file and retry the swap (Windows refuses to replace a file being read); Cancel stops Follow Focus while it tracks a point; views reframed from 360° are not offered the analysis (their depth is the view's); Content Credentials name the video model for Sky Replacement without the still model, and not for reframed views; MCP `size` 140 to 770 and whole, `ease` a number. Tests: test_media `steadyDepthCoverage` (120 fps footage read with an interpretation: analysed every fourth frame, a clip of all of it counts as analysed, a second analysis keeps the first's stretch, a frame's own depth until the next starts, a reframed clip not analysed). The steady sky votes on each cell (one frame's grey sliver at the hills' crest came and went, which the weighted average kept), and its test reads the drawn frames: through each system's FFmpeg the hills' colours differ by a hair, and on Windows the edge moved 1.83 px steady (it had passed on Linux only because the video's chroma blurred the crest).
- [x] 8. Automatic trims from the HDR master: a starting SDR trim (and a lower-peak HDR trim) for each shot from its own light levels, evened out across a scene, for the colourist to adjust; master and target side by side (S–M)
  - Done: `render/AutoTrims`. `measureShotLight` renders up to nine frames of each shot (the top picture clip, as trims take it) from the master at most 480 pixels wide, in PQ (an HLG master converted), and keeps a 1/32-stop histogram of each pixel's luminance and brightest channel in nits: the log-average (the shot's mid-grey; black counted as 0.005 nits), the 1st percentile of luminance, the 99.9th percentile of the brightest channel and the peak. `solveAutoTrims` gives each shot, for each target: a new trim control, `peak` (Shot Peak, nits), that the target's roll-off starts from instead of the master's peak (the 99.9th percentile, so a few glints do not compress a whole shot); into SDR the shot's light is rolled off with BT.2390's EETF in PQ to 280 nits (where Montage's HDR-to-SDR mapping reaches 98 % of white), in the target's primaries so colours its smaller gamut makes brighter are rolled off too, then tone mapped as before; a lower-peak PQ or HLG target rolls off from the shot's peak to its own. Saturation up to 10 % more, three stops over the target's headroom, makes up for the roll-off; SDR shadows under 2.5 % code get a small lift; gamma, solved last on a neutral grey through the same pass, puts the shot's mid-grey back where the mapping with no trim puts it (BT.2408's 203-nit white at SDR white). Shots of one colour group are aimed at the group's median mid-grey (within an eighth of a stop; a shot more than two stops off it is lit so and left). `edit::fillAutoTrims` fills only shots with no trim of their own for a target (none, or neutral with no keyframes). Trims panel: Analyse Trims (every target, In to Out or the whole sequence, behind a progress dialog, one undo step) and Master Beside (the Program monitor split: the master on the left of the divider, the target on the right, following the playhead while paused; off with the master shown, a gallery wipe or Compare with Reference). MCP `montage_trim` action `analyse` (`target`, `from`, `to`, `measure_only`; each shot's light and trims, which ones kept their own). Saturation above 1 in a trim now eases into what is left under white once the brightest channel would pass 90 % code, instead of clipping it (which turned its hue); below that it is as before.
  - Review fixes: a shot peak under the target's held the colour channels at the peak's code without touching alpha (it had clamped alpha too, so the frame came out partly transparent and too bright); letterbox bars and uncovered black are left out of a shot's light (they dragged its mid-grey down and earned it a lift); a trim switched off still counts as the shot's own; Master Beside renders once a scrub or edit settles, and follows a sequence switch; MCP analyse refuses `to` before `from` and says which trims were filled and which shots are on locked tracks.
  - Also: 3D LUTs are interpolated tetrahedrally (CPU and both GPU shaders). Trilinear mixed in the cube's corners off the grey axis, which the HDR-to-SDR mapping pulls down: every colour conversion through `conversionLut` dulled highlights (203-nit white came out at 94.5 % instead of 96 %, and SDR never reached white: 98.4 % at most, darker again at 3000 nits); a grey is now interpolated from greys only, matching the exact conversion to 0.1 %.
  - Tests: render `autoTrimsFromTheMaster` (synthetic 4000-nit PQ shots built from pixel pairs lit k times over and under a mid-grey, so it is their log-average: night, daylight with a blue sky, a sea with glints at 3000 nits, a sunset with an orange sky and the sun; each shot's log-average measured within 2 %, the glints' 99.9th percentile; the sea clipping over 2 % of pixels with no trim; after Analyse Trims each of the four reaches SDR with its mid-grey within 2 % of where the mapping puts it and under 0.5 % of pixels clipped; a shot with its own trim keeps it, a second fill fails; two shots a stop apart in one colour group end 0.1 to 0.3 stops apart; at 600 nits the sea stays under the peak with its mid-grey within 2 %, and a shot whose peak is under the target's stays opaque with its mid-grey where it is aimed; a switched-off trim counts as the shot's own; an SDR master refused), media `trimPassesOverMcp` (analyse, measure_only, an unknown target, one shot filled and one kept), app `trimPassesPanel` (Analyse Trims filling the brighter shot in one undo step and keeping the other's own trim, nothing left to fill the second time; Master Beside following the playhead and going off with the master); the GPU compositor still matches the CPU with the tetrahedral LUTs.
- [x] 9. Lens calibration: make a lens profile from a checkerboard clip, or from lines that should be straight, anamorphics included; ST maps for VFX (S–M)
  - Done: `media/LensCalibration`. `findCheckerboard` finds a board's inner corners as saddle points (minus the Hessian's determinant of the picture smoothed by its size, local maxima), keeps those with four light and dark arcs round them (an outer corner, an edge or a blob has two or none), refines each with cornerSubPix's equations (every nearby gradient perpendicular to the way to the corner, in a Gaussian window a third of the way to its nearest neighbour) and grows the grid square by square from the one nearest the middle (each neighbour looked for where the local steps say, a corner claimed twice failing the board); the whole cols x rows board is required, put in board order (rows along i, turned a quarter when seen so, mirrored to keep the board's handedness). `calibrateFromBoards`: Zhang's closed form for a planar target (homographies by normalised DLT in halves of the frame's shorter side, zero skew, the centre at the middle; three unknowns when a squeeze is fitted, two otherwise), each view's pose from its homography, then Levenberg–Marquardt (numeric central-difference Jacobians, normal equations built block by block, Cholesky from the new `core/LinearSolve`, moved out of CameraSolve) first on the focal length and poses alone, then on everything: focal length, the lens's centre (lensfun's `<center>`), the anamorphic squeeze, the poly3, poly5 or ptlens terms and every pose. The model is Lens Correction's own (`Correction` with no fit): a pinhole picture whose radius, widened by the squeeze and measured from the centre in calibration units, is distorted by lensfun's polynomial. `calibrateFromLines` (plumb lines): each line's points taken back through the distortion, the straight line through them by total least squares, residuals in pixels at the line's own recorded scale (so shrinking the picture cannot straighten it), a centre further than half the frame or a squeeze past 0.25–4 refused, the terms fitted first (with no distortion the centre and squeeze change nothing), then points over 3 x the rms (and half a pixel) dropped and the fit made again. `snapLineToEdge`: a drawn line sampled every 8 px, each point moved along its normal to the strongest change within 6 px, placed where the picture is half way between the edge's two sides (or between a thin line's two edges), points with much less contrast than the line's usual left out. `calibratedLens` makes the lensfun lens (the frame as the calibration sensor, so Lens Correction uses its units exactly; the real focal length from the fit; Montage's own `<anamorphic squeeze>`), `calibrateClip` reads up to 40 frames where the board has moved. `core/LensProfile`: `Lens` gained `centerX`/`centerY` (read and written as lensfun's `<center>`) and `squeeze`; `Correction` uses both (the centre in pixels, the squeeze widening x before the radius) and gained `corrected` (the inverse of `source`: Newton, then bisection) and its centre, unit and squeeze; `Lens::own` marks the user's lenses and `fixedLens` prefers one. `media/LensDatabase`: `userLensFolder` (MONTAGE_USER_LENSES, else none beside MONTAGE_LENS_DB, else Montage's data folder's lenses), `saveUserLens`, the database merging them in (or holding them alone), `lensfunDatabaseFound` (the effect still offers to download lensfun's when only the user's own are there). ST maps: `makeStMaps`/`writeStMaps` (Nuke's convention: red x over the width, green 1 minus y over the height, at pixel centres; 32-bit OpenEXR R, G, B with the overscan in the header): undistort at the overscan size (the corrected picture of the recorded frame's edge, equal on both sides, even), redistort at the recording's size. VFX pulls: `VfxPullOptions::stMaps` writes `<shot>_undistort.exr` and `<shot>_redistort.exr` for a shot whose clip has a Lens Correction (as at the cut's first frame, at the plate's size), and the pull list gains Overscan, Undistort Map and Redistort Map. App: Clip › Calibrate Lens… (app/LensCalibrationDialog: Checkerboard in the clip with its inner corners, or Lines that should be straight drawn on the clip's frame at the playhead as recorded (click, double-click to end, snapped points shown); distortion model, anamorphic squeeze, centre, maker, name, focal length; the fit's corners or straightness, focal length, centre and squeeze shown; Save and Apply saves the lens and puts a Lens Correction first on the clip or every clip of its media, one undo step). MCP `montage_calibrate_lens` (`board` or `lines` with `at` and `snap`, `from`/`to`, `model`, `anamorphic`, `squeeze`, `fit_center`, `maker`, `name`, `focal`, `same_media`, `fit_only`) and `montage_vfx_pull`'s `st_maps` (each shot's maps and overscan).
  - Also: `montage_vfx_pull` never reported a shot's layers (the shot was changed through a copy); it now does.
  - Tests: media `calibrateLensFromBoards` (a 10 x 7 board rendered through a poly5 lens with a centre offset (0.03, -0.02) and a 1.33 squeeze at eight poses, 36 jittered samples a pixel traced back through Lens Correction's own inverse: every corner within 0.15 px; reprojection rms 0.03 px; both terms within 1 % (0.14 % and 0.6 %), focal length within 1 %, squeeze within 0.2 %, centre within 0.002; two views refused; a board of the wrong size not found; as a lensfun lens and back, its correction within 0.05 px of the lens's where the boards were), `straightenLensLines` (a wall of bars through the lens, 17 edges drawn 1.5 px off and snapped: 95 % of points within 0.2 px of the edge; the fit's rms under 0.2 px; the edges themselves bent by 0.007 px through the fitted lens), `lensStMaps` (the undistort map against Lens Correction with no fit on a grid within 1e-3, undistorted then redistorted within 0.01 px, the recorded frame's corners inside the overscan, the EXRs, a pull over MCP with maps for the corrected shot only and the pull list's columns), `calibrateLensOverMcp` (the board clip encoded losslessly: eight views, rms under 0.15 px, the squeeze; saved to the user's folder and offered with no lensfun database; on the clip; lines on a still of the wall with the squeeze given: centre found within 0.003; refusals); core `lensCentreSqueezeAndOwnLenses` (the XML round trip, the inverse within 1e-6 px, the squeeze making the distortion radial once widened, the user's own lens preferred for a fixed-lens camera); app `calibrateLensInTheApp` (no board in a still, then four lines drawn on a poly3 grid: the term within 5 %, saved, applied as one undo step and offered by the database).

  - Done: `render/Iamf` (`exportIamf`, `readIamfInfo`, `iamfSubstreams`, `iamfDownmixToStereo`): IAMF 1.1, simple profile, written by Montage itself (no IAMF library linked). A 5.1, 7.1, 5.1.2, 5.1.4, 7.1.2 or 7.1.4 sequence is one channel-based audio element of one layer in its own loudspeaker layout, its audio objects rendered into the bed as the mix pans them, the channels in IAMF's substreams (the pairs, then C and the LFE); IAMF's 7.1.2 puts its overhead pair in front (U±045), where Montage's 7.1.2 has it at the sides, so that pair is carried as IAMF's; stereo is one pair; an ambisonic sequence (first to third order) a scene-based element in mono mode, a substream per ACN component (16 at third order). FLAC (libavcodec's encoder, 24-bit, a STREAMINFO in the codec config) or 24-bit little-endian PCM, 48 kHz, 960-sample frames (20 ms; libiamf's FLAC reader takes at most 8 KB a frame). One mix presentation (the title, the element, 0 dB mix gains) rendered binaurally on headphones or as stereo, with its loudness (BS.1770 integrated, sample peak and true peak, in Q7.8) for the element's layout and for stereo, measured as the reference renderer plays them: a bed folded by IAMF's downmix matrices, a field by libiamf's ambisonic decoders. The last frame's padding is trimmed (its OBUs' trimming field, and the MP4's edit list). Written as an MP4 (.mp4, .m4a: an `iamf` sample entry with its `iacb` descriptors, one chunk, 32-bit offsets with room for a 64-bit mdat past 4 GB) or the bare OBU stream (.iamf). File › Export Immersive Audio (IAMF)… (codec, binaural, In to Out; in the background with a progress dialog), MCP `montage_export_iamf`. `LoudnessMeter` now meters up to 24 channels (it took 8, and its stride was wrong past that). CI builds libiamf's reference decoder (`scripts/build-iamfdec.sh`, used only by the test).

- [x] 11. HDR stills in and out: Ultra HDR and ISO 21496-1 gain-map JPEG, AVIF and HEIF, and PNG with cICP. They import as HDR into HDR sequences, and HDR frames and thumbnails export in forms that still look right on SDR screens (S–M)
  - `render/HdrStill`. `writeHdrPng`: a 16-bit PNG (Qt's encoder) with PNG Third Edition's cICP (BT.2020 or P3, PQ or HLG, full range), mDCV (Montage's HDR10 mastering display: P3 D65, the master's peak, 0.0001 at black) and cLLI after IHDR, Qt's gAMA, sRGB, iCCP and cHRM left out. `writeAvifStill`: Y'CbCr made by Montage (BT.2020 non-constant luminance or BT.709, full range, 4:2:0, 10-bit for HDR and 8-bit otherwise), AV1 through FFmpeg (SVT-AV1, else libaom or rav1e; quality to CRF) in FFmpeg's AVIF muxer, colour tagged; FFmpeg writes neither clli nor mdcv in AVIF, so Montage adds both as properties of the primary item, moving the image data's locations (iloc) with the boxes. `readStillHdrInfo` reads PNG's cICP, cLLI (and the draft's cLLi) and mDCV, and AVIF's colr, clli and mdcv. Import: the probe takes light levels from stream side data, PNG's cLLI or AVIF's clli (`MediaItem::maxCll`/`maxFall`, saved with the project, `montage_probe_media`'s `color_space`, `max_cll`, `max_fall`); AVIF (the MP4 demuxer with an avif brand and no image-sequence brand) is a still. Export Frame (`exportStill` with `StillOptions`/`StillResult`, `stillRendition`): an HDR sequence's frame for SDR screens by default (its first SDR trim target, else tone mapped to Rec.709 as the viewer does), `master` (the HDR master as a 16-bit cICP PNG or 10-bit AVIF, light levels the sequence's measured ones, else the frame's own, as HDR10 states them), or any trim target; JPEG and TIFF refuse HDR; JPEG is written at quality 90 (Qt's default was 75). File › Export Frame offers PNG and AVIF for SDR screens or HDR for HDR sequences; MCP `montage_render_frame` `rendition` and `quality` (its returned image is the SDR rendition), `montage-cli frame --rendition`. Media thumbnails (bin and timeline) of HDR media are tone mapped to SDR.
  - Gain maps (this product includes Gain Map technology under license by Adobe; the IP check allowed it on condition that both Adobe's XMP and ISO 21496-1's metadata are written, and the notice shown in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES, the README and Help › Licences): `media/GainMap`, Montage's own code. `writeGainMapJpeg`: the SDR rendition encoded first (Qt's JPEG) and decoded again, so the map is made against what decoders see; per channel, log2 of (HDR + 1/64) over (SDR + 1/64) in linear light relative to SDR white (203 nits), the base linearised with sRGB's curve as decoders do and the master (PQ, or HLG through its OOTF) in the base's primaries; one minimum, maximum and gamma for all channels, capacity 0 to the maximum; an RGB map at full resolution through FFmpeg's MJPEG encoder at 4:4:4 (4:2:0 blurred the channels into each other, 2 % off in colour), with what the decoder reads back fed into the codes twice (bit-exact, no encoder comment). The file: the base with its XMP (hdrgm:Version and the container directory naming the map and its length), ISO 21496-1's version block and MPF's index; the map with Adobe's hdrgm properties in XMP and ISO 21496-1's metadata (each fraction with its own denominator: libavif reads the common-denominator flag as reserved). The SDR picture is in Rec.709 or Display P3 (an SDR trim to other primaries is converted to Rec.709). `readGainMapJpeg` reads only the metadata segments (seeking past the rest), finds the map by MPF (else the XMP's length) and reads ISO 21496-1 (both fraction forms, one or three channels), else the XMP (whatever prefix the namespace is bound to, either quotes; attributes, elements or a sequence per channel). A camera JPEG with an MPF preview and XMP of its own has no gain map. `applyGainMap` (bilinear map, gamma, the display's headroom weight) and `decodeGainMapJpeg` (to 16-bit PQ BT.2020 in one pass from the base's primaries: Display P3 when its ICC says so, other ICC profiles converted into Display P3 first; the last two decodes kept by path, size and time). Import: such a JPEG gets `MediaItem::gainMap` (`montage_probe_media`'s `gain_map`) and, on its path, the interpretation `gainmap=1`, which alone makes the decoder read its HDR rendition (PQ, full boost); a map that cannot be decoded fails to open rather than show SDR codes as PQ. Projects saved earlier keep the SDR picture. Interpret Colour as an SDR space reads the SDR picture; in an SDR sequence the clip shows the file's own SDR picture (`pictureColorSpace` says Rec.709). Export Frame's "HDR JPEG with gain map" (and `.jpg` with `master` or an HDR trim target over MCP and the CLI): the base is the SDR rendition (the SDR trim, else the viewer's tone mapping), the map is to the master. CI builds libultrahdr's `ultrahdr_app` (`scripts/build-ultrahdr.sh`, test only).
  - Gain-map AVIF (`writeGainMapAvif`; Export Frame's "HDR AVIF with gain map", `StillOptions::gainMap`, MCP `montage_render_frame` `gain_map`, `montage-cli frame --gain-map`): the HEIF structure libavif writes, built by Montage from two AVIFs FFmpeg encodes. The base (item 1, primary; 8-bit 4:2:0, tagged with sRGB's curve, how gain map readers linearise it) is encoded first and decoded again (`decodeAvifPicture`, dav1d through FFmpeg and swscale by its colr), so the map is made against it; the map (item 3, hidden) is 8-bit R, G and B with the identity matrix (libaom), else Y'CbCr 4:4:4 (rav1e), else 4:2:0 (SVT-AV1), fed back as the JPEG's is; the 'tmap' item (2) holds version 0 and ISO 21496-1's metadata, is derived ('dimg') from the base and the map, has its own ispe, the HDR rendition's colr (primaries and transfer; matrix BT.601, which libavif's tone mapping re-encodes with) and clli (the measured light levels), and is preferred over the base in an 'altr' group; the 'tmap' brand. Readers without gain maps show the base. Import: `readGainMapAvif` reads the meta box alone (top-level boxes skipped by size; iinf, iloc v0-2 with idat, iref, ipco/ipma), `decodeGainMapAvif` decodes both items (av1C as the decoder's extradata) and applies the map in the base's primaries (its nclx, else its ICC profile's: BT.709, Display P3 or BT.2020), or the tone-mapped item's when the metadata says so, with the base's curve (sRGB's, BT.709's as libavif does, gamma 2.2 or 2.8, or linear); a gain map in other colour, or to SDR from an HDR base, is not read (the file imports as its SDR picture). The parser bounds what a crafted file can ask for (sizes of 0, 4 or 8 bytes, stated lengths, a few thousand items, each item's properties listed once, overflow-safe extents); of equal-sized pictures FFmpeg finds, the default (primary) one is shown; probing, `gainmap=1` and the SDR picture in SDR sequences as for JPEG. CI builds libavif's `avifgainmaputil` (`scripts/build-avifgainmaputil.sh`, test only).
  - HEIF (`writeHeifStill`, `writeGainMapHeif`; Export Frame's "HEIF for SDR screens", "HDR HEIF, 10-bit" and "HDR HEIF with gain map", `.heic`/`.heif`/`.hif` over MCP and the CLI): HEVC through FFmpeg's libx265 into an MP4 in a temporary folder, whose picture and hvcC are taken (`readMp4Hevc`) into a HEIF Montage builds (`heifFile`, the layout the gain-map AVIF uses: hvc1 items with hvcC, ispe, pixi and colr nclx; the master's mdcv and clli; brands heic, or heix for 10 bits). 8-bit 4:2:0 for SDR, 10-bit 4:2:0 for PQ and HLG; the gain map's base 8-bit 4:2:0 (sRGB's curve) and its map 8-bit 4:4:4, fed back as AVIF's is. Pictures larger than 8192 go in a grid of 4096 tiles (the edge tiles filled out). Import (`readHeifStill`, `decodeHeifStill`): HEIC, HEIF and AVIF in tiles are read by Montage, not FFmpeg (FFmpeg 6 reads neither): the primary item, AV1 or HEVC, or a grid of either put together and cropped to its output size, then turned (irot) and mirrored (imir) as listed; its size as shown, its colour (nclx, else what its ICC profile's primaries and curve are), light levels (clli) and gain map (`readGainMapHeif`, `decodeGainMapHeif`, the AVIF code generalised); decoded to 16-bit code values (kept per version of the file). **Tests:** the HDR master as HEIF within 2 % in nits, PQ with its colr, clli and mdcv, and libheif's decoder (`MONTAGE_TEST_HEIFDEC`, `heif-dec` or `heif-convert`, test only) reading the same codes within 1 %; a grid of 96-pixel tiles reading back as the picture written; the gain-map HEIF's base within 3 codes of the SDR trim, its HDR rendition within 2 %, its SDR picture in Rec.709 sequences, libheif showing the base; an 8-bit SDR HEIF within 3 codes.
  - Tests: render `hdrStillsInAndOut` (a 1000-nit PQ master of eight flat patches from 10 nits to the peak and in colour: as a 16-bit PNG its cICP, mDCV and cLLI, read back as a PQ image with its MaxCLL and every patch within 0.5 % in nits; the sequence's measured levels stated when there are some; as a 10-bit AVIF within 2 % in nits with its colr, clli and mdcv, which libavif's avifdec also reads (checked locally); for SDR screens equal to the SDR trim's render, and as an 8-bit AVIF within 3 code values of it; without an SDR target, the viewer's tone mapping; HLG in both; TIFF and an unknown target refused; as a gain-map JPEG with both metadata kinds, its base within 3 code values of the SDR trim's render, imported as PQ and at full boost every patch within 2 % in nits in the base's gamut, and the same from libultrahdr's reference decoder within 2 % (1.3 % at worst locally); in a Rec.709 sequence the file's SDR picture within 2 code values; a project item saved before gain maps (no `gainmap` on its path) showing the SDR picture; Adobe's XMP alone under another prefix in single quotes reading the same metadata; a map that cannot be decoded refused, and its picture not opening; a camera JPEG with an MPF preview and XMP of its own probing as Rec.709 without a gain map, and a truncated one refused), as a gain-map AVIF: its metadata read back, its base (as readers without gain maps show it) within 3 code values of the SDR trim's render, imported as PQ within 2 % in nits, libavif's `avifgainmaputil tonemap` within 2 %, and its SDR picture in a Rec.709 sequence; media `trimPassesOverMcp` (a master frame as a PNG probing as PQ with its light levels, an SDR one, and a gain-map JPEG probing as PQ).
