- [x] 12. Film scans: negative inversion from the film base, printer lights in points, restoration of faded dyes, steadying on the perforations, and the dirt and scratches Restoration still misses (M)
  - Done: `render/FilmScan` and the new Effects › Film group, whose effects run on the source frame where it is read (`clipSource`), before anything else in the stack whatever their place in it: Steady on Perforations first, then (after Dirt Removal, noise reduction, Motion Blur and Deflicker) Film Negative, then Faded Colour Restore, so a lock listed after the negative still finds its holes and the fade is measured on the print. **Film Negative** (`invertNegative`): the scan read as the light through the film (by its own curve, as linear light, or as 10-bit log printing density, 0.002 density a code value), each channel's density above the film base (up to the 2.046 a scan holds, so an opaque speck stays a bright white) turned into light as printing density's standard conversion does (reference white at 685, black at 95, so 1.18 density between them, over the negative's gamma, 0.6 by default) with a print exposure in stops. The base (the orange mask on colour negative) is found in each frame (`filmBase`: the clearest film there, each channel's median over the clearest 2 % of the frame, so the scan's noise does not lift it; leaving out the perforations, as clear as the scan gets in every channel, and 4 pixels round them, where their edges and a video file's softer colour mix them with the base, unless no perforations are found and what is left is neutral, a clear-base black-and-white negative with no holes in the frame) or set: the Inspector's Take the Base From This Frame sets it from the frame under the playhead for every frame (`takeFilmBase`, undo "Take Film Base"). **Printer lights** on Color Correct (Printer Light R, G, B in points, −100 to 100, and Printer Point, 0.025 log exposure by default): each channel's light multiplied by 10^(points × point), before the rest of Color Correct (such a Color Correct runs on the CPU; the shader does not decode the light). In the Colour workspace the numeric keypad sets them as on a timer's console (elsewhere its digits still cut to a multicam angle and − zooms out): 7, 8, 9 add a point of red, green or blue, 4, 5, 6 take one away, + and − move all three; a run of them is one undo step ("Printer Lights") on the graded clip's Color Correct (made when it has none, through the Tangent panel's code), and the status bar shows all three. **Faded Colour Restore** (`fadeLevels`, `restoreFade`): each channel's darkest (0.5 %), middle and lightest (99.5 %) levels, averaged over the frames either side within the clip (12 by default, each frame's measured at 480 pixels wide through the negative, if any, and cached), and each channel's levels and gamma matched to those of the channel that kept the widest range, so black, white and the middle come back neutral, by Strength. **Steady on Perforations** (`findPerforations`, `perforationOffset`): in the rebate (left or right, 12 % of the width by default), what is brighter than halfway from the rebate's level to the brightest, in 4-connected pieces; whole pieces of the typical size that fill most of their box are holes, each centred to a fraction of a pixel (every pixel round it weighed by where it is between the rebate's level round it, its box's border, and the hole's, unclamped, so edges count as much as they cover). Each frame is moved by the mean offset of its holes from the nearest of the reference frame's (within half their spacing). The reference (the holes as fractions of the frame, so proxies keep it) is set by the Inspector's Set Reference From This Frame (`takePerforationReference`) and, over MCP, when `montage_add_effect` adds the effect (from the clip's first frame). Show Detected marks the holes. **Restoration**: Scratch Removal's Follow Slanting Scratches (on in new effects; older projects keep their look) also follows lines that lean or wander up to a pixel a row (seeded every 8 rows at the row's peaks over three times its 90th percentile of line strength, followed down while they last, kept when long, steady, thin and within about 22 degrees of upright, so a wire is left alone), and Dirt Removal also removes specks that last two frames: after the one-frame dirt is filled, what differs from the frames two away while those agree is found, and each speck is kept only when most of its core matches one neighbour and not the other (a one-frame speck matches neither; something on three frames matches both and is left); it is filled from the frames two away. Also: the camera looks run on the source picture (CDLs and LUTs in a scene-referred space) are no longer stopped by a Motion Blur, Deflicker or Faded Colour Restore before them, which run on the source too; MCP `montage_add_effect` refuses effects that work on the picture as it comes for a colour group's grade, where they did nothing.
  - **Tests:** render `filmScans` (a picture as a negative over an orange mask, inverted with the base found, with the base set (another base gives another picture), and as printing density: every patch within ΔE 2; opaque film stays finite and under 30 in linear light; a clear-base black-and-white negative's base found with no holes in the frame; printer lights exact in points and the GPU path refused for them; a picture with its green and blue faded (raised, flattened and bent) restored within 3 % per channel; ±3 px weave found and held to 0.1 px; slanting and wandering scratches, dark and light, removed with the rest of the picture unchanged, and a bar, an edge and a wire at 31 degrees left alone; specks on two frames of a still shot removed and nothing else changed, one on one frame or three not taken for them); media `filmScansOnFootage` (through the renderer from H.264 footage: lasting dirt on frames 4 and 5 removed and nothing else changed, weave held to 0.15 px with the reference taken as the Inspector takes it, a weaving colour negative with Film Negative listed before Steady on Perforations held to 0.25 px on its picture's dots and back neutral within 0.05 (a step of an 8-bit negative's dense blue is several of the print's), its base taken within 0.03, a faded shot restored within 4 %); app `filmScanControls` (the Inspector's two buttons set the reference, six perforations, and the base, each one undo step, the status lines follow; keypad 7 in the Editing workspace leaves the grade alone; in Colour, 7, 7, +, 5 give R +3, G 0, B +1 on a Color Correct made for them as one undo step, shown in the status bar).
- [x] 13. Words from the footage, on this computer: a description of each shot for logging and search, draft audio descriptions that fit the gaps, and chapters, summaries, show notes and story notes from transcripts (M)
  - Done: two models that run on this computer, downloaded once as model packs (`montage models`). **Pictures into words** (`media/DescribePicture`): Florence-2 large (MIT) through ONNX Runtime, the 4-bit vision encoder, encoder and merged decoder (790 MB), greedy decoding from `</s><s>` with `<s>` and `<pad>` never chosen (the exports otherwise repeat `<s>` for ever) and no 3-gram said twice; Brief ("What does the image describe?") or Detailed; the picture fitted into 768 × 768. Its tokenizer is CLAP's (the same RoBERTa vocabulary), which gained decoding. **Writing** (`media/LanguageModel`): llama.cpp (MIT, b11430, built on whisper.cpp's ggml) running Granite 4.0 Micro (IBM, Apache-2.0, 4-bit GGUF, 2.1 GB), the model's own chat template, greedy decoding held to a GBNF grammar so every answer parses, cancellable, with progress, refusing a question longer than its context (16k tokens). `render/FootageWords`: **Describe Shots** (Media Bin context menu, MCP `montage_describe_shots`): a video's shots from its scene cuts (a still is one shot), each described from its middle frame; kept with the media item (`MediaItem::shots`, saved with the project, trimmed by Consolidate, dropped by subclips and Replace), searched by the bin's search (a subclip by its base's shots in its range) and by Find in Speech by meaning, and filling an empty Description with the longest shot's (over MCP, an item that cannot be described is reported and the rest kept). **Chapters** (Suggest Chapters › Ask the Language Model, MCP `montage_suggest_chapters` `language_model`): a small model asked where chapters start answers a chapter a sentence, so it is asked two easier things: the broad subjects the talk moves between (a list, at most 8), then each numbered sentence's subject (the grammar spells out every line number and allows only the listed subjects; several sentences share a line past 150). Runs of one subject are chapters, the shortest joined into a neighbour (the next when it is on the same subject or the run is the first, else the one before) until each lasts the shortest chapter and there are at most the chapters asked for; each starts just before its first sentence and is titled with its subject. **Summary and Notes** (Sequence menu, MCP `montage_write_notes`): a summary (at most five sentences), show notes (two or three sentences, 3–8 topics, and the chapter markers with their times when there are some) and story notes (a logline, 3–12 beats, 1–5 open questions), each held to its form by its grammar, with Copy and Save. Questions too long for the model are asked again with each sentence shortened (to 30, 20, 14, 10, 7, 5 words). **Audio description drafts** (Audio Description › Draft From the Pictures, MCP `montage_audio_description` `draft`): for each empty gap, the program frame in its middle described in brief, and, with Write with the Language Model on, in detail too and written as a describer would by the model: at most the words the gap holds at 160 a minute, only the first word capitalised so people are described by how they look, never by a name the picture model guessed (a line opening with a name from the detailed description is replaced by the brief one); every line is then cut to fit and not left hanging on "in" or "the". One undo step each ("Describe Shots", "Draft Descriptions").
  - **Tests:** media `languageModelKeepsToItsGrammar` (the 260K-parameter story model llama.cpp tests with, committed: answers held to a grammar, the same twice, progress, a question too long refused, cancel, a bad grammar refused), `describesPictures` (the JFK portrait, brief, says "man" and "suit", detailed says "tie"; resized or cancelled), `speechSearchByMeaning` (shots found by meaning); core subclips (no shots on a subclip, search finds its base's shots in range, shots survive a save); render `footageWordsReadAnswers` (numbering, subject and label grammars and readers, chapters from labels: strays taken back, short first runs forward, the cap; word budgets, fitting lines; Description filled once), `footageWordsWithATinyModel` (the plumbing on the story model: subject lists and every line labelled parse, chapters start at 0 and are spaced, notes in their forms), `footageWordsWithTheRealModels` (a synthetic talk on cooking then football: chapters Cooking at 0 and Football within 4.6 s of the turn, the same again; a summary on both subjects in one paragraph of at most five sentences; show notes with topics and the chapter times; story notes in their form), `draftingAudioDescription` (the portrait held over gaps of 2.5 and 1.2 s: every draft, with and without the model, fits its gap at a describer's pace, is a sentence, and the longer one says who is there); app `wordsFromTheFootage` (the controls; Describe Shots on a clip and Show Notes written through the dialogs with the real models). Real-model tests skip unless `MONTAGE_DESCRIBE_MODEL` / `MONTAGE_LANGUAGE_MODEL` are set; CI runs the plumbing.

- [x] 14. Hand off to Media Composer with media: the cut's footage transcoded to Avid MXF OP-Atom (DNxHD or DNxHR, PCM) straight into an Avid MediaFiles folder, with an AAF whose clips point at it, so the sequence opens linked without AMA or importing (S)
  - Done: `render/AvidMedia` and an Avid media mode of the AAF export (`AafExportOptions::avid`, `AafAvidMedia`). The media goes into the next free numbered folder of "Avid MediaFiles/MXF" on the chosen drive (default the AAF's folder; the drive must exist; the drive, its "Avid MediaFiles" or its MXF folder may be given; the folder is claimed by making it, so two exports never share one, and a failed or cancelled export removes it and any folders made for it), as FFmpeg's OP-Atom muxer writes it: the picture as VC-3 at the sequence's size and rate (DNxHR LB, SQ, HQ, HQX 10-bit or 444; DNxHD 36/115/175-class at 1920 x 1080 and broadcast rates only, refused otherwise before anything is written), a file a track; the sound as mono 24-bit PCM, a file per channel of each source, whole; each with its source's name, tape name and non-drop timecode. The files' package IDs are read back (FFmpeg makes them up) and each file's packages become AAF mobs slot for slot as the file has them (timecode track 1, essence 2): its material package the master clip, its file package the file mob (CDCI with the VC-3 coding and the VC-3 frame-wrapped container, or PCM with the Broadcast Wave frame-wrapped container, and the file's URL), its tape package the tape mob, so Media Composer's index of the folder and the AAF agree. MXF UMIDs become mob IDs as pyaaf2 reads them (`aafMobIdFromUmid`, `aafMobIdFromUmidBytes`: the material number's UUID fields little-endian, and Media Composer's half-swapped form). **Effects left off** (default): each picture source's used stretches (what the clips and dissolves reach, and the handles, 30 frames by default, as far as the source goes) are merged where they meet and rendered from the source as plain clips (an EXR's layer kept), the composition's clips pointing into them (another speed as Motion Control); image sequences and Photoshop layers come too. **Baked**: each clip rendered through its effects, grade and transform to a file of its own with its handles (none for ramped or reversed clips, rendered as they play, nor at an end the clip animates in or out at), titles, generated clips and nested sequences included; clips above V1 are rendered over black (the media has no transparency), which the export warns of. File › Export AAF with Media (Media Composer)… (picture coding, handles, bake, media drive), MCP `montage_export_timeline` `avid_media` {`drive`, `codec`, `handles`, `bake`}, `montage-cli aaf --avid-media [--drive] [--codec] [--handles] [--bake]`. `ExportSettings` gained `format` (the muxer by name) and `formatMetadata`. AAF import finds a file that is not where its locator says as Media Composer does, by its file mob's ID: first at the same place under each "Avid MediaFiles" beside or above the AAF or at the top of a local drive, then among all the MXF files there (each file's package IDs read from its header, not decoded; network shares are not searched).
  - **Tests:** media `aafExportWithAvidMedia` (UMIDs as mob IDs, FFmpeg's and Media Composer's half-swapped, against pyaaf2's; a 40-frame grey ramp movie with a tone and timecode, cut with a dissolve, a still and a double-speed shot: two merged stretches of 15 and 14 frames with their timecodes, the still, and the sound, each read back as OP-Atom DNxHR SQ or 24-bit PCM with its package IDs, timecode and tape; imported by Montage frame for frame against the cut (within a third of a frame's grey step), again after the drive is moved so no locator holds and the media is found by mob ID; pyaaf2, which CI now installs, reads the AAF and the MXF files: every master mob is its file's material package and every file mob its source package, the slots used are the files' essence tracks, and the descriptors are VC-3 and PCM in their containers; an inverted first shot comes out as shot when left off (into the moved drive's MXF folder, as folder 2) and inverted when baked, each clip a file (the first 11 frames, from its source's timecode and tape) and frame for frame against the cut; over MCP; cancelled, nothing left; a drive that is not there refused; DNxHD refused for a 320 x 240 sequence with nothing written), app `exportAafFromTheFileMenu` (the menu item; the export with media into a numbered folder).
  - **Not yet:** opening the result in Media Composer (not available here); sound as the used stretches rather than whole sources, and a stereo source as one master clip (each mono file is its own, as Media Composer's own OP-Atom sound is per file); DNxHD's other rates and its interlaced and 720p flavours; transparency (titles over black when baked).
- [x] 15. MV-HEVC out (spatial video for Apple Vision Pro): both eyes coded as MV-HEVC with the spatial-video boxes, through x265's own multiview encoder (S–M)
  - Done: x265 4.2 is downloaded (hash checked) and built with its multiview encoder (`ENABLE_MULTIVIEW`, which distribution builds leave out) as an 8-bit static library with hidden symbols, so it never stands in for the x265 FFmpeg loads (`MONTAGE_WITH_SPATIAL_VIDEO`, on by default; nasm on Linux and Windows, NEON on Apple Silicon). `render/SpatialVideo`: `SpatialVideoEncoder` takes both eyes side by side and gives x265 the left as the base view and the right as the second (two pictures a frame, layer ids 0 and 1), its parameter sets once (the base view's VPS, SPS, PPS and the 3D reference displays, scene, acquisition and view position SEI messages for the hvcC; the second view's SPS and PPS as the L-HEVC configuration record); CRF or a bit rate, the preset, keyframes every 2 s and the colour tags. An export with Stereo 3D "Spatial video (MV-HEVC, for Vision Pro)" (`stereo` "mv") renders both eyes side by side through FFmpeg's raw video path, codes them with it, and has FFmpeg's MOV/MP4 muxer write them as an hvc1 track one eye wide; then `writeSpatialVideoBoxes` adds lhvC and Apple's vexu (eyes: stri left and right; hero: left; cams: the baseline in micrometres; cmfy: no disparity adjustment) and hfov (thousandths of a degree) to the sample entry (`addVideoEntryBoxes`, which shares SA3D's rewrite of the movie header). It needs H.265 (x265 or hardware HEVC chosen), MOV or MP4, and 8-bit SDR; alpha, interlacing and CEA-608 are refused. Export dialog: the choice with Camera baseline (mm, 64) and Field of view (°, 60), shown only for it. MCP `montage_render` `stereo_view` "mv", `spatial_baseline_mm` and `spatial_fov`. Packages carry x265's licence, and the release sources its tarball.
  - **Tests:** media `spatialVideoOut` (a sequence whose left eye is red and right blue, exported as spatial video: the hvc1 entry holds hvcC, lhvC, vexu and hfov; stri, hero, blin, dadj and hfov as set; lhvC version 1 with the second view's SPS and PPS; the hvcC's VPS and the 3D reference displays message; read back at one eye's size and length, the left eye red, and with FFmpeg 7.1 or later as "mv" with the right eye blue; ProRes, Matroska and PQ refused), `stereoscopic3d` (MCP: H.264 and a 200° field of view refused, a spatial render read back), app `stereoscopic3dUi` (the baseline and field of view rows only for spatial video). Checked by hand with FFmpeg 8.0: both views decode, the left red and the right blue, its view positions left and right and its field of view 71.5°.
  - **Not yet:** playing the file on a Vision Pro (not available here); 10-bit and HDR spatial video (x265's multiview encoder is 8-bit here); hardware MV-HEVC encoding.

# Phase 9

The eighth gap analysis (`docs/research/phase9-roadmap.md`, October 2026) ranks what is still missing against Premiere 26.5, After Effects 26.5, Resolve 21.1, Final Cut Pro 12.4 and Media Composer 2026.8, with Baselight, Nuke, Pro Tools, Nuendo, Descript, CapCut, Kdenlive and Shotcut, and open tools (FFmpeg 8.1, OpenColorIO 2.5, OpenTimelineIO 0.18, OpenFX 1.5, OpenAPV, Open Media Transport). All 15 approved by S on 2026-10-22. Two use models whose training data's terms are unclear (#5, #11); interop with a current Media Composer bin (#9) and with vMix and OBS (#15) cannot be checked here.

- [x] 1. Dialogue repair, the rest of the set: De-Clip for distorted recordings, De-Plosive for popped Ps and Bs, De-Wind for wind on location mics, and Breath Control to turn breaths down (S–M)
  - Done: in `audio/AudioRepair` beside De-Click, run on the clip's whole source audio and cached as it is (`audio/SpeechCleanup`), in the Effects browser under Restoration and through MCP `montage_add_effect` (`declip`, `deplosive`, `dewind`). De-Clip (`declip`): each side of zero's clip level is where its samples pile up in the histogram's top bins (so an offset recording that clips sooner on one side is handled), or one level is set for both (within the setting's 0.1 dB step); runs at it (with up to 32 samples between them grouped) are rebuilt by least-squares autoregressive interpolation (order 32, solved with a banded Cholesky, so long runs stay quick), first from a predictor fitted to the clean sound either side and then again from one fitted to that result, with samples that fall back inside the clip level held at it, and any more than 12 dB past it held there (up to 12 passes); when the rebuilt peaks pass −0.1 dBFS the whole clip is lowered just enough to fit, keeping their shape. De-Plosive (`deplosive`): a zero-phase 150 Hz low band of the channels' mix, its level in 5 ms frames against its own median over a second; a pop rises by 13 dB or more at the default sensitivity (20 at 0, 6 at 100), fast, with the low band 3 dB over the rest, and has each channel's own low band taken down by up to the set reduction, faded in and out (one channel's low band held at a time). De-Wind (`dewind`): short-time spectra (2048, hop 512). With two channels, each bin under 500 to 700 Hz is turned down by how little of its smoothed level the other channel shares, after allowing for a voice louder on one mic (found from 1 to 8 kHz over the whole recording, from the cross level, which the wind's part cancels out of). With one channel, identical ones, or one silent (the live one judged), gusts under 100 Hz are turned down by how far they jump over the bin's recent level, never taken to be under the bin's median over the recording (so a silent start does not leave the rest cut). Max Reduction floors both; only what is removed is overlap-added, so the rest of the sound is left exactly; a third channel and on are left. Breath Control: `media/Breaths` (`findBreaths`: 10 ms frames, unvoiced by autocorrelation at about 16 kHz and only where over the room, 6 dB over the room and 10 dB under the speech, not bright, 0.12 to 0.9 s, outside the transcript's words or at least 0.15 s without them; `breathsInClip`: just the stretch of media the clip plays and a second either side, each file decoded once a run; `lowerBreaths`: four volume keys a breath on the frames the clip plays it, whatever its speed or direction, a frame to fall and a frame to rise, breaths that touch keyed as one dip, keys already inside lowered with it, the line after it moving on as it did, breaths already down left, and a volume driven by an expression or with repeating keys left alone), Clip › Breath Control… (the amount remembered; Cancel keys nothing) and MCP `montage_breath_control` (audio clips or a track; the breaths found, on the timeline, how many were keyed and how many clips were left).
  - **Tests:** media `dialogueRepair` (a synthetic voice and a sweep clipped at −6 dBFS: the error where they were clipped from 12.2 to 23.5 dB and 7.4 to 18.6 dB under the signal, unclipped samples identical, an unclipped file unchanged, a level typed as −6.0 finding a clip at −6.02 dBFS; the voice clipped at +0.6 and −0.4, both sides rebuilt (the top 24 dB better); the voice clipped at full scale: peak −0.1 dBFS, error where clipped from 24.4 to 48.0 dB under the signal after matching the level; six 40 ms 80 Hz pops in a voice all found and 22 dB down with the sound above 200 Hz changed 0.01 dB, none found in the clean voice; wind on one mic of a stereo voice 14.5 dB down with the change to the clean mic 30 dB under the voice, a mono voice with no wind changed by less than −25 dB, the voice 6 dB quieter on one mic left on both, a dead second channel leaving the live one's voice, and one mic starting with a second of silence then the voice over a 70 Hz rumble left; three breaths between four lines found within 50 ms with and without words, and an "s" before a line not taken for one; keyed 12 dB down with the lines at their level, a second run keying nothing, a reversed clip keyed where it plays each breath, an expression-driven volume left; over MCP on a clip 0.3 s into the timeline playing from 0.2 s into its file), app `breathControlFromTheMenu` (nothing selected, then two breaths keyed down 12 dB and the lines left, and Undo).
  - **Not yet:** De-Plosive gated by the speech detector rather than the low band's own level; De-Wind's gust mode on a recording of real wind (the test's wind is modulated low noise); breaths found by spectral flatness as well; Breath Control cancelled in the middle of reading a long file (it stops between clips).
- [x] 2. Floating-point pictures: values above 1 kept end to end, so OpenEXR, ACES and scene-linear grades no longer clip; OpenFX plugins told the clip's colour space (L)
  - Done: decoded frames (`media/Image.h` `Frame16`) hold either 16-bit codes or IEEE half floats (`half`), the same eight bytes a pixel, so the frame cache keeps its size. Every OpenEXR file is now read with the OpenEXR library (when built in) into half floats, unclamped, its premultiplied colour made straight; FFmpeg's floating-point pictures (PFM, its own OpenEXR reader, float TIFF) are read component by component (`av_read_image_line2`) rather than through its scaler, which clips them, and scaled by `halfFrame` (an alpha-weighted area average down, bilinear up). Interpret Footage's alpha, rotation and the float picture's way into the compositor keep half floats; analysis that works on the visible range (scene detection, tracking, segmentation, visual search, faces, text, pulldown, thumbnails, lens calibration) reads them clamped to 0..1 (`Frame16::unit`, `code`). Colour conversion (`convertColor`) works out any pixel outside 0..1 exactly rather than clipping it into its table; Curves carry on past either end at their end's slope (CPU and GPU), Hue Curves stay as they were. A half-float frame goes to the GPU compositor as a float picture, so nothing clips there either. OpenFX 1.5 colour management (`render/Ofx.cpp`): the host offers the core colourspaces of the native config (`ofx-native-v1.5_aces-v1.3_ocio-v2.3`); with a plugin that takes it (core, or basic), Montage asks for its preferred colourspaces (`GetClipPreferences`) and gives it its pictures in the first it can make (the working space and its basic name, the display spaces, sRGB and gamma 2.4 textures, linear Rec.709, Rec.2020 and P3, ACEScg, ACES2065-1, ACEScct and the config's roles; raw left as it is), re-encoded with nothing clipped or tone mapped, and tells it on its clips; `GetOutputColourspace` says what its output is in (a clip it names, raw, or by default its source's), which is brought back into the working space.
  - **Tests:** media `floatPicturesKeepTheirRange` (a half-float EXR holding 16.0 decoded as 16.0 and analysed as 1.0; four stops down by Color Correct it reads 1.0, where it read 0.0625, and 0.25 reads 0.0156; Curves leave 16.0; exported to OpenEXR one stop down it keeps 8.0; a PFM read through FFmpeg keeps 6.0 and −0.25), plugins `hostsOpenFxVideoPlugins` (the test plugin Test Colour agrees the core style, asks for ACEScg and is given a grey of 4.0 in Rec.709 as 4^2.4 there, told its source is ACEScg; passed through, its output comes back as it went in).
  - **Not yet:** a scene-linear working space (sequences still work in a display space; ACEScg through an ACES 2.0 output, CPU against GPU, waits on it); effects that clip (keys' mattes, blend modes, LUTs baked on 0..1) not yet marked in the Inspector; half floats uploaded to the GPU directly (they go as 32-bit float pictures); OpenFX's OCIO and full styles (offered as core).
- [x] 3. Damaged-frame repair: find frames that are broken (decoder errors, blocky digital hits, tape drop-out lines, a single flash or black frame, a frame missing from the timestamps) and rebuild each from its neighbours (S–M)
  - Done: `render/FrameRepair`. `findDamagedFrames` reads the file at most 480 pixels wide. A frame is damaged when the decoder says so (`Frame16::damaged`, from FFmpeg's `decode_error_flags` and corrupt flag), when it repeats the frame before because its own time is missing (unless repeats run all through the file, as with a variable frame rate), when it is black within a shot (lit frames alike on both sides within four frames, so not the bottom of a fade nor black cut in between shots), or when it differs from what the last good frame and each of the next two make at its time (`interpolateFrames`, along the motion): by more than 0.15 in mean brightness while it shares no structure with them (a flash frame; one that keeps the picture's structure is a camera flash and left), or in more pixels than five times the median of the shot's recent frames (at least 0.3%, judged once three are known), as drop-out when most of that lies in whole lines, else as a hit. Two predictions, so a broken frame after this one does not make it look broken; no frame next to a cut (luma histograms 0.35 apart, or a layout of 32-across block means that no longer correlates) is judged, nor one more than three frames from the last good frame. The Frame Repair effect (Effects › Repair, run where the source frame is read, before the film-scan effects) lists the frames as text and rebuilds each (and both frames slow motion makes an in-between from) from the nearest good frames up to 12 away, by RIFE when installed, else along the optical flow; a hit or drop-out only where it differs from what is made, grown and softened; Show Repaired tints it red. `repairClip` / Clip › Repair Damaged Frames… (selected video clips, else the one under the playhead; found off the interface thread, one undo step) and MCP `montage_find_damaged_frames` (`repair` false only lists) add the effect and a red "Damaged: kind" marker where the clip shows each frame, replacing the clip's earlier ones.
  - **Tests:** media `damagedFramesFoundAndRepaired` (texture panning 3 px a frame over three shots: a hit, 12 drop-out lines, a black and a white flash frame found as such, and nothing else, not the two cuts, a camera flash or a black frame cut in between shots, nor anything in the clean file; repaired to 41 to 57 dB from 7 to 25 dB against the clean frames, the other frames untouched, four markers, a second scan listing nothing new, Show Repaired tinting, at half speed the in-between after the black frame made from the repaired one; a cut between two shots lit alike (one upside down) leaving nothing found; over MCP listing only, then repairing; a keyframe-only copy with a frame missing from its timestamps and a packet's bytes overwritten: the missing frame and the broken one found (as a decode error, or as a repeat where FFmpeg drops it), and only those), app `repairDamagedFramesFromTheMenu` (nothing to scan, then a white frame in a grey shot found, listed, marked, and Undo).
  - **Not yet:** a Quality Check entry; damage that lasts several frames in a row (each is judged against neighbours that may be broken too; all of a run are filled from the good frames round it once listed); interlaced drop-out on one field only; a broken frame at the very start or end of a shot (judged only with good frames of the same shot either side); tried only on synthetic footage, not yet a real damaged transfer or fast handheld shots.
- [x] 4. Dialogue intelligibility: a lane showing where the dialogue is hard to follow under music and effects, band by band, live or analysed, with Quality Check limits set per broadcaster (S–M)
  - Done: `render/Intelligibility`. The dialogue is what the heard Dialogue clips play and the masker the whole mix less it (so music ducked under the dialogue counts as heard); with no Dialogue clips, the mix separated by Bandit v2 (its dialogue stem, and the mix less it as the masker; 48 kHz sequences, when the model is installed). Each 400 ms block's 18 one-third-octave band levels (160 Hz to 8 kHz; Hann-windowed 8192-point spectra every 4096 samples, both channels' power) at the listening level (a −20 dBFS sound at 79 dB SPL by default) give the Speech Intelligibility Index by ANSI S3.5-1997's one-third-octave procedure: spectrum levels, the speech's self-masking 24 dB down, upward spread of masking, the internal noise for normal hearing, level distortion, and the band-importance weights for average speech. A block is speech where the dialogue is within 20 dB of its usual level (and over −60 dBFS). Stretches under a limit for a set time bridge pauses of up to a second. Presets judge the figure (Standard red under 0.45, amber under 0.6; Accessible 0.6 / 0.75; Lenient 0.35 / 0.5), not any broadcaster's own numbers, which are not published as an index. Sequence › Dialogue Intelligibility… (preset remembered; off the interface thread) fills a lane along the foot of the ruler, faded once the project is edited (by its edit history, so selecting a track does not), and the meters' INT readout follows the playhead; Quality Check's Dialogue row (preset and seconds; Dialogue clips only, never the slow separation) and `intelligibility` on MCP `montage_quality_check` mark the stretches; MCP `montage_intelligibility` returns the average, the stretches with their three most masked bands (by importance lost), and markers.
  - **Tests:** media `dialogueIntelligibility` (the standard speech spectrum alone above 0.95, under equal masking 0.35 to 0.6, under 15 dB more under 0.05, importance summing to 1; JFK over pink noise at −10, 0, +10 and +20 dB: 0.11, 0.31, 0.55 and 0.75, rising, red at −10 and green at +20; music under 500 Hz as loud as the noise scoring at least 0.15 more; a 3 s swell in a 33 s mix found from within 0.5 s of its start, alone, by the index and by Quality Check; over MCP the one stretch, its three bands and one marker; without Dialogue clips, asked for roles, it says to give the Dialogue role), app `dialogueIntelligibilityFromTheMenu` (nothing to measure, then red over a swell and green before it in the lane, the INT readout under 0.45 there, and both cleared for a new project).
  - **Not yet:** measured live during playback (the readout shows the analysis at the playhead); a hearing-loss setting (the threshold term is in the formula, at normal hearing); surround mixes measured per channel rather than as their stereo; the lane showing each band (bands are given only over MCP); the separated path has no test of its own yet.
- [ ] 5. Separate overlapping speakers: where two people talk at once on one mic, split them onto a track each, with a third track for everything else (M; model's training data flagged)
- [x] 6. Sample-accurate sound: move, trim and slip audio clips by single samples, fades and volume keys between frames, markers and keyframes at sub-frame times, and timecode shown with samples (S–M)
  - Done: a sound clip keeps whole frames on the timeline, with `Clip::head` and `tail` the silent part of its first and last frame, so its sound starts and ends to the sample; keys (`Keyframe::sub`) and markers (`Marker::sub`) can sit between frames. `edit::editSoundSamples` moves a clip's sound by samples (its keys with it; picture and linked clips stay), trims its start or end, or slips what it plays: within its own frames where it can, else into free frames beside it (not into another clip, past its media, through a transition whose frames would change, or with a speed ramp). Splitting gives each part only its own end's offset. The mixer plays each clip from its sound's start to its end, and keyed clip volume is worked out at every sample (`Param::atTime`, keys at fractional times; Bezier handles and Loop, Ping-pong and Offset repeats follow), so a fade or a volume key lands on its sample; track and bus automation reads keys between frames too. Projects store the offsets (older versions read whole frames). Clip › Edit Sound by Samples… (Move, Trim start, Trim end, Slip, in samples, one undo step; the sound's start and end as timecode and samples, "00:00:01:12+0801") and MCP `montage_sound_samples` (with `volume_keys` at timeline samples), `at_samples` on `montage_add_marker`; the timeline shades the silent part of a clip's first and last frame and draws a marker between frames where it sits.
  - **Tests:** media `soundToTheSample` (a click in a steady level from frame 10: moved 7 samples within its frames and 20 back into the frame before, trimmed 100 and 50 samples at its ends, slipped 10, the first and last samples heard and the click each on the sample; a Hold volume key at frame 20.5 silencing from that sample, and moving 3 samples later with the sound; saved and loaded; split parts each keeping their own end's offset; refused into the next clip, to nothing, past the media's start and for a picture clip; over MCP a move, a volume key and a marker at a sample), app `soundBySamplesDialog` (the menu asking for a sound clip; a move and an end trim in one undo step; a move into the next clip refused with nothing changed; Undo).
  - **Not yet:** the playhead and timecode fields stay on frames (samples are shown in the dialog, over MCP and in the drawing); dragging by samples on the timeline at high zoom; keys between frames set from the timeline's lanes (they are drawn on their frame); interchange (EDL, AAF, FCPXML, OTIO) rounds the sound to frames.
- [x] 7. Audio analysis: a phase correlation meter and goniometer per channel pair, a spectrum analyser (third-octave and FFT, with peak hold), and Quality Check entries for mono compatibility, an inverted channel and DC offset (S)
  - Done: `media/StereoAnalysis`. `StereoAnalyser` keeps the sums of left², right² and left×right over the last 300 ms: the correlation (0 when either side is silent) and what a mono fold-down ((L+R)/2) loses against the stereo (0 dB for mono, 3 dB for unrelated sides, capped at 60), and the latest samples as goniometer points (mid up, side across). `SpectrumAnalyser` takes Hann-windowed 8192-point spectra 20 times a second (both channels' power): 4096 bins scaled so a full-scale sine on a bin reads 0 dB, and the 31 ISO third-octave bands from 20 Hz to 20 kHz (each bin's share inside the band's edges) scaled so a full-scale sine reads 0 dB from about 63 Hz up (the bands below are narrower than the FFT resolves, so a tone there reads up to 4 dB low); held peaks fall 20 dB a second; K-weighting is BS.1770's two filters' response. The Meters dock gains a CORR and MONO readout, a correlation bar (green, amber under 0, red under −0.5), the goniometer and the spectrum (third octaves or FFT, K-weighted, peaks; remembered) of what is heard, fed by `PlaybackController::audioAnalysis` ten times a second. Quality Check (Sound: "Out of phase or a channel inverted", "DC offset"; MCP `phase`, `phase_seconds`, `dc_offset_db` on `montage_quality_check`) judges each 50 ms window over the 350 ms round it where both sides are above −60 dBFS: a channel inverted where the correlation is under −0.9 at levels within 6 dB, out of phase where it is under −0.5 (at equal levels the fold-down loses 6 dB or more), each for at least a second, with the fold-down's loss in the text; and DC offset where a side's mean over a second (a last part under a second joining the one before; nothing under half a second judged) is above −40 dBFS. Global Mute no longer silences the meters, as it was meant to.
  - **Tests:** media `stereoPhaseAndSpectrum` (the same noise both sides +1 and 0 dB lost, one inverted −1 and 60 dB, unrelated within 0.05 of 0 and 3 dB; only the last 300 ms counting; a left-only goniometer on the diagonal; a −6 dBFS tone on a bin read at −6 dB in its bin (0.1 dB) and band (0.5 dB) and under −60 two bands off, the held peak 10 dB down half a second after; K-weighting about +0.7 dB at 1 kHz and +4 dB at 4 kHz, applied to the bins; Quality Check over a 10 s mix finding the inverted 3 to 6 s (60 dB lost) and the out-of-phase 7.5 to 9 s, nothing when 4 s is asked for, a −26 dBFS DC offset from 2 s and nothing with the check off; over MCP), app `audioAnalysisMeters` (in the Meters dock, an inverted tone reading −1.00 and −60.0, −6 dB in its band and bin, the modes drawn, K-weighting asked of the players).
  - **Not yet:** each channel pair of a surround sequence (what is heard is its stereo); mono compatibility per frequency band (comb filtering from a delayed copy loses highs in mono without moving the broadband figure); the analysis while scrubbing or paused.
- [x] 8. Upmix: stereo to 5.1, 7.1, 5.1.4 or 7.1.4 (and first-order ambisonics), with dialogue kept in the centre, and folding back to the same stereo (S–M)
  - Done: `audio/Upmix` (`Upmixer`, streaming, 2048 samples' latency). A short-time Fourier transform (2048-point square-root Hann windows, 75 % overlap) splits each bin into a direct part and an ambient part: from the two channels' powers and cross-power, averaged over about 150 ms and four bins either side (with the coherence two unrelated noises keep after that averaging taken off), the ambient power is the smaller eigenvalue of their covariance, and each side's direct part its Wiener share. The direct part keeps its place: what both sides have in common, in phase, goes to the centre and is taken from each side as much as the centre folds back into it, so the fold-down gives exactly the direct sound's left and right whatever its level, phase or time between them (a centred sound all in the centre, a sound in one channel left there, one side inverted left at the sides), and Centre Width keeps more of it at the sides. The ambient part stays partly in front and goes partly (Surround Depth, default 50 %) to the speakers behind and, in layouts with them, above (Height, default 30 %), each speaker through three all-passes of its own after a delay (Surround Delay, 10 ms), at a level that keeps the fold-down's loudness. In an ambisonic sequence the direct sound is encoded across the front and the ambient sound from behind and above. The LFE can be given a copy of the sum under 120 Hz (off by default; the mains keep it). The Upmix effect goes on an audio track: in a surround or ambisonic sequence the track's stereo, after its other inserts, is spread over the speakers instead of panned (its LFE send kept), its latency made up like a plugin's, with its Dialogue-role clips (stems from Separate Stems are given that role) taken apart, through their own copy of the track's inserts, to the centre alone, as mono; routed to a bus it passes the bus's fader and mute but not its inserts; elsewhere (a stereo sequence, a clip, a bus) it only delays as much, so nothing moves. MCP `montage_track_effect` with `upmix`.
  - **Tests:** media `upmixStereoToSurround` (a tone in both channels in the centre at the fold-down's level and over 20 dB above every other speaker; a tone on the left only in L, 30 dB above the rest; independent noise 1.55 times as strong behind as in front, the surrounds' coherence 0.004; in 7.1.4 the overhead speakers over 40 dB down on a centred tone and fed by noise, two speakers on one side at most 0.19 alike at any lag up to 10 ms; a mix of a centred and a hard-left tone, a panned band of noise and unrelated noise folding back from 5.1, 7.1 and 7.1.4 within 0.1 LU, correlated 0.99 on each side, and a band of noise with one side inverted or 0.5 ms later folding back correlated over 0.99; the stereo given back exactly 2048 samples late; no depth leaving the surrounds silent, full width the centre; the LFE carrying 50 Hz and not 1 kHz; dialogue given apart only in the centre; ambisonics straight ahead; in a 5.1 sequence a click on its sample in the centre, also routed to a bus with an Upmix and with one on the master, a hard-left Dialogue clip in the centre (and left again with the option off), in a stereo sequence the mix sample-identical with and without it; over MCP).
  - **Not yet:** a track's dynamics (a gate, a compressor) hear its dialogue and the rest apart, as they are run apart; upmixing a bus or a clip; dialogue taken from a finished mix on the fly (it comes from Dialogue clips, so a mix is split with Separate Stems first); wides; listening tests against commercial upmixers.
- [x] 9. Open Avid bins: read Media Composer `.avb` bins directly: master clips, subclips, group and multigroup clips, sequences, locators, bin columns and custom metadata, linked to the Avid MediaFiles they name (S–M)
  - Done: `core/AvidBin` reads a bin into a model, rewritten in C++ from pyavb's reader (MIT, credited): the file's object table and every class pyavb knows (mobs and their tracks; sequences, source clips, fillers, timecode, edge code, track references, transitions, motion and capture-mask effects, selectors and group clips, pan and volume, track effects with their parameters and control points; file, tape, film and the picture and sound descriptors; file, URL and media stream locators; attribute lists, locators and bin view columns), either byte order, unknown classes skipped and damaged or looping ones reported rather than followed (a part that cannot be read keeps its length, so what follows keeps its place; copies of attribute lists and the parts of a sequence walked are counted, so a small hostile file cannot grow without bound). `core/AvidBinImport` brings it into a project: each master clip as media (a sound-only clip's channels as items of their own) in a bin named after the file, linked to its files (where the file mob's locator says, beside the bin, or Media Composer's own MXF media found by mob ID under any "Avid MediaFiles" folder above the bin or on a local drive, as for AAF; else the file it was linked to or transcoded from, a sound track playing its channel of it; else offline with its name and length, one item per file mob so two clips of one name stay two), its custom columns as metadata (Scene, Shot, Take, Tape, Camera, Description and Comments as Montage's own fields), subclips as subclips of it, and each sequence as an AAF of it would come in (`core/AafImport`'s timeline builder): clips followed through master clips to their files, fillers as gaps, dissolves at their cut points, a constant motion effect as clip speed (reversed too), a group clip's selected angle, nested sequences as nested sequence clips, locators as markers with their colours; effects Montage has no counterpart for are left off (what they were applied to kept) and named. The media finding is shared with AAF import (`core/AvidMediaIndex`). File › Import Media Composer Bin… (and Import Timeline), MCP `montage_import_timeline` with an `.avb`.
  - **Tests:** media `avidBinReader` (pyavb's sample bin: its 160 mobs, view and columns, the sequence's tracks and components; damaged copies; and with MONTAGE_TEST_PYAVB, every mob compared with pyavb's own reading of the sample, of the sample rewritten big-endian, and of a bin pyavb writes; a hostile bin whose sequence and attributes each name the next twice, 48 deep, read and imported at once with what was cut short said) and `avidBinImport` (the sample: 51 master clips offline in its bin, the sequence of 52 clips, the three effects it leaves off named; a bin pyavb writes from an AAF of a cut with its Media Composer media (a dissolve, a still over a gap, a clip at double speed, two-channel sound), with a subclip, custom columns and a locator added: opened linked to the drive's MXF files, clip for clip (start, length, speed, source in-point, channels) the cut its AAF imports as and frame for frame the cut Montage made, the columns as metadata, the subclip's stretch, the locator a blue marker; the drive moved, every file found by mob ID; through MCP; a file that is not a bin refused).
  - **Not yet:** checking against a bin from a current Media Composer (none here: pyavb's sample is from 2012); group and multigroup clips in a bin as multicam clips (a sequence plays a group clip's selected angle); bins written back; titles' and effects' renders.
- [x] 10. Film counts and lists: feet and frames in timecode displays, KeyKode and ink numbers from scans and logs, burnt-in key numbers, and cut, optical, dupe and pull lists, with change lists in feet and frames (S–M)
  - Done: `core/FilmCount`: feet and frames for 35 mm 4-perf, 3-perf (a frame's starting perforation given where a foot is not whole frames: `22+00.2`), 2-perf, 16 mm and 65 mm 5-perf, read back exactly; key numbers (a count every 64 perforations of 35 mm, 120 of 65 mm, 20 frames of 16 mm) and ink numbers (a count a foot) moved along a roll by frames and measured between two frames of a roll. A DPX or Cineon scan's film header (manufacturer and film type codes, prefix, count, perforation offset; format, slate, frame position) gives a scan's first key number as it is imported (`key_number`, its perforation offset kept; `film_format`, `slate`) and its gauge (`film_gauge`: how far the second frame's key number is past the first's), so its key numbers count in its own gauge whatever the sequence's; ALE's KN Start and Ink Number columns read and written. Each sequence has a film gauge (Sequence Settings › Film, saved); Playback › Counter shows every readout (the monitors, the timeline's ruler and playhead, the Inspector, the stacked timelines) as timecode, or feet and frames from zero or from the first picture (a leader counting back), and the Program monitor's readout takes feet and frames typed in. Burn-ins gain feet and frames, key number and ink number (export dialog, MCP `burn_in`). `render/FilmLists`: the cut list (each shot's footage, roll, key and ink numbers in and out), the optical list (dissolves, dips, wipes and fades with each side's negative, handles included and any the media lacks said; speed changes, reverse, ramps; repositions; superimpositions; titles), the dupe list (negative used twice, handles included), the pull list (shots by roll in negative order) and, from an earlier version, Compare with Sequence's change list, in feet and frames, as text, CSV or PDF. File › Export Film Lists…, MCP `montage_film_lists` (and `film_gauge` on `montage_create_project`).
  - **Tests:** core `filmCounts` (every gauge's feet and frames written and read back from -70 to 400 frames, 3-perf across its 64-frame cycle, key and ink numbers moved and measured, 16 mm's half-foot keys, a DPX and a Cineon film header, ALE columns in and out, the gauge saved); media `filmListsFromScans` (three rolls of DPX frames written with KeyKode in every header, two 4-perf and one 3-perf: each roll's gauge found from its frames (a 4-perf roll whose frames start off the key mark included), each frame's key number worked out from the first is the one written on it, across two 64-frame cycles; a cut of three shots with a dissolve and a range used twice gives each shot's key numbers in and out as the headers say, the dissolve with both sides' negative, the dupe and the pull list; a recut's change list in feet and frames; CSV, and a PDF whose rows read back laid out (with pdftotext where installed); 3-perf footage; through MCP; the burnt-in footage and key number read back by Montage's text reader); app `filmCounterAndLists` (the gauge in Sequence Settings, the counter's modes and typed feet and frames, the lists from the menu).
  - **Not yet:** FLEx telecine logs; key numbers of footage inside nested or multicam clips in burn-ins (the lists see multicam angles); Cinema Tools-style database matchback from video to film; audio EDLs in feet and frames; key numbers burnt in from the picture itself when a scan's header has none.
- [ ] 11. Shot logging that sees movement: camera moves tagged per shot, what happens across the shot, and the people in it by name (M; model's training data flagged)
- [x] 12. Sound effects library: index folders of effects outside the project, searched by name, UCS category, embedded descriptions and what the sound is; auditioned with a selection and at another pitch; dragged into the timeline (S–M)
  - Done: `core/Ucs`: the Universal Category System v8.2.1 (public domain) compiled in from its list by `scripts/gen-ucs.py` (82 categories, 753 subcategories with explanations and synonyms), CatIDs looked up regardless of case, and UCS file names taken apart (CatID, user category, FX name, creator and source ids, user data). `media/SoundLibrary`: a library kept in SQLite (the amalgamation built in, with FTS5) in Montage's data folder (or `$MONTAGE_SOUND_LIBRARY`): folders, and per sound its path, size, time, a fingerprint (size and a hash of its first and last 64 KB), length, channels, rate, description (bext, else iXML USER or ASWG DESCRIPTION, else tags), UCS category (from the name, else iXML's CatID), FX name, creator and source, keywords (iXML USER and ASWG fields such as KEYWORDS and LIBRARY, RIFF INFO, ID3 and other tags) and what CLAP heard. WAV, BWF and RF64 are read from their chunks (fast); other formats (AIFF, FLAC, MP3, Ogg, Opus, M4A, CAF, WavPack, W64) through FFmpeg. `scan` lists the folders, reads only new or changed files (outside the lock, so searching goes on), finds renamed or moved files by fingerprint (keeping their entry and what CLAP heard), drops what is gone, and keeps the sounds of a folder that is not there or lists nothing (a drive not mounted, an empty mount point); a file belongs to the deepest library folder it is in; written a few hundred rows a transaction, and a cancelled scan neither adds nor drops. `search` ANDs the start of every word over name, description, CatID, category, subcategory, FX name and keywords. `listen` runs CLAP over sounds not yet heard (10-second windows, as for project media; a file it cannot read is left unheard, not tried again while the library is open); `searchByMeaning` and `moreLike` (each of a sound's windows, up to 16, as a query) rank sounds by their best window. `edit::placeSoundRange`: a stretch of samples placed from a frame, its first sample at the frame's start (a fractional source in) and the rest of its last frame silent (`Clip::tail`), a stretch to the file's end included. The mix now plays a clip's last source sample (it was dropped, as the next one was needed to interpolate). App: the Sound Library panel (Window menu; in the Audio and Editing workspaces): Folders (add, remove, rescan), search as you type or Sounds Like (CLAP, the model offered first), Find More Like This and Show in Folder on a sound, a waveform to drag a stretch across, Space or Play to audition it at a pitch in semitones (pitch shift) or as varispeed, the stretch (or a row: the whole sound) dragged onto the timeline (a ghost of its length on the audio track; `EditorState::placeSound` imports the file once into a "Sound Effects" bin, copied first into a "Sound Effects" folder beside a saved project when that is on). Folders are watched and rescanned as files arrive, and after Montage starts; sounds are listened to in the background when the model is here. MCP `montage_search_sound_library` (words, sounds_like, more_like, add_folder, remove_folder, rescan). Bext descriptions are read as UTF-8 where they are.
  - **Tests:** media `soundLibrary` (500 WAVs in UCS names with bext descriptions across subfolders indexed in well under 10 s (0.08 s here); `DOORWood` finds exactly its 100, `creak` and `cre` exactly the three that creak, words ANDed, iXML keywords; each field read; a rescan with nothing changed doing nothing; a file renamed into another folder keeping its entry, one gone, one rewritten, one new with its category from ASWG; reopened from its file; an absent folder keeping its sounds; a removed folder losing them; an empty folder in a folder's place keeping its sounds; UCS names with a dot in the FX name; a 2 s stretch from 0.5 s and 7 samples dropped at frame 10 of a 29.97 fps sequence, heard from its first sample to its last exactly where they belong and matching the file's samples, and the whole 3 s file to its very last sample; MCP adding a folder and searching; and with the CLAP model, speech and a song each found by their descriptions and the song's first half found most like the song), app `soundLibraryPanel` (search as you type, a stretch chosen, auditioned an octave up and down by varispeed and by semitones (lengths), the drag's data, dropped on the timeline with copying on: the file copied beside the project, imported once, and just the stretch placed).
  - **Not yet:** a sound's own markers and regions (from bext or the file's cue chunk); renaming files to UCS from the panel and writing metadata back; spotting several sounds in a row; libraries on a shared server with one index for a team; waveforms of very long files drawn a part at a time (they are read whole, in the background past two minutes).
